## Mathlib (31 flagged)

### [`RatFunc.Luroth.Φ_natDegree_eq_φ_natDegree`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/FieldTheory/RatFunc/Luroth.lean#L182-L185) — `mathlib-0077`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0077.lean`

- `(h : E ≠ ⊥)` (theorem): The equation also holds when E = ⊥, because of junk values, so we can prove it by cases on `E = ⊥`. If E ≠ ⊥, the existing proof works unchanged. If E = ⊥, then X is transcendental over ⊥: ⊥ is algebraic over K, so if X were integral over ⊥ it would be integral over K by transitivity, which contradicts `transcendental_X`. Hence `φ ⊥ = minpoly ⊥ X = 0` (`minpoly.eq_zero`), and its natDegree is 0. This gives `(φ E).map _ = 0`. Then `Φ' E = integerNormalization _ 0 = 0` by `IsFractionRing.integerNormalization_eq_zero_iff` (K[X] is a domain). So `Φ E = primPart 0 = 1` by `Polynomial.primPart_zero`, since `primPart` is defined as `if p = 0 then 1 else …`. So `natDegree 1 = 0 = natDegree 0`, and the statement holds without `h`.

### [`Module.Basis.toMatrix_mulVec_repr`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Matrix/Basis.lean#L231-L236) — `mathlib-0083`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0083.lean`

- `[Finite ι']` (theorem): Nothing else in the statement mentions `[Finite ι']`. The product `b'.toMatrix b *ᵥ b.repr m` only needs `[Fintype ι]`, because `b'.toMatrix b : Matrix ι' ι R` and the sum in `mulVec` runs over `ι`. Both sides are functions `ι' → R`, and the identity holds entry by entry for any index type `ι'`. For each `i'`, the left side is `∑ i, b'.repr (b i) i' * b.repr m i` (by `Basis.toMatrix_apply`, which is `rfl`). The right side is `b'.repr m i'`. Rewrite `m` as `∑ i, b.repr m i • b i` using `Basis.sum_repr`, which needs only `[Fintype ι]` and is already used as `e.sum_repr` in this file. Then `map_sum`, `Finsupp.finset_sum_apply`, `map_smul`, `Finsupp.smul_apply` and `smul_eq_mul`/`mul_comm` turn the right side into the left side. No step uses finiteness of `ι'`. The degenerate cases (empty `ι`, trivial `R`) are covered by the same computation: the sums are empty or everything is zero. The current proof only needs `Finite ι'` because it goes through `LinearMap.toMatrix`. A direct proof (`ext i'`, then `conv_rhs => rw [← b.sum_repr m]`, then `simp [mulVec, dotProduct, Basis.toMatrix_apply, map_sum, mul_comm]`) works without it.

### [`MeasureTheory.projectiveFamilyContent_mono`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Constructions/ProjectiveFamilyContent.lean#L134-L137) — `mathlib-0116`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0116.lean`

- `(hs : s ∈ measurableCylinders α)` (theorem): `projectiveFamilyContent hP s` is definitionally `projectiveFamilyFun P s`: the lemma `projectiveFamilyContent_eq` proves this by `rfl`, because `IsSetRing.addContent_of_union` sets `toFun := m`. `projectiveFamilyFun P s` is defined as `if hs : s ∈ measurableCylinders α then P _ _ else 0`. The proof without `hs` splits on whether `s ∈ measurableCylinders α` (`by_cases hs : s ∈ measurableCylinders α`). If it is, the original proof `addContent_mono isSetSemiring_measurableCylinders hs ht hst` still works. If it is not, `rw [projectiveFamilyContent_eq, projectiveFamilyFun, dif_neg hs]` reduces the left side to `0`, and `0 ≤ _` holds in `ℝ≥0∞` by `zero_le _`. So the statement stays true and provable for every `s ⊆ t`. `ht` cannot be dropped the same way: if `t` is not a measurable cylinder, its content is `0`, while a measurable cylinder `s ⊆ t` can have positive content.

### [`AdicCompletion.tensor_map_id_left_injective_of_injective`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/RingTheory/AdicCompletion/AsTensorProduct.lean#L367-L373) — `mathlib-0139`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0139.lean`

- `[Module.Finite R N]` (variable): For a Noetherian ring R, `AdicCompletion I R` is flat over R. The instance `AdicCompletion.flat_of_isNoetherian` at line 378 of the same file proves this. Flatness makes `lTensor (AdicCompletion I R) f` injective for every injective `f`, with no finiteness condition on M or N. The lemma for this is `Module.Flat.lTensor_preserves_injective_linearMap` in Mathlib/RingTheory/Flat/Basic.lean. As a function, `AlgebraTensorModule.map LinearMap.id f` is the same map as `f.lTensor _`; both agree on pure tensors.

The flatness instance is currently proved from this very lemma, so the order needs care, but there is no real circularity. The flatness proof only uses the lemma for `J.subtype : J → R` with J an ideal. Both `J` and `R` are finite R-modules when R is Noetherian, and both live in universe u. So the new proof goes as follows. First prove flatness inside the proof: use `Module.Flat.iff_lTensor_injective'`, then the current argument (`tensor_map_id_left_eq_map` and `map_injective`) with M := J and N := R. Then apply `lTensor_preserves_injective_linearMap` to `f` and `hf`. This never uses `Module.Finite R N`. The only remaining hypotheses needed are `[IsNoetherianRing R]` and `hf`. The same argument also removes `[Module.Finite R M]`, so the two can be deleted together.
- `[Module.Finite R M]` (variable): The argument is the same as for `[Module.Finite R N]`. Since R is Noetherian, `AdicCompletion I R` is flat over R. This flatness can be proved inside the proof from the existing finite case: use `Module.Flat.iff_lTensor_injective'` with the inclusion of an ideal J into R, both finite over the Noetherian R, together with `tensor_map_id_left_eq_map` and `map_injective`. Then `Module.Flat.lTensor_preserves_injective_linearMap` gives injectivity of `f.lTensor (AdicCompletion I R)`, which as a function equals `AlgebraTensorModule.map LinearMap.id f`. This holds for any injective `f`, with no finiteness condition on M. Both finiteness hypotheses can be deleted, separately or together.

### [`Convex.norm_image_sub_le_of_norm_fderiv_le`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/MeanValue.lean#L498-L504) — `mathlib-0148`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0148.lean`

- `[IsRCLikeNormedField 𝕜]` (variable): Nothing else in the statement needs this instance to elaborate: `fderiv 𝕜`, `DifferentiableAt 𝕜` and the operator norm only use `NontriviallyNormedField 𝕜`, `NormedSpace 𝕜 E` and `NormedSpace 𝕜 G`. The current proof uses it only through `norm_image_sub_le_of_norm_hasFDerivWithin_le`, which builds an `RCLike 𝕜` instance and restricts scalars to give `G` a real structure. The mean value inequality holds for any nontrivially normed field 𝕜 by a direct argument. Let v = y - x and g(t) = f(x + t•v) for t ∈ [0,1].

(1) The segment lies in s because `hs.mapsTo_lineMap` is used with `xs` and `ys`. At each point of s, f is differentiable over 𝕜, so it is continuous there. Hence g is continuous on [0,1].

(2) Fix t ∈ [0,1) and put L = fderiv 𝕜 f (g t). For z > t, take w = (z-t)•v (real scalar multiplication), so w → 0 in E as z → t. `HasFDerivAt` over 𝕜 gives ‖f(g t + w) - f(g t) - L w‖ = o(‖w‖). The operator-norm bound `le_opNorm`, which holds over any normed field, gives ‖L w‖ ≤ ‖L‖‖w‖ ≤ C (z-t)‖v‖. So for every ε > 0, eventually as z → t⁺ we have ‖g z - g t‖ ≤ (C‖v‖ + ε)(z-t). By the triangle inequality, the slope of t ↦ ‖g t - g 0‖ is therefore eventually < C‖v‖ + ε.

(3) Apply Mathlib's `image_norm_le_of_liminf_right_slope_norm_lt_deriv_boundary` (MeanValue.lean, line 224). It only needs `[NormedAddCommGroup E]`, with no scalar field. Use it with the function t ↦ g t - g 0, the slope bound f' = C‖v‖ + ε, and the boundary B(t) = (C‖v‖ + 2ε)t with B' = C‖v‖ + 2ε. This gives ‖f y - f x‖ ≤ C‖v‖ + 2ε for every ε > 0, hence ‖f y - f x‖ ≤ C‖y - x‖.

The argument never uses an RCLike structure on 𝕜 or a real structure on G. Degenerate cases are fine: if x = y both sides are 0, and the bound on `fderiv` stays valid under its junk value because `hf` gives differentiability on s. So the theorem stays true and provable after deleting `[IsRCLikeNormedField 𝕜]` (via `omit [IsRCLikeNormedField 𝕜] in`).

### [`continuous_parametric_integral_of_continuous`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Bochner/Set.lean#L1121-L1139) — `mathlib-0190`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0190.lean`

- `[FirstCountableTopology X]` (theorem): The current proof needs first countability only for `continuousAt_of_dominated` (Bochner/Basic.lean). That lemma lives under `variable [FirstCountableTopology X]` because dominated convergence needs a countably generated filter. A uniform estimate replaces it, as in the interval version `intervalIntegral.continuous_parametric_primitive_of_continuous` (DominatedConvergence.lean), which assumes only `[TopologicalSpace X]` on X.

Argument: fix x₀ and ε > 0. `IsCompact.mem_uniformity_of_prod hs hf.continuousOn (mem_univ x₀) (dist_mem_uniformity δpos)` (HeineCantor.lean) gives v ∈ 𝓝 x₀ such that ‖f x y - f x₀ y‖ < δ for all x ∈ v and y ∈ s. This uses only compactness of s, not first countability or local compactness of X.

Integrability: each f x is continuous, so it is AEStronglyMeasurable for μ.restrict s by `Continuous.aestronglyMeasurable`, using `SecondCountableTopologyEither Y E` and `OpensMeasurableSpace Y`. It is bounded on s because s is compact. The set {t | ‖f x t‖ ≤ M} is closed, hence measurable, so `ae_restrict_iff` turns the pointwise bound on s into an a.e. bound, exactly as in the current proof. Also μ s < ∞ by `hs.measure_ne_top`.

Conclusion: by `integral_sub`, the difference of the integrals is ∫ y in s, (f x y - f x₀ y) ∂μ. By `norm_setIntegral_le_of_norm_le_const` (Bochner/Set.lean, which needs only μ s < ∞), its norm is at most δ · μ.real s < ε for small δ. So the map is continuous at every x₀.

Degenerate cases: if s is empty or μ s = 0, both integrals are 0 and the bound still holds. Both `[FirstCountableTopology X]` and `[LocallyCompactSpace X]` can be dropped together.
- `[LocallyCompactSpace X]` (theorem): Local compactness is used only to get a compact neighbourhood U of x₀, so that ‖f‖ is bounded by some M on U ×ˢ s, which gives the dominating constant. `IsCompact.mem_uniformity_of_prod hs hf.continuousOn (mem_univ x₀) (dist_mem_uniformity one_pos)` (HeineCantor.lean) gives instead a neighbourhood v of x₀ with ‖f x y - f x₀ y‖ < 1 for x ∈ v and y ∈ s. Since {x₀} ×ˢ s is compact, ‖f x₀ ·‖ ≤ M₀ on s, so ‖f x y‖ ≤ M₀ + 1 on v ×ˢ s.

The rest of the proof is unchanged: `continuousAt_of_dominated` with the constant bound M₀ + 1, which is integrable on s by `integrableOn_const hs.measure_ne_top`, with the a.e. bound obtained through `ae_restrict_iff` and the closed set {t | ‖f x t‖ ≤ M₀ + 1}. Alternatively, the uniform-estimate proof described for `[FirstCountableTopology X]` needs neither instance. Each instance can be deleted alone, and both can be deleted together.

### [`DifferentiableOn.continuousMultilinear_apply_const`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/FDeriv/CompCLM.lean#L208-L211) — `mathlib-0251`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0251.lean`

- `[Finite ι]` (variable): `DifferentiableOn` is defined in TVS generality (FDeriv/Defs.lean, section TVS: only `[AddCommGroup F] [Module 𝕜 F] [TopologicalSpace F]`). The topology on `ContinuousMultilinearMap 𝕜 M H` comes from Topology/Algebra/Module/Multilinear/Topology.lean (`instTopologicalSpace`, uniform convergence on bounded sets), which needs no finiteness of ι. The normed instances in Multilinear/Basic.lean need `[Fintype ι]`, which `[Finite ι]` does not supply, so even the current statement uses the general topology. Deleting `[Finite ι]` therefore leaves the statement elaborating identically. It stays true for arbitrary ι. `ContinuousMultilinearMap.apply 𝕜 M H u : ContinuousMultilinearMap 𝕜 M H →L[𝕜] H` exists in that same file without finiteness (it needs only `[ContinuousConstSMul 𝕜 H]`). Given x ∈ s, take c' with `HasFDerivWithinAt c c' s x`. Its field `isLittleOTVS` says (fun p ↦ c p.1 - c p.2 - c' (p.1 - p.2)) =o[𝕜; L] (fun p ↦ p.1 - p.2). Composing with the evaluation map via `ContinuousLinearMap.isBigOTVS_comp` (Asymptotics/TVS.lean, which assumes only a topology on each space) and `IsBigOTVS.trans_isLittleOTVS` gives the same little-o bound for p ↦ c p.1 u - c p.2 u - ((apply 𝕜 M H u) ∘L c') (p.1 - p.2), equal pointwise by linearity of evaluation. Then `HasFDerivAtFilter.of_isLittleOTVS` gives `HasFDerivWithinAt (fun y ↦ c y u) ((apply 𝕜 M H u) ∘L c') s x`, hence differentiability within s at every x ∈ s. The current proof uses finiteness only because it goes through the Fintype lemma and the chain rule in Comp.lean, which needs the middle space to be normed. The direct TVS argument above does not.

### [`ProbabilityTheory.Kernel.withDensity_rnDeriv_of_subset_compl_mutuallySingularSetSlice`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Probability/Kernel/RadonNikodym.lean#L336-L367) — `mathlib-0258`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0258.lean`

- `(hsm : MeasurableSet s)` (theorem): The equality also holds when `s` is not measurable. Write W = withDensity η (rnDeriv κ η) and S = singularPart κ η.

1. The current proof shows `W a t = κ a t` for every measurable `t ⊆ (mutuallySingularSetSlice κ η a)ᶜ`. Keep it as an internal `have`.
2. With step 1, the proof of `rnDeriv_add_singularPart` (line 380) goes through unchanged and gives `W + S = κ`. That proof only works with measurable sets: it does `ext a s hs` and applies this lemma to `hs.diff hm`.
3. Now take any `s ⊆ (mutuallySingularSetSlice κ η a)ᶜ`, measurable or not. We have `κ a s = (W + S) a s = W a s + S a s`. The second step holds for every set, because `Measure.add_apply`/`Measure.coe_add` (Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Measure/Module.lean) are `rfl` and need no measurability, and kernel addition is pointwise.
4. `S a s = 0` by `singularPart_of_subset_compl_mutuallySingularSetSlice` (line 296). That lemma needs no measurability: it is `measure_mono_null hs (singularPart_compl_mutuallySingularSetSlice κ η a)`. Its instance requirements `[IsSFiniteKernel κ] [IsFiniteKernel η]` follow from the theorem's `IsFiniteKernel` hypotheses.

So `W a s = κ a s` for every such `s`. There are no degenerate cases to worry about, since every step is valid for arbitrary sets. The other hypotheses are needed: `hαγ` is required to elaborate `rnDeriv`/`mutuallySingularSetSlice`, `hs` is essential, and the finiteness of the kernels underlies the whole construction.

### [`add_tsub_add_le_tsub_right`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Algebra/Order/Sub/Defs.lean#L160-L162) — `mathlib-0260`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0260.lean`

- `[AddLeftMono α]` (variable): `[Preorder α] [AddCommSemigroup α] [Sub α] [OrderedSub α]` already make addition monotone, so `AddLeftMono` adds nothing.

**Monotonicity from `OrderedSub`.** Suppose x ≤ y and take any c.
1. `add_tsub_le_right` (Defs.lean line 68, which needs only `OrderedSub`) gives x + c - c ≤ x.
2. Then x + c - c ≤ x ≤ y.
3. `tsub_le_iff_right` (a - b ≤ c ↔ a ≤ c + b, which is the definition of `OrderedSub`) turns this into x + c ≤ y + c.

With commutativity this gives monotonicity on both sides, which is all `AddLeftMono` provides.

**Proof of the theorem without it.**
1. By `tsub_le_iff_left` (line 91, no monotonicity needed), the goal a + c - (b + c) ≤ a - b is equivalent to a + c ≤ (b + c) + (a - b).
2. `le_tsub_add` (line 71, no monotonicity needed) gives a ≤ (a - b) + b.
3. By the monotonicity argument above, a + c ≤ ((a - b) + b) + c.
4. By associativity and commutativity, ((a - b) + b) + c = (b + c) + (a - b), which closes the goal.

The argument does not depend on any special case: empty types or a trivial order cause no problem.

### [`MeasureTheory.Measure.prod_smul_left`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Measure/Prod.lean#L877-L881) — `mathlib-0275`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0275.lean`

- `[hν : SFinite ν]` (variable): `Measure.prod` is defined (Prod.lean, line 172) as `if SFinite ν then bind μ (fun x => map (Prod.mk x) ν) else 0`. The lemma stays true without the instance; split on `SFinite ν`.

(1) If `ν` is s-finite, the existing proof works unchanged. It uses `prod_apply` and `Measure.smul_apply`, which is the only place the instance is needed.

(2) If `ν` is not s-finite, unfolding `Measure.prod` makes both `(c • μ).prod ν` and `μ.prod ν` equal to `0`. The goal becomes `0 = c • (0 : Measure (α × β))`. Prove it with `ext s`, `Measure.smul_apply`, and `c • (0:ℝ≥0∞) = 0`. That last fact follows from `IsScalarTower R ℝ≥0∞ ℝ≥0∞`: c • 0 = c • (0 • 0) = (c • 0) • 0 = (c • 0) * 0 = 0.

The lemma's `c` is an arbitrary `R` with these instances, and no other hypothesis constrains `ν`, so the degenerate case needs nothing else.

Another route for the s-finite case: `prod_eq_of_sfinite` reduces it to `Measure.bind_smul` (GiryMonad.lean, line 303), which is built from `Measure.map_smul` and `join_smul`.

The other `variable` instance, `[SFinite μ]`, is not an argument of this lemma. It refers to the section `μ`, which the binder `{μ : Measure α}` shadows.

### [`openSegment_translate_preimage`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Analysis/Convex/Segment.lean#L275-L277) — `mathlib-0342`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0342.lean`

- `[AddRightMono 𝕜]` (variable): It is an argument because it is instance-implicit and mentions only `𝕜`, which is already an argument. The current proof uses it only indirectly, through mem_openSegment_translate → vadd_openSegment → image_openSegment → openSegment_eq_image, which uses order lemmas such as lt_add_of_pos_left and sub_pos. The statement itself is pure algebra. By definition, openSegment 𝕜 x y = {z | ∃ α β, 0 < α ∧ 0 < β ∧ α + β = 1 ∧ α • x + β • y = z}. When α + β = 1, smul_add, add_smul and one_smul give α • (a + b) + β • (a + c) = (α + β) • a + (α • b + β • c) = a + (α • b + β • c). Then add_left_cancel in the group E shows a + z ∈ openSegment 𝕜 (a+b) (a+c) ↔ z ∈ openSegment 𝕜 b c, with the same witnesses α, β in both directions, so the conditions 0 < α, 0 < β carry over unchanged. Nothing compares sums in 𝕜, so only Ring 𝕜, PartialOrder 𝕜, AddCommGroup E and Module 𝕜 E are needed. Degenerate cases (trivial 𝕜, or no elements strictly between 0 and 1) are covered: both sides are empty together. New proof under `omit [AddRightMono 𝕜] in`: Set.ext, unfold openSegment, rewrite with smul_add, ← add_smul, the hypothesis α + β = 1 and one_smul, and finish with add_right_inj.

### [`MeasureTheory.StronglyMeasurable.tprod'`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Constructions/Polish/StronglyMeasurable.lean#L128-L141) — `mathlib-0348`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0348.lean`

- `[L.NeBot]` (variable): The proof uses `[L.NeBot]` in only one place: the final branch calls `stronglyMeasurable_of_tendsto`, which requires `[NeBot u]`. The statement still holds when `L.filter = ⊥`, so we can split on `L.NeBot`. If it holds, the existing proof goes through unchanged. If it fails, `tprod_bot` (Topology/Algebra/InfiniteSum/Defs.lean, which takes `hL : ¬L.NeBot`) rewrites `∏'[L] i, f i` to `∏ᶠ i, f i`. Here the finite product is taken in the monoid `X → E`. Next, `finprod_def'` (or `finprod_def`) gives two cases. If the multiplicative support is finite, the result is a `Finset` product of the functions `mulIndicator (f i)`, and each of these is `f i` or `1`. That product is strongly measurable by `Finset.stronglyMeasurable_prod`, which needs only `[ContinuousMul E]`, together with `h` and `stronglyMeasurable_one`. If the support is infinite, the result is `1`, which is strongly measurable by `stronglyMeasurable_one`. This is the same reasoning as the first two branches of the current proof, none of which uses `NeBot`. The `h.choose` branch of `tprod_def` can never be reached in the `⊥` case, because `HasProd f 1 L` always holds there (`hasProd_bot`). So the theorem stays true and provable without `[L.NeBot]`.

### [`Affine.Triangle.prod_eq_prod_one_sub_of_mem_line_point_lineMap`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/AffineSpace/Ceva.lean#L134-L195) — `mathlib-0355`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0355.lean`

- `[NoZeroDivisors k]` (variable): The statement holds over any commutative ring. The current proof only needs `NoZeroDivisors` for the final `mul_left_cancel₀`; a different argument avoids it.

**Reduction to equations in k.** By `mem_affineSpan_pair_iff_exists_lineMap_eq`, which only needs a `Ring`, each hypothesis gives some s_i with p' = lineMap (t.points i) (lineMap p_{i+1} p_{i+2} (r i)) s_i. The barycentric weights of this point are (1 - s_i) at i, s_i(1 - r_i) at i+1 and s_i r_i at i+2. `AffineIndependent` is defined over any ring and makes barycentric coordinates unique. So there are weights w with w0 + w1 + w2 = 1 such that, for every i, s_i = 1 - w_i, s_i(1 - r_i) = w_{i+1} and s_i r_i = w_{i+2}.

**The key identity.** Let D = ∏ r - ∏ (1 - r). Compute s1 s2 D = r0 (s1 r1)(s2 r2) - (1 - r0)(s1(1 - r1))(s2(1 - r2)). This equals r0 w0 w1 - (1 - r0) w2 w0 = w0 (r0 s0 (1 - r0) - (1 - r0) s0 r0) = 0. By cyclic symmetry, s0 s1 D = 0 and s2 s0 D = 0 as well.

**The ideal (s0 s1, s1 s2, s2 s0) is the whole ring.** Suppose a maximal ideal m contained two of the s_i, say s0 and s1. Then w0 ≡ 1 and w1 ≡ 1 mod m, so w2 ≡ -1 mod m. But w2 = s0 r0 lies in m, so -1 ∈ m, which is impossible. Hence no maximal ideal contains this ideal, so it equals the whole ring. Then 1 lies in the annihilator of D, so D = 0.

**Degenerate cases.** If k is the trivial ring, the conclusion holds by `Subsingleton.elim`, as in the existing proof. So `[NoZeroDivisors k]` can be dropped, for example with `omit [NoZeroDivisors k] in`.

### [`limsup_finset_sup`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Order/LiminfLimsup.lean#L946-L959) — `mathlib-0369`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0369.lean`

- `(h₁ : ∀ i ∈ s, f.IsCoboundedUnder (· ≤ ·) (F i) := by exact fun _ _ ↦ by isBoundedDefault)` (theorem): `[OrderBot β]` already implies this hypothesis. `f.IsCoboundedUnder (· ≤ ·) (F i)` unfolds to `(map (F i) f).IsCobounded (· ≤ ·)`. The lemma `Filter.isCobounded_le_of_bot` (Mathlib/Order/Filter/IsBounded.lean:369, `[LE α] [OrderBot α] {f : Filter α} : f.IsCobounded (· ≤ ·)`, proved by `⟨⊥, fun _ _ => bot_le⟩`) gives it for every filter, including `f = ⊥`, with no further assumptions. So the proof stays the same: the one place it uses `h₁` is the call to `limsup_finset_sup' s_nemp h₁ h₂`, and there `fun _ _ ↦ isCobounded_le_of_bot` can be passed instead. The earlier steps (the `f = ⊥` case and the `s = ∅` case) do not use `h₁`. The dual statement (`liminf_finset_inf` with `[OrderTop β]`) works the same way using `isCobounded_ge_of_top`. By contrast, `h₂` is genuinely needed. Counterexample without it: take β = ℕ, `f = atTop`, `F 1 = id`, `F 2 = fun _ ↦ 5`, `s = {1, 2}`. Since `sInf ∅ = 0`, the left side is 0, while the right side is `max 0 5 = 5`.

### [`ValueDistribution.logCounting_add_const`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/ValueDistribution/LogCounting/Basic.lean#L503-L509) — `mathlib-0417`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0417.lean`

- `(hf : Meromorphic f)` (theorem): The statement still holds without it, because of the junk value of `divisor`. By `logCounting_top`, `logCounting f ⊤` is `(divisor f univ)⁻.logCounting`. In Mathlib/Analysis/Meromorphic/Divisor.lean, `divisor f U z` is defined as `if MeromorphicOn f U ∧ z ∈ U then ... else 0`, and the file also has `divisor_eq_zero_of_not_meromorphicOn`. New proof: split on `by_cases hf : Meromorphic f`. If `f` is meromorphic, use the existing proof `logCounting_add_analyticOn hf analyticOn_const`. If it is not, then `f + fun _ ↦ a₀` is not meromorphic either. Otherwise `f = (f + c) - c` would be meromorphic by `Meromorphic.sub` and `Meromorphic.const` (Mathlib/Analysis/Meromorphic/Basic.lean, lines 798 and 816). `Meromorphic g` is defined as `∀ x, MeromorphicAt g x`, and line 762 of Basic.lean gives its equivalence with `MeromorphicOn g univ`. So both divisors are `0` by `divisor_eq_zero_of_not_meromorphicOn`, and both sides equal `(0 : locallyFinsupp)⁻.logCounting`, so they agree. The other arguments, including `[ProperSpace 𝕜]`, are needed for `logCounting` to elaborate.

### [`meromorphicOrderAt_add`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Analysis/Meromorphic/Order.lean#L607-L640) — `mathlib-0421`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0421.lean`

- `(hf₁ : MeromorphicAt f₁ x)` (theorem): meromorphicOrderAt is defined as `if hf : MeromorphicAt f x then ... else 0` (Order.lean line 50; simp lemma meromorphicOrderAt_of_not_meromorphicAt). Without hf₁, split on whether MeromorphicAt f₁ x holds. If it does, use the existing proof. If it does not, f₁ + f₂ is not meromorphic either: otherwise MeromorphicAt.sub (Basic.lean line 178) applied to it and hf₂ would make (f₁ + f₂) - f₂ = f₁ meromorphic. So meromorphicOrderAt f₁ x = 0 and meromorphicOrderAt (f₁+f₂) x = 0, and the goal min 0 (ord f₂) ≤ 0 follows from min_le_left. hf₁ and hf₂ can each be dropped, but not both: with f₁ non-meromorphic and f₂ = (·-x)^(-1) - f₁, both orders are 0 while the sum has order -1.
- `(hf₂ : MeromorphicAt f₂ x)` (theorem): Symmetric to hf₁. If f₂ is not meromorphic at x, then f₁ + f₂ is not meromorphic either: otherwise (f₁+f₂) - f₁ = f₂ would be, by MeromorphicAt.sub with hf₁. Both orders are then the junk value 0, and min (ord f₁) 0 ≤ 0 holds by min_le_right. Otherwise use the original proof. Only one of hf₁, hf₂ can be removed; removing both makes the statement false (counterexample in the hf₁ entry).

### [`CategoryTheory.ObjectProperty.isLocal_iff_isIso_map`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/CategoryTheory/Localization/Bousfield.lean#L223-L230) — `mathlib-0453`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0453.lean`

- `[F.Full]` (variable): The equivalence holds for any adjunction `adj : G ⊣ F`, with no condition on F. By `isLocal`, `f : X ⟶ Y` is local for `range F.obj` iff for every `W : C` the map `(Y ⟶ F.obj W) → (X ⟶ F.obj W)`, `g ↦ f ≫ g`, is bijective. Transport this map along the bijections `(adj.homEquiv _ W).symm`. By `Adjunction.homEquiv_naturality_left_symm` it becomes `h ↦ G.map f ≫ h` from `(G.obj Y ⟶ W)` to `(G.obj X ⟶ W)`. So f is local iff precomposition with `G.map f` is bijective for every `W : C`. By Yoneda this holds iff `G.map f` is an isomorphism. Concretely: surjectivity at `W = G.obj X` gives `r` with `G.map f ≫ r = 𝟙`. Injectivity at `W = G.obj Y` then gives `r ≫ G.map f = 𝟙`. The converse is clear. Fullness is not used anywhere in this argument. The current proof uses it only through `isLocal_adj_unit_app` and `isIso_of_fully_faithful`, and the direct argument above replaces both. This entry and the `[F.Faithful]` entry can be removed separately, and also together.
- `[F.Faithful]` (variable): Same argument as for `[F.Full]`. The bijection `(Y ⟶ F.obj W) ≃ (G.obj Y ⟶ W)` given by `adj.homEquiv`, with naturality from `Adjunction.homEquiv_naturality_left_symm`, turns locality of f for `range F.obj` into bijectivity of precomposition with `G.map f` on every `Hom(-, W)` with `W : C`. By the Yoneda argument this is equivalent to `IsIso (G.map f)`. Faithfulness of F is never needed. It is used only by the current proof (`isLocal_adj_unit_app`, `isIso_of_fully_faithful`), which can be replaced by this direct argument. It can be removed on its own, or together with `[F.Full]`.

### [`multiplicity_self`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/RingTheory/Multiplicity.lean#L716-L726) — `mathlib-0590`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0590.lean`

- `[IsCancelMulZero α]` (variable): The statement holds in any commutative monoid (with zero). By `FiniteMultiplicity.multiplicity_eq_iff` (proved for a plain Monoid), the goal is `a ^ 1 ∣ a ∧ ¬ a ^ 2 ∣ a`. The first part is trivial. For the second, suppose `a = a^2 * v`. By induction, `a = a^n * v^(n-1)` for every n ≥ 1; the step is `a^(n+1) v^n = (a^n v^(n-1)) * (a v) = a * (a v) = a^2 v = a`, which uses only commutativity. So `a^n ∣ a` for all n, i.e. `¬FiniteMultiplicity a a` by `FiniteMultiplicity.not_iff_forall`, contradicting `ha`. The cases a = 0 and a a unit are already excluded by `ha` (`FiniteMultiplicity.ne_zero`, `FiniteMultiplicity.not_isUnit`), and the argument needs neither. The current proof uses cancellation only, via `mul_eq_mul_left_iff`, to cancel `a`; the argument above replaces that step with commutativity alone. `CommMonoidWithZero` stays because the statement needs it to elaborate, and `ha` stays because without it `multiplicity 0 0 = 0`.

### [`indepFun_pi_of_bcf`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Probability/Independence/BoundedContinuousFunction.lean#L140-L148) — `mathlib-0593`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0593.lean`

- `[Finite T]` (variable): The theorem stays true for arbitrary `T`. The rest of the statement does not need `Finite T`: `(Π t, F t) →ᵇ ℝ` only uses the product topology. Proof without it, in three steps.

(1) Apply `h` with `f = 1` and `g = 1`. This gives `P.real univ = P.real univ * P.real univ`. Since `P` is finite, `P univ` is 0 or 1, so `IsZeroOrProbabilityMeasure P` holds.

(2) Fix `J : Finset T`. The theorem itself, applied to the finite type `J` (which is `Finite`), gives `Z ⟂ᵢ[P] (fun ω (t : J) ↦ Y t ω)`. Its hypothesis is obtained from `h f (g'.compContinuous ⟨J.restrict, by fun_prop⟩)` for each `g' : (Π t : J, F t) →ᵇ ℝ`, because `g' ∘ restrict` is a bounded continuous function on `Π t, F t`. The measurability hypothesis is `fun t ↦ mY t`.

(3) Conclude with `IndepFun.indepFun_process₀` (Mathlib/Probability/Independence/Process/Basic.lean:376). It needs only a.e.-measurability of `Z` and of each `Y t`, independence for every finite `J`, and `[IsZeroOrProbabilityMeasure P]`, which step (1) provides.

This is exactly how the file proves `indepFun_process_of_bcf` (line 351), with `h · J` there replaced by the composition with `restrict`. Degenerate cases: if `T` is empty, `J = ∅` is covered by the same argument, and `P = 0` is allowed by `IsZeroOrProbabilityMeasure`.

### [`Equiv.Perm.list_cycles_perm_list_cycles`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/GroupTheory/Perm/Cycle/Factors.lean#L445-L457) — `mathlib-0622`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0622.lean`

- `[Finite α]` (theorem): The theorem holds for any type α. Finiteness enters the current proof only through `mem_list_cycles_iff`, which assumes `[Finite α]` because it works with Finset supports. The two other lemmas it uses need no finiteness: `nodup_of_pairwise_disjoint_cycles` (Cycle/Basic.lean:471) and `List.perm_ext_iff_of_nodup`. So it is enough to show, with no finiteness, that every σ ∈ l₁ lies in l₂ (and symmetrically).

Mathlib's definitions: `IsCycle f` means ∃ x, f x ≠ x ∧ ∀ y, f y ≠ y → SameCycle f x y, and SameCycle means some integer power of f maps x to y. Neither involves finiteness.

(1) If σ ∈ l is a cycle in a pairwise-disjoint list l, then l.prod a = σ a whenever σ a ≠ a. Proof: l ~ σ :: l.erase σ, so by `disjoint_prod_perm` (Support.lean:134), l.prod = σ * (l.erase σ).prod. By `disjoint_prod_right` (Support.lean:119), σ is disjoint from ρ' := (l.erase σ).prod. Since σ a ≠ a, disjointness gives ρ' a = a, so l.prod a = σ a. No finiteness is used.

(2) Take σ ∈ l₁ and let ρ = l₁.prod = l₂.prod. Choose a with σ a ≠ a. By (1), ρ a = σ a ≠ a. If every member of l₂ fixed a, then so would their product (induction on the list), so some τ ∈ l₂ has τ a ≠ a. By (1) again, τ agrees with ρ on its support.

(3) Show σ = τ. For y in the support of σ, σ y is in the support too, so σ (σ y) = ρ (σ y). Also z = σ⁻¹ y is in the support and ρ z = σ z = y, so σ⁻¹ y = ρ⁻¹ y. By induction on the exponent, σ^n a = ρ^n a for every integer n. The same holds for τ. Since σ and τ are cycles moving a, each support is the orbit of a, which is {ρ^n a}. So the two supports coincide, σ and τ both agree with ρ on that set, and both are the identity elsewhere. Hence σ = τ ∈ l₂.

Degenerate cases: empty lists, or empty or one-element α, are covered by the same argument; there both lists are forced to be empty, since a cycle must move some point. With both lists duplicate-free and having the same members, `List.perm_ext_iff_of_nodup` gives l₁ ~ l₂ without `[Finite α]`.

### [`Algebra.isUnramifiedIn_bot`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/NumberTheory/RamificationInertia/Unramified.lean#L121-L125) — `mathlib-0649`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0649.lean`

- `[FaithfulSMul R S]` (theorem): The goal `IsUnramifiedIn S ⊥` unfolds (Mathlib/RingTheory/Unramified/Locus.lean) to: for every prime P of S with `P.LiesOver ⊥`, `IsUnramifiedAt R P`. Take such a P. `Ideal.eq_bot_of_liesOver_bot` (GoingUp.lean:436) needs only `[Algebra.IsIntegral R S] [Nontrivial R] [IsDomain S]`, not injectivity, and gives P = ⊥. Then `LiesOver` says `(⊥ : Ideal S).under R = ⊥`. That comap is `RingHom.ker (algebraMap R S)`, so the kernel is ⊥ and `algebraMap R S` is injective. So `FaithfulSMul R S` follows from the other hypotheses inside the proof, through `faithfulSMul_iff_algebraMap_injective`. From there `Module.isTorsionFree_iff_algebraMap_injective` (Algebra/Basic.lean:485) or the instance `FaithfulSMul.to_isTorsionFree` gives `Module.IsTorsionFree R S`, and `isUnramifiedAt_bot` finishes as in the current proof. Degenerate case: if the map is not injective, no prime of S lies over ⊥ and the statement holds vacuously; the argument above covers this case. The other binders are not affected: `[IsDomain R]` can be dropped at the same time (see the other entry).
- `[IsDomain R]` (theorem): `Ideal R` and `IsUnramifiedIn` only need `CommRing R`, so the statement still elaborates without this binder. With `[FaithfulSMul R S]` and `[IsDomain S]` still present, `IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul R S` (Mathlib/Algebra/Algebra/Basic.lean:455) gives `IsDomain R`, and the proof then runs unchanged. It also works with `[FaithfulSMul R S]` removed as well. `R` is nontrivial because of `CharZero R`, so `Ideal.eq_bot_of_liesOver_bot` applies. For any prime P lying over ⊥ it gives P = ⊥, hence `ker (algebraMap R S) = ⊥`, so the map is injective. That gives `FaithfulSMul R S` and then `IsDomain R` locally, and `isUnramifiedAt_bot` finishes. So this binder and `[FaithfulSMul R S]` can each be removed, and also both together.

### [`RootPairing.GeckConstruction.lie_h_f`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/RootSystem/GeckConstruction/Relations.lean#L70-L86) — `mathlib-0676`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0676.lean`

- `[Finite ι]` (variable): The theorem also takes `[Fintype ι]`. It needs `Fintype ι` anyway: without it the matrices over `b.support ⊕ ι` have no ring structure, so the bracket `⁅h j, f i⁆` and `ω b * _` would not elaborate. `Finite ι` follows from `Fintype ι` by the Mathlib instance `Finite.of_fintype`, so the `[Finite ι]` argument is implied by another hypothesis. The definitions `e` and `f` in Basic.lean, and the lemmas `ω_mul_f` and `lie_h_e`, take `[Finite ι]` as an instance argument. If the theorem's own `[Finite ι]` is removed (e.g. with `omit [Finite ι] in`), instance search fills those arguments through `Finite.of_fintype`, and the statement still elaborates. `Finite` is a `Prop`-valued class, so every instance of it is definitionally the same, and the existing proof goes through unchanged with the derived instance. The theorem is universally quantified over this instance, so the version without the argument is just as strong. There is no degenerate case: whenever `Fintype ι` holds, `Finite ι` holds.

### [`IntervalIntegrable.comp_add_right_iff`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/IntervalIntegral/Basic.lean#L445-L451) — `mathlib-0704`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0704.lean`

- `(h : ‖f (min a b + c)‖ₑ ≠ ⊤ := by finiteness)` (theorem): `IntervalIntegrable f μ a b` is defined (line 73) as `IntegrableOn f (Ioc a b) μ ∧ IntegrableOn f (Ioc b a) μ`. These intervals are half-open on the left, so the value of `f` at the left endpoint `min a b + c` never matters. The hypothesis is only there because the existing proof goes through `comp_add_right`, which uses `intervalIntegrable_iff'` (closed interval `uIcc`). Moving between `Ioc` and `Icc` is the only step where a finiteness condition at the endpoint is needed.

Proof without `h`: unfold the definition and treat each conjunct separately. For any `p q`: `IntegrableOn f (Ioc (p+c) (q+c)) volume` ↔ `IntegrableOn f (Ioc (p+c) (q+c)) (volume.map (· + c))`, by `map_add_right_eq_self volume c` (Lebesgue measure is add-right invariant). This is ↔ `IntegrableOn (f ∘ (· + c)) ((· + c) ⁻¹' Ioc (p+c) (q+c)) volume` by `MeasurableEmbedding.integrableOn_map_iff`, applied to the embedding `(Homeomorph.addRight c).isClosedEmbedding.measurableEmbedding`. That lemma holds for a general `ε` and has no finiteness hypothesis. Finally, `Set.preimage_add_const_Ioc` (the `to_additive` version of `preimage_mul_const_Ioc`) gives `Ioc (p+c-c) (q+c-c) = Ioc p q`. Using this with `(p,q) = (a,b)` and `(b,a)` proves both directions of the iff for every `f`, `a`, `b`, `c`, including when `f` takes the value `⊤` (infinite enorm) at that point. Degenerate cases such as `a = b` (empty `Ioc`) are covered by the same argument.

### [`ProbabilityTheory.riskIncrease_le_iInf'`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Probability/Decision/Risk/RiskIncrease.lean#L83-L85) — `mathlib-0724`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0724.lean`

- `[Nonempty 𝓨]` (theorem): The bound holds without it. Proof: riskIncrease ℓ P π ≤ bayesRisk ℓ (discard 𝓧 ∘ₖ P) π by tsub_le_self. Then bayesRisk_le_iInf' (Basic.lean:96, which assumes only hl : Measurable (uncurry ℓ), for any kernel and any measure) gives ≤ ⨅ y, ∫⁻ θ, ℓ θ y * (discard 𝓧 ∘ₖ P) θ univ ∂π. Kernel.comp_discard' (Comp.lean:149) gives (discard 𝓧 ∘ₖ P) θ = P θ univ • dirac (), so that measure of univ is P θ univ, and mul_comm gives the stated right-hand side. If 𝓨 is empty, the right-hand side is ⨅ over an empty type, i.e. ⊤, so the bound is trivial. Nonempty is only needed by the current route through the equality riskIncrease_eq_iInf_sub'. It can be deleted together with [SFinite π].
- `[SFinite π]` (theorem): Same proof as for [Nonempty 𝓨]: tsub_le_self, then bayesRisk_le_iInf' hℓ (discard 𝓧 ∘ₖ P) π, which holds for an arbitrary measure π, then Kernel.comp_discard' to get (discard 𝓧 ∘ₖ P) θ univ = P θ univ, then mul_comm. None of these steps needs π to be s-finite. SFinite is only needed by the equality riskIncrease_eq_iInf_sub' used in the current proof. It can be deleted together with [Nonempty 𝓨].

### [`FiniteDimensional.range_orthonormalBasisSingleton`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/PiL2.lean#L1294-L1296) — `mathlib-0743`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0743.lean`

- `[Fintype ι]` (variable): `[Fintype ι]` follows from the other argument `[Unique ι]`: Mathlib has the global instance `Unique.fintype {α} [Unique α] : Fintype α` (Mathlib/Data/Fintype/Basic.lean:111). If the theorem omits `[Fintype ι]` (via `omit [Fintype ι] in`), `orthonormalBasisSingleton ι 𝕜 h v hv` and its coercion to a function still elaborate, with the Fintype instance found by `Unique.fintype`. This is the same pattern as `[Nonempty α]` next to `[Inhabited α]`. The proof `simp` still works: the simp lemma `orthonormalBasisSingleton_apply` holds for any `Fintype ι` instance, so it rewrites the function to the constant `fun _ => v`. The range of a constant function on a nonempty type (nonempty because of `Unique ι`) is `{v}`. There is no degenerate case: `ι` cannot be empty because of `[Unique ι]`.

### [`CategoryTheory.Functor.eval_section_surjective_of_surjective`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/CategoryTheory/CofilteredSystem.lean#L309-L320) — `mathlib-0790`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0790.lean`

- `[∀ j : J, Nonempty (F.obj j)]` (variable): The conclusion is that `fun s => s.val i` is surjective. The proof begins by introducing `x : F.obj i`. If `F.obj i` is empty, surjectivity holds vacuously. Otherwise, the element `x` together with the other hypotheses makes every `F.obj j` nonempty. Take any `j`. Because `J` is `IsCofilteredOrEmpty`, the field `IsCofilteredOrEmpty.cone_objs i j` gives some `k` with morphisms `f : k ⟶ i` and `g : k ⟶ j`. By `Fsur f`, `x` has a preimage `y : F.obj k`, and then `F.map g y : F.obj j`. So at the start of the proof, after `fun x =>`, we can add `haveI : ∀ j, Nonempty (F.obj j) := fun j => by obtain ⟨k, f, g, -⟩ := IsCofilteredOrEmpty.cone_objs i j; exact ⟨F.map g (Fsur f x).choose⟩`. The rest of the proof is unchanged: this local instance is all that `toPreimages_nonempty_of_surjective` (and indirectly `nonempty_sections_of_finite_cofiltered_system`) need. So the instance argument can be dropped, for example with `omit [∀ j : J, Nonempty (F.obj j)] in`. The other hypotheses are needed: Finite (Kőnig's lemma), the cofilteredness (a parallel pair with maps id and swap on a 2-element set has no sections), and Fsur.

### [`WeierstrassCurve.Jacobian.comp_equiv_comp`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/EllipticCurve/Jacobian/Basic.lean#L471-L482) — `mathlib-0796`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0796.lean`

- `(hP : W.Nonsingular P)` (theorem): The backward direction needs no hypothesis: from `P = u • Q`, take `Units.map f u` and use `comp_smul`. Forward direction, given `f ∘ P = u • (f ∘ Q)` with `u : Kˣ`, and keeping only `hQ`:

Case `f (P z) ≠ 0`: the existing proof (`equiv_of_X_eq_of_Y_eq`, `X_eq_of_equiv`, `Y_eq_of_equiv`, injectivity of `f`) never uses `hP`.

Case `f (P z) = 0`: then `P z = 0` and `Q z = 0` (by `Z_eq_zero_of_equiv` and injectivity). Since `Q` is nonsingular with `Q z = 0`, `X_ne_zero_of_Z_eq_zero` and `Y_ne_zero_of_Z_eq_zero` give `Q x ≠ 0` and `Q y ≠ 0`. From `f (P x) = u^2 f (Q x)` and `f (P y) = u^3 f (Q y)`, with `u` a unit, we get `P x ≠ 0`. So `u = f (P y / Q y) / f (P x / Q x) = f v` with `v := (P y * Q x) / (Q y * P x) ≠ 0`. Injectivity of `f` then gives `P x = v^2 Q x`, `P y = v^3 Q y` and `P z = 0 = v Q z`, so `P = (Units.mk0 v _) • Q`, hence `P ≈ Q`.

Only `equiv_of_Z_eq_zero`, which needs both points nonsingular, has to be replaced by this argument. `W` still appears in `hQ`, so the statement still elaborates. `hP` and `hQ` cannot both be deleted. Counterexample: `F = ℚ`, `K = ℝ`, `P = ![1,0,0]`, `Q = ![2,0,0]`. Then `f ∘ P = (1/√2) • (f ∘ Q)` in `ℝ`, but in `ℚ` we would need `v^2 · 2 = 1`, which has no solution.
- `(hQ : W.Nonsingular Q)` (theorem): Symmetric to `hP`, because `≈` is an equivalence relation. Keeping only `hP`, the case `f (P z) ≠ 0` (and so `Q z ≠ 0`) goes through `equiv_of_X_eq_of_Y_eq` exactly as now, with no nonsingularity needed. If `P z = 0 = Q z`, then `hP` gives `P x ≠ 0` and `P y ≠ 0` (`X_ne_zero_of_Z_eq_zero`, `Y_ne_zero_of_Z_eq_zero`). From `f (P x) = u^2 f (Q x)` and `f (P y) = u^3 f (Q y)` we get `Q x ≠ 0` and `Q y ≠ 0`, and `u = f v` with `v = (P y * Q x) / (Q y * P x)`. Injectivity of `f` then gives `P = v • Q`. Only one of `hP` and `hQ` can be deleted, not both (same counterexample `![1,0,0]` vs `![2,0,0]` over `ℚ → ℝ`).

### [`Polynomial.quo_add_sum_rem_div_unique`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/PartialFractions.lean#L442-L463) — `mathlib-0904`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0904.lean`

- `(hg : ∀ i ∈ s, (g i).Monic)` (theorem): The statement stays true without `hg`, because the other hypotheses force `R[X]` to be an integral domain. `[FaithfulSMul R[X] K]` makes `algebraMap R[X] K` injective into a field, so `R[X]` has no zero divisors, and neither does `R`. Over a domain, partial-fraction uniqueness does not need monic denominators.

Proof without `hg`:
1. For `i ∈ s`, `hr₁ i` says `degree (r₁ i) < degree (g i)`, so `g i ≠ 0`. By injectivity its image in `K` is nonzero too.
2. Let `d i = r₁ i - r₂ i`. Then `degree (d i) < degree (g i)`. From `hf`, `(q₁ - q₂) + ∑ d i / g i = 0` in `K`.
3. Multiply by `G = ∏_{i∈s} g i` and pull back along the injective map. This gives `(q₁ - q₂) * G + ∑_i d i * ∏_{k≠i} g k = 0` in `R[X]`.
4. Fix `j ∈ s`. `g j` divides every term except `d j * ∏_{k≠j} g k`, so it divides that term too.
5. `IsCoprime (g j) (∏_{k≠j} g k)` follows from `hcop` and `IsCoprime.prod_right`. Then `IsCoprime.dvd_of_dvd_mul_left` gives `g j ∣ d j`.
6. Suppose `d j ≠ 0`, say `d j = g j * c`. Then `c ≠ 0`, and since `R` has no zero divisors, `Polynomial.degree_mul` gives `degree (d j) = degree (g j) + degree c ≥ degree (g j)`. This contradicts step 2, so `r₁ j = r₂ j`.
7. Then `(q₁ - q₂) * G = 0` with `G ≠ 0`, so `q₁ = q₂`.

Degenerate case: if `s` is empty, `hf` gives `q₁ = q₂` directly by injectivity.

### [`hasSum_eulerFunction_pentagonal`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Combinatorics/Enumerative/Pentagonal/EulerFunction.lean#L114-L117) — `mathlib-0914`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0914.lean`

- `[NormOneClass R]` (variable): `eulerFunction` itself does not depend on `NormOneClass`: `eulerFunction_def` and `eulerFunction_eq_tsum_pentagonal` both state it under `omit [NormOneClass R] in`. The final `simpa` step (rewriting with `PowerSeries.coeff_pentagonalSeries_mul_eq_extend` and `pentagonal_injective`) is purely algebraic. That step is exactly what `eulerFunction_eq_tsum_pentagonal` does, and that theorem also omits `NormOneClass`. So `NormOneClass` only matters for summability in `hasSum_eulerFunction_pentagonalSeries`, which bounds ‖x^n‖ ≤ ‖x‖^n, and that bound fails at n = 0 without `NormOneClass`. Summability can be shown without it. Each coefficient `(pentagonalSeries R).coeff n` is 0, 1 or -1 (`coeff_pentagonalSeries_pentagonal` gives `negOnePow`, otherwise `coeff_pentagonalSeries_eq_zero`), so its norm is at most ‖1‖, using `norm_neg`. Then ‖c_n * x^n‖ ≤ ‖1‖ * ‖x^n‖. The sequence ‖x^n‖ is summable for ‖x‖ < 1 by `summable_norm_geometric_of_norm_lt_one` (Mathlib/Analysis/SpecificLimits/Normed.lean:543), which only needs `[NormedRing R]`. With `[CompleteSpace R]`, `Summable.of_norm_bounded` makes the series summable, so it has sum `eulerFunction x` = its tsum. The reindexing to ℤ then goes through as in the current proof. The degenerate trivial ring is fine: there everything is 0. `CompleteSpace R` is genuinely needed: in ℚ with x = 1/2 the lacunary series has an irrational limit, so it is not summable and the tsum is 0.

### [`MultilinearMap.domDomRestrict_aux_right`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Multilinear/Basic.lean#L733-L738) — `mathlib-0990`

Snippet: `samples/mathlib/mathlib-0990.lean`

- `[DecidableEq {a // ¬ P a}]` (theorem): The theorem also takes `[DecidableEq ι]`. Without the subtype binder, `Function.update z i c` in the statement still elaborates: instance search finds Mathlib's/core's `Subtype` decidable-equality instance, built from `DecidableEq ι`. Nothing else uses the binder. The statement stays true because `Function.update` gives the same value whichever `DecidableEq` instance it uses, since `Decidable` is a subsingleton. The identity holds for every choice of instance, including the derived one. The proof still works. `domDomRestrict_aux` takes an arbitrary `[DecidableEq {a // P a}]`, and with the predicate `fun a => ¬ P a` its instance argument is filled by the derived subtype instance. That is the same instance the statement now uses, so `simpa only [dite_not] using domDomRestrict_aux _ z (fun j ↦ x ⟨j.1, not_not.mp j.2⟩) i c` goes through unchanged. Proving it directly by `grind`, the way `domDomRestrict_aux` itself is proved, should also work. `[DecidableEq ι]` cannot be removed instead: the update on the right-hand side is over `ι` and needs it, and no other hypothesis supplies it.


## Tau Ceti (68 flagged)

### [`CongruenceSubgroup.Gamma0_index_mul_prod_primeFactors`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/ModularForms/CongruenceSubgroups/Basic.lean#L952-L963) — `tauceti-0001`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0001.lean`

- `(hN : N ≠ 0)` (theorem): The identity also holds for N = 0. Here `Nat.primeFactors 0 = ∅`, so both products are 1, the RHS is `0 * 1 = 0`, and the LHS is `(Gamma0 0).index`. So the case N = 0 comes down to showing `(Gamma0 0).index = 0`, i.e. that Γ₀(0) has infinite index. Γ₀(0) is the group of upper-triangular matrices, and its index is indeed infinite. A short proof using lemmas that already exist: for every prime p, `Gamma0 0 ≤ Gamma0 p`, because by `mem_Gamma0_iff_dvd` membership means `(0:ℤ) ∣ γ 1 0`, so `γ 1 0 = 0`, which p divides. By Mathlib's `Subgroup.index_dvd_of_le`, `(Gamma0 p).index ∣ (Gamma0 0).index`. By `Gamma0_prime_power_index p hp 1` (or `Gamma0_prime_index`), `(Gamma0 p).index = p + 1`. If m := (Gamma0 0).index were nonzero, take a prime p ≥ m (`Nat.exists_infinite_primes`). Then p + 1 ∣ m with m > 0 gives p + 1 ≤ m ≤ p, a contradiction. So the index is 0 and both sides are 0. For N ≠ 0 the existing proof is unchanged. So the theorem stays true and provable without `hN`, by first splitting on whether N = 0.

### [`RootPairing.coroot'_nonpos_of_mem_negRoots`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/LinearAlgebra/RootSystem/Chamber.lean#L325-L331) — `tauceti-0007`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0007.lean`

- `[P.flip.IsReduced]` (variable): This follows from the other hypotheses. Mathlib's `RootPairing.instFlipIsReduced [P.IsReduced] [IsTorsionFree R N] : P.flip.IsReduced` (Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/RootSystem/Reduced.lean) also needs `[Finite ι] [CharZero R] [IsDomain R] [Module.IsTorsionFree R M]`. The theorem already has the first three. Torsion-freeness is automatic for a root pairing over a domain: `RootPairing` carries `isPerfPair_toLinearMap`, so `P.toLinearMap : M → (N →ₗ[R] R)` is injective (`LinearMap.IsPerfPair.bijective_left`). `R` is torsion-free over itself (instance in Torsion/Free.lean), and `N →ₗ[R] R` is torsion-free by the instance in Algebra/Module/LinearMap/Basic.lean. `Function.Injective.moduleIsTorsionFree` (with `map_smul`) then gives `IsTorsionFree R M`; the same argument with `P.flip` (perfect pairing `P.toLinearMap.flip`) gives `IsTorsionFree R N`. The modified proof builds these two instances and `P.flip.IsReduced` with `have`, then runs the existing proof unchanged. It cannot be deleted together with `[P.IsReduced]`: each one can be deleted only while the other stays.
- `[P.IsReduced]` (variable): Symmetric to the other entry. `P.flip.flip = P` holds by `rfl` (`RootPairing.flip_flip`), so `instFlipIsReduced` applied to `P.flip` (using `[P.flip.IsReduced]`, `[Finite ι] [CharZero R] [IsDomain R]`, and torsion-freeness of `N` and `M`) yields `P.flip.flip.IsReduced`, which is `P.IsReduced`. The torsion-freeness instances come from the perfect pairing over the domain `R`, as described in the other entry (`IsPerfPair.bijective_left`/`bijective_right`, the `LinearMap` torsion-free instance, and `Function.Injective.moduleIsTorsionFree`). The proof adds a `have` for `P.IsReduced` and is otherwise unchanged. Only one of `[P.IsReduced]` and `[P.flip.IsReduced]` can be deleted, not both.

### [`HeckeRing.GLn.posDetInt_le_commensurator`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/HeckeRing/GLn/Basic.lean#L712-L717) — `tauceti-0020`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0020.lean`

- `[NeZero n]` (variable): The statement does not need `[NeZero n]`: `posDetInt n` (line 257) and `SLnZ n` (line 74) are defined in sections without it, and Mathlib's `commensurator` has no such requirement. The proof uses it only through `mem_commensurator_of_hasIntEntries`. That lemma inherits it via `inv_conjugate_mem_SLnZ_of_mem_ker` and `conj_mat_det_one`, which uses `NeZero.pos n` to rewrite `n - 1 + 1 = n` (line 470). The theorem also holds for n = 0. Then `Matrix (Fin 0) (Fin 0) ℚ` has exactly one element, so `GL (Fin 0) ℚ` has only the element 1. Every g in `posDetInt 0` therefore equals 1, and 1 lies in the subgroup `commensurator (SLnZ 0)` by `one_mem`. Proof without the instance: `rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos n with rfl | hn`. For n = 0, show `g = 1` via `Units.ext (Subsingleton.elim _ _)` and close with `one_mem _`. For `0 < n`, run `haveI := NeZero.of_pos hn` and apply the existing proof `mem_commensurator_of_hasIntEntries n hg.1`.

### [`TauCeti.Nat.gcd_eq_gcd_ordCompl_mul_pow_min`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Data/Nat/Factorization/GcdSplit.lean#L56-L71) — `tauceti-0078`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0078.lean`

- `(hp : p.Prime)` (theorem): The statement holds for non-prime p. If ¬p.Prime, Mathlib's `Nat.factorization_eq_zero_of_not_prime` (Mathlib/Data/Nat/Factorization/Defs.lean) gives m.factorization p = 0 and n.factorization p = 0; this also covers p = 0 and p = 1. `ordCompl[p] m` is notation for `m / p ^ m.factorization p`, so it becomes m / p^0 = m / 1 = m, and likewise for n. The factor p ^ min 0 0 is 1, so the goal reduces to gcd m n = gcd m n. New proof: `by_cases hp : p.Prime`. In the prime case keep the existing proof. In the other case rewrite with `Nat.factorization_eq_zero_of_not_prime m hp` and `Nat.factorization_eq_zero_of_not_prime n hp`, then close with simp (pow_zero, Nat.div_one, min_self, mul_one). hm and hn are genuinely needed: p=2, m=0, n=2 gives 2 on the left and gcd 0 1 * 1 = 1 on the right.

### [`TauCeti.RoughForm.inChartAt_mpullback`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Geometry/Manifold/DifferentialForm/Pullback/Chart.lean#L37-L53) — `tauceti-0108`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0108.lean`

- `(hf : MDifferentiableAt I I' f ((extChartAt I α).symm z))` (theorem): Write x := (extChartAt I α).symm z. After `inChartAt_apply` and `mpullback_apply`, the left side evaluated at v is ω (f x) (fun i ↦ mfderiv I I' f x (mfderivWithin 𝓘(ℝ,E) I (extChartAt I α).symm (range I) z (v i))). The right side is ω (f x) (fun i ↦ mfderivWithin 𝓘(ℝ,E) I' (f ∘ (extChartAt I α).symm) (range I) z (v i)). Proof: case split on `MDifferentiableAt I I' f x`. If it holds, the existing proof applies unchanged. If it fails, `mfderiv_zero_of_not_mdifferentiableAt` gives mfderiv f x = 0, so the left side is ω (f x) (fun _ ↦ 0). On the right, g := f ∘ (extChartAt I α).symm is also not MDifferentiableWithinAt (range I) at z. Suppose it were. Since x ∈ (chartAt H α).source (from `(extChartAt I α).map_target hz` and `extChartAt_source`), `mdifferentiableAt_extChartAt` makes extChartAt I α MDifferentiableAt x; this lemma uses the `[IsManifold I 1 M]` that is still present. Because extChartAt I α x = z (by `right_inv hz`) and extChartAt maps everything into range I (its coercion is I ∘ chartAt), `MDifferentiableWithinAt.comp` with s = univ, plus `mdifferentiableWithinAt_univ`, shows g ∘ extChartAt I α is MDifferentiableAt x. This function equals f on the open chart source, which contains x (by `left_inv`). So `MDifferentiableAt.congr_of_eventuallyEq` would make f MDifferentiableAt x, a contradiction. Hence `mfderivWithin_zero_of_not_mdifferentiableWithinAt` gives mfderivWithin g = 0, and the right side is also ω (f x) (fun _ ↦ 0). The two sides agree in both cases. Degenerate cases (k = 0, trivial spaces) need nothing extra, since the argument is pointwise and uses only the junk value 0 of mfderiv/mfderivWithin.

### [`LipschitzWith.ae_finite_fiber`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/MeasureTheory/Function/AreaFormula/Real.lean#L119-L126) — `tauceti-0115`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0115.lean`

- `(hs : NullMeasurableSet s volume)` (theorem): Without `hs`, apply the theorem as stated to `t := toMeasurable volume s`. `t` is measurable (`measurableSet_toMeasurable`), so `t` is also null-measurable. The volume on a finite-dimensional real inner product space is an additive Haar measure, so it is sigma-finite and therefore s-finite. Mathlib's `Measure.restrict_toMeasurable_of_sfinite` (Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Measure/Typeclasses/SFinite.lean) then gives `volume.restrict (toMeasurable volume s) = volume.restrict s`. `IntegrableOn F s` unfolds to `Integrable F (volume.restrict s)`, so `hint` turns into `IntegrableOn (fun x ↦ (fderiv ℝ f x).normDet) t`. The current theorem, applied to `hf`, the null-measurability of `t` and this rewritten `hint`, gives that for almost every `y`, `(t ∩ f ⁻¹' {y}).Finite`. Since `s ⊆ t` (`subset_toMeasurable`), we have `s ∩ f ⁻¹' {y} ⊆ t ∩ f ⁻¹' {y}`. `Set.Finite.subset` then gives `(s ∩ f ⁻¹' {y}).Finite` for the same `y`, inside a `filter_upwards`. No degenerate case causes trouble: if `s` is empty, every fiber is empty and hence finite. `hs` appears nowhere else in the statement, so the statement is unchanged; only the proof needs the change above.

### [`TauCeti.exists_zmultiples_eq_top_groupCohomology_two_res_padicCompletionUnits`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/Padics/MultiplicativeCompletion/Cohomology.lean#L195-L206) — `tauceti-0118`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0118.lean`

- `[CharZero K]` (variable): This follows from the other instances. `ℚ_[p]` has characteristic zero and `algebraMap ℚ_[p] K` is injective (it is a ring hom out of a field into a nontrivial ring), so `charZero_of_injective_algebraMap` (Mathlib/Algebra/CharP/Algebra.lean) gives `CharZero K` from `[Algebra ℚ_[p] K]`. The same lemma also gives it from `[CharZero L]` together with the injective map `algebraMap K L`. The degenerate cases do not arise: both are fields, so they are nontrivial. New proof: `haveI : CharZero K := charZero_of_injective_algebraMap (algebraMap ℚ_[p] K).injective`, then the original term `(groupCohomology_res_padicCompletionUnits p K L S).2`. The statement itself does not change.
- `[CharZero L]` (variable): This also follows from the other instances. `charZero_of_injective_algebraMap` applied to the injective `algebraMap ℚ_[p] L` gives `CharZero L` from `[Algebra ℚ_[p] L]`, since `ℚ_[p]` has characteristic zero. It also follows from `[CharZero K]` through the injective `algebraMap K L`. New proof: `haveI : CharZero L := charZero_of_injective_algebraMap (algebraMap ℚ_[p] L).injective`, then the existing proof. Because the derivation goes through `ℚ_[p]`, `[CharZero K]` and `[CharZero L]` can even be deleted together.
- `[FiniteDimensional K L]` (variable): `FiniteDimensional K L` is by definition `Module.Finite K L`. It follows from `[Module.Finite ℚ_[p] L]` and `[IsScalarTower ℚ_[p] K L]` (with `[Algebra ℚ_[p] K]`, which supplies the `SMul ℚ_[p] K`) by `Module.Finite.of_restrictScalars_finite ℚ_[p] K L` (Mathlib/RingTheory/Finiteness/Basic.lean). New proof: `haveI : FiniteDimensional K L := Module.Finite.of_restrictScalars_finite ℚ_[p] K L`, then the original proof `(groupCohomology_res_padicCompletionUnits p K L S).2`.

### [`Matrix.ProjectiveSpecialLinearGroup.isParabolic_inv_iff`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/LinearAlgebra/Matrix/ProjectiveSpecialLinearGroup/FinTwo.lean#L103-L117) — `tauceti-0134`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0134.lean`

- `[NoZeroDivisors R]` (variable): The proof uses `[NoZeroDivisors R]` only through `isParabolic_mk_iff`, which needs it to say that every representative is parabolic. The equivalence still holds over any commutative ring if we work straight from the definition `IsParabolic g := ∃ a : SL(2, R), ↑a = g ∧ (a : GL (Fin 2) R).IsParabolic`.

Step 1. For every `a : SL(2, R)`, `(a⁻¹ : GL).IsParabolic ↔ (a : GL).IsParabolic`. Mathlib defines `Matrix.IsParabolic m` as `m ∉ Set.range (scalar _) ∧ m.discr = 0`, and `discr_fin_two` gives `discr = trace ^ 2 - 4 * det`. In SL(2, R) the inverse is the adjugate `!![d, -b; -c, a]`. It has the same trace and determinant as `a`, and it is scalar exactly when `b = 0`, `c = 0` and `a = d`, which is exactly when `a` is scalar. This is the calculation the existing proof does with `simp` after rewriting with `isParabolic_mk_iff`: the local `hscalar`, `adjugate_fin_two`, `trace_fin_two` and `det_fin_two` are all commutative-ring facts, and none of them needs `NoZeroDivisors`.

Step 2. If `IsParabolic g⁻¹`, choose `a` with `↑a = g⁻¹` and `a` parabolic. Then `↑a⁻¹ = g` (by `QuotientGroup.mk_inv` and `inv_inv`), and `a⁻¹` is parabolic by Step 1, so `IsParabolic g`. The converse is the same argument applied to `g⁻¹`, using `inv_inv`.

Degenerate case: if `R` is the trivial ring, every matrix is scalar, so both sides are false and the equivalence holds. The statement is therefore true without the instance, and it can be removed with `omit [NoZeroDivisors R] in`.

### [`TauCeti.IsFiniteType.exists_chain_of_reachable`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/LinearAlgebra/RootSystem/FiniteType/Diagram.lean#L154-L185) — `tauceti-0149`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0149.lean`

- `(h : IsFiniteType A)` (theorem): Without `h` the statement still holds for every integer matrix. Adjacency in `diagramGraph A` means `i ≠ j ∧ A i j ≠ 0 ∧ A j i ≠ 0` (`diagramGraph_adj`). The conclusion only involves forward entries along the chain: `A (w i) (w (i+1)) ≠ 0`, and `A (w i) (w j) = 0` for `i+1 < j ≤ n`. Backward entries never appear.

Proof without `h`. Call a pair `(n, w)` a chain if `w 0 = u`, `w n = v` and `A (w i) (w (i+1)) ≠ 0` for all `i < n`. A chain exists: take any walk `q` from `u` to `v` given by `huv`. Then `(q.length, q.getVert)` is a chain, because `q.adj_getVert_succ` together with `diagramGraph_adj` gives `A (q.getVert i) (q.getVert (i+1)) ≠ 0`; no symmetry is needed. Let `n` be the least length of a chain (`Nat.find`), with witness `w`.

(1) Injectivity on `{i | i ≤ n}`: if `w a = w b` with `a < b ≤ n`, drop the segment from `a+1` to `b`. This gives a chain of length `n - (b - a) < n`, contradicting minimality.

(2) No chords: if `A (w i) (w j) ≠ 0` with `i+1 < j ≤ n`, the sequence `w 0, …, w i, w j, …, w n` is a chain of length `n - (j - i) + 1 < n`, again a contradiction.

Degenerate cases: when `u = v` the minimal chain has `n = 0` and the conditions hold vacuously. Diagonal entries never occur in the chord condition, since `i+1 < j` forces `i ≠ j`.

The current proof uses `h` only to turn adjacency into a single nonzero entry (`diagramGraph_adj_iff`) and for acyclicity. Neither is needed, because the minimal-chain argument above produces the conclusion directly.

### [`MeasureTheory.Measure.rnDeriv_mul_measure_singleton`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/MeasureTheory/Measure/Decomposition/Lebesgue.lean#L46-L58) — `tauceti-0170`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0170.lean`

- `[MeasurableSingletonClass α]` (variable): The proof uses `MeasurableSingletonClass` only in `withDensity_apply _ (measurableSet_singleton x)` and `lintegral_singleton`. The statement holds without it, with `μ {x}` and `ν {x}` read as outer measures.

(1) The step proving `singularPart μ ν {x} = 0` uses only `measure_mono_null` and the measurable null set of `mutuallySingular_singularPart`. It works for any set.

(2) `haveLebesgueDecomposition_add` needs only the HaveLebesgueDecomposition instance. `Measure.add_apply` holds for arbitrary sets (it is `rfl`, Module.lean:91). So `μ {x} = withDensity ν f {x}` with `f = μ.rnDeriv ν`.

(3) Show `withDensity ν f {x} = f x * ν {x}` without measurable singletons. `f` is measurable (`measurable_rnDeriv`), so `S0 := f ⁻¹' {f x}` is a measurable set containing `x`, and `f = f x` on it.
- Upper bound: let `T = toMeasurable ν {x}`. Monotonicity gives `withDensity ν f {x} ≤ withDensity ν f (S0 ∩ T)`. By `withDensity_apply` this equals `∫⁻ in S0 ∩ T, f ∂ν = f x * ν (S0 ∩ T) ≤ f x * ν T = f x * ν {x}`.
- Lower bound: by `measure_eq_iInf` (MeasureSpaceDef.lean:167) it suffices to treat measurable `T ⊇ {x}`. Then `withDensity ν f T = ∫⁻ in T, f ∂ν ≥ ∫⁻ in T ∩ S0, f ∂ν = f x * ν (T ∩ S0) ≥ f x * ν {x}`.

Degenerate cases: `f x = ∞` and empty types need no special treatment, because no `ENNReal.mul_iInf` step is used. `hx` and the decomposition instance are still used, so this hypothesis can be removed on its own, e.g. via `omit [MeasurableSingletonClass α] in`.

### [`TauCeti.exists_coprimePart_indK0_of_trivial_eq_nsmul`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/RepresentationTheory/GrothendieckGroup/GroupAlgebra/Permutation/PrimePower.lean#L131-L148) — `tauceti-0194`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0194.lean`

- `[Fact p.Prime]` (variable): Since k is a field with [CharP k p], Mathlib's `CharP.char_is_prime_or_zero` (Mathlib/Algebra/CharP/Defs.lean) gives `p.Prime ∨ p = 0`. Prime case: build the `Fact` instance from the primality proof and run the existing proof unchanged. Case p = 0: take D = C. `C ≤ C` is `le_rfl`. `¬ 0 ∣ Nat.card C` holds because `Nat.card C ≠ 0` (C is a finite, nonempty subgroup of the finite group G). Since 0 is not prime, `(Nat.card C).factorization 0 = 0`, so `0 ^ 0 = 1`; the index condition becomes `C.relIndex C = 1`, which is `Subgroup.relIndex_self`, and the K0 equation becomes `x = 1 • x` (`one_nsmul`). Nothing else in the statement needs the Fact instance to elaborate: indK0, ExactK0.of, FGModuleCat.of and relIndex need only Field k, Group G, Finite G and the subgroups.

### [`TauCeti.IsGradedExtInternallyFinite.shiftSourceInverse`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Algebra/Homology/EulerCharacteristic/ExtEuler/Graded/Shift.lean#L304-L308) — `tauceti-0229`
