- `[e.functor.Additive]` (variable): Nothing else in the statement needs this instance to elaborate. `IsGradedExtInternallyFinite` only needs `Field k`, `Linear k C` and `HasExt C`. In current Mathlib, `Functor.Linear` (Mathlib/CategoryTheory/Linear/LinearFunctor.lean) does not take `[F.Additive]` as a parameter, so `[e.functor.Linear k]` still makes sense without it. The instance also follows from the remaining hypotheses: `Functor.additive_of_preserves_binary_products` (Mathlib/CategoryTheory/Preadditive/AdditiveFunctor.lean) needs `[HasBinaryProducts C]`, which holds because C is abelian; `[PreservesLimitsOfShape (Discrete WalkingPair) e.functor]`, which every equivalence functor has; and `[e.functor.PreservesZeroMorphisms]`, given by the instance `preservesZeroMorphisms_of_full` because `e.functor` is full. So the proof starts with `have : e.functor.Additive := Functor.additive_of_preserves_binary_products _` and then runs unchanged through `gradedExtShiftSourceInverseEquiv`. No degenerate case matters, since the derivation works for every abelian C and every equivalence e.

### [`TauCeti.cwEulerChar_preimage_of_card_fiber`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/AlgebraicTopology/Cellular/EulerCharacteristic/Covering.lean#L75-L88) — `tauceti-0231`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0231.lean`

- `(hfin : ∀ b, Finite ↥(p ⁻¹' {b}))` (variable): The proof uses hfin only through `nat_card_cell_preimage_of_card_fiber`, which needs it for `Nat.card_sigma`. The per-degree identity Nat.card (cell (p⁻¹' C) n) = d * Nat.card (cell C n) holds without it. By `cellPreimageEquiv` the n-cells upstairs are Σ i : cell C n, p⁻¹' {map n i 0}. Each centre lies in C (`closedCell_subset_complex`, `map_zero_mem_closedCell`), so hd gives Nat.card of each such fibre = d. If d = 0, `Nat.card_eq_zero` makes each fibre empty or infinite. The sigma type is then empty, or infinite through the injective `Sigma.mk i`, so its Nat.card is 0 = 0 * _. If d > 0, each fibre over a centre is finite (Nat.card > 0 forces Finite). C has finitely many n-cells ([RelCWComplex.Finite C]), so `Nat.card_sigma` (which needs Finite only for these fibres) gives d * #cells. The rest of the proof (`cwEulerChar_eq_sum_range` for both complexes, the `hN'` step via `cellPreimageEquiv`) never uses hfin. Fibres outside C never matter. So hfin can be omitted. The proof changes only in replacing the call to `nat_card_cell_preimage_of_card_fiber` by this short case split.
- `[RelCWComplex.Finite C]` (variable): `cwEulerChar` is a plain `finsum` and `cwComplexPreimage` needs no finiteness, so the statement still elaborates without this instance. It is still true. Per degree, Nat.card (Σ i : cell C n, p⁻¹'{map n i 0}) = d * Nat.card (cell C n) for any C. If d = 0, every fibre over a centre is empty or infinite, so the sigma type is empty or infinite and has Nat.card 0. If d > 0, every fibre is nonempty with d points. When cell C n is finite, `Nat.card_sigma` gives d * card; when it is infinite, `Sigma.fst` is surjective, so the sigma type is infinite (`Infinite.of_surjective`) and both sides are 0. Hence the term for p⁻¹' C is d times the term for C, namely (-1)^n * (d * c_n) = d * ((-1)^n * c_n). Mathlib's `mul_finsum` for `ℤ` (with [NoZeroDivisors], no finite-support hypothesis; Finprod.lean in current master) then gives ∑ᶠ = d * ∑ᶠ. Without that lemma: if the support is infinite and d ≠ 0, both supports are infinite and both sums are 0; if d = 0, both sides are 0. The proof then avoids the `eventually_isEmpty_cell` / `cwEulerChar_eq_sum_range` route. Each of hfin and this instance can be removed on its own; the argument above also works with both removed.

### [`TauCeti.schwarzChristoffelEdgeAngle_comp_strictMono`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/Complex/Conformal/SchwarzChristoffel/GlobalTurning.lean#L114-L125) — `tauceti-0238`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0238.lean`

- `[LocallyFiniteOrder ι]` (variable): The statement never uses this instance. `schwarzChristoffelEdgeAngle` (Edge.lean) is `Real.pi * ∑ i, if c < a i then e i else 0`, which needs only `[Fintype ι]`, and `StrictMono` needs only the order on ι. The instance appears only in the proof, through the helper lemma `schwarzChristoffelEdgeAngle_sub_eq_neg_pi_mul_sum_Ioc` and its sum over `Finset.Ioc i j`. Proof without it: for i < j we have a i < a j. `schwarzChristoffelEdgeAngle_sub a e (ha hij).le` (Edge.lean, needs only Fintype) gives `angle (a i) - angle (a j) = π * ∑ k ∈ univ.filter (fun k ↦ a k ∈ Ioc (a i) (a j)), e k`. Every term is negative by `he`, and the filter contains j because a i < a j ≤ a j, so `Finset.sum_neg` makes the sum negative. With `Real.pi_pos` this gives angle (a i) < angle (a j). Alternatively, use `classical` and install `Fintype.toLocallyFiniteOrder` as a local instance, then keep the current proof. Degenerate cases (ι empty or with one element) are trivial. The other hypotheses are all needed: Fintype and LinearOrder for the statement to elaborate, `ha` because a constant `a` gives equal angles, and `he` because zero exponents give constant angles.

### [`TauCeti.PDE.exists_holderOnWith_preciseRepresentative_of_isCompact`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/PDE/Regularity/HolderContinuity.lean#L238-L258) — `tauceti-0281`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0281.lean`

- `[Nonempty ι]` (theorem): If ι is empty, EuclideanSpace ℝ ι is a subsingleton: two points agree at every coordinate because there are none. So any K has at most one element, and the conclusion holds trivially. Proof without the binder: split on `isEmpty_or_nonempty ι`. In the empty case take α = 1 (this gives 0 < α and α ≤ 1). For any Omega, a, u, K take C = 0 and close the goal with `Set.Subsingleton.holderOnWith` (Mathlib/Topology/MetricSpace/Holder.lean: `(hs : s.Subsingleton) (C r) (f) : HolderOnWith C r f s`), with `hs` given by `Set.subsingleton_of_subsingleton`; none of the other hypotheses are used. In the nonempty case the existing proof via `exists_holderOnWith_preciseRepresentative_of_isCompact_drift` works unchanged. [Nonempty ι] is mentioned nowhere else in the statement, so it can be deleted.

### [`TauCeti.WittVector.isOpen_windowSubring`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/RingTheory/WittVector/Window/Subring.lean#L474-L519) — `tauceti-0322`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0322.lean`

- `(hq' : q' ≠ 0)` (theorem): The theorem stays true without `hq'`. If `q' ≠ 0`, the existing proof applies unchanged. If `q' = 0`, then `q'.num = 0` and `q'.den = 1`, so `radiusUpperFrac p ϖ L 0 = mono p ϖ L (-1) 0`, which is `[ϖ]⁻¹` (definition at line 163). Next, `h₁` together with `hϖ'` forces `q.num ≥ 1`. This is the argument at lines 439–442 of `algebraMap_teichmuller_pow_mul_mem_windowSubring_of_norm_le_one`: if `q.num = 0`, `h₁` reads `1 ≤ v(ϖ)^q.den`, which contradicts `pow_lt_one₀` since `v(ϖ) < 1` and `q.den ≠ 0`. So `radiusLowerFrac q = mono q.den (-q.num)` with `q.num ≥ 1`. Then `radiusLowerFrac q * radiusUpperFrac 0 ^ q.den = mono 0 (-q.num)`, and multiplying by `p^(q.num-1)` from `𝕎 O` shows `p⁻¹ = mono 0 (-1)` is in the window subring. Both `[ϖ]⁻¹` and `p⁻¹` are then in it, so `mono (-m) (-m)` is in it for every `m`. By `exists_eq_algebraMap_mul_mono`, every element of `L` has the form `algebraMap y * mono (-m) (-m)`. Hence `windowSubring p ϖ L q 0 = ⊤`, and its carrier `Set.univ` is open (`isOpen_univ`). With the condition `q' ≠ 0` removed, the proof is: case split on `q' = 0`, prove `⊤` in that case (using `h₁` and `hϖ'`), and use the existing proof otherwise. The other hypotheses are different: `h₁` and `h₂` are needed to put the boundary directions of the norm cone inside the cone of the subring, and the remaining arguments are needed for `L` to elaborate.

### [`TauCeti.norm_eval_hasseDeriv_le_pow_of_norm_sub_le_one`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/DiophantineApproximation/Thue/AnalyticBound.lean#L54-L80) — `tauceti-0324`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0324.lean`

- `[NormOneClass R]` (variable): The bound holds in any `SeminormedRing`. The proof needs `NormOneClass` only because it calls two library lemmas that assume it: `Polynomial.norm_eval_le_l1Norm_mul` and `Polynomial.l1Norm_hasseDeriv_le_pow` (through `l1Norm_hasseDeriv_le`). Both statements stay true without it, and the rest of the proof only uses facts about real numbers and `norm_nonneg`.

(1) `norm_eval_le_l1Norm_mul` (TauCeti/Analysis/Polynomial/L1Norm.lean:216). The proof uses `NormOneClass` only in `norm_pow_le z i`, to get ‖z^i‖ ≤ ‖z‖^i. For i ≥ 1, Mathlib's `norm_pow_le'` (Analysis/Normed/Ring/Basic.lean:412, which needs `0 < n`) gives this. For i = 0 the term is `P.coeff 0 * z^0 = P.coeff 0`, whose norm is ‖P.coeff 0‖ = ‖P.coeff 0‖ * ‖z‖^0. So the bound ‖coeff i * z^i‖ ≤ ‖coeff i‖ * ‖z‖^i holds for every i, and the rest of that proof is unchanged.

(2) `l1Norm_hasseDeriv_le` (line 275). Here `NormOneClass` is used only in `Nat.norm_cast_le` with `norm_one`, to bound ‖(i.choose k : R) * c‖ ≤ choose * ‖c‖. Without it, rewrite `(n : R) * c = n • c` with `nsmul_eq_mul` (Algebra/Ring/Defs.lean:186) and apply `norm_nsmul_le` (Analysis/Normed/Group/Basic.lean:435, ‖n • a‖ ≤ n * ‖a‖, valid in any seminormed group). `l1Norm_hasseDeriv_le_pow` then follows exactly as before.

The other steps (`hα`, `hC₁` via `l1Norm_nonneg`, `hzα`, `Real.pow_le_rpow_pow_of_le_mul`, `natDegree_hasseDeriv_le`, `ring`) do not involve `NormOneClass`. Degenerate cases also work, because the argument goes through the same chain of inequalities: the zero ring, or a seminorm with ‖1‖ = 0, where the left side is 0 and the right side is ≥ 0 by `hC₁` and `positivity`; and r = 0. A ring where ‖1‖ > 1 is also fine, since nothing above uses ‖1‖ = 1. The hypotheses `hQ`, `hQC` and `hz` are all needed. For example, without `hz` or `hQ`, Q = X^100 over ℝ gives a counterexample.

### [`TauCeti.IsDemushkin.range_demushkinCharacter_ne_unitsPlusMinus_of_demushkinRank_eq_two`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Topology/Algebra/Group/Profinite/Demushkin/NormalForm/Two/Even/Image.lean#L112-L119) — `tauceti-0328`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0328.lean`

- `(hf : 2 ≤ f)` (theorem): The statement stays true for f = 0 and f = 1. By definition `unitsPlusMinus f = unitsPrincipal 2 f ⊔ zpowers (-1)` (DyadicUnits.lean:84). Two lemmas in PrincipalUnits.lean give `unitsPrincipal_zero : unitsPrincipal p 0 = ⊤` and `unitsPrincipal_two_one : unitsPrincipal 2 1 = ⊤`. So for f ≤ 1, `le_sup_left` gives `⊤ ≤ unitsPlusMinus f`, hence `unitsPlusMinus f = ⊤`. DyadicUnits.lean:146 has `unitsPlusMinus_two : unitsPlusMinus 2 = ⊤`. So for f < 2, `unitsPlusMinus f = unitsPlusMinus 2`.

Proof without `hf`: split on whether `2 ≤ f`. If it holds, the current proof works unchanged. Otherwise, rewrite `h : range = unitsPlusMinus f` into `range = unitsPlusMinus 2`, using the equalities above. Then apply `not_exists_topologicalClosure_zpowers_eq_unitsPlusMinus (le_refl 2)` to `⟨u, hu.trans h⟩`, where `u` comes from `exists_topologicalClosure_zpowers_eq_range_demushkinCharacter_of_demushkinRank_eq_two hn`.

The mathematical reason: ℤ_2ˣ is not procyclic, but the image of the character in rank two is procyclic. The other hypotheses (`hG`, `hn`, and the instances on `G` that `IsDemushkin` needs) are used or needed to state the theorem.

### [`Matrix.ProjectiveSpecialLinearGroup.isParabolic_upperRightHom_iff`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/LinearAlgebra/Matrix/ProjectiveSpecialLinearGroup/FinTwo.lean#L167-L172) — `tauceti-0334`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0334.lean`

- `[NoZeroDivisors R]` (variable): The statement is true for every commutative ring R. Definitions used: `IsParabolic g := ∃ a : SL(2,R), ↑a = g ∧ (a : GL).IsParabolic`. `Matrix.IsParabolic m := m ∉ range (scalar _) ∧ m.discr = 0`. `discr_fin_two : A.discr = A.trace^2 - 4*A.det`, which needs only a commutative ring.

(←) Suppose x ≠ 0. Take a = transvection x as the witness: its class is `upperRightHom x` by `rfl` (`upperRightHom_apply`). Its matrix is !![1,x;0,1], so trace = 2 and det = 1, giving discr = 4 - 4 = 0. It is not scalar because its (0,1) entry is x ≠ 0, while every scalar matrix has (0,1) entry 0. This direction never uses `isParabolic_mk_iff`, which is the only lemma here that needs NoZeroDivisors.

(→) Suppose x = 0. Then `upperRightHom 0 = 1`. A witness a would satisfy `(a : PSL) = 1`, i.e. a ∈ center SL(2,R) (`QuotientGroup.eq_one_iff`). Mathlib's `SpecialLinearGroup.mem_center_iff` holds over any commutative ring and makes every central element scalar (`scalar n r = A`), so a is not parabolic, a contradiction.

Degenerate case: if R is the trivial ring, every matrix is scalar, so both sides are False and the statement still holds.

So the proof only needs to change by avoiding `isParabolic_mk_iff` and `Matrix.isParabolic_iff_of_upperTriangular` (which needs `[IsReduced R]`). The direct argument above replaces them, so `omit [NoZeroDivisors R] in` works.

### [`TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.IsIsogeny.dualCotangentMap_mem_adjointWeightSpace_iff`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Algebra/AlgebraicGroup/Isogeny/RootSpace.lean#L51-L60) — `tauceti-0356`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0356.lean`

- `(hf : IsIsogeny f)` (theorem): The proof only needs `Function.Injective (dualCotangentMap f.hom)`, which it gets from the bijectivity lemma `dualCotangentMap_bijective_of_etaleKernel`. The isogeny hypothesis (through `finrank_lie_eq`) is used there only for surjectivity. Injectivity follows from `hker` alone. `TauCeti.CommHopfAlgCat.algebraEtale_quotient_kernelHopfIdeal_iff [Algebra.FiniteType k K] (f : H ⟶ K)` (HopfIdeal/Quotient/Kernel/Etale.lean) says that `Algebra.Etale k (K ⧸ ker)` is equivalent to `Function.Injective (derivationCompLieHom (B := k) f.hom)`. It needs no isogeny and no smoothness. `Algebra.FiniteType k K` comes from `[Algebra.Smooth k K]`, since smooth includes finite presentation, and Mathlib has the instance from finite presentation to finite type. Proof of injectivity: suppose `dualCotangentMap f.hom x = dualCotangentMap f.hom y`. Apply `cotangentLinearEquiv (A := H)` and rewrite with `Derivation.cotangentLinearEquiv_dualCotangentMap`. This gives `derivationComp f.hom (e_K x) = derivationComp f.hom (e_K y)`, which is `derivationCompLieHom f.hom` applied to both sides (`derivationCompLieHom_apply`). Injectivity of that map gives `e_K x = e_K y`, so `x = y`. This is the same pattern as the proof of `BialgHom.dualCotangentMap_injective_of_surjective`. Then `f.hom.dualCotangentMap_mem_adjointWeightSpace_iff_of_injective` closes the goal. `hf` appears nowhere else in the statement. This deletion is independent of the `[Algebra.Smooth k H]` deletion; both can be removed together.
- `[Algebra.Smooth k H]` (variable): The statement never mentions this instance. It is used only inside `dualCotangentMap_bijective_of_etaleKernel`, through `finrank_lie_eq`, to get surjectivity of the differential. The theorem only needs injectivity. As explained for `hf`, injectivity follows from `algebraEtale_quotient_kernelHopfIdeal_iff`, which needs only `[Algebra.FiniteType k K]` (supplied by `[Algebra.Smooth k K]`), together with `Derivation.cotangentLinearEquiv_dualCotangentMap`. Then `BialgHom.dualCotangentMap_mem_adjointWeightSpace_iff_of_injective` applies, and it has no smoothness assumptions. So `omit [Algebra.Smooth k H] in` leaves a true and provable theorem.

### [`TauCeti.AlgebraicGeometry.EllipticCurveGeom.isClopen_naiveLocus`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/EllipticCurve/Scheme/Geom/NaiveLevel.lean#L239-L253) — `tauceti-0381`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0381.lean`

- `[Finite A]` (theorem): The proof uses `[Finite A]` only through `isClopen_iInter_of_finite`, but the statement is still true for infinite `A`. Proof without it: split on whether `A` is finite. If it is, the existing proof works unchanged.

If `A` is infinite, the naive locus is empty, so it is clopen. Take a point `h` of `H.left`, let `κ = κ(h)` and let `t : Spec κ → H.left` be the canonical map, viewed as a morphism in `Over S`. Precomposition with `t` is a group homomorphism `ψ : A →* E(κ)` into the group of `κ`-valued `S`-points of the group object `E`. Its values are killed by `N` because `hu` gives `u a ^ N = 1`. The `N`-torsion of `E(κ)` is finite: it is the set of `κ`-points of `E[N]` base-changed to `Spec κ`. That scheme is finite over a field (`isFinite_torsion_hom` in `Scheme/Geom/MulBy/Finite.lean`, with `N ≠ 0`), and a finite algebra over a field has only finitely many `κ`-points. This step only needs `N ≠ 0`. That follows from `hN` when `S` is nonempty. When `S` is empty, `H.left` is empty and the result is trivial.

Since `ψ` has finite image and `A` is infinite, `ker ψ` contains some `a ≠ 1`. Then `t ≫ (u a).left = t ≫ (H.hom ≫ E.zero)`. Evaluating at the unique point of `Spec κ`, which `t` sends to `h`, gives `(u a).left h = E.zero (H.hom h)`, so `h` is not in `naiveLocus u` (see `mem_naiveLocus_iff`). So for infinite `A` the naive locus is `∅`, and `isClopen_empty` closes this case.

The other hypotheses `hN` and `hu` stay needed. Without `hu` the locus need not be closed even for finite `A`.

### [`TauCeti.toZModPow_three_localCyclotomicCharacter_eq_one_or_eq_three`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/FieldTheory/Galois/AbsoluteGaloisGroup/Cyclotomic/Range.lean#L137-L187) — `tauceti-0383`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0383.lean`

- `[NeZero (2 : K)]` (theorem): The theorem stays true when 2 = 0 in K, because the character then takes Mathlib's junk value 1.

**Definitions.** In the library, `localCyclotomicCharacter 2 K σ` is defined as `cyclotomicCharacter (AlgebraicClosure K) 2 σ.toRingEquiv`. In Mathlib, `cyclotomicCharacter L p` is `MonoidHom.toHomUnits` applied to `cyclotomicCharacter.toFun p`. That function is defined as `if H : ∀ i, ∃ ζ : L, IsPrimitiveRoot ζ (p ^ i) then ... else 1`.

**When 2 = 0 in K.** Then 2 = 0 in `AlgebraicClosure K`. Suppose ζ were a primitive 2nd root of unity there. By `IsPrimitiveRoot.eq_neg_one_of_two_right`, which needs no characteristic assumption, ζ = -1. In characteristic 2, -1 = 1, so ζ = 1. This contradicts `IsPrimitiveRoot.ne_one` with 1 < 2. So the condition of the `if` fails at i = 1. By `dif_neg`, `toFun` equals 1, so the coercion of the unit to ℤ_[2] is 1. Then `PadicInt.toZModPow 3 1 = 1` by `map_one`, and the left disjunct holds. The hypothesis `hs` gives nothing extra here: it only says s = 0.

**When 2 ≠ 0 in K.** We get `NeZero (2 : K)` from that fact, and the existing proof applies unchanged.

So a proof without the binder starts with `by_cases (2 : K) = 0`, handles the first case as above, and reuses the current proof in the second. The conclusion does not mention the binder, so deleting it needs no change to the statement.

### [`WeierstrassCurve.natCard_tateCubicRoots_le`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/EllipticCurve/TateAlgorithm/LocalIndex.lean#L339-L357) — `tauceti-0390`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0390.lean`

- `(h₆ : ϖ ^ 3 ∣ W.a₆)` (theorem): Proof: split on `ϖ ^ 3 ∣ W.a₆`. If it holds, the existing proof applies. If not, `tateCubicRoots` is empty. Suppose t = mk t₀ with ϖ⁴ ∣ (ϖt₀)³ + a₂(ϖt₀)² + a₄(ϖt₀) + a₆. By h₂ and h₄, ϖ³ divides each of the first three terms (ϖ³t₀³, ϖ·ϖ²t₀², ϖ²·ϖt₀). Since ϖ³ ∣ ϖ⁴, `dvd_sub` gives ϖ³ ∣ a₆, a contradiction. So `Nat.card` is 0 ≤ 3. This argument needs h₂ and h₄, so h₆ can be deleted only on its own.
- `(h₄ : ϖ ^ 2 ∣ W.a₄)` (theorem): Proof: split on `ϖ ^ 2 ∣ W.a₄`. If it holds, the existing proof applies. If not, `tateCubicRoots ⊆ {0}`. Suppose t = mk t₀ with ϖ⁴ ∣ g(t₀). By h₂ and h₆, ϖ³ divides ϖ³t₀³, a₂ϖ²t₀² and a₆, so ϖ³ ∣ ϖ·(a₄t₀). Cancel ϖ, which is allowed because R is a domain and ϖ ≠ 0 (`mul_dvd_mul_iff_left`). This gives ϖ² ∣ a₄t₀. Now assume ϖ ∤ t₀. Since ϖ is prime, ϖ ∣ a₄, so a₄ = ϖc. Then ϖ² ∣ ϖct₀, so ϖ ∣ ct₀, so ϖ ∣ c, so ϖ² ∣ a₄, a contradiction. Hence ϖ ∣ t₀, and t = mk t₀ = 0 by `Ideal.Quotient.eq_zero_iff_mem` and `Ideal.mem_span_singleton`. Then `Nat.card` ≤ ncard {0} = 1 ≤ 3, using `Nat.card_coe_set_eq`, `Set.ncard_le_ncard` and `Set.ncard_singleton`. This argument needs h₂ and h₆, so h₄ can be deleted only on its own.
- `(h₂ : ϖ ∣ W.a₂)` (theorem): Proof: split on `ϖ ∣ W.a₂`. If it holds, the existing proof applies. If not, `tateCubicRoots ⊆ {0}`. Suppose t = mk t₀ with ϖ⁴ ∣ (ϖt₀)³ + a₂(ϖt₀)² + a₄(ϖt₀) + a₆. By h₄ and h₆, ϖ³ divides ϖ³t₀³, a₄ϖt₀ and a₆, so ϖ³ ∣ ϖ²·(a₂t₀²). Cancel ϖ² using `mul_dvd_mul_iff_left` (R is a domain and ϖ ≠ 0), giving ϖ ∣ a₂t₀². Since ϖ is prime and ϖ ∤ a₂, ϖ ∣ t₀², so ϖ ∣ t₀ by `Prime.dvd_of_dvd_pow`. Hence mk t₀ = 0, and `Nat.card` ≤ ncard {0} = 1 ≤ 3, using `Nat.card_coe_set_eq`, `Set.ncard_le_ncard` and `Set.ncard_singleton`. This argument needs h₄ and h₆, so h₂ can be deleted only on its own; I did not check whether two of h₂, h₄, h₆ can be deleted at once.

### [`TauCeti.perronIntegrand_zero`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/ArithmeticDirichletSeries/Perron/Basic.lean#L93-L98) — `tauceti-0408`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0408.lean`

- `(hc : c ≠ 0)` (theorem): By definition (line 75 of the file), `perronIntegrand 0 c t = (0 : ℂ) ^ s / s` with `s = c + t * I`. If `s ≠ 0`, then `Complex.zero_cpow` gives `0 ^ s = 0`, so the value is `0 / s = 0`. If `s = 0`, the value is `0 ^ 0 / 0 = 1 / 0`, and this is `0` by the junk-value convention `div_zero`. So the statement holds for every `c`, including `c = 0`. The case `s = 0` happens only when `c = 0` and `t = 0`. The proof uses `hc` only to show `s ≠ 0`. Without it the proof becomes: `rw [perronIntegrand_def, Complex.ofReal_zero]`, then `by_cases h : (c : ℂ) + t * I = 0`. In the case `h`, `rw [h, div_zero]` closes the goal. Otherwise, `rw [Complex.zero_cpow h, zero_div]` closes it.

### [`TauCeti.primePowerSummatory_isLittleO_of_le_higherPrimePowerWeight`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/ArithmeticDirichletSeries/HigherPrimePowers.lean#L368-L377) — `tauceti-0414`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0414.lean`

- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (theorem): `hw` already forces the conclusion for every real `C`. The lemma `higherPrimePowerWeight_nonneg` (HigherPrimePowers.lean, line 204) says `0 ≤ higherPrimePowerWeight A` for all `A`. So from `hw` we get, for every `A`, `‖w A‖ ≤ C * higherPrimePowerWeight A ≤ max C 0 * higherPrimePowerWeight A`, using `mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right (le_max_left C 0) (higherPrimePowerWeight_nonneg A)`. The proof without `hC` is: `(primePowerSummatory_isBigO_of_le_higherPrimePowerWeight K (le_max_right C 0) (fun A ↦ (hw A).trans (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right (le_max_left C 0) (higherPrimePowerWeight_nonneg A)))).trans_isLittleO isLittleO_sqrt_mul_log_sq`. This applies the big-O lemma with the constant `max C 0`, which is nonnegative. Negative `C` is not a counterexample. In that case `hw` forces `‖w A‖ ≤ 0`, so `w = 0` and the summatory function is zero, which is trivially o(x). The constructed proof covers this case anyway. Other degenerate cases (for example a trivial `E`) cause no issue.

### [`MeasureTheory.exists_intervalStep_dist_lt`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/MeasureTheory/Function/IntervalStep.lean#L74-L95) — `tauceti-0416`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0416.lean`

- `(hab : a < b)` (theorem): `intervalStep a δ g t` is defined as `g (a + ⌊(t - a) / δ⌋₊ * δ)` (line 37). Without `hab`, the statement still holds. Split on `lt_trichotomy a b`. (1) If `a < b`, use the existing proof. (2) If `b < a`, then `Icc a b = ∅` (`Set.Icc_eq_empty`). Take `N = 1`; the condition on `t` holds vacuously. (3) If `a = b`, take `N = 1`. Then `Icc a a = {a}`, so `t = a`, and the step is `(b - a) / 1 = 0`. So `intervalStep a 0 g a = g (a + ⌊0/0⌋₊ * 0) = g a`, because the product with `0` is `0` whatever the floor is. Hence `dist (g a) (g a) = 0 < ε`, using `hε`. The other hypotheses are still needed. `hε` is needed because `Icc a b` is nonempty when `a ≤ b` and a distance cannot be `< ε ≤ 0`. `hg` is needed because without continuity a fixed sampling cannot approximate uniformly.

### [`TauCeti.mem_unitFiltration_of_val_eq_smul_div`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/LocalField/UnitFiltration/RamificationGroup.lean#L182-L193) — `tauceti-0417`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0417.lean`

- `(hϖ : Irreducible ϖ)` (theorem): The statement holds for any ϖ : 𝒪[L]. The same file proves `TauCeti.smul_div_mem_unitFiltration` (line 213): for σ ∈ ramificationGroup G 𝒪[L] (i : ℤ) and any units y z : Lˣ with (z : L) = σ • (y : L) / (y : L), we have z ∈ unitFiltration L i. It needs only hσ and picks its own uniformizer via `IsDiscreteValuationRing.exists_irreducible`. Proof without hϖ: split on (ϖ : L) = 0. If (ϖ : L) = 0, then hy gives (y : L) = σ • 0 / 0 = 0, which contradicts y.ne_zero, so this case is vacuous. Otherwise apply `smul_div_mem_unitFiltration hσ (y := Units.mk0 (ϖ : L) h) hy`. That theorem currently calls this one for its chosen irreducible ϖ, so to avoid circularity keep the present proof (with hϖ) as a private lemma and use it in both `smul_div_mem_unitFiltration` and the hϖ-free version. The general fact is also true mathematically: write ϖ = π^k·u; σπ/π lies in U(L,i) and σu/u lies in U(L,i+1), and the unit filtration is a subgroup.

### [`TauCeti.ae_eq_zero_of_forall_integral_mul_sin_eq_zero`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/Fourier/SineSeries.lean#L211-L227) — `tauceti-0449`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0449.lean`

- `(hab : a < b)` (theorem): `hab` is used only in the proof. The conclusion is an a.e. statement for `volume.restrict (Ioc a b)`, so the case `b ≤ a` holds trivially. New proof: do `by_cases hab : a < b`. If `a < b`, run the existing proof. Otherwise `Ioc a b = ∅` by `Set.Ioc_eq_empty hab` (its argument is `¬a < b`, as the uses in Mathlib/Order/Interval/Set/Basic.lean show). Then `Measure.restrict_empty` turns the measure into `0`, and `ae_zero : ae 0 = ⊥` makes any `EventuallyEq` hold, e.g. by `simp [Set.Ioc_eq_empty hab]`. The other hypotheses are genuinely needed. Without `hg`, take `g x = 1/(x-c)^2` with `(c-a)/(b-a)` irrational. Then every `g * sin(kπ(x-a)/(b-a))` is non-integrable near `c`, so every interval integral is 0 by the junk-value convention, yet `g` is not a.e. zero.

### [`exists_eq_mulInvariantOneParameterSubgroup`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Geometry/Lie/Exponential/Classification.lean#L186-L242) — `tauceti-0454`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0454.lean`

- `[IsManifold I 1 G]` (variable): The theorem takes this variable because its type mentions only I and G. But `LieGroup I ∞ G` extends `ContMDiffMul I ∞ G`, which extends `IsManifold I ∞ G`. Mathlib's instance `instance {a : ℕ∞ω} [IsManifold I ∞ M] [h : LEInfty a] : IsManifold I a M` (Mathlib/Geometry/Manifold/IsManifold/Basic.lean:844), with the `LEInfty (1 : ℕ∞ω)` instance, gives `IsManifold I 1 G`. Since `IsManifold` is a Prop, the statement keeps its meaning, and every use in the proof or the auxiliary lemmas is filled by the derived instance (or a `haveI := IsManifold.of_le ...`). The hypotheses can be deleted independently or together.
- `[BoundarylessManifold I G]` (theorem): The library's own `ContMDiffMul.boundarylessManifold [ContMDiffMul I 1 G] : BoundarylessManifold I G` (TauCeti/Geometry/Lie/Interior.lean:66) needs only C^1 multiplication. `LieGroup I ∞ G` extends `ContMDiffMul I ∞ G`, and Mathlib's `ContMDiffMul.of_le` turns that into `ContMDiffMul I 1 G`. So the proof can start with `haveI : BoundarylessManifold I G := ...` (sibling files already use `attribute [local instance] ContMDiffMul.boundarylessManifold`) and continue unchanged.
- `[T2Space G]` (theorem): `ModelWithCorners.t1Space` (Mathlib/Geometry/Manifold/IsManifold/Basic.lean:459) gives `T1Space G` from `[ChartedSpace H G]` with the model `I`. `topologicalGroup_of_lieGroup I ∞` (Mathlib/Geometry/Manifold/Algebra/LieGroup.lean:133) gives `IsTopologicalGroup G`. Then `IsTopologicalGroup.t2Space_iff_one_closed` with `isClosed_singleton` gives `T2Space G`, since a T1 topological group is Hausdorff. So the proof can derive the instance locally with `haveI` and continue unchanged. This works for every G, including trivial or empty ones.

### [`TauCeti.exists_notMem_nsmul_mem_smul_sub_mem_of_isProP`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Topology/Algebra/Group/Profinite/ProP/FixedPoints.lean#L129-L163) — `tauceti-0483`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0483.lean`

- `(hN : ∀ g : G, ∀ x ∈ N, g • x ∈ N)` (theorem): `hN` appears nowhere else in the statement. The proof uses it only to put an action on `M ⧸ N`. The conclusion still holds for any subgroup `N ≠ ⊤`, by reduction to the current (stable) version applied to a maximal `G`-stable subgroup inside `N`.

Argument: let S be the set of `G`-stable `AddSubgroup`s of M contained in N. S is nonempty, since `⊥` is in it. S is finite, since `M` is finite. So S has a maximal element K. K ≤ N, and K ≠ ⊤ because N ≠ ⊤. Apply the current theorem (with hN) to K. This gives x with x ∉ K, p • x ∈ K and g • x - x ∈ K for all g.

The subgroup K ⊔ zmultiples x is G-stable: g • (k + n • x) = g • k + n • (g • x - x) + n • x. If x were in N, this subgroup would be a G-stable subgroup of N strictly larger than K, contradicting maximality. So x ∉ N. Since K ≤ N, we also get p • x ∈ N and g • x - x ∈ N for all g. This is exactly the conclusion.

Edge cases: if N = ⊥, then K = ⊥ and the result is the original one. N = ⊤ is excluded by hN', which is still assumed. A trivial M is excluded by N ≠ ⊤.

Equivalently, one can use the G-stable filtration 0 = M_0 < … < M_r = M with 𝔽_p factors and trivial action from the Filtration file. Take the least i with M_i ⊄ N and any x ∈ M_i \ N. Then p • x and g • x - x lie in M_{i-1} ≤ N.

### [`TauCeti.Measure.ext_of_forall_integral_pow_eq`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Probability/Moments/Determinacy.lean#L211-L218) — `tauceti-0517`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0517.lean`

- `[IsFiniteMeasure μ]` (theorem): This instance follows from `hμ`. By `interior_subset`, `hμ` gives `0 ∈ integrableExpSet id μ`. By the definition of `integrableExpSet` (Mathlib/Probability/Moments/IntegrableExpMul.lean:112), that means `Integrable (fun ω ↦ exp (0 * id ω)) μ`, and `simp` turns this into `Integrable (fun _ ↦ (1:ℝ)) μ`. Then `integrable_const_iff_isFiniteMeasure one_ne_zero` (Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/L1Space/Integrable.lean:145) yields `IsFiniteMeasure μ`. `charFun_eq_of_forall_integral_pow_eq` takes no finiteness instance. So the new proof builds this instance with `haveI` before calling `Measure.ext_of_charFun`; nothing else changes. There are no degenerate cases to worry about: whenever `hμ` holds, the constant 1 is integrable, so μ is finite. This can be removed together with the analogous instance for ν.
- `[IsFiniteMeasure ν]` (theorem): This instance follows from `hν` by the same argument. `interior_subset hν` gives `Integrable (fun ω ↦ exp (0 * id ω)) ν`, which `simp` turns into `Integrable (fun _ ↦ (1:ℝ)) ν`. Then `integrable_const_iff_isFiniteMeasure one_ne_zero` gives `IsFiniteMeasure ν`. Build it with `haveI` before applying `Measure.ext_of_charFun (charFun_eq_of_forall_integral_pow_eq hμ hν hmom)`. This can be removed independently of, or together with, the instance for μ.

### [`TauCeti.IsManifoldNondegenerateCriticalPoint.mem_criticalPoints`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Geometry/Manifold/Morse/CriticalPoints.lean#L166-L170) — `tauceti-0534`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0534.lean`

- `[I.Boundaryless]` (variable): `IsManifoldNondegenerateCriticalPoint I f x` is defined as `IsNondegenerateCriticalPoint (f ∘ (extChartAt I x).symm) (extChartAt I x x)` (TauCeti/Geometry/Manifold/Morse/Basic.lean). That structure contains `contDiffAt : ContDiffAt ℝ 2 g p` and `fderiv_eq_zero : fderiv ℝ g p = 0`, where `g = f ∘ ψ.symm`, `ψ = extChartAt I x` and `p = ψ x`. Both facts are about the full neighbourhood of `p` in `E`. Boundaryless is only needed for the *iff* `mem_criticalPoints_iff_fderiv_comp_extChartAt_symm_eq_zero`. Here we only need one direction, and that direction holds for any model with corners.

Proof without the instance. First rewrite with `mem_criticalPoints_iff_mfderiv_eq_zero`, which is stated outside the Boundaryless section and takes no extra instances. The goal becomes `mfderiv I 𝓘(ℝ) f x = 0`. Then split on `MDifferentiableAt I 𝓘(ℝ) f x`.
- If `f` is not MDifferentiableAt `x`, `mfderiv_zero_of_not_mdifferentiableAt` gives 0. This is the junk value in the definition of `mfderiv` (`if MDifferentiableAt … then … else 0`, Mathlib/Geometry/Manifold/MFDeriv/Defs.lean).
- If it is, `mfderiv` unfolds to `fderivWithin ℝ (writtenInExtChartAt I 𝓘(ℝ) x f) (range I) (ψ x)`, up to the identity casts `tangentSpaceCastModel`. `writtenInExtChartAt I 𝓘(ℝ) x f = extChartAt 𝓘(ℝ) (f x) ∘ f ∘ ψ.symm`, and this is `g` by `extChartAt_model_space_eq_id`. `g` is DifferentiableAt `p`, from `h.contDiffAt.differentiableAt` (2 ≠ 0). `range I` is UniqueDiffWithinAt `p`, by `I.uniqueDiffOn _ (mem_range_self _)` or `ModelWithCorners.uniqueDiffWithinAt_image`, with no boundaryless assumption. So `DifferentiableAt.fderivWithin` turns the derivative into `fderiv ℝ g p`, which is 0 by `h.fderiv_eq_zero`.

The statement does not mention `I.Boundaryless`, and no other binder depends on it, so it can be dropped (e.g. with `omit [I.Boundaryless] in`) and the theorem stays true and provable.

### [`TauCeti.tsum_fiber_inter_setOf_regular_ae_eq`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/MeasureTheory/Function/AreaFormula/Multiplicity.lean#L128-L143) — `tauceti-0536`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0536.lean`

- `[BorelSpace U]` (variable): Nothing in the statement depends on the σ-algebra on `U`. The only measure is `μHE[finrank ℝ U]`, which is `Measure V` (`euclideanHausdorffMeasure` in Mathlib's `Geometry/Euclidean/Volume/Measure.lean` uses only the metric, `MeasurableSpace V` and `BorelSpace V`). The rest of the statement (tsums over subsets of `U`, `DifferentiableAt`, `fderiv`, `normDet`) does not mention `MeasurableSpace U` at all. The proof uses `[MeasurableSpace U] [BorelSpace U]` only through `LipschitzWith.euclideanHausdorffMeasure_image_compl_setOf_regular` (Lipschitz.lean:175). That lemma needs them for Rademacher's theorem and volume on `U`, but its conclusion `μHE[finrank ℝ U] (f '' {x | …}ᶜ) = 0` also does not involve the measurable structure of `U`. So without `[BorelSpace U]` (keeping the arbitrary `[MeasurableSpace U]`, which the statement then ignores), the proof becomes: `letI : MeasurableSpace U := borel U; haveI : BorelSpace U := ⟨rfl⟩`, then the same `filter_upwards [measure_eq_zero_iff_ae_notMem.mp hf.euclideanHausdorffMeasure_image_compl_setOf_regular]` and the rest of the proof as is. The lemma's conclusion is literally the same proposition whichever `MeasurableSpace U` instance is used. No degenerate cases arise, because the change is only about which σ-algebra on `U` is used inside the proof. `[MeasurableSpace U]` cannot be deleted on its own, because the type of `[BorelSpace U]` mentions it. It is only extra together with `BorelSpace U`, so only `[BorelSpace U]` is reported.

### [`TauCeti.baseChangeExp_lie`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Algebra/Lie/Derivation/IntegralExp.lean#L151-L171) — `tauceti-0548`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0548.lean`

- `(hD : IsNilpotent D.toLinearMap)` (theorem): `baseChangeExp x M hM t` is defined (BaseChangeAction.lean, l.259) as `∑ n ∈ range (nilpotencyClass x), t ^ n • (integralDividedPower x M n (hM n)).baseChange R`. Mathlib defines `nilpotencyClass x := sInf {k | x ^ k = 0}`. If `D.toLinearMap` is not nilpotent, this set is empty and the class is 0: by contraposition of Mathlib's `isNilpotent_of_pos_nilpotencyClass` (no extra hypotheses), `¬ 0 < nilpotencyClass _`. Then `baseChangeExp D.toLinearMap M hM t` is the empty sum, i.e. `0`, and the statement reduces to `0 = ⁅0, 0⁆`, which holds by `zero_lie`/`lie_zero`. So the proof is: `by_cases hD : IsNilpotent D.toLinearMap`. In the positive case, keep the existing proof. In the negative case, rewrite the class to 0, unfold `baseChangeExp` (`range 0 = ∅`, `sum_empty`), use `LinearMap.zero_apply` and `zero_lie`. The degenerate case where L is trivial is consistent: there the class is 0 and both sides are 0. `hD` is used nowhere else in the statement, so it can be deleted without changing anything else.

### [`TauCeti.exists_tendsto_nhdsWithin_circleMap_image_Ioo`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/Complex/Conformal/Crosscut/EndpointLimit.lean#L292-L312) — `tauceti-0560`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0560.lean`

- `(hab : a < b)` (theorem): `hθ₀ : θ₀ ∈ Icc a b` already gives `a ≤ b`. So if `a < b` fails, then `a = b`. In that case `Ioo a a = ∅`, so `circleMap ζ ρ '' Ioo a b = ∅` (`Set.image_empty`). The filter is then `𝓝[∅] (circleMap ζ ρ θ₀) = ⊥` (Mathlib `nhdsWithin_empty`), and `Tendsto f ⊥ (𝓝 v)` holds for any `v` (Mathlib `tendsto_bot`), e.g. `v = 0`. The proof without `hab`: split with `rcases hθ₀.1.lt_or_eq`. When `a < b`, run the existing proof (the only places it uses `hab` are `circleMap_mem_closure_image_Ioo` and `subsingleton_clusterSetOn_circleMap_image_Ioo`). When `a = b`, take `⟨0, by simp [nhdsWithin_empty, tendsto_bot]⟩` after rewriting `Ioo_self` / `image_empty`. Every other hypothesis is genuinely needed. `hab2π` is needed: with `b - a = 2π` and `f` a branch of `log` on the unit circle minus one point, the one-sided limits at the endpoint differ. `hUo` is needed: on a non-open `U` such as the arc itself, `deriv f` can be the junk value 0, which makes `hfin` vacuous while `f` oscillates along the arc.

### [`Polynomial.map_psc_of_right_degree_drop`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/RingTheory/Polynomial/Subresultant/DegreeDrop/Basic.lean#L192-L205) — `tauceti-0567`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0567.lean`

- `(hm : (p.map f).natDegree ≤ m)` (theorem): `psc` never reads coefficients of the left input above the left bound. In `subresultantMatrix p q m n j` (Subresultant/Basic.lean:67), a `p`-block entry is `if k ≤ i+j ∧ i+j ≤ k+m then p.coeff (i+j-k) else 0`, so the index is always ≤ m. With `coeff_reductum` (`(p.reductum k).coeff i = if i < k then p.coeff i else 0`), `ext` + `Fin.addCases` + `simp [subresultantMatrix, coeff_reductum]` + `omega` give `subresultantMatrix P Q m N j = subresultantMatrix (P.reductum (m+1)) Q m N j` for every P, Q, N. Hence `psc P Q m N j = psc (P.reductum (m+1)) Q m N j`. Proof without `hm`: set `p' := p.reductum (m+1)`. It satisfies `(p'.map f).natDegree ≤ m` by `natDegree_map_le` and `natDegree_reductum_lt`. Apply the current theorem to `p'` (the other hypotheses do not involve p). Then rewrite: `psc p q m N j = psc p' q m N j`; `p'.coeff m = p.coeff m` by `coeff_reductum`; and `p'.map f = (p.map f).reductum (m+1)` by `reductum_map`, so `psc (p'.map f) _ m n j = psc (p.map f) _ m n j` by the same truncation fact. No degenerate case is excluded: the truncation argument holds for all p, m, j, including p = 0. Sanity check: p = X^2, q = 1, m = 1, n = j = 0, N = 1 gives 0 on both sides.

### [`TauCeti.PDE.UniformlyEllipticOn.ae_inv_value_le_mul_setIntegral_rpow`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/PDE/Regularity/Supersolution/Basic.lean#L323-L405) — `tauceti-0576`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0576.lean`

- `(hR : 0 < R)` (theorem): If R ≤ 0, then R/2 ≤ 0, so `ball x₀ (R / 2) = ∅` (`Metric.ball_eq_empty`). Then `mu.restrict ∅ = 0` and every `∀ᵐ` statement for that measure holds, including the conclusion. The proof uses hR only to show `ball x₀ (R/2) ⊆ ball x₀ R` (via `half_le_self hR.le`) and so get `hhalf`. So the new proof starts with `rcases le_or_lt R 0`: if R ≤ 0 the conclusion is trivial as above, and if R > 0 the existing proof runs unchanged with the local fact `0 < R`.
- `(hK : 0 ≤ K)` (theorem): `0 ≤ K` follows from the other hypotheses (keeping hR). Suppose K < 0. Take k = 0 and w = `W1p.contDiffComp … invApprox (c 0) … u`, exactly as in the proof. The existing argument (`hw`, which does not use hK) gives `hsub w …`: a.e. on ball x₀ (R/2), `value w x ≤ K * I^{p⁻¹}` with I = ∫ max(...)^p ≥ 0. Since `Real.rpow_nonneg` gives I^{p⁻¹} ≥ 0, the right side is ≤ 0. By `hval` and `hum` (restricted with `hhalf`), a.e. on that ball `value w x = invApprox c (u x) = u x / (u x² + c²) > 0`, because u x ≥ m > 0. These two facts contradict each other a.e., so `mu (ball x₀ (R/2)) = 0`. But R > 0 makes the ball nonempty and open, and an add-Haar measure is `IsOpenPosMeasure`, so the ball has positive measure (`Metric.measure_ball_pos`). Hence K ≥ 0, and the rest of the proof (`mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left … hK`) goes through with this derived fact. hR and hK can also be deleted together: in the case R > 0 this argument applies, and the case R ≤ 0 is trivial.

### [`MonoidHom.twoRank_le_twoRank_add_of_card_ker_le_two_pow`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Algebra/Group/ElementaryTwoQuotient/Basic.lean#L527-L547) — `tauceti-0584`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0584.lean`

- `[Module.Finite (ZMod 2) (ElementaryTwoQuotient G)]` (theorem): `twoRank G` is defined (line 376) as `Module.finrank (ZMod 2) (ElementaryTwoQuotient G)`. Split on whether `Module.Finite (ZMod 2) (ElementaryTwoQuotient G)` holds. If it does, the existing proof works unchanged. If it does not, Mathlib's `Module.finrank_of_not_finite` (Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Dimension/Free.lean) gives `twoRank G = 0`. That lemma needs `StrongRankCondition` and `Module.Free`, and both hold because `ZMod 2` is a field (through the instance `Fact (Nat.Prime 2)`). The goal then becomes `0 ≤ twoRank H + n`, which holds in ℕ. So the statement stays true and provable without this instance: `by_cases` on it, run the current proof in the finite case, and in the other case rewrite with `Module.finrank_of_not_finite` and close with `Nat.zero_le`. The other hypotheses are needed. For example, `[Finite (ker f)]` cannot be dropped: take G = ℤ (written multiplicatively), H trivial and n = 0. The kernel is infinite, so its `Nat.card` is 0 and `hn` holds, but twoRank G = 1 > 0.

### [`TauCeti.eLpNorm_le_mul_measure_rpow_mul_eLpNorm_fderiv`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/Sobolev/Trudinger.lean#L286-L311) — `tauceti-0587`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0587.lean`

- `(hΩ : MeasurableSet Ω)` (theorem): Besides hΩ, Ω appears only in `hsupp : tsupport u ⊆ Ω` and in the factor `μ Ω ^ (q:ℝ)⁻¹`. That factor uses the outer measure, so Ω does not need to be measurable for it to make sense. To prove the statement without hΩ, apply the current theorem to Ω' := toMeasurable μ Ω. The lemmas `measurableSet_toMeasurable μ Ω` (gives MeasurableSet Ω'), `hsupp.trans (subset_toMeasurable μ Ω)` (gives tsupport u ⊆ Ω') and `measure_toMeasurable Ω : μ (toMeasurable μ Ω) = μ Ω` (all in Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Measure/MeasureSpaceDef.lean) then rewrite the conclusion into exactly the stated bound with μ Ω. measure_toMeasurable holds for every set, including those of infinite measure, so no case is lost.

### [`TauCeti.Contour.argumentPrinciple_nullHomologous_of_analyticOnNhd`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/Contour/Argument/Cycle.lean#L166-L207) — `tauceti-0632`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0632.lean`

- `(hU : IsOpen U)` (theorem): The statement still holds for any `U`. The proof applies the theorem itself to a smaller open set `W`, so `hU` is only used for that open `W`.

**Building `W`.** Let `V = {z | AnalyticAt ℂ f z}`. It is open, and `U ⊆ V` by `hf`. Call `u ∈ U` *bad* if `f` is identically zero on a neighbourhood of `u`, and *good* otherwise.
- For a good `u`, the isolated-zeros principle (`AnalyticAt.eventually_eq_zero_or_eventually_ne_zero`) gives an open ball `N_u ⊆ V` with `f ≠ 0` on `N_u \ {u}`.
- Set `W = ⋃_{u good} N_u`. Then `W` is open and `AnalyticOnNhd ℂ f W` holds because `W ⊆ V`.
- Every zero of `f` in `W` is the centre `u` of some `N_u`. That centre lies in `U` and `f u = 0`, so `u ∈ S` by `hzeros`. So the zeros hypothesis holds for `W` with the same `S`.
- Each `γ t` (for `t ∈ [[a,b]]`) lies in `U` and has `f (γ t) ≠ 0`, so it is good and lies in `W`.

**Null-homology for `W`.** Take `w ∉ W`. Either `w ∉ U`, and then `windingNumber γ a b w = 0` by `hnull`. Or `w` is a bad point of `U`. In that case, `w` is off the curve because `f w = 0` while `f ≠ 0` on the curve. The winding number is locally constant off a closed piecewise-C¹ curve (`TauCeti/Analysis/Contour/Winding/LocallyConstant.lean`; this uses `hclosed` and `hγ`). So there is a ball `B` around `w` on which `f ≡ 0` and the winding number is constant. By `hzeros`, `B ∩ U ⊆ S`, which is finite. Hence `B` contains a point outside `U`, where the winding number is `0`, so `windingNumber γ a b w = 0`.

**Conclusion.** Every hypothesis of the theorem holds for `(W, S)`, with `IsOpen W` in place of `hU`, so the theorem applies. Its conclusion does not mention `U` or `W`, so it is exactly the original conclusion.

**Degenerate cases.** `U` empty, finite, or a single point, and `a = b`, are all covered by this reduction: if no point of `U` is good, `W = ∅`.

The other hypotheses are needed:
- `hclosed`: for `f = exp`, `S = ∅`, `U = ℂ` and a non-closed path from 0 to 1, the left side is `1` and the right side is `0`.
- `hf`: take `f z = z` for `z ≠ 0` and `f 0 = 1` on the unit circle. Then `f` has no zeros and `S = ∅`, but the integral is `2πi`.

### [`TauCeti.exists_mem_cuspFormCharSpace_qExpansion_coeff_eq_coeff_mul_of_coprime`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/ModularForms/Newforms/Descent/Coefficient.lean#L289-L321) — `tauceti-0635`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0635.lean`

- `(hL : Squarefree L)` (theorem): `L` only appears in the statement through coprimality conditions (`Nat.Coprime p L`, `Nat.Coprime n (p * L)` in `hvan`, and `Nat.Coprime m L` in the conclusion) and through `L.primeFactors` in `hLN`. All of these depend only on the set of prime factors of `L`, as long as `L ≠ 0`.

The case `L = 0` cannot occur: `hpL : Nat.Coprime p 0` forces `p = 1`, which contradicts `hp : p.Prime`.

For `L ≠ 0`, set `R := ∏ q ∈ L.primeFactors, q` (the radical of `L`). Then:
- `R ≠ 0`.
- `R.primeFactors = L.primeFactors` by Mathlib's `Nat.primeFactors_prod_primeFactors`, so `hLN` carries over to `R`.
- `R` is squarefree, by `Finset.squarefree_prod_of_pairwise_isCoprime`: the factors are distinct primes, which are squarefree and pairwise coprime.
- For nonzero `x`, `Nat.Coprime x L ↔ Nat.Coprime x R`. This follows from `Nat.disjoint_primeFactors` (`Disjoint a.primeFactors b.primeFactors ↔ Coprime a b` for nonzero `a`, `b`) and the equality of prime-factor sets.
- For `x = 0`, `Coprime 0 L ↔ L = 1 ↔ L.primeFactors = ∅ ↔ R = 1 ↔ Coprime 0 R`.
- `Coprime n (p * L)` splits as `Coprime n p ∧ Coprime n L` (`Nat.coprime_mul_iff_right`).

So `hpL`, `hvan` and `hLN` hold with `R` in place of `L`. Applying the current theorem to `R` (where `hL` holds) gives an `F`, and its conclusion for `m` coprime to `R` is exactly the conclusion for `m` coprime to `L`. The statement therefore stays true and provable without `hL`. The proof changes only by first replacing `L` with its radical.

### [`TauCeti.RiemannSurface.FiniteHolomorphicMap.notMem_branchValues_iff`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/Complex/RiemannSurface/BranchValues.lean#L79-L88) — `tauceti-0638`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0638.lean`

- `[PreconnectedSpace X]` (variable): The proof uses this instance only through `localMultiplicity_pos f x`. That lemma (Degree.lean:350) needs it only for `FiniteHolomorphicMap.not_eventuallyConst`, which derives non-constancy from `f.nonconstant` via the identity theorem. The fact `¬ EventuallyConst f (𝓝 x)` already follows from the structure field `finite_fiber` with no connectedness assumption.

Argument: suppose `f` is eventually constant near `x`. The constant must be `f x`, so `f ⁻¹' {f x} ∈ 𝓝 x`. Let `c := chartAt ℂ x`. Since `x ∈ c.source`, `OpenPartialHomeomorph.image_mem_nhds` gives `c '' (f ⁻¹' {f x}) ∈ 𝓝 (c x)` in ℂ. ℂ is T1 and has `NeBot (𝓝[≠] z)` (it is a nontrivially normed field), so `infinite_of_mem_nhds` (Topology/Separation/Basic.lean) makes this image infinite. Then `Set.Infinite.of_image` makes `f ⁻¹' {f x}` infinite, which contradicts `f.finite_fiber (f x)`.

With non-constancy in hand, `localMultiplicity_pos_iff (.of_forall fun z ↦ f.holomorphic z)` (LocalMultiplicity.lean:210) gives `0 < localMultiplicity f x`. That lemma needs only `[IsManifold 𝓘(ℂ) 1 X] [IsManifold 𝓘(ℂ) 1 Y]`, which stay. The rest of the proof (`mem_branchValues_iff`, which needs no manifold or connectedness instances, then `simp` and `omega`) is unchanged.

Degenerate case: if `X` is empty, both sides of the iff are trivially true. So the statement holds for any (possibly disconnected) source, and the proof can drop the instance with `omit [PreconnectedSpace X] in`.

### [`TauCeti.HasGaloisLabel.isSquare_discr_iff_five`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/FieldTheory/GaloisGroups/Quintic.lean#L134-L140) — `tauceti-0648`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0648.lean`

- `(hf : f.Monic)` (theorem): The library already proves the discriminant test without monicity. `TauCeti.isSquare_discr_iff_mem_range` (RingTheory/Polynomial/Resultant/Discriminant.lean:573, the 'nonmonic analogue') needs only `f.Separable` and a numbering `e : Fin f.natDegree ≃ f.rootSet E`. `TauCeti.discrSqrt_mem_range_iff` (Discriminant/Basic.lean) needs only `[IsGalois F E]`, `hchar` and a splitting `Fact`. The private lemma `HasGaloisLabel.isSquare_discr_iff_three` in Cubic.lean (lines 75-82) chains them into the same test as `HasGaloisLabel.isSquare_discr_iff`, without monicity, and its proof never uses that the degree is 3: `have : IsGalois F f.SplittingField := IsGalois.of_separable_splitting_field h.separable; let _ : Fact ((f.map (algebraMap F f.SplittingField)).Splits) := ⟨SplittingField.splits f⟩; obtain ⟨e⟩ := nonempty_rootSet_splittingField_equiv_fin f h.separable; exact (isSquare_discr_iff_mem_range h.separable e.symm).trans ((discrSqrt_mem_range_iff hchar e.symm).trans h.range_le_alternatingGroup_iff)`. Following this with `referenceSubgroup_five_le_alternatingGroup_iff` gives the theorem without `hf`. Mathematically, a non-monic discriminant differs from the root-difference square by the square factor lc^(2n-2), which does not change whether it is a square. Degenerate cases are excluded by `h`: it makes `f` separable, so `f ≠ 0`, the leading coefficient is nonzero, and the degree is 5. `hchar` is not removable: in characteristic 2 the discriminant of a monic polynomial is always a square (`isSquare_discr_of_char_two`), while label 5T5 (S₅) is not in the list.

### [`TauCeti.spinAction_bijective`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/RepresentationTheory/Spin/Structure.lean#L161-L165) — `tauceti-0651`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0651.lean`

- `[Invertible (2 : K)]` (variable): The current proof uses `[Invertible (2 : K)]` in one place only. Injectivity comes from `CliffordAlgebra.finrank_eq_two_pow`, which goes through `CliffordAlgebra.equivExterior` and needs 2 invertible. The other ingredients do not need it. `spinAction_surjective` (Representation.lean) assumes only `[CommRing K]` with `[Module.Free K P.W] [Module.Finite K P.W]`. `line_eq_bot_of_even_finrank` and `finrank_W_eq_of_finrank_eq_two_mul` are plain dimension counts on the polarization data. `finrank_exteriorAlgebra_W_of_finrank_eq_two_mul` even carries `omit [Invertible (2 : K)]`.

The statement stays true in characteristic 2. Let n = finrank V = 2l. The even dimension forces `P.line = ⊥` and finrank W = l. So End(⋀W) has dimension (2^l)^2 = 2^n, and `spinAction` maps onto it.

A proof without 2 invertible: take a basis e_1..e_n of V. In `CliffordAlgebra Q` the relations ι(v)^2 = Q v • 1 and ι(v)ι(w) + ι(w)ι(v) = polar Q v w • 1 hold over any ring; they follow from `CliffordAlgebra.ι_sq_scalar` and `ι_mul_ι_add_swap`. By induction on the length of a word, every product of the ι(e_i) is a combination of increasing products e_{i1}⋯e_{ik} with i1<…<ik: reorder adjacent pairs with the anticommutation relation, and collapse repeated factors with the square relation. So these 2^n monomials span the algebra, and finrank (CliffordAlgebra Q) ≤ 2^n = finrank End(⋀W). A surjective linear map onto a space of at least the same finite dimension is injective (rank–nullity). So `spinAction` is bijective without `[Invertible (2 : K)]`.

This is the classical fact that the Clifford algebra of a hyperbolic space is a full matrix algebra in every characteristic. The other hypotheses are needed. `[FiniteDimensional K V]` is needed because with infinite-dimensional V we get finrank = 0, `h` holds trivially, and the action is not surjective. `P` and `h` are used directly.

### [`TauCeti.Contour.hungerbuhlerWasem_residueTheorem_of_simple_poles`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/Contour/HungerbuhlerWasem.lean#L251-L269) — `tauceti-0653`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0653.lean`

- `(hSU : (S : Set ℂ) ⊆ U)` (theorem): `IsNullHomologous γ a b U` is by definition (TauCeti/Analysis/Contour/Winding/Number/Basic.lean:147) `∀ w ∉ U, windingNumber γ a b w = 0`, so poles outside U contribute zero to the residue sum. Proof without hSU: let S' := S.filter (· ∈ U). Then S' ⊆ U; U \ S' = U \ S, so hf holds for S'; γ a ∉ S', hmero and h_simple restrict to S' since S' ⊆ S. Applying the current theorem to S' gives HasCauchyPV γ a b f (2πi · ∑_{s∈S'} windingNumber γ a b s * residue f s). For s ∈ S \ S' we have s ∉ U, so hnull gives windingNumber γ a b s = 0 and that term vanishes. Hence the sum over S equals the sum over S' (Finset.sum_filter_of_ne / Finset.sum_subset), and the conclusion follows unchanged. No degenerate case arises because the vanishing comes directly from the definition of IsNullHomologous.

### [`TauCeti.Place.eq_of_restrict_inertiaField_eq`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/FieldTheory/FunctionField/Place/Extension/Inertia.lean#L665-L670) — `tauceti-0674`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0674.lean`

- `[IsGalois F F']` (variable): `inertiaField F P` is by definition `IntermediateField.fixedField H` (line 468), where H is a subgroup of `F' ≃ₐ[F] F'`. With `[FiniteDimensional F F']` this automorphism group is finite, so H is finite. Artin's theorem then makes `F'` Galois over the inertia field: Mathlib's instance `IsGalois.of_fixed_field` (Mathlib/FieldTheory/Galois/Basic.lean:92), which Mathlib itself applies to an `IntermediateField.fixedField` at line 511. So `IsGalois (inertiaField F P) F'` holds without `IsGalois F F'`. The proof needs no other change. `exists_smul_eq_of_restrict_eq` (F := inertiaField) only needs FiniteDimensional and IsGalois of `F'` over the inertia field. `restrictScalars_smul` has no Galois assumption (Action section of Galois.lean). `restrictScalars_smul_eq_self_of_inertiaField` is stated at line 647, before `variable [IsGalois F F']` at line 663. The statement's `restrict k (inertiaField F P)` needs `Algebra.IsIntegral (inertiaField F P) F'`, which finite-dimensionality provides. Mathematically: F'/T is Galois with group H; two places over the same place of T are conjugate by some τ over T; τ, read over F, lies in the inertia group and so fixes P; hence Q = P.

### [`TauCeti.inflH2AbsoluteGaloisProP_injective`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/FieldTheory/Galois/AbsoluteGaloisGroup/Inflation.lean#L57-L61) — `tauceti-0692`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0692.lean`

- `[Fact p.Prime]` (variable): The statement doesn't need it. `cohomFpMap` (ContCohomology/TrivialFp.lean, variables `(p : ℕ) {G H K}` with group/topology instances only) and `absoluteGaloisGroupProPQuotientMap` (AbsoluteGaloisGroup/ProP.lean, variables `(p : ℕ) (K) [Field K]`) take no primality assumption. The binder is only an argument because it is an instance variable in scope. The proof doesn't need it either. It calls `inflH2MaximalProP_injective`, which takes `[Fact p.Prime]` only from its `variable` line; its proof never uses it. That proof uses `trivialZModAction`, `zmodEquivFixedPointsOfTrivialAction` and `cohomFpAddEquivH2` (TrivialFp/Explicit.lean, variables without Fact), `explicitMap2Equiv`, `cohomFpAddEquivH2_cohomFpMap_quotientMk_eq_explicitInfl2` (TrivialFp/Inflation.lean, variables `(p : ℕ) (G) ... [CompactSpace G]`, no Fact) and `explicitInfl2_proPKernel_injective`. The last one is stated in a section with `{p : ℕ}` and no Fact, so its dependency `subsingleton_h1ConjInvariants_proPKernel` cannot need primality either. Its hypothesis `p • m = 0` on `ZMod p` comes from `nsmul_eq_mul, ZMod.natCast_self`, which holds for every p. So the same proof works for any natural number p, including 0, 1 and composites. The fix is to copy that proof with `omit [Fact p.Prime]` and apply it to `Field.absoluteGaloisGroup K`, which is a compact, totally disconnected topological group. This was not compiled. A small remaining risk is that instance search inside that proof silently routes through the prime instance, which is unlikely since every lemma it calls is stated without one.

### [`TauCeti.not_forall_map_spinRep_spinPlus_le`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/RepresentationTheory/Spin/Irreducible.lean#L642-L650) — `tauceti-0712`

Since `2 * l + 1 = (2 * l).succ`, Mathlib's `Module.finite_of_finrank_eq_succ` (Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Dimension/Free.lean; it uses `finite_of_finrank_pos`, because finrank is 0 for infinite-dimensional modules) gives `Module.Finite K V`, which is `FiniteDimensional K V`. The proof only changes by one line: `haveI : FiniteDimensional K V := Module.finite_of_finrank_eq_succ hV` (or `Module.finite_of_finrank_pos (by omega)`), followed by the same term `not_forall_map_spinRep_spinPlus_le_of_isIrreducible P (spinRep_isIrreducible_of_odd P l hV) hW`. ### [`TauCeti.isPrimitive_neg`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Algebra/Module/Primitive.lean#L84-L87) — `tauceti-0732`

Mathlib also shows that all ℤ-module structures on an additive group are equal (`AddCommGroup.uniqueIntModule`), so removing it loses no generality. ### [`TauCeti.isGorensteinProjective_of_moduleInjective_self`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Algebra/Homology/GorensteinProjective/SelfInjective.lean#L40-L65) — `tauceti-0739`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0739.lean`

- `(hl : Module.Injective (Aᵐᵒᵖ)ᵐᵒᵖ Aᵐᵒᵖ)` (theorem): B := Aᵐᵒᵖ is a finite-dimensional k-algebra, because A is. For such algebras, being self-injective on one side implies it on the other side (they are quasi-Frobenius; Lam, Lectures on Modules and Rings, Thm 15.1, a reference the file itself cites). So hl follows from hr. Short argument: let e_1,…,e_n be a complete set of primitive orthogonal idempotents, one per isomorphism class. If B_B is injective (hr), each indecomposable projective e_iB is an indecomposable injective. The modules e_iB are pairwise non-isomorphic, and there are exactly n isomorphism classes of indecomposable injective right modules, namely D(Be_j) with D = Hom_k(-, k). So every indecomposable injective is projective. Then D(_B B), a finite direct sum of indecomposable injectives, is projective as a right module, and _B B ≅ D(D(_B B)) is injective as a left module because the k-duality D swaps projectives and injectives on finite-dimensional modules. That is hl (Mathlib's `Module.Injective` is the standard notion, and B lives in the same universe as the modules involved). In the proof, hl is only used to build `FGModuleCat.abelian_isFrobenius`; it can be derived from hr first. This needs this QF-symmetry lemma, which does not seem to be in Mathlib or the library, but the statement is still true and provable. hl and hr can each be deleted, but not both: without either one, finitely generated modules over a non-self-injective algebra need not be Gorenstein-projective.
- `(hr : Module.Injective Aᵐᵒᵖ Aᵐᵒᵖ)` (theorem): Symmetric to hl. B := Aᵐᵒᵖ is finite-dimensional over k, and B^op ≅ A is too, so the same quasi-Frobenius argument applied to B^op shows that right self-injectivity of B (hl) implies left self-injectivity (hr): each indecomposable projective left module Be_i is an indecomposable injective, and counting isomorphism classes shows that all indecomposable injectives are projective. Then D(B_B) is projective, so _B B ≅ D(D(B_B)) is injective. Once hr is derived from hl, the existing proof (Frobenius structure plus `isTotallyAcyclic_of_acyclic_of_moduleInjective_self hr`) goes through unchanged. Only one of hr and hl can be deleted, not both.

### [`TauCeti.wassersteinEDist_add_rpow_le`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/MeasureTheory/OptimalTransport/Wasserstein/Mixture/Basic.lean#L87-L94) — `tauceti-0744`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0744.lean`

- `(hp0 : p ≠ 0)` (theorem): If p = 0 then p.toReal = 0. Every power in the statement is then x ^ (0:ℝ), which is 1 by `ENNReal.rpow_zero` (`x ^ (0 : ℝ) = 1` for every x : ℝ≥0∞, including 0 and ⊤). The goal reduces to 1 ≤ 1 + 1, which holds. If p ≠ 0, the existing proof applies unchanged with the remaining hypotheses. So the proof splits on `p = 0`: that case is closed by `simp [ENNReal.rpow_zero]` (or `ENNReal.toReal_zero`, `rpow_zero`, `one_le_two`), and the other case uses the current proof.
- `(hp : p ≠ ∞)` (theorem): If p = ∞ then p.toReal = ∞.toReal = 0, so again every power equals 1 by `ENNReal.rpow_zero`. The goal becomes 1 ≤ 1 + 1, which is true. In the case p ≠ ∞ the existing proof via `wassersteinEDist_rpow_eq_transportCost hd hp0 hp` and `transportCost_add_le` works as is. So the proof splits on `p = ∞`, closing the ∞ case with `simp`. (hp0 and hp can even be dropped together: either p.toReal = 0 and the bound is trivial, or both p ≠ 0 and p ≠ ∞ hold.)
- `(hd : Measurable fun z : X × X ↦ edist z.1 z.2)` (theorem): hd is only used to rewrite through the bridge `wassersteinEDist_rpow_eq_transportCost`; the inequality holds without it. Let r = p.toReal and d = edist. If r = 0 the bound is 1 ≤ 2, as above. If r > 0, take any couplings π of (μ, ν) and π' of (μ', ν'). Then π + π' couples (μ+μ', ν+ν') (`IsCoupling.add`, used in this library), so W(μ+μ', ν+ν')^r ≤ eLpNorm d p (π+π')^r. Two cases: (a) d is AEStronglyMeasurable for both π and π'. Then by `AEStronglyMeasurable.add_measure` it is for π+π' too, and eLpNorm^r = ∫⁻ d^r, by Mathlib's `eLpNorm_rpow_eq_lintegral` or the definition of eLpNorm'. `lintegral_add_measure` splits ∫⁻ over π+π' into the integrals over π and π'. (b) Otherwise one of eLpNorm d p π or eLpNorm d p π' is ⊤ (`eLpNorm_of_not_aestronglyMeasurable`), so the right side ⊤^r + … = ⊤ and the bound is trivial. In both cases W(sum)^r ≤ eLpNorm(π)^r + eLpNorm(π')^r. Now take the infimum over π and π'. x ↦ x^r is an order isomorphism of ℝ≥0∞ (`ENNReal.orderIsoRpow`), so it commutes with ⨅, and `ENNReal.iInf_add` / `ENNReal.add_iInf` pull the infima out of the sum. If no coupling exists, the empty infimum is ⊤ and the right side is ⊤. This gives the statement without hd.

### [`TauCeti.AlgebraicGeometry.EllipticCurveGeom.exists_iso_baseChange_iterateFrobenius`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/EllipticCurve/Scheme/Geom/Frobenius.lean#L40-L79) — `tauceti-0745`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0745.lean`

- `[CharP k p]` (variable): The statement never uses `[CharP k p]`. On the `k` side it only mentions `algebraMap k R` and `EllipticCurveGeom (Spec (.of k))`. The only characteristic-dependent term is `iterateFrobenius R p n`, which needs `ExpChar R p`, and that comes from `[CharP R p]` and `[Fact p.Prime]`. The hypothesis is only used in the proof, through `iterateFrobenius k p n` and the helper lemma `TauCeti.exists_iterateFrobenius_eq_algebraMap_comp`. It can be derived from the remaining hypotheses: `R` is nontrivial because of `[IsLocalRing R]`, and `k` is a field. Mathlib's `RingHom.charP_iff_charP` (Mathlib/Algebra/CharP/Algebra.lean) takes `{K L} [DivisionRing K] [NonAssocSemiring L] [Nontrivial L] (f : K →+* L) (p)` and gives `CharP K p ↔ CharP L p`. So the proof can start with `haveI : CharP k p := (RingHom.charP_iff_charP (algebraMap k R) p).mpr inferInstance` and then run unchanged. The converse does not work: `[CharP R p]` cannot be deleted instead, because `iterateFrobenius R p n` in the statement needs `ExpChar R p` from instance search.

### [`TauCeti.HopfIdeal.exists_conjugate_eq_of_isBorelOverAlgClosed`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Algebra/AlgebraicGroup/Borel/Conjugation.lean#L159-L172) — `tauceti-0758`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0758.lean`

- `[IsAlgClosed k]` (variable): Another hypothesis already gives this one. In TauCeti/Algebra/AlgebraicGroup/Borel/Basic.lean, `IsBorelOverAlgClosed k H I` is defined as `IsAlgClosed k ∧ Minimal (IsBorelCandidate k H) I`, and it only needs `[Field k]`. So `hI.1` (or `((isBorelOverAlgClosed_iff _ _ _).mp hI).1`) is a proof of `IsAlgClosed k`. In Mathlib, `IsAlgClosed` is a Prop-valued class, so nothing depends on which proof of it is used. The fix: omit the variable, start the proof with `haveI : IsAlgClosed k := hI.1`, and keep the rest of the proof unchanged. The proof calls `isBorelOverAlgClosed_iff_exists_eq_conjugate`, which needs the instance, and it now gets it from `hI.1`. The rest of the statement (`IsBorelCandidate`, `IsBorelOverAlgClosed`, `HopfIdeal.conjugate`, `FiniteTypeCommHopfAlgCat.of`) only needs `[Field k]`, so it still elaborates without the instance. There is no degenerate case to worry about: `hI` is always among the remaining hypotheses.

### [`TauCeti.Associative.isNilpotent_of_commutator_eq`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Algebra/Ring/Commutator.lean#L172-L187) — `tauceti-0778`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0778.lean`

- `(hyz : Commute y z)` (theorem): The statement stays true without `hyz`. Deleting it removes no term that the rest of the statement needs.

Setup: `A` is a ℚ-algebra, so it has additive inverses (`-a = (-1 : ℚ) • a`) and every `m! ≠ 0` acts invertibly. Define `D a := x * a - a * x`. Because left and right multiplication are associative, `D` is a derivation: `D (a * b) = D a * b + a * D b`, with no commutativity needed.

Facts used:
- From `hxy`, `D y = z`. From `hxz`, `D z = 0`, so `D (D y) = 0`.
- The generalized Leibniz rule for a product of `m` factors holds in noncommutative rings and keeps the order of the factors. It gives `D^m (y^m) = Σ multinomial · D^{k₁}y ⋯ D^{k_m}y`. Every term with some `kᵢ ≥ 2` vanishes, so only `kᵢ = 1` for all `i` survives, and `D^m (y^m) = m! • z^m`. This step never needs `y` and `z` to commute.
- `L_x` and `R_x` commute, so `D^N a = Σ_j (-1)^j C(N,j) x^{N-j} a x^j`. If `x^n = 0` and `N = 2n-1`, each term has `N-j ≥ n` or `j ≥ n`, so `D^{2n-1} = 0`.

Therefore `(2n-1)! • z^{2n-1} = 0`. Applying `((2n-1)! : ℚ)⁻¹ •` gives `z^{2n-1} = 0`, so `z` is nilpotent.

Degenerate case: if `n = 0`, then `1 = 0` and `A` is trivial, so the conclusion holds anyway.

What changes in Lean: the current proof uses `hyz` only in the private helper, where it moves `y` past `z^k`. That step would be replaced by the derivation argument above, carried out in the ring structure on `A` induced by the ℚ-module structure. The other hypotheses are still needed. Without `Algebra ℚ A` there is a counterexample in characteristic p: `d/dt` and multiplication by `t` on `F_p[t]/(t^p)` have commutator 1, while `d/dt` is nilpotent.

### [`TauCeti.Probability.rnDeriv_logitNormalMeasure`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Probability/Distributions/LogitNormal/PDF.lean#L149-L153) — `tauceti-0804`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0804.lean`

- `(hv : v ≠ 0)` (theorem): At v = 0 the statement still holds. RHS: logitNormalPDF m 0 x = ENNReal.ofReal (logitNormalPDFReal m 0 x) = ENNReal.ofReal 0 = 0 by the library lemma `logitNormalPDFReal_zero_var`, so the density is the zero function. LHS: `logitNormalMeasure_zero_var` gives logitNormalMeasure m 0 = dirac (sigmoid m), and the same file proves `mutuallySingular_logitNormalMeasure_zero_var m : logitNormalMeasure m 0 ⟂ₘ volume`. Mathlib's `Measure.rnDeriv_eq_zero (μ ν) [μ.HaveLebesgueDecomposition ν] : μ.rnDeriv ν =ᵐ[ν] 0 ↔ μ ⟂ₘ ν` applies (the measure is a probability measure and volume is σ-finite, so the instance exists), so rnDeriv =ᵐ[volume] 0 = logitNormalPDF m 0. Proof: split on v = 0. If v ≠ 0, use the existing proof. If v = 0, subst, rewrite logitNormalPDF m 0 = 0 (funext; simp [logitNormalPDF]), then close with `(Measure.rnDeriv_eq_zero _ _).2 (mutuallySingular_logitNormalMeasure_zero_var m)`. hv is not mentioned anywhere else in the statement.

### [`TauCeti.tendsto_mul_logDeriv_deriv_upperHalfPlaneSet_of_eqOn_neg_inv`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/Complex/Conformal/Reflection/Infinity.lean#L128-L168) — `tauceti-0806`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0806.lean`

- `(hb : b ≠ 0)` (theorem): `hb` follows from `hr`, `hgf` and `hupper`, so deleting it keeps the theorem true. Suppose b = 0. Mathlib's convention x / 0 = 0 turns `hgf` into `g w = 0` for every w in `Metric.ball 0 r ∩ upperHalfPlaneSet`. That set is not empty because r > 0: take w = (r/2) * I, whose norm is r/2 < r and whose imaginary part is r/2 > 0. `hupper` then puts g w = 0 in `upperHalfPlaneSet`, which Mathlib defines as `{z : ℂ | 0 < z.im}` (an abbrev in `Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/UpperHalfPlane/Basic.lean`). That would need 0 < 0, which is false. So b ≠ 0 always holds. To adapt the proof, add at the start `have hb : b ≠ 0 := by rintro rfl; have h := hupper ⟨hw_ball, hw_im⟩; rw [hgf ⟨hw_ball, hw_im⟩] at h; simp at h`, with w = (r/2) * I. The rest of the proof stays as written: it uses `hb` only in `logDeriv_mul_const z b⁻¹ (inv_ne_zero hb)`.

### [`TauCeti.IntegralLattice.isUnimodular_iff_natAbs_gramDet_eq_one`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/LinearAlgebra/IntegralLattice/Unimodular.lean#L192-L198) — `tauceti-0807`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0807.lean`

- `[L.IsNondegenerate]` (variable): Both sides of the iff separately imply nondegeneracy, so the instance can be rebuilt locally in each direction. (1) `IsUnimodular.nondegenerate` (same file, line 68) proves `L.IsUnimodular → L.form.Nondegenerate` and needs no instance. (2) `gramDet_ne_zero_iff` (Gram.lean line 192, whose section has only `variable (L : IntegralLattice V)`, with no nondegeneracy) proves `L.gramDet e ≠ 0 ↔ L.form.Nondegenerate`. If `(L.gramDet e).natAbs = 1`, then `gramDet e ≠ 0`, since `natAbs 0 = 0 ≠ 1`, so the form is nondegenerate. `IsNondegenerate` is a Prop class whose only field is `nondegenerate : L.form.Nondegenerate` (Basic.lean line 143). Proof without the instance: `constructor`. Forward: `intro h; haveI : L.IsNondegenerate := ⟨h.nondegenerate⟩`, then apply the current proof (`isUnimodular_iff_discriminant_eq_one` plus `discriminant_eq_natAbs_gramDet`, which needs no instance). Backward: `intro h`; from `h` get `L.gramDet e ≠ 0` (assume it is 0 and simp at `h`); then `haveI : L.IsNondegenerate := ⟨(L.gramDet_ne_zero_iff e).mp hne⟩` and apply the current proof. Degenerate case: if the form is degenerate, both sides are false. `IsUnimodular` fails by (1), and `gramDet e = 0` by (2), so `natAbs = 0 ≠ 1`. The iff therefore holds for every lattice.

### [`TauCeti.Semigroups.StronglyContinuousSemigroup.IsSmoothVector.realOperator`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/Semigroups/Generator/SmoothVectors.lean#L70-L74) — `tauceti-0808`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0808.lean`

- `(ht : 0 ≤ t)` (theorem): `realOperator` is defined in TauCeti/Analysis/Semigroups/Basic.lean as `S.realOperator t = S t.toNNReal`. Its docstring says it is "extended by `id` for `t < 0`". So for a negative `t`, `S.realOperator t x = S 0 x = x`, and that is smooth because `hx` says `x` is. In general, `S.realOperator t = S.realOperator ((t.toNNReal : ℝ≥0) : ℝ)`: rewrite with `realOperator_def` and then `realOperator_coe`, or note that `Real.toNNReal_coe` gives `((t.toNNReal : ℝ)).toNNReal = t.toNNReal`. The time `(t.toNNReal : ℝ)` is nonnegative by `NNReal.coe_nonneg`. So a proof without `ht` is: rewrite to the nonnegative time, then use `fun n => S.realOperator_mem_domainPow (NNReal.coe_nonneg _) (hx n)`. The statement therefore holds for every real `t`.

### [`TauCeti.IsOptimalCoupling.prod`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/MeasureTheory/OptimalTransport/Cost/Product.lean#L124-L134) — `tauceti-0823`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0823.lean`

- `[IsProbabilityMeasure ν₁]` (variable): `IsCoupling π μ ν` is a structure with fields `fst_eq : π.fst = μ` and `snd_eq : π.snd = ν`, so coupled measures have the same total mass (`IsCoupling.measure_univ_eq`). The library lemma `IsCoupling.isProbabilityMeasure_right [IsProbabilityMeasure μ] (hπ : IsCoupling π μ ν) : IsProbabilityMeasure ν` therefore gives the instance from `[IsProbabilityMeasure μ₁]` and `h₁.toIsCoupling`. Without the instance, the proof starts with `have : IsProbabilityMeasure ν₁ := h₁.toIsCoupling.isProbabilityMeasure_right` and then runs unchanged. In particular `transportCost_prod_add` still gets all four instances it needs. There is no degenerate case: the instance always follows from the remaining hypotheses. This works symmetrically: instead one could delete `[IsProbabilityMeasure μ₁]` and keep `ν₁`, but not both instances of the pair (μ₁, ν₁).
- `[IsProbabilityMeasure ν₂]` (variable): Same argument as for `ν₁`, using `h₂`. `IsCoupling.isProbabilityMeasure_right` with `[IsProbabilityMeasure μ₂]` and `h₂.toIsCoupling` gives `IsProbabilityMeasure ν₂`. Adding `have : IsProbabilityMeasure ν₂ := h₂.toIsCoupling.isProbabilityMeasure_right` at the start lets the proof go through unchanged. You can delete either `ν₂` or `μ₂`, but not both.
- `[IsProbabilityMeasure μ₁]` (variable): Alternative to deleting `ν₁` (not both). From `[IsProbabilityMeasure ν₁]` and `h₁.toIsCoupling`, `IsCoupling.isProbabilityMeasure_of_right` makes `π₁` a probability measure. Then `μ₁ = π₁.fst` by `h₁.fst_eq`, and Mathlib's instance `Measure.fst.instIsProbabilityMeasure` makes `π₁.fst` a probability measure. So the proof can begin with `have : IsProbabilityMeasure μ₁ := h₁.fst_eq ▸ inferInstance`, after `have := h₁.toIsCoupling.isProbabilityMeasure_of_right`, and otherwise stay the same.
- `[IsProbabilityMeasure μ₂]` (variable): Alternative to deleting `ν₂` (not both). `h₂.toIsCoupling.isProbabilityMeasure_of_right` makes `π₂` a probability measure, using `[IsProbabilityMeasure ν₂]`. Then `μ₂ = π₂.fst` (`h₂.fst_eq`) is a probability measure by `Measure.fst.instIsProbabilityMeasure`. With that instance re-established at the start, the rest of the proof is unchanged.

### [`TauCeti.irreducible_cyclotomic_of_coprime_finrank`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/Cyclotomic/Irreducible.lean#L174-L228) — `tauceti-0840`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0840.lean`

- `[FiniteDimensional F K]` (variable): The theorem stays true without `[FiniteDimensional F K]`. Split on whether `K` is finite-dimensional over `F`.

- **Finite-dimensional case:** the existing proof applies unchanged.
- **Otherwise:** `Module.finrank_of_not_finite` (in Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Dimension/Free.lean) gives `finrank F K = 0`. Since `Nat.gcd a 0 = a`, the hypothesis `hcop : n.totient.Coprime 0` forces `n.totient = 1`. Then `natDegree_cyclotomic` (or `degree_cyclotomic`) gives `degree (cyclotomic n K) = 1`, and `Polynomial.irreducible_of_degree_eq_one` (in Mathlib/Algebra/Polynomial/FieldDivision.lean) proves `Irreducible (cyclotomic n K)` over the field `K`.

The degenerate cases agree with this: `totient n = 1` means `n = 1` or `n = 2`, where the polynomial is `X - 1` or `X + 1`, both irreducible. For `n = 0`, `totient 0 = 0` is not coprime to `0`, so `hcop` is false and there is nothing to prove.

It is also likely that the original proof needs no change, because `IntermediateField.LinearDisjoint.of_finrank_coprime` assumes no finiteness. I did not check every step for that, so the argument above relies on the case split. Nothing else in the statement mentions this instance, so it can be removed with `omit [FiniteDimensional F K] in`.

### [`TauCeti.isCompleteBernsteinFunction_stieltjesBernsteinTransform`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Analysis/CompletelyMonotone/Stieltjes/CompleteBernstein.lean#L223-L229) — `tauceti-0873`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0873.lean`

- `(hμ : Integrable stieltjesWeight μ)` (theorem): The statement stays true without `hμ`, by splitting on whether `Integrable stieltjesWeight μ` holds.

**Integrable case.** This is the current proof: `⟨a, b, μ, representsCompleteBernstein_stieltjesBernsteinTransform hzero hμ⟩`.

**Non-integrable case.** By definition, `stieltjesBernsteinTransform μ a b t = a + b*t + ∫ x, t/(t+x) ∂μ` (Bernstein.lean:75) and `stieltjesWeight x = (1+x)⁻¹`. Take `t > 0`. The function `x ↦ t/(t+x)` is continuous on ℝ≥0, hence measurable, and pointwise `t/(t+x) ≥ min t 1 * (1+x)⁻¹ ≥ 0`:
- if `t ≥ 1`, then `t(1+x) ≥ t+x`;
- if `t < 1`, then `t+x ≤ 1+x`.
