So if `x ↦ t/(t+x)` were integrable, then `stieltjesWeight` would be integrable too, by `Integrable.mono` after scaling by `(min t 1)⁻¹`. That is a contradiction, so `integral_undef` gives `∫ x, t/(t+x) ∂μ = 0`. At `t = 0` the integrand is `0/(0+x) = 0` (including at `x = 0`, since `0/0 = 0`), so the integral is also 0.

Hence on `Ici 0` the transform equals `a + b*t`, which is `stieltjesBernsteinTransform 0 a b`. So `⟨a, b, 0, ⟨by simp, integrable_zero_measure, eqOn⟩⟩` witnesses `IsCompleteBernsteinFunction`, in the same way as `isCompleteBernsteinFunction_affine` in this file.

**Why `hzero` cannot be dropped instead.** If `μ` is integrable and `0 < μ {0} < ∞`, the transform equals `a` at 0 but tends to `a + μ{0}` as `t → 0⁺`. Every complete Bernstein representation is right-continuous at 0, by dominated convergence with the bound `t/(t+x) ≤ 2/(1+x)` for `t ≤ 1` together with `μ{0} = 0`. So in that case the transform is not complete Bernstein.

### [`TauCeti.ContCohomology.IsHeisenbergCochain.gradedRestrict_gradedBracket_gradedMkZero`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Topology/Algebra/Group/LowerCentralSeries/Graded/Heisenberg.lean#L197-L204) — `tauceti-0906`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0906.lean`

- `(htrivM : ∀ (g : G) (m : M), g • m = m)` (variable): After `gradedBracket_gradedMkZero, gradedRestrict_gradedMk, coe_mk` the goal is `h ⁅g,g'⁆ = μ (a g) (b g') - μ (a g') (b g)`; htrivM only enters through `apply_commutatorElement_of_smul_eq_self`, where it is used solely to make `a` additive. It is not needed. With htrivA and htrivP, `hh.apply_mul_of_smul_eq_self htrivA htrivP` gives h(uv) = h u + h v + F(u,v) with F(x,y) = μ(a x)(b y). F is additive in y because b is a homomorphism (`map_mul_of_smul_eq_self_of_mem_Z1 htrivA b.2`). Comparing h((xy)z) with h(x(yz)) gives F(y,z) - F(xy,z) + F(x,yz) - F(x,y) = 0, hence F(xy,z) = F(x,z) + F(y,z). So F is biadditive whatever the action on M, and a is never required to be additive. Expanding h(x y x⁻¹ y⁻¹) with h 1 = 0 and h x⁻¹ = -h x + F(x,x) gives F(x,y) - F(y,x), the stated right-hand side. htrivM does not appear in the statement, so dropping it is harmless for elaboration. Not checked whether htrivM and htrivA can be removed together; each can be removed on its own.
- `(htrivA : ∀ (g : G) (x : A), g • x = x)` (variable): Symmetric argument, keeping htrivM and htrivP. Here a is additive (`map_mul_of_smul_eq_self_of_mem_Z1 htrivM a.2`) and h(uv) = h u + h v + F(u,v) with F(x,y) = μ(a x)(x • b y), which is just hh.apply_mul with trivial action on P. Associativity of h gives F(y,z) - F(xy,z) + F(x,yz) - F(x,y) = 0. Expanding this with the cocycle law b(yz) = b y + y • b z and additivity of a leaves μ(a y)(y • b z) = μ(a y)(x • y • b z) for all x, y, z. Taking x = y⁻¹ gives F(x,y) = μ(a x)(b y). This is additive in x since a is, and additive in y since μ(a x)(b(yz)) = μ(a x)(b y + y • b z) = μ(a x)(b y) + μ(a x)(b z). The same commutator expansion then yields μ(a x)(b y) - μ(a y)(b x). htrivA does not appear in the statement. Each of htrivM and htrivA can be removed separately; not checked whether both can be removed together.

### [`TauCeti.edist_gromovWassersteinSize_le_gromovWassersteinEDist`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/MeasureTheory/OptimalTransport/GromovWasserstein/Size.lean#L45-L72) — `tauceti-0934`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0934.lean`

- `[IsProbabilityMeasure ν]` (theorem): By definition `gromovWassersteinEDist p μ ωX ν ωY = ⨅ π, ⨅ (_ : IsCoupling π μ ν), distortion`. Case 1: no coupling of μ and ν exists. Then every inner infimum is over a false proposition, so the RHS is ⊤ and the inequality is `le_top`. This covers degenerate cases such as ν = 0. Case 2: some `hπ : IsCoupling π μ ν` exists. Then `IsCoupling.isProbabilityMeasure_right` (Coupling.lean:302, which needs `[IsProbabilityMeasure μ]`) gives `IsProbabilityMeasure ν`, since ν univ = π univ = μ univ = 1. With `haveI` the original proof applies. This deletion relies on keeping `[IsProbabilityMeasure μ]`. Symmetrically `[IsProbabilityMeasure μ]` can be deleted instead, but I do not claim both can be deleted together.
- `[IsProbabilityMeasure μ]` (theorem): Mirror of the ν case. If no `IsCoupling π μ ν` exists, the RHS `gromovWassersteinEDist` (an iInf over couplings) is ⊤ and the inequality is trivial. Otherwise `hπ.measure_univ_eq : μ univ = ν univ` (Coupling.lean:279), together with `[IsProbabilityMeasure ν]`, gives μ univ = 1, so `IsProbabilityMeasure μ` holds. With `haveI` the original proof goes through unchanged. This deletion relies on keeping `[IsProbabilityMeasure ν]`; either instance can be deleted alone, but I do not claim both at once.

### [`TauCeti.subsingleton_homotopyGroup_universalGSpace`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/AlgebraicTopology/ClassifyingSpace/WeakContractibility.lean#L44-L49) — `tauceti-0936`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0936.lean`

- `[Nonempty G]` (theorem): `universalGSpace G` is an abbreviation for `(indexedJoin fun _ : ℕ => G).Realization` (CountableJoin.lean:41). `Realization K` is the space of the standard geometric complex in `ι →₀ ℝ` with ι = Σ _ : ℕ, G. For every complex K and every point x, `AbstractSimplicialComplex.Realization.sum_eq_one` (Realization/Basic.lean:386) gives `x.1.sum (fun _ r => r) = 1`. If G were empty, Σ _ : ℕ, G would be empty, so x.1 = 0 (Finsupp on an empty type is a subsingleton) and its coordinates would sum to 0, giving 0 = 1. So the point `x` in the statement already forces `Nonempty G` (equivalently, the support of x.1 is nonempty and contains some vertex ⟨i, g⟩). When G is empty the space has no points and the statement is vacuous. New proof: `haveI : Nonempty G := by by_contra h; rw [not_nonempty_iff] at h; have := AbstractSimplicialComplex.Realization.sum_eq_one _ x; simp [Subsingleton.elim x.1 0] at this`, then the existing term `subsingleton_homotopyGroup_realization_indexedJoin (Set.infinite_univ.mono fun _ _ => ‹Nonempty G›) (Fin (n + 1)) x`. Nothing else in the statement depends on `[Nonempty G]`.

### [`NumberField.pow_index_eq_one_of_ramificationIdx_eq_one`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/NumberTheory/NumberField/Inertia.lean#L269-L278) — `tauceti-0951`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0951.lean`

- `[Finite G]` (variable): The theorem stays true and provable without `[Finite G]`. Split classically on `Finite G`. If `G` is finite, the instance is available again and the current proof (`disjoint_inertia_of_ramificationIdx_eq_one` plus `Subgroup.pow_index_mem`) works unchanged. If `G` is infinite, `H` is still finite: `K` is finite-dimensional over ℚ, and `IsScalarTower ℚ F K` is supplied by the Mathlib instance `IsScalarTower.rat` (Mathlib/Algebra/Module/Rat.lean) even though the variable was omitted, so `Module.Finite.of_restrictScalars_finite ℚ F K` makes `K` finite over `F`. Then `IsGaloisGroup.finite H F K` (Mathlib/FieldTheory/Galois/IsGaloisGroup.lean, which assumes no finiteness of the group) gives `Finite H`. `Subgroup.card_mul_index` gives `Nat.card H * H.index = Nat.card G = 0`, and `Nat.card H > 0`, so `H.index = 0` and the goal `g ^ 0 = 1` holds by `pow_zero`. `IsGaloisGroup.finite` was found in current Mathlib master; it was not confirmed in the older Mathlib the library builds against. Even without it, `H` acts faithfully on `K` by `F`-algebra automorphisms of a finite extension, so it is finite. (`[H.Normal]` is genuinely needed: in S₃ with a non-normal `H = ⟨(12)⟩` and a cubic field of discriminant −23, a prime with inertia `⟨(13)⟩` is unramified over `F`, yet `(13)^3 ≠ 1`.)

### [`TauCeti.Probability.integrable_id_generalizedGammaMeasure_iff`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/Probability/Distributions/GeneralizedGamma/Moments.lean#L85-L91) — `tauceti-0954`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0954.lean`

- `(hc : c ≠ 0)` (theorem): The statement stays true without `hc`. Split on whether `c = 0`. If `c ≠ 0`, the existing proof works unchanged. If `c = 0`, then `generalizedGammaMeasure a 0 s` is the zero measure: the definition is `if 0 < a ∧ c ≠ 0 ∧ 0 < s then … else 0`, and `generalizedGammaMeasure_of_not_valid` rewrites it to `0`. So the left side holds by Mathlib's `integrable_zero_measure`. On the right side, Mathlib's junk value `(0:ℝ)⁻¹ = 0` (`inv_zero`) turns `0 < a + c⁻¹` into `0 < a + 0`, which is `ha`. Both sides are true, so the iff holds. The other two hypotheses are needed, because without them the left side is trivially true while the right side can fail: without `ha`, take a = -1, c = 1, s = 1, which makes the right side `0 < 0`; without `hs`, take a = 1, c = -1, s = -1, which also makes the right side `0 < 0`.

### [`TopPair.comp_singularHomologyMap_ofSubsetMap_eq_one_add_smul`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/AlgebraicTopology/Singular/LocalHomology/Involution.lean#L138-L203) — `tauceti-0969`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0969.lean`

- `(hp : a p ≠ p)` (theorem): The proof uses `hp` in one place only: to build the cover `hcov` needed for the decomposition when a p ≠ p. If hp is deleted, the remaining case a p = p is true for degenerate reasons.

(1) `hapU : a p ∉ U` becomes p ∉ U. So `interPair U {p}ᶜ = TopPair.ofSubset (X := TopCat.of U) (Subtype.val ⁻¹' {p}ᶜ)` (an abbrev in Excision.lean) has subspace equal to all of U. Its relative singular homology, `(toSSetPair.obj P).homology R n`, is that of an SSetPair whose mono A → X is an isomorphism. Its relative chains vanish, so it is a zero object and `t = 0`.

(2) Since a p = p, `ofSubsetMap (𝟙 S) : ofSubset ({p}ᶜ ∩ {a p}ᶜ) ⟶ ofSubset {p}ᶜ` is the identity on S and surjective on subspaces. By `TopPair.isIso_of_isIso_fst_of_surjective_snd` (TauCeti/Topology/Category/TopPair.lean) it is an isomorphism of pairs. Hence its `singularHomologyMap` is an isomorphism.

(3) `ht` then reads `0 = s ≫ (iso)`, so `s = 0`.

(4) The left side `s ≫ …` is then 0, and the right side `(1+ε) • (t ≫ …)` is 0 because t = 0. So the conclusion holds.

A proof without hp splits on whether a p = p. If a p ≠ p, the existing proof runs unchanged. If a p = p, use the argument above. Every other binder is still needed. `ha` is used inside the proof term in `hs`. `hapU` is used in the statement's proof terms. `[T1Space S]` is needed: take S an indiscrete two-point space {p, q} with a the swap, U = {p}, Y a point, ε = 1, t = 0 and s the identity of H₀(S). Here H₀(S, {q}) = 0, so `ht` holds, but the conclusion would say s = 0.

### [`CategoryTheory.ShortComplex.IsAlmostSplit.isIso_of_isIso_τ₃`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/CategoryTheory/AlmostSplit/Uniqueness.lean#L196-L219) — `tauceti-0994`

Snippet: `samples/tauceti/tauceti-0994.lean`

- `[IsLocalRing (End S.X₁)]` (theorem): This instance follows from `hS.isLeftAlmostSplit_f`. In any preadditive category, if `f : X ⟶ Y` is left almost split, then `End X` is a local ring. The definition is `IsLeftAlmostSplit f := ¬ IsSplitMono f ∧ ∀ Z (g : X ⟶ Z), ¬ IsSplitMono g → ∃ h, f ≫ h = g` (Basic.lean:136).

Proof. Let I be the set of non-split-mono endomorphisms of X. I is exactly the set of maps of the form `f ≫ g`: the factorization clause gives one inclusion. For the other, if `f ≫ g ≫ r = 𝟙` then f would be split mono, which is excluded. So I contains 0, is closed under addition (`f ≫ g₁ + f ≫ g₂ = f ≫ (g₁ + g₂)`) and is closed under post-composition. It does not contain `𝟙`. In particular `𝟙 X ≠ 0`: if `𝟙 X = 0`, then 0 would be a retraction of f. So `End X` is nontrivial.

Next, every split mono x is an isomorphism. Take y with `x ≫ y = 𝟙` and set `e = y ≫ x`, which is idempotent. Since `x ≫ (𝟙 - e) = 0`, the map `𝟙 - e` cannot be split mono: otherwise x = 0 and then `𝟙 = 0`. So `𝟙 - e ∈ I`. Then `e ∉ I`, because otherwise `𝟙 = e + (𝟙 - e) ∈ I`. Hence e is split mono, say `e ≫ w = 𝟙`, and `e = e ≫ e ≫ w = e ≫ w = 𝟙`, so `y ≫ x = 𝟙`.

So the nonunits of `End X` (via `isUnit_iff_isIso`) are exactly I, which is closed under addition. `IsLocalRing.of_nonunits_add` (Mathlib/RingTheory/LocalRing/Basic.lean, stated for any semiring) then gives `IsLocalRing (End S.X₁)`.

How to remove the hypothesis: derive the instance from hS as a first `have` step. The proof as written then goes through unchanged, since this instance is only used by `isIso_τ₁_of_τ₃_eq_id` for hcomp. Degenerate cases are covered: a zero object X is excluded because f is not split mono.
- `[IsLocalRing (End S'.X₁)]` (theorem): The argument is the same as for `[IsLocalRing (End S.X₁)]`, applied to `hS'.isLeftAlmostSplit_f : IsLeftAlmostSplit S'.f`. Let I be the set of non-split-mono endomorphisms of S'.X₁. I equals {S'.f ≫ g}, so it is an additive subgroup closed under post-composition and does not contain 𝟙. Using the idempotent `e = y ≫ x` as above, every split mono endomorphism is an isomorphism. So the nonunits of `End S'.X₁` are exactly I, which is closed under addition, and `End S'.X₁` is nontrivial because S'.f is not split mono. `IsLocalRing.of_nonunits_add` then gives the instance.

The instance is only used for hcomp'. Deriving it inside the proof leaves the rest of the proof unchanged. This hypothesis and the one for `S.X₁` can each be removed on its own, and they can also both be removed at once.


## Lean Pool (150 flagged)

### [`ViscositySolns.exists_coordinateSemiconvexOn_strictifiedQuadraticObjective_closedBall`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ViscositySolutionTheory/ViscositySolns/Analysis/SemiconvexJensen/Aleksandrov/JetCalculus/Strictification/StrictifiedObjective.lean#L337-L370) — `lean-pool-0000`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0000.lean`

- `(hr : 0 <= r)` (theorem): `CoordinateSemiconvexOn lambda C f` is defined (Comparison/Semiconvex.lean) as `∀ x ∈ C, ∀ y ∈ C, ∀ a b, ... → f (a•x+b•y) ≤ ...`. If `r < 0`, then `Metric.closedBall (0 : Point n) r = ∅` by Mathlib's `Metric.closedBall_eq_empty : closedBall x ε = ∅ ↔ ε < 0`, so the property holds vacuously for any `lambda` (e.g. `lambda = 0`), whatever `g` and `B` are. New proof: `by_cases hr : 0 ≤ r`. In the first case keep the existing proof. Otherwise give `⟨0, fun x hx => ...⟩` and rewrite `hx` with `Metric.closedBall_eq_empty.mpr (lt_of_not_ge hr)` to get membership in `∅`, which is a contradiction. `hconv` is still needed: for `n ≥ 1` and `g` with a concave kink, the objective `g - quadratic - quartic` is not semiconvex on a ball of positive radius.

### [`NavierStokes.ParticularWaveBounds.pressure_class`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/ParticularWaveBounds.lean#L1371-L1389) — `lean-pool-0009`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0009.lean`

- `(hupper : ∀ n x, x ∈ s.domain → ‖N n x‖ ≤ M)` (theorem): `hupper` is used in exactly one place. The proof passes it to `pressureCoefficient_class`, which hands it to `CurlClassBounds.normalInverse_unweighted`. That lemma hands it to `PolynomialJets.inv` (CurlClassBounds.lean:917). There, the upper bound only serves to make the image set `closedBall 0 M ∩ {r | b ≤ |r|}` compact for `compact_comp`; the mathematical result does not need it.

Argument without `hupper`, for g = ‖N n ·‖², which is `PolynomialJets` by `hN.norm_sq`. Since `hb : 0 < b` and `hlower` give b² ≤ g on `s.domain`, g maps the domain into the open set t = {r | b²/2 < |r|}, and inv is smooth on t.
- By `iter_deriv_inv` (Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/Deriv/ZPow.lean), the k-th derivative of inv at r has norm k!/|r|^{k+1}. At r = g x this is at most k!/(b²)^{k+1}, a constant independent of n and x.
- The jets of g satisfy ‖D^i g‖ ≤ C·scale^m with C ≥ 1 and scale ≥ 1 (`PolynomialJets.bound`, `one_le_scale`). Hence ‖D^i g‖ ≤ (C·scale^m)^i for i ≥ 1.
- Mathlib's Faà di Bruno bound `norm_iteratedFDerivWithin_comp_le` (ContDiff/Bounds.lean:444) then gives ‖D^j (g⁻¹)‖ ≤ j!·K·(C·scale^m)^j, which is again polynomial in the scale.
- `s.domain` is open (`isOpen_domain`), so `iteratedFDerivWithin` there agrees with the `iteratedFDeriv` used in `FiniteJetBound`.

So `fun n x => (‖N n x‖^2)⁻¹` is still `PolynomialJets`, hence `UnweightedClass s 0`, without any upper bound. The rest of the proof (the inner-product classes, `mul`, `map ofRealCLM`, `constant_complex_mul`, `band_smul hfreq`) never uses `hupper`.

Degenerate cases: an empty domain makes everything vacuous. `hb` and `hlower` must stay, because without them N could vanish and the junk value 0⁻¹ = 0 would break smoothness. After deleting `hupper`, the implicit `M` is mentioned nowhere and is left as an unused argument.

### [`CKN.Leray.integral_ae_eq_integral_jointRep`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CaffarelliKohnNirenberg/Leray/ForcedRegularisedJointRep.lean#L247-L298) — `lean-pool-0024`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0024.lean`

- `[CompleteSpace E]` (variable): The theorem takes this as an argument because it is an instance-implicit variable whose type mentions only E. It follows from the other hypotheses: Mathlib's `FiniteDimensional.complete` (Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Module/FiniteDimension.lean) gives `CompleteSpace E` for a finite-dimensional space over a complete nontrivially normed field. Here the field is ℂ (complete) and E is a normed group (so Hausdorff) with a normed ℂ-module structure coming from `InnerProductSpace ℂ E`, so its conditions hold, and `[FiniteDimensional ℂ E]` is already assumed. To prove the theorem without it, add `haveI := FiniteDimensional.complete ℂ E` at the start; the rest of the proof, including the call to `integrable_jointRep_restrict_prod`, is unchanged. Degenerate cases do not matter because the instance is reconstructed in every case.

### [`IsoGraph.Canon.splitCell_tr_general`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/IsoGraph/Canon/Equivariance.lean#L3331-L3342) — `lean-pool-0028`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0028.lean`

- `(hsc : scatterFrom st.lab cnt st.cen[c]! (st.cen[c]! - c) c (Array.replicate (st.cen[c]! - c) 0) bc1 = (block, bc2))` (theorem): In `splitCell` (Algorithm.lean, line 376), the `tr` field of the result in the general branch is the fourth component of `boundsFrom ks sizes ks.size 0 st.cst st.cen #[] c (mixN st.tr c)`. That value depends only on `ks` (from `bucketFrom`, fixed by `hb`), `sizes` (from `offsetFrom`, fixed by `ho`) and fields of `st`. It does not depend on `block` or `bc2`, the outputs of `scatterFrom`, nor on the outputs of `writeFrom`. So the conclusion `(splitCell cnt c st).tr = tr` follows from hb, h1, h2, ho and hbd alone. After deleting hsc, `block` stays as a free variable that appears only in `hw`, and the statement stays well-formed. Proof without hsc: name the real result with `obtain ⟨block', bc2'⟩ := ...` together with its equation hsc', and likewise for `writeFrom block' c block'.size 0 st.lab st.pos = (lab', pos')` (hw'). Then `rw [splitCell_eq_general hb h1 h2 ho hsc' hw' hbd]`, and the goal closes by rfl. Alternatively, unfold `splitCell` and rewrite with h1, hb, h2, ho and hbd. hsc and hw can each be deleted on their own, and both can also be deleted together.
- `(hw : writeFrom block c block.size 0 st.lab st.pos = (lab, pos))` (theorem): The `tr` field produced by `splitCell` comes only from `boundsFrom (sortNats ks0) sizes ...`. It does not depend on the `(lab, pos)` result of `writeFrom`, which only fills the `lab` and `pos` fields. After deleting hw, `lab` and `pos` are unconstrained variables that the rest of the statement does not use, so the statement stays well-formed and true. Proof: get the real result of `writeFrom block c block.size 0 st.lab st.pos` together with its equation hw' (e.g. `obtain ⟨lab', pos'⟩ := ...` with an equation). Then `rw [splitCell_eq_general hb h1 h2 ho hsc hw' hbd]` reduces the goal to `tr = tr`. This can be deleted on its own, or together with hsc.

### [`ESS.buCut_gradient_absorption`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/EscauriazaSereginSverak/Linear/BUShortGradient.lean#L28-L102) — `lean-pool-0033`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0033.lean`

- `(hM : 0 ≤ M)` (theorem): This follows from `hderiv`. `Fin 3` is nonempty, so `hderiv 0` gives `|spatialPartial (buCutScalar κ) 0 z| ≤ M`. Combined with `abs_nonneg`, this yields `0 ≤ M := (abs_nonneg _).trans (hderiv 0)`. The proof then runs unchanged with this local `hM`, which is used only in `hU`.
- `(hκ : 0 ≤ buCutScalar κ z)` (theorem): Split on the sign of ξ = buCutScalar κ z. If 0 ≤ ξ, the existing proof works. If ξ < 0, the left side ξ * √(spatialGradientSq v Dv z) is ≤ 0 by `mul_nonpos_of_nonpos_of_nonneg` (ξ ≤ 0 and `Real.sqrt_nonneg`). The right side 3 * (√(...) + M * vec3EuclideanNorm (v z)) is ≥ 0. This uses `Real.sqrt_nonneg`, `vec3EuclideanNorm_nonneg` (already used in the proof) and 0 ≤ M, which comes from `hM` or from `hderiv 0` as in the other entry. So the inequality holds without `hκ`, which the proof uses only in `abs_of_nonneg hκ` within `hroot`. Deleting both `hκ` and `hM` together also works.

### [`IsoGraph.Canon.individualize_refines`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/IsoGraph/Canon/Progress.lean#L192-L202) — `lean-pool-0038`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0038.lean`

- `(hv : v < n)` (theorem): `Refines n q p` (Progress.lean l.41) only looks at `q.cst`. By `individualize_cst_eq` (Equivariance.lean l.523, proved by `rfl`), `(individualize p v).1.cst` depends on `v` only through `p.pos[v]!`: it is `setCstFrom c ec (ec - (c+1)) (c+1) p.cst` with `c = p.cst[p.pos[v]!]!` and `ec = p.cen[p.pos[v]!]!`. Proof without `hv`: if `v < n`, use the current proof. Otherwise `v ≥ n`. If `n = 0`, `Refines 0 _ _` holds trivially because no `i < 0` exists. If `n > 0`, `hp.posSize` gives `p.pos.size = n ≤ v`, so `p.pos[v]! = default = 0` (by `getElem!_neg`). Take `w := p.lab[0]!`. Then `hp.labLt 0` gives `w < n` and `hp.posLab 0` gives `p.pos[w]! = 0`. Rewriting with `individualize_cst_eq` therefore gives `(individualize p v).1.cst = (individualize p w).1.cst`, and the existing proof applies to `w` since `w < n`. So the statement stays true and provable without `hv`. `hp` is still needed. Counterexample without it: `n = 3`, `cst = [0,1,2]`, `cen = [3,3,3]`, `pos[0] = 0`. Individualising vertex 0 sets the new `cst[2]` to `1`, so cell start 2 is lost.

### [`TauCeti.DavisKahanExt.le_riccati_small_root_of_quadratic`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/DavisKahan/DavisKahan/Riccati/BoundedSharpEstimates.lean#L41-L89) — `lean-pool-0045`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0045.lean`

- `(hd : 0 < d)` (theorem): It follows from the other hypotheses: from `hb : 0 ≤ b` and `hsmall : 2 * b < d` we get 0 ≤ 2b < d, so `have hd : 0 < d := by linarith` restores it. The proof uses `hd` only in `linarith`/`nlinarith` steps, so nothing else changes. Either `hd` or `hsmall` can be dropped, but not both (counterexample: b = 0, d = 0, t = 1/2).
- `(hb : 0 ≤ b)` (theorem): The statement stays true for b < 0, as long as d > 0. Let s = √(d²−4b²) and r = 2b/(d+s); note d+s > 0.
- If d² < 4b²: b·t² − d·t + b has a negative leading coefficient and a negative discriminant, so it is always < 0. Then `hquad` (which says it is ≥ 0) cannot hold, and the statement is vacuous.
- If d² ≥ 4b²: we still have s² = d²−4b², and the proof's `hroot` (b(1+r²) = d·r) is derived without `hb`. Suppose t > r. The existing factorisation is b(1+t²) − d·t = (t−r)(b(t+r) − d). Since r = (d−s)/(2b), we have 2br = d−s. As b < 0 and t+r > 2r, b(t+r) − d < 2br − d = −s ≤ 0. The product is then negative, which contradicts `hquad`.
So the proof only needs a case split on the sign of b; the case b ≥ 0 is the existing proof (`hb` is used only in `hcoef`).
- `(hsmall : 2 * b < d)` (theorem): Given `hb`, `hd` and `ht1`, the case d ≤ 2b also satisfies the conclusion. Then b > 0 and d² − 4b² ≤ 0, so Real.sqrt of it is 0 (Mathlib's junk value for negative arguments; at d = 2b it is √0 = 0). The right-hand side becomes 2b/d ≥ 1 > t by `ht1`. The proof splits on 2b < d: in that case use the existing argument; otherwise rewrite the square root to 0 (Real.sqrt_eq_zero'.2) and conclude from (one_le_div hd).2 and `ht1`. `hsmall` can also be dropped together with `hb`, since b < 0 < d makes it automatic; it cannot be dropped together with `hd`.

### [`NavierStokes.HeatedOutgoing.E_times_U`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/HeatedOutgoing.lean#L1197-L1206) — `lean-pool-0047`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0047.lean`

- `(hX : 0 < X)` (theorem): The patch part never needs hX: `OutgoingDilation.correction_times_U_zero` only takes hXR. hX is used only through `heatE_times_U` → `heatE_before` → `HeatTailEdit.switch_zero`. Definitions: `heatE F XR p = OutgoingDilation.E F XR p * HeatTailEdit.multiplier h ν K p.1` with K = switchRadius F XR; `multiplier h ν K X = 1 + switch K X * (...)`; `switch K X = OutgoingSchedule.sigma (Real.log (X/K) / (3/10))`; `OutgoingDilation.U F XR p = F.U (p.1/XR, p.2)` with `Profile.U F p = F.logU (Real.log p.1, p.2)`. Since `Real.log_neg_eq_log` gives log(-x) = log x, for X ≤ 0 and Y = -X ≥ 0 we get switch K X = switch K Y and U F XR (X,eta) = U F XR (Y,eta). K > 0 by `switchRadius_pos` (uses hXR). Case Y ≤ K, which includes X = 0 because log 0 = 0: 0 ≤ Y/K ≤ 1, so `Real.log_nonpos` gives log(Y/K) ≤ 0, and `OutgoingSchedule.sigma_zero` (hypothesis x ≤ 0) gives switch = 0. Then the multiplier is 1 and heatE = OutgoingDilation.E at (X,eta). Case Y > K: `HeatedOutgoing.U_after_switch F XR eta Y hXR` gives U F XR (Y,eta) = 0, hence U F XR (X,eta) = 0, and both sides are 0. For X > 0 the existing proof applies. So the statement holds for all real X given hXR, and the proof just adds a case split on the sign of X.

### [`RS.tensorPow_id_ne_zero`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/RegtsSevenster/RS/Classical/Deligne/SuperEmbed/Standard.lean#L336-L359) — `lean-pool-0048`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0048.lean`

- `[MonoidalPreadditive A]` (theorem): The proof uses `MonoidalPreadditive` at exactly one point: `MonoidalPreadditive.zero_whiskerRight`, to get from `𝟙 (T_k) = 0` to `𝟙 (T_k ⊗ U) = 0`, where `T_k = tensorPow A U k`. Without that instance, the hypothesis `hUU : U ⊗ U ≅ 𝟙_ A` already gives the same step.

Let F be the functor `- ⊗ U` (Mathlib's `tensorRight U`). The composite `(α_ X U U) ≪≫ whiskerLeftIso X hUU ≪≫ ρ_ X` is an isomorphism `(X ⊗ U) ⊗ U ≅ X`, and it is natural in X because the associator, `whiskerLeftIso` and the right unitor are all natural. So F ⋙ F ≅ 𝟭 A. Feeding F, F and this isomorphism to `Equivalence.mk` makes F part of an equivalence, so F is full.

Mathlib's instance `Functor.preservesZeroMorphisms_of_full` (Mathlib/CategoryTheory/Limits/Preserves/Shapes/Zero.lean) says that a full functor preserves zero morphisms. It needs only `HasZeroMorphisms`, which `Preadditive` supplies. Therefore `𝟙 (T_k ⊗ U) = F.map (𝟙 T_k) = F.map 0 = 0`. This replaces the `zero_whiskerRight` rewrite. Equivalently, `Functor.map_isZero` shows that F sends the zero object T_k to a zero object.

The rest of the proof stays as it is: the base case uses only `hone` and `IsZero.iff_id_eq_zero`, and `id_zero_transfer` uses only `Preadditive`. Degenerate cases: with `hone` still in place, k = 0 is unchanged, and the argument above holds for every object T_k, so the statement is still true for all k.

### [`EulerOrdinarySobolev.Evolution.gradient_logarithmic_envelope`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/OrdinaryEulerBKM.lean#L109-L141) — `lean-pool-0078`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0078.lean`

- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (variable): hC can be derived from hlog, hW and the lemma `U.gradientNormPath_nonneg t` (OrdinaryEulerGradientControl.lean:416), which needs only U and t. For the given t: 0 ≤ U.gradientNormPath t ≤ C * F with F = 1 + ‖(U.velocity t).toLp‖ + W t * log (exp 1 + tensorNorm 3 (U.velocity t)). F ≥ 1 > 0 because the norm is ≥ 0, W t ≥ 0 by hW t, and log (exp 1 + tensorNorm 3 _) ≥ 0 by Real.log_nonneg, since exp 1 ≥ 1 and tensorNorm_nonneg gives exp 1 + tensorNorm 3 _ ≥ 1. Mathlib's `nonneg_of_mul_nonneg_left (h : 0 ≤ a * b) (hb : 0 < b) : 0 ≤ a` then gives 0 ≤ C. No degenerate case arises: t is an argument, so the interval is nonempty, and the argument works for every real C. Proof without hC: derive hC this way at the start (via `omit hC in`) and keep the rest of the proof unchanged.

### [`NavierStokes.SignedCopyBounds.local_mul`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/SignedCopyBounds.lean#L91-L97) — `lean-pool-0090`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0090.lean`

- `(hw : ∀ n x, x ∈ s.domain → 0 ≤ w n x)` (theorem): `LocalJets` (PeriodizedWaveBounds.lean:52) requires, for every m, constants C ≥ 0 and p with ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ j (f n i) x‖ ≤ majorant s w α C p n x for all x ∈ s.domain ∩ K n i and all j ≤ m. Here `majorant s w α C p n x = C * ε^α * growth^p * w n x`. In the proof of `LocalJets.bilinear`, `hw` is used only once: in `majorant_nonneg s w α hA p n x (hw n x hx)`, which gives `0 ≤ majorant s w α A p n x` at a point x ∈ s.domain ∩ K n i. That fact already follows from `hf`: take j = 0 (and 0 ≤ m), so `0 ≤ ‖f n i x‖ ≤ majorant s w α A p n x`, i.e. `(norm_nonneg _).trans (ha n i x hx hi 0 (Nat.zero_le m))`. The rest of the proof is unchanged: the Leibniz bound from `bilinear_jet_at` / `JetBounds.norm_iteratedFDeriv_bilinear_le_on` (a public theorem), then `majorant_mul`, which has no sign condition. So the same argument, with L = `lsmul ℝ ℝ`, proves the statement without `hw`. Degenerate cases: if w is negative at some point of s.domain ∩ K n i, the bound at that point forces every C_m to be 0. The argument above still applies, because it only needs the majorant to be nonnegative at the points where it is used, and the j = 0 bound gives that. You cannot call `LocalJets.bilinear` as stated without `hw`, so the proof has to repeat its short argument with this one change. `hv` can be dropped the same way, and both can be dropped together.
- `(hv : ∀ n x, x ∈ s.domain → 0 ≤ v n x)` (theorem): This is symmetric to `hw`. In the proof of `LocalJets.bilinear`, `hv` is used only to get `majorant_nonneg s v β hB q n x (hv n x hx)` at points x ∈ s.domain ∩ K n i. That nonnegativity follows from `hg` itself: the j = 0 bound gives `0 ≤ ‖g n i x‖ ≤ majorant s v β B q n x`, i.e. `(norm_nonneg _).trans (hb n i x hx hi 0 (Nat.zero_le m))`. The Leibniz estimate (`JetBounds.norm_iteratedFDeriv_bilinear_le_on`, public) and `majorant_mul` (no sign condition) then finish the proof exactly as before. So the theorem stays true and provable without `hv`, and also with both `hw` and `hv` removed.

### [`RS.kernelSubobject_tensorHom_le`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/RegtsSevenster/RS/Classical/Deligne/KernelPow.lean#L729-L761) — `lean-pool-0091`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0091.lean`

- `[MonoidalPreadditive A]` (theorem): The statement does not need it to elaborate: kernelSubobject, imageSubobject and ⊔ come from [Abelian A]. The proof only uses it through `exists_kernel_ι_whiskerRight/Left`, where `PreservesKernel.iso (tensorRight W) p` needs `(tensorRight W).PreservesZeroMorphisms`. In a rigid category that already holds. The library's TensorExact.lean builds `tensorRightAdjunction X Xᘁ` and `tensorLeftAdjunction (ᘁX) X`, so `tensorRight X` and `tensorLeft X` are left adjoints. Mathlib's `preservesZeroMorphisms_of_isLeftAdjoint` (Limits/Preserves/Shapes/Zero.lean) then gives the instance; `preservesZeroMorphisms_of_preserves_initial_object` with the preserves-colimits instances works too. With that instance supplied by hand, the same proof goes through using only [Abelian A] and [RigidCategory A]. No degenerate case is affected, since the conclusion is an inequality of subobjects that never mentions additivity of the tensor product.
- `[Epi p₁]` (theorem): The theorem remains true for arbitrary p₁. Reduction to the epi case: (1) split p₁ = e ≫ m with e = factorThruImage p₁ epi and m = image.ι p₁ mono. (2) Then p₁ ⊗ₘ p₂ = (e ⊗ₘ p₂) ≫ (m ▷ W₂) by functoriality of ⊗ₘ. (3) m ▷ W₂ is mono because tensorRight W₂ is a right adjoint in a rigid category (TensorExact.lean: tensorRight_preservesLimits), so Mathlib's `kernelSubobject_comp_mono` gives kernelSubobject (p₁ ⊗ₘ p₂) = kernelSubobject (e ⊗ₘ p₂). (4) The present argument applied to the epi e bounds this by imageSubobject (kernel.ι e ▷ Z₂) ⊔ imageSubobject (Z₁ ◁ kernel.ι p₂). (5) Since m is mono, kernel.ι e = iso ≫ kernel.ι p₁, so kernel.ι e ▷ Z₂ = (iso ▷ Z₂) ≫ (kernel.ι p₁ ▷ Z₂); whiskering an iso gives an iso, so `imageSubobject_iso_comp` shows the two images are equal. That is exactly the original bound. Degenerate case p₁ = 0: kernel.ι p₁ is an iso, so imageSubobject (kernel.ι p₁ ▷ Z₂) = ⊤ and the claim is trivial. Rigidity is not removable (counterexample in ℤ-Mod with p₁ : ℤ → ℤ/2, p₂ = ·2), but the epi assumption is. This and [MonoidalPreadditive A] can each be removed on their own, and also together.

### [`Egrs75.ClearingP3.lowDigits_half_pred_pow`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Egrs75/SubtractBranch.lean#L172-L181) — `lean-pool-0093`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0093.lean`

- `(hp : 3 ≤ p)` (theorem): `hodd : Odd p` already rules out p = 0 and p = 2, so the only case `hp` removes is p = 1. That case holds too: 1 ^ m = 1, so (1 ^ m - 1) / 2 = 0, and `LowDigits 1 0` follows from `RoundUp.lowDigits_zero` (RoundUp.lean:86), which proves `LowDigits p 0` for every p because `Nat.digits p 0 = []`. Proof without `hp`: from `hodd` get p = 1 ∨ 3 ≤ p (for example `obtain ⟨k, rfl⟩ := hodd` and then `rcases k` / omega). If p = 1, rewrite with `one_pow` and `Nat.sub_self` and apply `lowDigits_zero 1`. If 3 ≤ p, run the existing induction unchanged; it only needs 2 ≤ p, for `half_pred_pow_succ` and `RoundUp.lowDigits_cons`. `hodd` cannot be dropped: for p = 4 and m = 2, (16 - 1) / 2 = 7 has base-4 digits [3, 1], and 3 > (4 - 1) / 2 = 1.

### [`NFChoice.Compiler.NFCompactLeafFinalGate.axSiCompact`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NFWeakPartition/NFCompactLeafFinalGate.lean#L209-L255) — `lean-pool-0096`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0096.lean`

- `(hzw : z ≠ w)` (theorem): `Wff.Holds` uses ordinary Tarski semantics (SemanticCore/Basic.lean: `.all x p` holds iff `∀ a, Wff.Holds S (update v x a) p`). The class terms `synCsn` and `synCopk` pick their bound variables with `freshVar` over the free variables, so they cannot capture anything. Suppose z = w. Then `.all z (.all z φ)` under `update v y Y` says that φ holds under `update (update (update v y Y) z Z) z W = update (update v y Y) z W` for every W. That is exactly the case Z = W of the axiom instance `hY`, which reads ⟨{Z},{W}⟩ ∈ Y ↔ ⟨Z,W⟩ ∈ v x. So the statement still holds.

The proof uses `hzw` only to prove `hz : v' z = lookup xs 1`. A uniform fix needs no case split. Take `xs := DVec.cons W (DVec.cons (v' z) (DVec.cons Y (DVec.cons (v x) DVec.nil)))` and `hiff := formulaHolds_biimp.mp (hY (v' z) W)`. Then `hz` holds by definition, `hw : v' w = W` follows from `update_same`, `hy` uses only `hyw` and `hyz`, and `hx` uses `hxw`, `hxz` and `hxy`. Finally, `holds_literalSingletonPairMem` and `holds_literalPairMem` (NFCompactLeafEndpoints.lean) only need the three equations `ha hb hr`; they assume no distinctness. The calc goes through unchanged.

### [`NavierStokes.TerminalCompensation.physicalProfile_family_contDiffOn`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/TerminalCompensation.lean#L1082-L1099) — `lean-pool-0105`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0105.lean`

- `(hR : 0 < R)` (theorem): The proof uses `hR` in exactly one place: the step `hb`, which proves that `z ↦ baseProfile lam (z.2 / R) = (z.2 / R) ^ slope lam` (a real power, `rpow`) is smooth on `U ×ˢ Ioi 0`. There it only supplies `z.2 / R ≠ 0` to `Real.contDiffAt_rpow_const_of_ne`. That lemma (Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Pow/Deriv.lean:477) needs only `x ≠ 0`, not `0 < x`. The steps `ha'` and `hr` do not use `hR`: `correction_family_contDiffOn` is applied with the second coordinate ranging over `univ`. Without `hR`, split on whether `R = 0`. (1) If `R ≠ 0`: for `z ∈ U ×ˢ Ioi 0` we have `z.2 ≠ 0`, so `div_ne_zero hz.2.ne' hR0` gives `z.2 / R ≠ 0`, and the original argument goes through unchanged. (2) If `R = 0`: then `z.2 / 0 = 0` by Mathlib's convention for division by zero, so `z ↦ baseProfile lam (z.2 / 0)` is the constant `0 ^ slope lam` and `contDiffOn_const` handles `hb`. In fact the whole function is then `z ↦ a z.1 * (const + correction P (c z.1) 0)`, which is smooth by `ha'` and `hr`. In both cases the final `ha'.mul (hb.add hr)` gives the result. So the statement holds for every real `R`.

### [`NavierStokes.PolarCharts.chart_finiteJets_uniform`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/PhysicalGraphBounds.lean#L336-L354) — `lean-pool-0108`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0108.lean`

- `(ha : 0 < a)` (theorem): The proof uses `ha` only to get `ContDiff ℝ ∞ (chart a j)` from `chart_contDiff ha`. The rest of the proof needs only that each `iteratedFDeriv ℝ k (chart a j)` is continuous, so that it is bounded on the compact closed ball, and then takes a finite sum over `Index × Fin (m+1)`. But `chart a j` is smooth for every real `a`.

Here `chart a j p = ((extendedBase a (rotate j p)).1, (extendedBase a (rotate j p)).2 + offset j)`, where `extendedBase a p = (√(positiveRadius (a^2/32) (p.1^2+p.2^2)), arctan (p.2 / positiveRadius (a/8) p.1))` and `positiveRadius ℓ x = ℓ + (x-ℓ) * smoothTransition ((x-ℓ)/ℓ)`. `positiveRadius_contDiff` holds for every `ℓ`. The only places `extendedBase_contDiff` uses `0 < a` are the two non-vanishing side conditions (for `sqrt` and for the division).

- Case `a = 0`: `(x-0)/0 = 0` and `smoothTransition 0 = 0`, so `positiveRadius 0 x = 0` for every `x`. Then `extendedBase 0 p = (√0, arctan (p.2/0)) = (0, 0)`, so `chart 0 j` is constant and hence smooth.
- Case `a ≠ 0`: `a^2/32 > 0`, so `positiveRadius_pos` makes the `sqrt` argument positive. For `a > 0` the existing argument applies. For `a < 0`, `ℓ = a/8 < 0` and `positiveRadius ℓ x < 0` for all `x`. If `x ≥ ℓ`, then `(x-ℓ)/ℓ ≤ 0`, the transition is `0`, and the value is `ℓ < 0`. If `x < ℓ`, then `(x-ℓ) * smoothTransition(...) ≤ 0` because `smoothTransition ≥ 0`, so the value is `≤ ℓ < 0`. So the denominator never vanishes, and the same `ContDiff.sqrt` / `ContDiff.div` / `arctan` composition gives smoothness.

With `ContDiff ℝ ∞ (chart a j)` established for every `a`, the rest of the proof works unchanged: `continuous_iteratedFDeriv`, `IsCompact.exists_bound_of_continuousOn`, and the bound `1 + ∑ |B i|`. Degenerate cases: if `b < 0` the ball is empty and the statement is trivially true. The statement stays true and provable without `ha`.

### [`NavierStokes.SlowStressSupport.axial_stress_support`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/SlowStressSupport.lean#L2093-L2133) — `lean-pool-0111`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0111.lean`

- `(hn : 0 < n)` (theorem): The proof uses `hn` only once, to prove `hprev_le : n - 1 ≤ n` via `Nat.sub_lt hn ...`. In ℕ with truncated subtraction, `n - 1 ≤ n` always holds, by core's `Nat.sub_le n 1`. The proof itself already uses `Nat.sub_le _ _` for this fact when it builds `hout`. None of the lemmas it calls (`axial_integral_zero`, `smooth_axialDensity`, `exterior_axialDensity`, `axialWeighted_eq_density`, `stress_smooth`, `stress_radialSupport`, `stress_weighted_hasDerivAt`) assumes `0 < n`. If n = 0, then n - 1 = 0, `hprev` is the same statement as `hum`, and the argument goes through unchanged. Replacing the first line with `have hprev_le : n - 1 ≤ n := Nat.sub_le _ _` proves the theorem without `hn`.

### [`CKN.Leray.mildShiftedStokesIntegral_continuous`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CaffarelliKohnNirenberg/Leray/FourierMildIntegral.lean#L216-L225) — `lean-pool-0117`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0117.lean`

- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (theorem): `hC` follows from `hFC`. Every norm is nonnegative, so `0 ≤ ‖F 0‖ ≤ C`. In Lean: `(norm_nonneg _).trans (hFC 0)`. The proof stays the same, except that this term is passed to `mildShiftedStokesIntegral_continuousAt` instead of `hC`. `hT` can also be deleted independently of this one.
- `(hT : 0 ≤ T)` (theorem): If `0 ≤ T`, use the existing proof. If `T < 0`, the function is identically zero, hence continuous. For any `t`, apply `intervalIntegral.integral_congr` on `uIcc 0 T = Icc T 0`. Every `τ` there satisfies `τ ≤ 0`, so the condition `0 < τ ∧ τ < t` in `mildShiftedStokesIntegrand` (an `if h : ...`, extended by zero) fails and the integrand is `0`. The integral is then `∫ 0 = 0`, so the map is `fun _ => 0`, which is continuous by `continuous_const`. So the theorem holds for every real `T`.

### [`ESS.buGaussian_shifted_trace_strip_tendsto`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/EscauriazaSereginSverak/Linear/BUGaussianAverageTools.lean#L334-L436) — `lean-pool-0118`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0118.lean`

- `(hB : MeasurableSet B)` (theorem): The proof uses hB only in the two calls `setIntegral_congr_ae (hB.prod measurableSet_Ioc)` inside `hprodEq`. In both, the integrands are equal at every point, not just almost everywhere on the set. In `hleft` the membership `hqmem` is introduced but never used, and the rewrites are by `hτsymm` (proved for all q), `parabolicHomeomorph_symm_apply` (true by rfl) and `buGaussian_timeShift_point_symm_apply σ z` (true for all σ and z). In `hright`, `F (τprod.symm (τprod q)) = F q` holds for every q. So both steps can use `integral_congr_ae` with `filter_upwards [] with q` (or congrArg plus funext), with no measurability needed. The rest of the proof works for arbitrary sets: `MeasurePreserving.setIntegral_image_emb` needs a measurable embedding but no measurable set, `himage` is purely set-theoretic, and `setIntegral_parabolic_to_product` (CaffarelliKohnNirenberg/Foundation/ParabolicMeasure.lean) takes arbitrary Ω and I with no measurability hypothesis. So the theorem holds and is provable for every B.

### [`NavierStokes.PressureStream.reconstructed_divergence_zero`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/PressureStream.lean#L773-L791) — `lean-pool-0120`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0120.lean`

- `(hab : a < b)` (theorem): `RadiallySupported a b γd` is defined as `support γd ⊆ Prod.fst ⁻¹' Icc a b` (TransportPrimitive.lean:62). So the case where `hab` fails is `b ≤ a`.

Step 1: in that case γd is zero everywhere.
- If b < a, then `Icc a b = ∅`, so the support of γd is empty and γd = 0.
- If b = a, every point where γd is nonzero has first coordinate a. γd is continuous by `hγ`, so if γd(p) ≠ 0 then also γd(p.1+ε, p.2) ≠ 0 for small ε > 0. That point has first coordinate a + ε ≠ a, a contradiction. So γd = 0.

Step 2: with γd = 0, every term in the goal is identically zero.
- `weightedSource 0 = fun p => p.1 * 0 = 0`.
- `normalizeSource d a 0 z = sourceMultiplier .. • 0 = 0`.
- `compactIntegral χ M v 0 z = pastIntegral .. - χ z.1 • totalIntegral .. = ∫ 0 - χ • ∫ 0 = 0`; both are Bochner integrals of the zero function.
- `pullback d a 0 = 0 ∘ liftChart _ = 0`. So `physicalCompact d a b M v 0 = 0`, and `streamPotential = divideRadius 0 = 0 / p.1 = 0`.
- `streamBeta w 0 = -fderiv ℝ 0 p (0,w) = 0`. `streamGamma k v 0 = graphDr k v 0 + divideRadius 0 = 0` (using `fderiv_zero`).
- Hence `graphDivergence = fderiv 0 .. + 0/p.1 + fderiv 0 .. = 0`.

New proof: split on `a < b`. If it holds, use the existing proof. Otherwise show γd = 0 as in Step 1 (using `hs` and `hγ.continuous`), substitute it, and finish by unfolding the definitions and simplifying with `integral_zero`, `fderiv_zero` / `fderiv_const`, `smul_zero`, `zero_div`, `mul_zero`. The degenerate cases are b < a (empty support) and a = b (handled by continuity).

### [`PDL.localInterpolantStep_oneSidedR`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/PDL/Interpolation/Local.lean#L307-L366) — `lean-pool-0128`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0128.lean`

- `(def_rule : rule = LocalRule.oneSidedR orule YS_def)` (theorem): `def_rule` appears nowhere else in the statement. The proof uses it in one place only, the step `c_in' := hC ▸ def_rule ▸ c_in` in the third part. That step does not need it. `applyLocalRule` is defined in `LeanPool/PDL/Local/Rules.lean` (line 326) as `| _, ⟨L, R, O⟩ => ress.image ...`, so it ignores its rule argument. This means `applyLocalRule rule (L,R,o)` and `applyLocalRule (LocalRule.oneSidedR orule YS_def) (L,R,o)` are definitionally equal. The statement itself already depends on this: it passes `hC : C = applyLocalRule rule (L,R,o)` as the `hC` field of a `LocalRuleApp` whose `lr` is `LocalRule.oneSidedR orule YS_def`. That field expects `C = applyLocalRule lr (L,R,o)`, so the statement only elaborates because the two are definitionally equal. In the proof, `c_in` comes from `oneSidedR_sat_down` and has type `(L',R',o') ∈ applyLocalRule (LocalRule.oneSidedR orule YS_def) (L,R,o)`. So `c_in' : (L',R',o') ∈ C` can be obtained as `by rw [hC]; exact c_in`, which works up to definitional equality, with no use of `def_rule`. The rest of the proof (the `voc` part and the second part, which uses `hC ▸ c_in` and `oneSidedR_preserves_left`) never mentions `def_rule`. The theorem therefore stays true and provable without this hypothesis.

### [`GerverSofa.PartC.Stage2.rhoC_nonneg`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MovingSofa/GerverSofa/KernelOnly/Core/Bundle006.lean#L896-L906) — `lean-pool-0129`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0129.lean`

- `(ht : t ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) T)` (theorem): `rhoC t` (line 724) is defined by cases: `1/2` if `t ≤ params.phi`; `t/2 - params.b1` if `params.phi < t ≤ params.theta`; `1 + params.c2 + t` if `params.theta < t ≤ eta`; a quadratic if `eta < t ≤ tau`; and `0` otherwise. The proof uses only `ht.1 : 0 ≤ t`, and only in the phase 2 and phase 3 branches. The phase 4 branch uses `eta ≤ t` and `t ≤ tau`, which come from the if-conditions. The first and last branches are the constants `1/2` and `0`, which `norm_num` handles.

We can get `0 ≤ t` in phases 2 and 3 without `ht`. In phase 2, `split_ifs` gives `hφ : ¬ t ≤ params.phi`. Together with `phi_nonneg : 0 ≤ params.phi` (line 662 of the same file, before this theorem), this yields `0 ≤ t`. In phase 3, `hθ : ¬ t ≤ params.theta` and `zero_le_theta : 0 ≤ params.theta` (line 669) give `0 ≤ t`. Each is one `linarith` step.

So the new proof is: `rhoC_phase2_nonneg (by linarith [phi_nonneg, lt_of_not_ge hφ])`, `rhoC_phase3_nonneg (by linarith [zero_le_theta, lt_of_not_ge hθ])`, and the other branches unchanged. The statement therefore holds for every real `t`, and `ht` can be deleted.

### [`AVenhance.Infra.Section5.Contracts.sds_gradMatrix_entry_periodic`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AnomalousDiffusion/AVenhance/Infra/Section5/Contracts/TermCenteredAnalyticSmooth.lean#L49-L57) — `lean-pool-0130`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0130.lean`

- `(hF : Differentiable ℝ F)` (theorem): Unfolding the definitions, `gradMatrix F x i j = spaceGrad (fun y => F y j) x i = fderiv ℝ (fun y => F y j) x (basisVec i)` (see `gradMatrix` and `spaceGrad`). `IsZ2Periodic g` means `∀ n x, g (x + latticeShift n) = g x`. So the goal is `fderiv ℝ f (x + L) = fderiv ℝ f x` with `f := fun y => F y j` and `L := latticeShift k`, and this can be proved without differentiability. Mathlib's `fderiv_comp_add_right` (FDeriv/Add.lean) has no hypotheses and gives `fderiv ℝ f (x + L) = fderiv ℝ (fun y => f (y + L)) x`. By `he` and funext, `fun y => f (y + L)` is `fun y => F y j + L j`, because `(F y + L) j = F y j + L j` by `Pi.add_apply`. `fderiv_add_const` also has no hypotheses and gives `fderiv ℝ (fun y => f y + c) x = fderiv ℝ f x`. These two steps prove the claim. If `F` is not differentiable, both sides are the junk value 0 together: translations and adding a constant keep and reflect differentiability, and these lemmas cover that case. So the statement stays true without `hF`. The proof only needs `hF` because it goes through `amnr_fderiv_periodic_of_equivariant` and `fderiv_apply`, and the argument above replaces both.

### [`RS.LaurentTail.injG_apply_of_not_mem`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/JacobianDiffgeo/LaurentTail/Comparison.lean#L1239-L1247) — `lean-pool-0131`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0131.lean`

- `[T1Space X]` (variable): The theorem also takes `[T2Space X]` from the same `variable` line. Mathlib has the instance `T2Space.t1Space`, so a `T1Space X` instance follows from `[T2Space X]`. `injG` does take a `T1Space X` instance: it comes in through `ψVOf`, whose body uses `restrict_ψ_mem_linSysOn` (stated with only `omit [ConnectedSpace X] in`), and through `cleanNbhd`/`exists_clean_nhds`. If the theorem drops `[T1Space X]` (e.g. `omit [ConnectedSpace X] [T1Space X] in`), instance search fills that argument of `injG` from the T2 instance, so the statement still elaborates. `T1Space` is a `Prop`-valued class, so by proof irrelevance the resulting statement is definitionally the same as the original. The proof is unchanged: it only unfolds `injG` with `change` to the `dite` and closes it with `dite_eq_right` and `hnot`, and nothing in it uses the T1 instance specifically. This is the same pattern as `[Nonempty α]` next to `[Inhabited α]`.

### [`polytopeRoundedSupportCurve_regular`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/OperatorTheory/Operator/Crouzeix/SmoothSupportCurve.lean#L231-L242) — `lean-pool-0133`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0133.lean`

- `(hu : u.Nonempty)` (theorem): The statement still holds when `u = ∅`, because of how the empty sum and `Real.log 0` are defined. In that case `polytopeSoftPartition ∅ delta theta = ∑ z ∈ ∅, … = 0` (`Finset.sum_empty`). Then `Real.log 0 = 0` (`Real.log_zero`), so `polytopeSoftSupport ∅ delta theta = delta * 0 = 0`. Hence `polytopeRoundedSupport ∅ delta rho = fun _ => rho`, which can be shown with `funext` and `simp [polytopeRoundedSupport, polytopeSoftSupport, polytopeSoftPartition]`.

Proof without `hu`: case split on whether `u` is nonempty.
- If `u` is nonempty, use the existing proof unchanged.
- Otherwise `u = ∅` (`Finset.not_nonempty_iff_eq_empty`). Rewrite `polytopeRoundedSupport ∅ delta rho` to the constant function and apply `smoothSupportCurve_regular` with `contDiff_const`. The curvature hypothesis then reads `0 < rho + deriv (deriv fun _ => rho) theta`. The double derivative of a constant is 0 (`deriv_const'` twice), so this reduces to `0 < rho`, which is `hrho`.

Geometrically, the curve is then the circle `rho • smoothSupportUnitNormal`, whose velocity `rho • smoothSupportUnitTangent` is never zero.

The other two hypotheses are needed:
- `hrho`: with `rho = 0` and `u = {z}`, the support function is linear in cos and sin, so `h + h'' = 0` and the derivative vanishes.
- `hdelta`: a negative `delta` gives a soft-min whose `h + h''` changes sign, so it has a zero.

### [`QuadraticCarleson.packetPoint_abs_sub_right_ge`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticCarleson/CounterexampleGeometry.lean#L68-L86) — `lean-pool-0135`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0135.lean`

- `(hN : 100 ≤ N)` (theorem): The proof uses hN only to get `(k.toNat : ℝ) = k` through `paperBohrIndex_cast_toNat`, which in turn only needs `0 ≤ k`. That already follows from hk alone. By `mem_paperBohrIndices`, hk gives `3 * (N:ℝ) / 5 ≤ k`, and `(N:ℝ) ≥ 0` because N is a natural number, so `0 ≤ k`. Then `Int.toNat_of_nonneg` gives `(k.toNat : ℤ) = k`, and the rest of the proof is unchanged. The degenerate case N = 0 is fine: it forces k = 0. In fact hN and hk can even be deleted together (see the hk entry).
- `(hk : k ∈ paperBohrIndices N)` (theorem): The inequality holds for every integer k. Write m = k.toNat. `Int.self_le_toNat` (Init/Data/Int/Order.lean) gives k ≤ m, and hj gives m + 1 ≤ j. From hτ, τ < 1/2. So k + τ - j ≤ m + τ - (m + 1) < 0. Hence `abs_of_nonpos` gives |k + τ - j| = j - k - τ ≥ j - m - 1/2. The left-hand side is `Nat.cast_sub hj`: (j - (m+1)) + 1/2 = j - m - 1/2. So the claim follows by linarith from `(k:ℝ) ≤ m` (a cast of `Int.self_le_toNat k`) in place of `hkcast`. Check with negative k: then m = 0, and the right-hand side is j - k - τ ≥ j + 1/2, which is at least the left-hand side j - 1/2. Without hk, hN is mentioned nowhere else, so both can be dropped. Taken one at a time, each of hN and hk can be deleted.

### [`NavierStokes.PhysicalCoordinateBounds.xLift_homogeneous`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/PhysicalCoordinateBounds.lean#L421-L426) — `lean-pool-0136`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0136.lean`

- `(_hy : y ∈ positiveTime)` (theorem): `xLift y = y.2.1 / y.1` and `dilation_apply` gives `dilation a r y = (r * y.1, (r * y.2.1, r ^ D a * y.2.2))`. After `Real.rpow_zero` and `one_mul`, the goal is `(r * y.2.1) / (r * y.1) = y.2.1 / y.1`. `mul_div_mul_left _ _ hr.ne'` closes it using only `r ≠ 0`, for any `y`; if `y.1 = 0`, both sides are 0 by the `x / 0 = 0` convention. `_hy` appears nowhere else in the statement and the proof never uses it, so the same proof works after deleting it. (`hr` is needed: with r = 0 the left side is 0 but the right side is y.2.1 / y.1.)

### [`NavierStokes.ActivationCone.natural_reference_bounds`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/ActivationCone.lean#L819-L863) — `lean-pool-0138`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0138.lean`

- `(hδlim : 2 * δ < ReferencePath.rampLimit)` (theorem): The conclusion only involves `A = referenceP1 (refLog N δ)` and `B = referenceP2 N.endpoint (refLog N δ) (refAxial N δ)` on `Icc 0 τ ×ˢ Icc (-1) 1`, for some `τ ≤ δ` that we get to choose. Both are built from `radialPartial = fderiv ℝ · p (1,0)`, so they only depend on how `refLog N δ` and `refAxial N δ` behave near each point. `ReferencePath.continuation δ G p = G (0,p.2) + ∫ x in 0..p.1, slopeCutoff δ x * radialPartial G (x,p.2)`. `slopeCutoff_one` says `slopeCutoff δ x = 1` for `x ≤ δ` (it only needs `0 < δ`). So if `δ' ≤ δ` with `0 < δ'`, the integrands of `continuation δ G` and `continuation δ' G` agree pointwise on `uIcc 0 p.1` whenever `p.1 ≤ δ'`. Hence `continuation δ G = continuation δ' G` on the open set `Iio δ' ×ˢ univ`, with no smoothness needed. Proof without `hδlim`: set `δ' := min δ (rampLimit / 4)`, so `0 < δ'`, `2δ' < rampLimit` and `δ' ≤ δ`. Apply the theorem itself (in its current form, which keeps `hδlim`) to `δ'`. This gives `τ, α, M` with `τ ≤ δ'`. Take `τ/2` in place of `τ`: then `0 < τ/2 ≤ δ`, and every `y ∈ Icc 0 (τ/2)` satisfies `y < δ'`. Near such points `refLog N δ` agrees with `refLog N δ'` and `refAxial N δ` with `refAxial N δ'`, so their `fderiv`s are equal there. `N.endpoint` does not depend on `δ`. So `A` and `B` for `δ` equal those for `δ'` on the shrunken box, and all the bounds carry over. Degenerate cases: if `δ` is large, `refLog N δ` may behave badly far from 0, but that region never enters the argument. `hδ` itself cannot be removed, because `0 < τ ≤ δ` forces `δ > 0`.

### [`NavierStokes.TerminalStress.swirlCoefficient_contDiffAt`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/TerminalStress.lean#L412-L420) — `lean-pool-0158`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0158.lean`

- `(hh1 : h < 1 / 2)` (theorem): The bound h < 1/2 is only used through `flattening_contDiffAt`, which needs `SimilarityProfile.X h` to be smooth and positive at p. When h ≥ 1/2 the statement still holds because of a junk value. `SimilarityProfile.q h p` is defined as `SimilarityCoordinates.coordinateQ (2*h) (1 - p.1, p.2.2)`. `coordinateQ a p` is defined as `if hp : 0 < a ∧ a < 1 ∧ 0 < p.1 then Classical.choose … else 1`. With a = 2h ≥ 1 the condition is false at every point, so `q h = fun _ => 1` and `X h = fun p => p.2.1 / 1`. This map is smooth (`contDiffAt_snd.fst.div_const`, or `.div` with `contDiffAt_const`), and its value at p is p.2.1, which is positive by `hs`. So `flattening h f = fun p => f (Real.log (X h p))` is C² at p by the argument in `flattening_contDiffAt`: `hf.comp p ((hX.log hXpos.ne').of_le …)`. The heat factor uses only `physicalHeat_contDiffAt C (1 < 1 + h, from hh) ht hs`, which does not need hh1. The square-root denominator uses only `hs`. Proof without hh1: split on `h < 1/2`. In the first case use the existing proof. In the second case rewrite `q h` to the constant 1 by unfolding `q` and `coordinateQ` (`dif_neg`, since ¬(2*h < 1)), get smoothness of `flattening` as above, and finish with the same `.mul`/`.div` steps. The other hypotheses are not shown to be removable: hh is needed for `1 < 1 + h` in `physicalHeat_contDiffAt`, and ht and hs are needed for the smoothness of the heat profile and of the square root.

### [`NavierStokes.TemporalMeanUpdate.meanClass_axialAlias`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/TemporalMeanUpdate.lean#L1535-L1567) — `lean-pool-0159`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0159.lean`

- `(hM : ∀ n, M n ≠ 0)` (theorem): **Where `hM` is used.** The proof passes `hM` to `meanClass_dividedAlias`. That lemma passes it to `dividedAlias_global_bounds`. There its only use is the call `hbA := UniformFourierAlias.real_exactAlias_finiteJets .radial … m 0`, which needs `M n ≠ 0`. That call is made with Fourier exponent `p = 0`, so the bound it delivers is just `KA * B`: it gets no gain from oscillation and needs no information about M. The other steps are stated for arbitrary `M`: `physicalAlias_contDiff`, `physicalAlias_supported`, `dividedAlias_interior_support` (∀ M), `dividedAlias_finiteJets_transfer` (`hbT` takes any `M`) and `meanClass_of_interior_bounds`.

**A bound that holds for every M, including 0.** `exactAlias χ M v G = deriv χ z.1 • totalIntegral M v G z`, and `totalIntegral M v G z = ∫ u, G (z + (u, (M*u)•v))`. The source bound `hsource` from `meanClass_global_finiteJets` holds for all `k ≤ m` and all `x` (the proof uses it with `Set.mem_univ x`).
- `TransportPrimitive.iteratedFDeriv_totalIntegral_norm_le` holds for any `M`. It gives `‖D^i totalIntegral M v (G n) y‖ ≤ B * (b^d - a^d)`, using that `G n` is supported radially in `[a^d, b^d]` (`normalizeSource_supported`).
- `RadialPullback.radial_multiplier_finiteJets_uniform` with `h = deriv χ` (smooth) multiplies this by a constant `K`.
- Outside `Icc (a^d) (b^d)` the derivatives vanish by `exactAlias_supported` and `iteratedFDeriv_supported`.

Together these give `‖D^i exactAlias χ (M n) v (G n) y‖ ≤ K (b^d - a^d) B` for every `M`, including `M = 0`. This is exactly the argument of the library lemma `exactAlias_bounded_fiber` in `LocalSignedRequest.lean`, which is stated for all `M` with no nonzero condition. That file comes later in the import chain, so here the short argument would be repeated inline from the same lemmas, which are available to this file. Replacing `KA` by this `K'` in `dividedAlias_global_bounds` proves it, and hence `meanClass_dividedAlias` and this theorem, without `hM`.

**Degenerate case.** At `M n = 0`, `totalIntegral` is just the radial integral of a compactly supported smooth source. It is smooth and bounded by the estimate above, so nothing breaks. `M` stays an argument of the theorem because it appears in the conclusion; only `hM` is removed.

### [`PDL.LoadedCluster.satDown_three_not_basic`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/PDL/Interpolation/ClusterSatDown.lean#L575-L602) — `lean-pool-0171`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0171.lean`

- `(hlen : next.length = (C.stepOfL Δ).length)` (theorem): The proof uses `hlen` in exactly one place: the `omega` step that turns `hi : i < (C.stepOfL Δ).length` into `hi' : i < next.length`. That bound already follows from `hlab` and `hx`. `hlab i hi` says `C.Q.labelAt (x ++ [i]) = some _`. By definition `labelAt q y = (q.atOpt y).map label`. `QuasiTab.atOpt_snoc` (PreInterpolant.lean:202) gives `q.atOpt (x ++ [i]) = (q.atOpt x).bind (fun n => n.children[i]?)`. Rewriting with `hx` and using that `(QNode k Δ next).children = next` holds by `rfl`, we get `(next[i]?).map label = some _`. So `next[i]? ≠ none`. If `next.length ≤ i`, then `List.getElem?_eq_none` would make it `none`, a contradiction; hence `i < next.length`. Replacement proof: `have h := hlab i hi; rw [QuasiTab.labelAt, QuasiTab.atOpt_snoc, hx] at h; by_contra hc; rw [List.getElem?_eq_none (by omega)] at h; simp at h` (with `QuasiTab.children` unfolded if needed). The rest of the proof is unchanged. `hlen` appears nowhere else in the statement, so it can be deleted.

### [`NavierStokesAndEuler.SmoothCutoff.cutoff_eq_one`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/ForMathlib/SmoothCutoff.lean#L86-L90) — `lean-pool-0181`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0181.lean`

- `(hR : 0 < R)` (theorem): `hx : ‖x‖ ≤ R` already gives `0 ≤ R`, since `0 ≤ ‖x‖ ≤ R`. So the only case `hR` rules out is `R = 0`. There `hx` forces `x = 0`, and `cutoff E 0 0 = baseCutoff E (0⁻¹ • 0) = baseCutoff E 0 = 1` (Lean's convention `0⁻¹ = 0`, plus `baseCutoff_eq_one` with `‖0‖ ≤ 1`). Proof without `hR`: `apply baseCutoff_eq_one`; set `hR0 : 0 ≤ R := (norm_nonneg x).trans hx`; `rw [norm_smul, Real.norm_eq_abs, abs_inv, abs_of_nonneg hR0]`, which turns the goal into `R⁻¹ * ‖x‖ ≤ 1`; close it with `exact inv_mul_le_one_of_le₀ hx hR0`. That Mathlib lemma is in Mathlib/Algebra/Order/GroupWithZero/Basic.lean: from `a ≤ b` and `0 ≤ b` it gives `b⁻¹ * a ≤ 1`, and it also covers `b = 0`.

### [`BooleanIsoperimetry.harper_macaulay_min`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/BooleanIsoperimetry/MacaulayMin.lean#L154-L176) — `lean-pool-0191`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0191.lean`

- `(ha : a ≤ 2 ^ N)` (theorem): `H n k` is `(neighborhood 1 (simplicialInitSeg n k)).card` (Cube.lean:490), so it is defined for every `k` and is at most `2 ^ n` by `H_le_cube`. Without `ha`, split on `a ≤ 2 ^ N`. If it holds, apply the existing theorem with `ha` and `hb`. If `a > 2 ^ N`, then `H_mono` and `H_full` give `H N a ≥ H N (2 ^ N) = 2 ^ N`. The right-hand side is at least `max (H N a) b + max (H N b) a ≥ H N a + a ≥ 2 ^ N + 2 ^ N + 1`. That is larger than `2 ^ (N+1)`, and `H (N+1) (a+b) ≤ 2 ^ (N+1)` by `H_le_cube`, so the inequality holds; `omega` closes it after rewriting `2^(N+1) = 2^N + 2^N` with `pow_succ`. The degenerate case N = 0 is covered by the same argument. The case split does not use `hb`'s bound when `a > 2^N`, so `ha` can be deleted on its own. The same argument works for `hb` (next entry), and in fact both can be deleted together.
- `(hb : b ≤ 2 ^ N)` (theorem): This mirrors the argument for `ha`. Split on `b ≤ 2 ^ N`. If it holds, use the original theorem (with `ha`). If `b > 2 ^ N`, then `H N b ≥ H N (2 ^ N) = 2 ^ N` by `H_mono` and `H_full`. So the right-hand side is at least `b + max (H N b) a ≥ b + H N b > 2 ^ N + 2 ^ N = 2 ^ (N+1)`. By `H_le_cube (N+1) (a+b)` this bounds the left-hand side. All lemmas named are in LeanPool/BooleanIsoperimetry/Cube.lean (`H_mono` line 497, `H_le_cube` line 507, `H_full` line 519).

### [`PebblingLean.Hypercube.PaperParameters.normalizedCost_recursiveCostBound_step_of_ceiling`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/PebblingLean/UpperBoundParameters.lean#L2099-L2138) — `lean-pool-0205`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0205.lean`

- `(hsmall : splitM K n < n)` (theorem): `hsmall` follows from `hslack`. Proof by contradiction: suppose `¬ splitM K n < n`. Then `splitA K n = n - splitM K n = 0` (truncated subtraction), so `hslack` becomes `1 ≤ loss n * 1`. By definition `loss n = ((n:ℝ)^2)⁻¹`. For n = 0 this is `0⁻¹ = 0 < 1`. For n ≥ 2 it is at most 1/4 < 1. So n = 1. But `splitM K 1 = ⌈K * Real.sqrt ((1:ℝ) * Real.log 1)⌉₊`, and `Real.log 1 = 0`, so this is `⌈0⌉₊ = 0 < 1`. That contradicts the assumption. The theorem can therefore derive `hsmall` at the start (the case split on n is short, using `Real.log_one`, `Real.sqrt_zero` and `Nat.ceil_zero`) and then run the existing proof unchanged. That proof uses `hsmall` only for `hsplit` and `recursiveCostBound_step hn hsmall`. The other hypotheses are needed. Without `hn`, if n < n0 the left side is the base value 2^n·2^(n/5), which beats the right side, e.g. with K = 0 and n large. Without `hslack`, the ceiling in `costMultiplier` can exceed (1+2·loss n)(4/3)^a, e.g. when splitA = 1 and n is large.

### [`LeanPool.Besicovitch.exists_nonnegative_score_of_matched_endpoint_zero`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Besicovitch/SixPoint/EndpointFailureClosed.lean#L27-L46) — `lean-pool-0208`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0208.lean`

- `(hweighted : weightedPairScore configuration.rootDisplacement barC lambda mu (configuration.redDisplacement .left) (configuration.redDisplacement .right) (configuration.bluePullback .left) (configuration.bluePullback .right) ≤ 0)` (theorem): The conclusion is `SixPointFiniteProperty barS` (FiniteProperty.lean: every configuration admissible at s has a packing with nonnegative score) applied to `configuration` and `h`. The library proves this property with no extra assumptions: `sixPointFiniteProperty_barS_of_weightedGeometricBound gramLambda_pos gramMu_pos weightedGeometricBound_gram` (EndpointPacking.lean and GramWeightedBound.lean; used in Main/RationalBound.lean), and there is no sorry in LeanPool/Besicovitch. So `exact sixPointFiniteProperty_barS_of_weightedGeometricBound gramLambda_pos gramMu_pos weightedGeometricBound_gram configuration h` proves the statement without hweighted. weightedGeometricBound_gram does not depend on this theorem, so the argument is not circular once the weaker statement is placed after GramWeightedBound.lean. hweighted is not used to state anything else.
- `(hlambda : 0 < lambda)` (theorem): The conclusion depends only on `configuration` and `h`. It follows from the library's unconditional `SixPointFiniteProperty barS`: `sixPointFiniteProperty_barS_of_weightedGeometricBound gramLambda_pos gramMu_pos weightedGeometricBound_gram configuration h`, which is sorry-free. No other binder or the conclusion mentions hlambda; `lambda` remains bound through hweighted.
- `(hmu : 0 < mu)` (theorem): Same argument as for hlambda. The conclusion follows from `configuration` and `h` alone, via the library's sorry-free proof of `SixPointFiniteProperty barS` (with the weights gramLambda, gramMu and weightedGeometricBound_gram). Nothing else in the statement mentions hmu.
- `(hmatching : SelectedDiagonalMatchingFails configuration)` (theorem): The library proves `SixPointFiniteProperty barS` unconditionally, without assuming this matching failure (`sixPointFiniteProperty_barS_of_weightedGeometricBound gramLambda_pos gramMu_pos weightedGeometricBound_gram`). Applied to `configuration h`, it gives the conclusion. Nothing else in the statement mentions hmatching.
- `(hred : redSiblingTriangleFailure configuration (.endpoint 0))` (theorem): The conclusion follows from `configuration` and `h` alone, via the library's unconditional, sorry-free `SixPointFiniteProperty barS` applied to `configuration h`. Nothing else in the statement mentions hred.
- `(hblue : blueSiblingTriangleFailure configuration (.endpoint 0))` (theorem): The conclusion follows from `configuration` and `h` alone, via the library's unconditional, sorry-free `SixPointFiniteProperty barS` (sixPointFiniteProperty_barS_of_weightedGeometricBound gramLambda_pos gramMu_pos weightedGeometricBound_gram) applied to `configuration h`. Nothing else in the statement mentions hblue.

### [`AleksandrovDifferentiability.prod_measure_diff_eq_zero_of_forall_ae_restrict_section₀`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ViscositySolutionTheory/AleksandrovDifferentiability/Analysis/GoodSet.lean#L97-L106) — `lean-pool-0218`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0218.lean`

- `(hs : MeasurableSet s)` (theorem): The proof only uses `hs` so that it can rewrite with `restrict_apply_eq_zero'`. That rewrite turns `ν.restrict A B = 0` into `ν (B ∩ A) = 0`, where `A = {y | (x,y) ∈ s}`. Only the inequality is needed, and Mathlib's `Measure.le_restrict_apply (s t : Set α) : μ (t ∩ s) ≤ μ.restrict s t` gives it with no measurability assumption. Proof without `hs`: use `hbad.exists_measurable_subset_ae_eq` to get a measurable `t ⊆ s \ G` with `t =ᵐ s \ G`. By `Measure.measure_prod_null htmeas` (which needs `SFinite ν`, still available), it is enough to show `ν (Prod.mk x ⁻¹' t) = 0` for every x. We have `Prod.mk x ⁻¹' t ⊆ Prod.mk x ⁻¹' (s \ G) = {y | (x,y) ∉ G} ∩ {y | (x,y) ∈ s}`. By `le_restrict_apply`, the measure of that set is at most `ν.restrict {y | (x,y) ∈ s} {y | (x,y) ∉ G}`, which is 0 by `ae_iff` applied to `hsection x`. Then `measure_congr htae.symm` finishes as in the current proof. Measurability of `s` is not needed anywhere else, and no degenerate case depends on it.
- `[SFinite ν]` (theorem): `Measure.prod` is defined as `if SFinite ν then bind μ (fun x => map (Prod.mk x) ν) else 0` (`protected irreducible_def prod` in Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Measure/Prod.lean). The statement still elaborates without the instance, since `Measure.prod` and `NullMeasurableSet _ (μ.prod ν)` take no instance argument. Proof without it: split on `SFinite ν` by cases. If it holds, apply the existing proof with that instance. If not, `rw [Measure.prod_def, if_neg h]` makes `μ.prod ν = 0`, so `μ.prod ν (s \ G) = 0` holds trivially. This hypothesis can be removed independently of `hs`.

Then 0 < t follows from `lt_of_lt_of_le (by norm_num) h1`. The proof only uses h0 to pass it on to tc_302 and tc_303, which need 0 < t. Without h0, define `h0 : 0 < t := lt_of_lt_of_le (by norm_num : (0:ℝ) < 723732917263 / 2560000000000) h1` and pass it to both calls. In the tc_303 branch, t > 4542766622207/16000000000000 > 0, so the same h0 works there too. Nothing else in the statement changes.

### [`TauCeti.DavisKahan.ExactSinTheta.generalizedSinTheta_unbounded_real_reducingSubspace`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/DavisKahan/DavisKahan/SinTheta/Natural/Reducing.lean#L496-L543) — `lean-pool-0223`

- `(hAdense : Dense (A.domain : Set ER))` (theorem): This follows from `hA : IsSelfAdjoint A` by the Mathlib lemma `IsSelfAdjoint.dense_domain` (Mathlib/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/LinearPMap.lean): if the domain were not dense, the junk value of `A†` would have domain ⊤, which is dense, a contradiction. `hAdense` is used only to fill the `A_dense` field of the problem record. Without it, fill that field with `hA.dense_domain`; the rest of the proof does not change. The library already uses this lemma (Unbounded/Core.lean says these hypotheses are derivable). Each of `hAdense`, `hAclosed`, `hA0dense` and `hA0closed` can be deleted on its own, and all four can also be deleted together.
- `(hAclosed : A.IsClosed)` (theorem): This follows from `hA` by the Mathlib lemma `IsSelfAdjoint.isClosed`: every self-adjoint `LinearPMap` is closed. The lemma needs `[CompleteSpace ER]`, which is available. `hAclosed` is used only to fill the `A_closed` field. Without it, fill that field with `hA.isClosed`; the rest of the proof does not change.
- `(hA0dense : Dense (A0.domain : Set FR))` (theorem): This follows from `hA0 : IsSelfAdjoint A0` by `IsSelfAdjoint.dense_domain`. It is used only to fill the `A₀_dense` field. Without it, fill that field with `hA0.dense_domain`; the rest of the proof does not change.
- `(hA0closed : A0.IsClosed)` (theorem): This follows from `hA0` by `IsSelfAdjoint.isClosed`. The lemma needs `[CompleteSpace FR]`, which is available. `hA0closed` is used only to fill the `A₀_closed` field. Without it, fill that field with `hA0.isClosed`; the rest of the proof does not change.

### [`BGS.CorvajaZannier.exists_support_direction_of_not_rankTwo`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MarkoffModP/BGS/CorvajaZannier/PlaneCurveSupportRank.lean#L186-L216) — `lean-pool-0224`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0224.lean`

- `(hnonsingleton : ∃ s ∈ f.support, s ≠ r)` (theorem): The statement is still true without this hypothesis; it only picks out the degenerate case, which can be handled directly. By definition (line 175), `planeExponentDifference r s = fun i => (s i : ℤ) - (r i : ℤ)`. Proof without the hypothesis: split on whether some `s ∈ f.support` satisfies `s ≠ r`. If one does, run the existing proof unchanged; it only uses this hypothesis to obtain such an `s`. If none does, every `s ∈ f.support` equals `r`, so `planeExponentDifference r s = planeExponentDifference r r = 0`. Take `v := fun _ => 1`, which is nonzero, and `k := 0`; then `0 • v = 0` holds (by `funext` and `simp [planeExponentDifference]`). An empty support is a special case of this branch and holds trivially. The other two hypotheses are needed. Without `hr`: take `f` with support `{s, t}` and `r` off the line through `s` and `t`. Any determinant built from two support points has a repeated vector and is zero, so the rank-two condition fails, but `s - r` and `t - r` are not parallel. Without `hnrank`: take three non-collinear support points.

That proof uses only lemmas and definitions from ConvexMollification.lean itself (contDiff_two_normedBumpConvolution_left l.320, convexOn_normedBumpConvolution_of_convexOn_extension l.452, exists_pos_forall_rOut_le_abs_normedBumpConvolution_sub_extension_le l.676, jensenMollifierBump l.712, tendsto_jensenMollifierBump_rOut l.730, jensenMollifierBump_rOut_le_rho l.744). None of these depend on `of_extensionMollification`, which is referenced only in MainTheorems.lean. So `SmoothConvexApproximationOnInteriorClosedBallTheorem n` holds with no hypothesis: run the current proof with `hmoll` replaced by that unconditional proof, inlined or with the theorem moved after those lemmas. MainTheorems.lean already does exactly this in `SmoothConvexApproximationOnInteriorClosedBallTheorem.proof`. There are no edge cases, since the hypothesis is a closed statement true for all n.

### [`CarlsonFunctions.SeveralComplexVariables.norm_sub_le_of_separately_analytic_bounded`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CarlsonFunctions/SeveralComplexVariables/LocallyBounded.lean#L67-L108) — `lean-pool-0237`

- `(hr : 0 < r)` (theorem): The statement holds for r ≤ 0 too. If r < 0, closedBall c r = ∅ (Metric.closedBall_eq_empty : closedBall x ε = ∅ ↔ ε < 0), so hx is contradictory. If r = 0, closedBall c 0 = {c} (Metric.closedBall_zero), so x = y = c; the left side is ‖f c - f c‖ = 0 and the right side is card ι * (M / 0) * ‖c - c‖ = card ι * 0 * 0 = 0, so 0 ≤ 0 holds. Proof: split on `r ≤ 0` and handle that branch as above. In the remaining branch `0 < r` holds, so the existing proof applies unchanged.

### [`EulerPacketTerminalDatum.forwardInitializedPacket_normalized_bound`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/PacketForwardInitializedCorrectionData.lean#L76-L84) — `lean-pool-0242`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0242.lean`

- `(hα : 0 < α)` (variable): `hα` follows from `hgrowth : timeProfileChange S.growth hTime = α • L.g` plus positivity built into the structures. `S : Scales (Icc 0 M.T)` has the field `growth_pos : ∀ t, 0 < growth t` (PacketTimeProfiles.lean). `timeProfileChange_pos` (PacketBudgetTimeChange.lean) carries this positivity over to `timeProfileChange S.growth hTime t`. `L : EulerTransversePacketForward.Budget D (Fin 4) 6` has the field `positive : ∀ t, 0 < L.g t` (TransversePacketForwardBudget.lean). `D.T_pos : 0 < D.T` (TransversePacketData.lean), so the interval `Icc 0 D.T` contains t₀ := ⟨0, le_rfl, D.T_pos.le⟩. Evaluating `hgrowth` at t₀ gives 0 < (α • L.g) t₀ = α * L.g t₀ with L.g t₀ > 0, hence 0 < α (if α ≤ 0 the product would be ≤ 0). There are no degenerate cases: the interval is never empty and both profiles are strictly positive. Repair: drop the binder with `omit hα in` and start the proof with `have hα : 0 < α := by have h := timeProfileChange_pos S.growth S.growth_pos hTime t₀; rw [hgrowth] at h; have := L.positive t₀; simp only [ContinuousMap.smul_apply, smul_eq_mul] at h; nlinarith`. The term proof then continues unchanged, passing `hα` to `forwardPacket_normalized_bound` and `forwardInitialized_primary_budget`. This repair was not compiled; the exact simp lemma for applying `α • L.g` pointwise may need adjusting.

### [`LeanPool.Besicovitch.sigma_mul_radius_div_two_le_diam_localAttachmentComponent`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Besicovitch/Rectifiability/ComponentDiameter.lean#L38-L191) — `lean-pool-0244`
