- `(hsigma_one : sigma < 1)` (theorem): The proof uses it only to get `hR_rho : sigma * rho / 2 < rho` by nlinarith. `hannulus` already implies this. It gives a w with `dist w z < rho` (w ∈ ball z rho) and `¬ dist w z < sigma * rho / 2` (w ∉ ball z (sigma*rho/2)), so sigma*rho/2 ≤ dist w z < rho. If sigma*rho/2 ≥ rho the annulus would be empty. So obtain w from hannulus first and prove hR_rho by linarith; the rest of the proof is unchanged.
- `(hsigma : 0 ≤ sigma)` (theorem): Split on the sign of sigma. If sigma < 0, then since 0 < rho we have sigma * rho / 2 < 0 ≤ Metric.diam _ (Metric.diam_nonneg), so the conclusion holds trivially. If 0 ≤ sigma, the existing proof applies verbatim: it uses hsigma only for `hR_nonneg` (positivity) and as the `hβ` argument of `uniformDensitySet_ball_measure_gt`. It can be deleted independently of, or together with, hsigma_one.

### [`EulerTransversePacketProvider.Forcing.velocityPath_reflection_neg`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/TransversePacketParity.lean#L374-L380) — `lean-pool-0247`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0247.lean`

- `(hM : ∀ t x, D.M.field t (-x) = D.M.field t x)` (variable): The proof uses hM only in `D.frameDerivative_even hF hM`, i.e. to get that the frame derivative `D.frameDerivative.field t` is even in x (the hypothesis `hE₁` of `EulerSourceCylinderParity.velocity_reflection_neg`). This follows from hF alone. The structure `Data` contains `frame_time` (F₁ is the time derivative of F within Icc 0 T), and `Data.frame_derivative` gives `HasDerivWithinAt (fun s => extendPath D.T D.T_pos.le D.frame.field s x) (extendPath D.T D.T_pos.le D.frameDerivative.field t x) (Icc 0 D.T) t`. Since `extendPath T hT f s = f (projIcc 0 T hT s)`, `D.frame_even hF` makes the functions for -x and x equal (funext). Derivatives within Icc 0 D.T are unique: Mathlib's `uniqueDiffOn_Icc D.T_pos` (D.T_pos : 0 < D.T is a field of Data, so the interval is never a single point) together with `UniqueDiffWithinAt.eq_deriv`. So the derivative values agree, and `projIcc_of_mem` turns this into `D.frameDerivative.field t (-x) = D.frameDerivative.field t x` for t : Icc 0 D.T. That term replaces `D.frameDerivative_even hF hM`, and the rest of the proof is unchanged. hM appears nowhere in the statement, so deleting it (dropping it from the `include`) leaves the statement well-formed and true.

### [`CKN.norm_sub_integralAverage_le_volumeAverage_integral_norm_fderiv_mul_norm_sub_along_segment`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CaffarelliKohnNirenberg/Foundation/Sobolev/Poincare/Lp.lean#L159-L202) — `lean-pool-0254`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0254.lean`

- `[MeasureTheory.IsFiniteMeasure (volumeMeasureOn U)]` (theorem): `volumeMeasureOn U` is an abbrev for `volumeOn U`, which is `MeasureTheory.volume.restrict U` (RestrictedVolume.lean). The hypothesis `hvol : 0 < (volume U).toReal` gives `volume U < ∞` via `ENNReal.toReal_pos_iff : 0 < a.toReal ↔ 0 < a ∧ a < ∞`. Mathlib's `MeasureTheory.isFiniteMeasure_restrict : IsFiniteMeasure (μ.restrict s) ↔ μ s ≠ ∞` then gives the instance. So the proof can start with `haveI : IsFiniteMeasure (volumeMeasureOn U) := isFiniteMeasure_restrict.2 (ENNReal.toReal_pos_iff.1 hvol).2.ne`, and the rest of the proof is unchanged (`hconstInt` and the call to `norm_sub_integralAverage_le_volumeAverage_integral_norm_sub` use the local instance). No degenerate case is left: `hvol` rules out both infinite and zero volume.
- `(hu : MeasureTheory.IntegrableOn u U)` (theorem): Write g y := ∫ t in 0..1, ‖fderiv ℝ u (segmentBlend x t y)‖ * ‖x - y‖. `hsegment` says g is integrable on U. The library lemma `norm_sub_le_integral_norm_fderiv_mul_norm_sub_along_segment huDiff x y` gives ‖u x - u y‖ ≤ g y for every y. `huDiff.continuous` makes y ↦ u x - u y continuous, hence AE-strongly measurable. So `Integrable.mono' hsegment (by fun_prop/continuity).aestronglyMeasurable (ae_of_all _ bound)` shows that y ↦ u x - u y is integrable on U. The restricted measure is finite (from the instance, or from `hvol` as in the other entry), so the constant y ↦ u x is integrable on U. Then u = (fun _ => u x) - (fun y => u x - u y) is integrable on U. That recovers `hu`, and the existing proof goes through. Edge cases: if U had infinite measure, `hvol` would fail, so nothing is lost. Either this hypothesis or the IsFiniteMeasure instance can be deleted on its own, and both can be deleted together, since the instance is recovered from `hvol` first.

### [`EGZ.CommonMeasure.final_mass_le`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ErdosGinzburgZiv/EGZ/Decomposition/CommonMeasure.lean#L109-L119) — `lean-pool-0276`

- `(hε : 0 < ε)` (theorem): If ε ≤ 0 the conclusion is trivial from hu: mass u P ≥ 0 by WeightedIncidence.mass_nonneg u hu P, so ε/2 * mass u P ≤ 0 (mul_nonpos_of_nonpos_of_nonneg), and 0 ≤ mass u Q by mass_nonneg u hu Q. If 0 < ε, the existing proof applies. So the proof splits on the sign of ε. This hypothesis can be deleted together with hR.
- `(hR : 0 < R)` (theorem): If R ≤ 0 (and ε > 0): mass u P ≤ mass w P ≤ R ≤ 0 by CommonMeasure.mass_weight_mono w u hle P and hP. So ε/2 * mass u P ≤ 0 ≤ mass u Q, the last step by WeightedIncidence.mass_nonneg u hu Q. If R > 0, the existing proof applies. This can be deleted together with hε: the case ε ≤ 0 is handled as in that entry.

The proof steps are: norm_abs_eq_norm, abs_inner_le_inner_abs (which uses positivity preservation), hlam₀_top, hT_sa.eq_smul_self_of_isLocalExtrOn (which needs only self-adjointness), and computing the Rayleigh quotient. The other lemma called, `ground_state_strictly_positive`, takes no compactness argument. hT_compact is not mentioned anywhere else in the statement. So the theorem holds without it. ### [`QuadraticCarleson.annularQuadraticKernel_correlation_le_ordered`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticFixedHeightAveragingCorrelation.lean#L49-L127) — `lean-pool-0281`

- `(hD : 0 ≤ D)` (theorem): `hD` follows from `hab` and `hR`. For any point, say 0, we have `0 ≤ ‖a 0‖ ≤ D / R`. Since `0 < R`, `le_div_iff₀ hR` turns this into `0 * R ≤ D`, so `0 ≤ D`. The proof can therefore start with `have hD : 0 ≤ D := by have h := (le_div_iff₀ hR).mp ((norm_nonneg _).trans (hab 0)); simpa using h` and then run unchanged. Inside the proof, `hD` only serves the `positivity` side goals (e.g. `0 ≤ D / R`, `0 ≤ D / S`) and `hP`, and the derived fact covers all of them. ### [`AVenhance.Infra.Section3.ChiMKCorrector.chiMK_spaceLap_three_active`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AnomalousDiffusion/AVenhance/Infra/Section3/ChiMKCorrector.lean#L159-L209) — `lean-pool-0282`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0282.lean`

- `(hk : k % 4 = 3)` (theorem): The identity also holds when `k % 4 ≠ 3`, so `hk` can be deleted. `chiMK κ m k t x = (-(corrTime κ m k t)) • uShear β Λ m k x` (ChiMK.lean), and `uShear` (UShear.lean) is `![0, …]` when `k % 4 = 1`, `![-(…cos(2π x 1/ε)), 0]` when `k % 4 = 3`, and `0` otherwise. So whenever `k % 4 ≠ 3`, the coordinate `uShear β Λ m k y 0` is `0` for every `y`, and `fun y => I.chiMK κ m k t y 0` is the constant zero function.

Proof without `hk`: do `by_cases hk : k % 4 = 3`. In the positive case, use the existing proof. In the negative case:
1. Show `(fun y => I.chiMK κ m k t y 0) = fun _ => 0` with `funext y; simp [Ingredients.chiMK, uShear, hk]`, splitting on `k % 4 = 1` if needed. Either way the 0-coordinate is 0.
2. `spaceGrad (fun _ => 0) y i = fderiv ℝ (fun _ => 0) y (basisVec i) = 0` by `fderiv_const`. The proof already does this step via `simp [AVenhance.spaceGrad]` for `hzeroGrad`.
3. Then the inner gradient function is identically 0, the outer one is too, and `spaceLap = 0 + 0 = 0`.
4. The right-hand side is `-(4π²/ε²) * I.chiMK κ m k t x 0 = -(4π²/ε²) * 0 = 0`.

No degenerate case breaks this argument: the right-hand side has the factor `chiMK … x 0 = 0` whatever the value of ε. So the statement stays true and provable without `hk`.

### [`TauCeti.sum_sq_principalCosines_eq_sum_sq_norm_projection`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/DavisKahan/ForTauCeti/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/AngleGeometry.lean#L747-L767) — `lean-pool-0283`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0283.lean`

- `[U.HasOrthogonalProjection]` (theorem): Implied by the other hypotheses. `[FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E]` (from `variable`) gives `FiniteDimensional 𝕜 U` through the standard submodule instance. Mathlib's `RCLike.properSpace_submodule` (Analysis/RCLike/Lemmas.lean) then gives `ProperSpace U`, so `U` is a `CompleteSpace`, and `HasOrthogonalProjection.ofCompleteSpace` (Projection/Basic.lean) gives `U.HasOrthogonalProjection`. `HasOrthogonalProjection` is a `Prop`-valued class, so by proof irrelevance the synthesized instance is definitionally the same as the binder. The statement (`principalCosines U V`, `projection V`) elaborates the same way, and the proof goes through unchanged, with the lemmas it calls (`singularValues_cosThetaMap_comp_subtype`, `starProjection_eq_self_iff`, ...) getting the synthesized instance. Removing this binder together with `[V.HasOrthogonalProjection]` also works.
- `[V.HasOrthogonalProjection]` (theorem): Same argument as for `U`. `FiniteDimensional 𝕜 V` follows from `[FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E]`, then `RCLike.properSpace_submodule` gives `ProperSpace V`, hence `CompleteSpace V`, and `HasOrthogonalProjection.ofCompleteSpace` gives `V.HasOrthogonalProjection`. The class is a `Prop`, so the synthesized instance agrees with the binder by proof irrelevance. The statement and the proof are unchanged.

### [`Malliavin.memLp_mderiv_cylinder_expGrowth`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Malliavin/Malliavin/CylindricalGrowth.lean#L488-L496) — `lean-pool-0295`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0295.lean`

- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (theorem): `hC` follows from `hfg'`. Take `y = 0`, which exists in `Fin n → ℝ` for every `n`, including `n = 0`. Then `0 ≤ ‖fderiv ℝ f 0‖ ≤ C * Real.exp (c * ‖0‖) = C * Real.exp 0 = C`. In Lean: `have hC : 0 ≤ C := (norm_nonneg _).trans (by simpa using hfg' 0)`. The existing proof then goes through unchanged. This works whether or not `hc` is kept.
- `(hc : 0 ≤ c)` (theorem): The statement still holds when `c < 0`. Set `c' := max c 0 ≥ 0`. Since `‖y‖ ≥ 0`, we have `c * ‖y‖ ≤ c' * ‖y‖`, hence `Real.exp (c * ‖y‖) ≤ Real.exp (c' * ‖y‖)`. Multiplying by `C ≥ 0` (kept, or derived from `hfg'` as for `hC`) gives `hfg'' : ∀ y, ‖fderiv ℝ f y‖ ≤ C * Real.exp (c' * ‖y‖)`. The current proof then runs with `c'` in place of `c`: `memLp_mderiv_cylinder_of_dominated μ hf L (ψ := fun x ↦ C * Real.exp (c' * ((∑ i, ‖L i‖) * ‖x‖))) hfg'' (expGrowth_coord_le hC (le_max_right c 0) L) (integrable_expGrowth_sq μ C c' _)`. Here `integrable_expGrowth_sq` holds for any real `c`. Equivalently, apply the theorem itself with `c'`. The conclusion does not mention `c`, so nothing else changes. `hc` and `hC` can also be removed together.

### [`Malliavin.BrownianIteratedConstruction.wickPurePowerMixedInnerIdentity_of_generated`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Malliavin/Malliavin/BrownianPurePowerDuality.lean#L393-L402) — `lean-pool-0298`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0298.lean`

- `(hsm : ∀ t, StronglyMeasurable (B t))` (theorem): The other hypotheses already imply `hsm`. For each `t`, `coordinate_apply t` together with `funext` gives `B t = ⇑(coordinate t)`. Here `coordinate t : StrongDual ℝ W` is a continuous linear map, so `(coordinate t).continuous` holds. Mathlib's `Continuous.stronglyMeasurable` (Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/StronglyMeasurable/Basic.lean:723) then gives `StronglyMeasurable (B t)`. Its instance requirements are met: `[OpensMeasurableSpace W]` follows from `[BorelSpace W]`; `ℝ` is pseudo-metrizable; and `SecondCountableTopologyEither W ℝ` holds because `ℝ` (and also `W`) is second countable. No degenerate case needs separate treatment, since this argument works for every `t`. So `hsm` can be removed from the statement. The proof starts with `have hsm : ∀ t, StronglyMeasurable (B t) := fun t => by rw [show B t = ⇑(coordinate t) from funext (coordinate_apply t)]; exact (coordinate t).continuous.stronglyMeasurable` and then calls the same two lemmas as before. A second route also works: `generated` makes each `B t` measurable (`IsWienerGenerated.measurable`), and `Measurable.stronglyMeasurable` applies because `ℝ` is second countable.

### [`jensen_map_maxIdeal_eq_of_surj_closed`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AndersonConjecture/Jensen/Jensen.lean#L479-L486) — `lean-pool-0305`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0305.lean`

- `(h_closed : ∀ (I : Ideal ↥R), I.FG → ∀ (c : ↥R), (c : T) ∈ Ideal.map R.subtype I → c ∈ I)` (theorem): The proof uses h_closed only for the inclusion `Ideal.map R.subtype (maximalIdeal R) ≤ maximalIdeal T`, through `map_maxIdeal_le_of_closed`. That inclusion already follows from h_surj and the locality of R. Compose `r ↦ Ideal.Quotient.mk (M_T^2) r`, surjective by h_surj, with the quotient map T ⧸ M_T^2 → T ⧸ M_T = ResidueField T, which is surjective because M_T^2 ≤ M_T. This gives a surjective ring hom from R onto the field ResidueField T. Its kernel is `(maximalIdeal T).comap R.subtype`. By `RingHom.ker_isMaximal_of_surjective` (Mathlib/RingTheory/Ideal/Maps.lean) that kernel is a maximal ideal of R. Since R is local, `IsLocalRing.eq_maximalIdeal` gives kernel = maximalIdeal R. Hence maximalIdeal R ≤ comap R.subtype M_T, which is equivalent to the `≤` inclusion by `Ideal.map_le_iff_le_comap`. The reverse inclusion in `map_maxIdeal_eq_of_surj_closed` is a Nakayama argument that uses only h_surj, `comap_maxIdeal_le_of_local` and T being Noetherian; none of these involve h_closed. There are no degenerate cases: T is local, so its residue field is a nontrivial field. Removing h_closed is necessary-safe on its own; h_surj cannot also be removed (R = ℂ ⊆ T is a counterexample).

### [`QuadraticCarleson.integral_le_four_mul_succ_of_le_lowDecayMajorant`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticCarleson/LowOscillatoryMajorant.lean#L307-L344) — `lean-pool-0312`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0312.lean`

- `(hFmeas : AEStronglyMeasurable F (volume.restrict (Icc (z - ℓ) (z + ℓ))ᶜ))` (theorem): Split on `IntegrableOn F (Icc (z-ℓ) (z+ℓ))ᶜ`. If `F` is not integrable there, `integral_undef` makes the left side 0, and `0 ≤ 4*(B+1)*A` holds by `hA` and `B ≥ 0`. If it is integrable, that integrability is exactly the `hFint` the proof builds from `hFmeas`, and the rest of the calc (integral_mono_ae, integral_const_mul, integral_lowDecayMajorant_two_pow_le, hA) goes through unchanged. Deleting this needs `hA` to stay.
- `(hFnonneg : ∀ x ∉ Icc (z - ℓ) (z + ℓ), 0 ≤ F x)` (theorem): `hFnonneg` is used only to show `F` is integrable by domination. Instead, split on integrability. If `F` is not integrable on the complement, the integral is 0 ≤ 4*(B+1)*A by `hA`. If it is integrable, `integral_mono_ae` with `hFbound` needs only the upper bound, and the rest of the calc is unchanged. Deleting this needs `hA` to stay, so it cannot be dropped together with `hA`.
- `(hA : 0 ≤ A)` (theorem): `hA` follows from the other hypotheses. With `ℓ > 0`, take `x = z + 2ℓ`, which lies outside `Icc (z-ℓ) (z+ℓ)`. There `lowDecayMajorant (2^(2B)) ℓ z x = min (1/(2ℓ)) (2^(2B)*ℓ/(4ℓ^2)) > 0`. Then `hFnonneg` and `hFbound` give `0 ≤ F x ≤ A * (positive)`, hence `0 ≤ A` (e.g. `nonneg_of_mul_nonneg_left`, or a short case argument). The original proof then goes through with this derived `hA`. This needs `hFnonneg`, `hFbound` and `hℓ` to stay.
- `(hℓ : 0 < ℓ)` (theorem): `lowDecayMajorant D ℓ z x = min (1/|x-z|) (D*ℓ/|x-z|^2)`, with `D = 2^(2B) > 0`. If `ℓ = 0`, the complement is `{z}ᶜ`, and for `x ≠ z` the majorant is `min (positive) 0 = 0`. So `0 ≤ F x ≤ A*0 = 0`, `F` vanishes on the set, and the integral is 0 ≤ 4*(B+1)*A by `hA`. If `ℓ < 0`, `Icc (z-ℓ) (z+ℓ)` is empty, so the bounds hold at every `x`. For `x ≠ z`, `D*ℓ/|x-z|^2 < 0`, so the majorant is negative and `A*majorant ≤ 0`. At `x = z`, both terms are junk `0`. Hence `F ≡ 0` and the integral is 0 ≤ RHS. If `ℓ > 0`, use the original proof. Deleting this needs `hA` and `hFnonneg` to stay.

### [`NavierStokes.LocalSignedRequest.SlowRegion.chartQ_pos`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/LocalSignedRequest.lean#L2030-L2032) — `lean-pool-0332`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0332.lean`

- `(hx : x.2.1 ∈ U.carrier)` (theorem): `MeanRankUpdate.chartQ coord x` is positive for every `coord` and every `x`, so the membership hypothesis is not needed. By `chartQ_eq_slow` (LocalSignedRequest.lean:1243), `MeanRankUpdate.chartQ coord p = SimilarityCoordinates.coordinateQ coord (p.2.1.1, p.2.1.2)`. The proof term in the theorem also relies on this, since it uses `U.q_mem x.2.1 hx`. In SimilarityCoordinates.lean:135, `coordinateQ a p` is defined as `if hp : 0 < a ∧ a < 1 ∧ 0 < p.1 then Classical.choose (exists_positive_solution ...) else 1`. In the first branch, `Classical.choose_spec` gives `0 < q`, because `exists_positive_solution` states `∃ q, 0 < q ∧ …`. In the junk branch the value is `1 > 0`. There is no other case, so no extra assumption on `coord` or `x` is needed. Proof without `hx`: `unfold MeanRankUpdate.chartQ PhysicalCoordinateBounds.qCoord SimilarityCoordinates.coordinateQ; split_ifs with h; exacts [(Classical.choose_spec (SimilarityCoordinates.exists_positive_solution h.1 h.2.1 h.2.2 _)).1, one_pos]`. Alternatively, rewrite with `chartQ_eq_slow` first and then case-split in the same way. After deleting `hx`, `U` remains an argument that the proof does not use. That is allowed, because only `hx` is being deleted. `U` cannot be deleted on its own, since `hx` mentions `U.carrier`.

### [`NavierStokes.RadialPullback.pullback_contDiff`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/RadialPullback.lean#L1400-L1402) — `lean-pool-0338`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0338.lean`

- `(ha : 0 < a)` (theorem): pullback d a F = F ∘ liftChart (powerChart d a), where powerChart d a R = (positiveRadius (a/4) R) ^ d and positiveRadius ℓ x = ℓ + (x - ℓ) * smoothTransition ((x - ℓ)/ℓ). positiveRadius_contDiff ℓ holds for every ℓ. ha is used only to get positiveRadius (a/4) R ≠ 0, which ContDiff.rpow_const_of_ne needs. Without ha: (1) if a = 0, then (x-0)/0 = 0, so positiveRadius 0 x = x * smoothTransition 0 = 0 (Real.smoothTransition.zero). powerChart d 0 is the constant 0^d, smooth by contDiff_const. (2) if a < 0, put ℓ = a/4 < 0. For x ≥ ℓ, (x-ℓ)/ℓ ≤ 0, so smoothTransition gives 0 (smoothTransition.zero_of_nonpos) and the value is ℓ. For x < ℓ, (x-ℓ) < 0 and smoothTransition ≥ 0 (smoothTransition.nonneg), so the value is ≤ ℓ. Hence positiveRadius ℓ x ≤ ℓ < 0 never vanishes, and ContDiff.rpow_const_of_ne applies. (3) if a > 0, this is the existing proof. Then liftChart_contDiff and hF.comp finish as before. Nothing else in the statement mentions ha.

### [`NavierStokes.EndpointCoordinates.composition_endpoint_smoothAt`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/EndpointCoordinates.lean#L337-L342) — `lean-pool-0344`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0344.lean`

- `(hh : 0 < h)` (theorem): Write a = 2h. The proof uses `hh` in only two ways. It does not need it for the endpoint being in the domain, and it needs it for smoothness only because a library lemma asks for 0 ≤ a.

(1) Membership. `cartesian_endpoint_mem` goes through `endpoint_mem_domain`, which calls `zeroTime_mem_stableTarget`. In that lemma the positivity argument is named `_ha` and is never used. The witness `(endpointRoot a z, z)` works for every a < 1 and z ≠ 0: `endpointRoot_pos`, `endpointRoot_equation` and `endpointRoot_slope` (slope = 1 - a > 0) only need `a < 1`, and that follows from `hh1` alone. So `(1, x) ∈ cartesianDomain h` for every h < 1/2.

(2) Smoothness. `cartesianExtension_smoothAt` reduces to `qExtension_smoothAt`, then to `PositiveRepresentatives.stableInverse_smoothAt (ha : 0 ≤ a) (ha1 : a ≤ 1)`. That lemma uses `ha` only through `stableInverse_forwardMap`, i.e. `forwardMap_injOn_stable` (injectivity of `q ↦ forwardScalar a z q` on `stableSource`). `forwardMap_smooth`, `forwardMap_hasFDerivAt`, `stableSource_open` and `stableTarget_open` take no sign assumption on a.

When a ≤ 0 (h ≤ 0), injectivity still holds. We have `scalarSlope a z q = 1 - z^2 * a * q^(a-1) ≥ 1` for q > 0, because z^2 ≥ 0, -a ≥ 0 and q^(a-1) > 0. This is the derivative of `forwardScalar a z` (`forwardScalar_hasDerivAt`). So `strictMonoOn_of_deriv_pos` gives strict monotonicity on any `Icc q r` with q > 0, exactly as in `forwardScalar_lt_on_stable`, with the bound `slope ≥ 1` replacing `scalarSlope_mono`. Hence `stableInverse (forwardMap a p) = p` on `stableSource a` for every a ≤ 1, and the inverse-function argument in `stableInverse_smoothAt` goes through unchanged.

The remaining steps (`innerExtension_smoothAt` via `qExtension_pos`, composition with `contDiff_profilePoint`, and finally `hf.comp`) do not involve h's sign.

Degenerate case h = 0: forwardScalar 0 z q = q - z^2 and the endpoint root is z^2 > 0, so the argument works. Thus the statement holds without `hh`, as long as `hh1` and `hx` stay. `hx` is genuinely needed: at x 2 = 0 the point leaves the stable target and the extension is discontinuous there. `hh1` is also kept.

### [`QuantumParallelRepetition.unconditionalCanonicalAcceptedCoefficient_sourceScale`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuantumParallelRepetition/Part09.lean#L1438-L1492) — `lean-pool-0384`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0384.lean`

- `(dimension : 0 < d)` (theorem): The theorem also takes `i : Fin d`, and `Fin.pos i : 0 < d` (Init/Data/Fin/Basic.lean) derives the same fact. When d = 0 the type Fin 0 is empty, so the statement holds vacuously. The proof only uses `dimension` to get `(d:ℝ) ≠ 0` and for `positivity`; adding `have dimension := Fin.pos i` at the start leaves the rest of the proof unchanged.
- `(grid : 0 < N)` (theorem): Case N = 0. The lemma `dSVUniformDensityThresholdRaw_norm_sq` (no hypotheses) gives ‖raw‖² = d·0 = 0, so ‖raw‖ = 0. Its inverse is 0⁻¹ = 0, so the left side is 0. On the right, `dSVDensityRationalPhysicalAcceptedRank_gridPrefix` (no hypotheses) rewrites the grid prefix as r/0 = 0, so the right side is √(w·0) = 0. Both sides are 0. Case N > 0: use the original proof. So `grid` is not needed; the proof just splits on whether N = 0.
- `(width : 0 < w)` (theorem): Case w ≤ 0. We have d ≥ 0, so w·d ≤ 0 and `Real.sqrt_eq_zero'` gives √(w·d) = 0; the left side is 0. On the right, unfold `dSVDensityRationalCanonicalAcceptedCoefficient` (= √(w * gridPrefix …)). The rank lemma `dSVDensityRationalPhysicalAcceptedRank_gridPrefix` (no hypotheses) rewrites the grid prefix as r/N, which is ≥ 0 because r ≥ 0 and N ≥ 0 as reals. So w·(r/N) ≤ 0 and the right side is also 0. Case w > 0: use the original proof, which needs `width` only through `..._targetCoefficient_sq`. So `width` can be removed by splitting on the sign of w. Each of the three hypotheses (`dimension`, `grid`, `width`) can be removed on its own, and the same case analyses also work with all of them removed together.

### [`NatOrdinal.exists_lt_forall_add_add_le`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ConwayRefinement/ConwayRefinement/SetTheory/Ordinal/PairBounds.lean#L206-L237) — `lean-pool-0393`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0393.lean`

- `(ht₁ : t₁ ≠ 0)` (theorem): Implied by he₁. `leastTerm_zero : leastTerm (0 : NatOrdinal) = 0` (ConwayRefinement/SetTheory/Ordinal/LeastTerm.lean) and `wpow_ne_zero (x : NatOrdinal) : ω^ x ≠ 0` (CombinatorialGames/NatOrdinal/Pow.lean). If t₁ = 0, then he₁ becomes 0 = ω^ e₁, which contradicts wpow_ne_zero. To fix the proof, first recover the hypothesis with `have ht₁ : t₁ ≠ 0 := by rintro rfl; rw [leastTerm_zero] at he₁; exact wpow_ne_zero e₁ he₁.symm`, then run the existing proof unchanged.
- `(ht₂ : t₂ ≠ 0)` (theorem): Same argument, using he₂. If t₂ = 0, then `leastTerm_zero` turns he₂ into 0 = ω^ e₂, which contradicts `wpow_ne_zero e₂`. Recover it with `have ht₂ : t₂ ≠ 0 := by rintro rfl; rw [leastTerm_zero] at he₂; exact wpow_ne_zero e₂ he₂.symm` and keep the rest of the proof. Both ht₁ and ht₂ can be deleted together, because each is derived from its own leastTerm equation.

### [`RS.EdgeSubset.isOut_pushData_liftData_succ_closed_at`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/RegtsSevenster/RS/Novel/Skein/ConverseFamily.lean#L868-L926) — `lean-pool-0394`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0394.lean`

- `(hEL : (EdgeSubset.mk (liftSubsetClosed (V.dropSubset (cutL n) (cutR n) s) (decide (V.boundaryFlag (cutL n) ∈ s))) hcL : EdgeSubset V).Eulerian)` (theorem): By `RS.Fragment.liftSubsetClosed_dropSubset` (GlueSubsetBij.lean:382), whose inputs are `hij : i ≠ j`, `hclosed` and closure of `s`, we get `liftSubsetClosed (V.dropSubset (cutL n) (cutR n) s) (decide (V.boundaryFlag (cutL n) ∈ s)) = s`. Here we supply `cutL_ne_cutR n` (already used in the proof), `hcl` and `hc`. Because the left side mentions `s`, `subst` cannot be used. Instead prove `∀ t (ht : t = s) hct, (EdgeSubset.mk t hct).Eulerian` by `rintro t rfl hct; exact hE`. This works because by proof irrelevance `EdgeSubset.mk s hct` and `EdgeSubset.mk s hc` are definitionally equal. Applying it to the lifted set, the lemma's equality and `hcL` recovers `hEL`, which is then passed to `isOut_unglue_glueDataClosed` as before. Nothing else in the statement mentions `hEL`. It can be deleted together with hneL.
- `(hneL : Nonempty (EdgeSubset.mk (liftSubsetClosed (V.dropSubset (cutL n) (cutR n) s) (decide (V.boundaryFlag (cutL n) ∈ s))) hcL : EdgeSubset V).CanonData)` (theorem): Same argument as for hEL. `liftSubsetClosed_dropSubset (cutL_ne_cutR n) hcl s hc` shows the lifted set equals `s`. Then `∀ t (ht : t = s) hct, Nonempty (EdgeSubset.mk t hct).CanonData`, proved by `rintro t rfl hct; exact hne` (proof irrelevance makes the two EdgeSubsets definitionally equal), recovers `hneL` from `hne`. The rest of the proof is unchanged, and `hneL` is not mentioned elsewhere in the statement. It can be deleted together with hEL.

### [`LeanStationaryHarmonicMaps.StationaryHarmonicMap.weak_radial_cutoff_integrable_of_W12Loc_contDiffBump`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/LeanStationaryHarmonicMaps/StationaryHarmonicMap/RadialIntegrability.lean#L309-L324) — `lean-pool-0396`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0396.lean`

- `(hRout : f.rOut < R0)` (theorem): The proof uses `hRout` only to build an `AdmissibleRadialCutoff` through `ContDiffBump.exists_admissibleRadialCutoff`. The library's own lemma `weak_radial_cutoff_integrable_of_locallyL2` (same file) does not need that structure. It needs: `GradientAEStronglyMeasurableIn Du Ω` and `GradientLocallyL2In Du Ω` (both come from `hW` via `hW.gradient_aestronglyMeasurable` and `hW.gradient_locallyL2`), `hclosedBall_subset`, `ContDiff ℝ 1 phi`, bounds `‖phi t‖ ≤ M0` and `‖deriv phi t‖ ≤ M1` for t in [0, R0], and `0 ≤ R0`. None of these involves where the bump vanishes, so how `rOut` compares with `R0` does not matter. The coefficients `weakRadialMainCoeff n phi x = (n-2)·phi ‖x‖ + ‖x‖·deriv phi ‖x‖` and `weakRadialRhsCoeff phi x = ‖x‖·deriv phi ‖x‖` are bounded on the ball for any C¹ function phi.

Proof without `hRout`, with phi = (f ·):
- Case R0 < 0: `Metric.ball 0 R0` is empty, so both `IntegrableOn` statements hold by `integrableOn_empty`.
- Case 0 ≤ R0: apply `weak_radial_cutoff_integrable_of_locallyL2` with:
  - `f.contDiff` (Mathlib `ContDiffBump.contDiff`, valid at every smoothness order) for `ContDiff ℝ 1`;
  - M0 = 1, using `f.nonneg` and `f.le_one`;
  - M1 = a bound on `deriv f` over the compact set `Icc 0 R0`. This bound exists because `deriv f` is continuous (`ContDiff.continuous_deriv` applied to `f.contDiff`), via `IsCompact.exists_bound_of_continuousOn`.

The degenerate case R0 ≤ 0 is covered by the empty ball (or, at R0 = 0, by the lemma itself). So the statement stays true and provable after deleting `hRout`.

### [`TauCeti.hasFiniteReciprocalFourierInterpolation_pi_div_two_add_eps_of_integrableKernel`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/DavisKahan/ForTauCeti/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/Sylvester/Internal/ReciprocalMultiplier/Fourier.lean#L697-L713) — `lean-pool-0410`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0410.lean`

- `(hkernel : HasIntegrableReciprocalFourierKernel (Real.pi / 2))` (theorem): The hypothesis is a fixed proposition that does not depend on any other argument. The same file proves it unconditionally, earlier than this theorem: `hasIntegrableReciprocalFourierKernel_pi_div_two : HasIntegrableReciprocalFourierKernel (Real.pi / 2)` (line 317). That proof builds the Haagerup–Zsidó kernel from the lemmas `HaagerupZsido.measurable_reciprocalKernel`, `integrable_reciprocalKernel`, `reciprocalKernel_fourier` and `integral_norm_reciprocalKernel`, none of which uses `sorry`. Without `hkernel`, the proof stays the same except that `hasIntegrableReciprocalFourierKernel_pi_div_two` is passed to `hasApproximateFiniteReciprocalFourierInterpolation_of_integrableKernel` where `hkernel` was. `hkernel` is not mentioned anywhere else in the statement.

### [`QuadraticCarleson.lintegral_selectedAverageKernel_mul`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticAveragingTTStar.lean#L74-L88) — `lean-pool-0413`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0413.lean`

- `(hf : Measurable f)` (theorem): Write ρ = r (σ x) and c = (ENNReal.ofReal (2ρ))⁻¹. After unfolding and `lintegral_indicator measurableSet_closedBall` (which needs only the set to be measurable), the left side is ∫⁻ y in closedBall x ρ, c * f y. Since `centeredAverage ρ f x = (∫⁻ y in closedBall x ρ, f y) / ofReal (2ρ)` and division is multiplication by the inverse, the right side is (∫⁻ y in closedBall x ρ, f y) * c. There are two cases. (1) If ρ > 0, then c ≠ ∞, and Mathlib's `lintegral_const_mul' (r) (f) (hr : r ≠ ∞)` (Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Lebesgue/Add.lean:417), which does not need f to be measurable, gives the equality. (2) If ρ ≤ 0, closedBall x ρ is either empty (ρ < 0) or the singleton {x} (ρ = 0). Either way it has Lebesgue measure zero, so `volume.restrict (closedBall x ρ) = 0` and both integrals are 0. The left side is then 0 and the right side is 0 * c = 0 (in ℝ≥0∞, 0 * ∞ = 0). So the identity holds for every f, measurable or not, and `hf` can be deleted. The proof only changes in replacing `lintegral_const_mul _ hf` with this case split.

### [`CKN.Leray.lerayLimit_everyTime_pairing_tendsto_of_local_energy`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CaffarelliKohnNirenberg/Leray/ForcedLerayLimitPropEveryTime.lean#L37-L233) — `lean-pool-0428`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0428.lean`

- `(hSliceBound : ∀ C : Set Vec3, IsCompact C → C ⊆ (Set.univ : Set Vec3) → ∀ b₁ b₂ : ℝ, Icc b₁ b₂ ⊆ Ioi (0 : ℝ) → ∀ M : ℝ≥0∞, M < ⊤ → (∀ n t, t ∈ Icc b₁ b₂ → (∫⁻ x in C, ENNReal.ofReal (vec3EuclideanNorm (G n (x, t))) ^ (2 : ℝ) ∂volume) ≤ M) → ∀ t ∈ Icc b₁ b₂, (∫⁻ x in C, ENNReal.ofReal (vec3EuclideanNorm (v (x, t))) ^ (2 : ℝ) ∂volume) ≤ M)` (theorem): hSliceBound is used only in `hubound`, to show ∫⁻ x in K k, ofReal(vec3EuclideanNorm (v (x,s)))^2 ≤ A^2 for 0 < s ≤ t. (For s > t, u' s is identically zero and the branch already works without it.) The bound follows from hlocal, hGF and hEnergy because the norm cannot grow under a weak limit. Fix 0 < s ≤ t and the compact C = K k. `hlocal ⟨s,hs⟩ C` gives MemLp witnesses hs k (for G (σ k) (·,s)) and hl (for v (·,s)) in L²(volume.restrict C), and weak convergence ⟪g_k, w⟫ → ⟪g, w⟫ for every w, where g_k and g are the toLp elements of G (σ k) (·,s) and v (·,s). Bound on g_k: by hGF (s > 0), G (σ k) (x,s) = F (σ k) (x,s), so ‖g_k‖² = ∫⁻ x in C, ‖toLp 2 (F (σ k) (x,s))‖ₑ^2. By setLIntegral_le_lintegral this is at most the full integral, which hEnergy t ht (the same A) bounds by A², using s ≤ t. Hence ‖g_k‖ ≤ A.toReal. Bound on g: take w = g. Then ‖g‖² = lim ⟪g_k, g⟫, and |⟪g_k, g⟫| ≤ A.toReal·‖g‖ by Cauchy–Schwarz, so ‖g‖ ≤ A.toReal (the case g = 0 is trivial). Translating back through ‖toLp‖ = (eLpNorm f 2 _).toReal, eLpNorm for p = 2 being (∫⁻ ‖f‖ₑ^2)^(1/2), and henorm (vec3EuclideanNorm y = ‖toLp 2 y‖, from vec3EuclideanNorm_eq_l2), this gives ∫⁻ x in K k, ofReal(vec3EuclideanNorm (v (x,s)))^2 ≤ A^2, which is exactly what hSliceBound supplied. The rest of the proof (hlocal', the call to lerayLimit_globalSlices_of_localWeak, and the final pairing) does not use hSliceBound.

### [`ErdosProblems.MulticolourTriangleRamsey.recursiveCrossColour_same_right_coordinate`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MulticolorTriangleRamsey.lean#L953-L979) — `lean-pool-0438`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0438.lean`

- `(_hb : ∀ d, b d ∈ P \ Q)` (theorem): `_hb` appears nowhere else in the statement, and the proof never uses it (the underscore name already marks it as unused). The proof needs only three things. First, `recursiveCrossColour_spec` (line 884), which takes just `a b f g hcover x y` and has no hypothesis about `b`. Second, `ha` together with `hactive`, which rule out the forward case: there the colour is `a d ∈ Q`, but `colour ∉ Q`. Third, `b.injective`, which comes from `b` being an embedding. So the existing proof still compiles once the binder is deleted.

### [`Erdos137.F_le_pow`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Erdos137/Finiteness.lean#L90-L92) — `lean-pool-0440`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0440.lean`

- `(hk : 1 ≤ k)` (theorem): `F k n = ∏ i ∈ range k, (n + i)`. If k = 0, the product is empty, so `F 0 n = 1`, and `(0 * n) ^ 0 = 1`. The bound `1 ≤ 1` holds, so the statement is true without `hk`. New proof: split on k. For k = 0, `simp [F]` closes the goal. For k = m + 1, `1 ≤ m + 1` holds, so `hk` can be rebuilt and the existing proof applies. This works whether or not `hn` is kept; both hypotheses can be deleted at the same time.
- `(hn : 1 ≤ n)` (theorem): If n = 0 and k ≥ 1, the product `∏ i ∈ range k, (0 + i)` contains the factor for i = 0, which is 0. So `F k 0 = 0`, by `Finset.prod_eq_zero` with `0 ∈ range k`, and `0 ≤ (k * 0) ^ k` holds trivially. If n = 0 and k = 0, both sides equal 1. So the statement is true without `hn`. New proof: split on n. For n = 0, use `Finset.prod_eq_zero` (or the empty product when k = 0) to get `F k 0 ≤ anything`. For n ≥ 1, use the existing proof. The current proof does use `hn`, through `factor_le_mul`, so only the n = 0 case needs this separate argument. This works whether or not `hk` is kept; both hypotheses can be deleted at the same time.

### [`MarkoffRiemannRoch.FunctionField.clifford`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MarkoffModP/RiemannRoch/CoordinateFree/RiemannRoch.lean#L331-L344) — `lean-pool-0444`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0444.lean`

- `(hdeg₀ : 0 ≤ D.deg)` (theorem): The proof passes this hypothesis on to `Chart.clifford` (RiemannRochTheorem/Corollaries.lean, line 500). There it is the unused binder `_hdeg₀`: `Chart.clifford` is just `clifford_of_ell_pos k K hW D hℓD hℓW`. `Chart.clifford_of_ell_pos` (line 487) needs only `IsCanonical`, `0 < ell D` and `0 < ell (W - D)`. Its docstring says the bounds `0 ≤ deg D ≤ 2g − 2` are not required. So the intrinsic proof can be rewritten without `hdeg₀` and `hdeg₀'`: keep `hW'` and the `ell_eq_chart` rewrites, then use `simpa using Chart.clifford_of_ell_pos k K hW' ((divisorEquivChart k K).symm D) hℓD hℓW`. The `simpa` step that turns the chart degree into `D.deg` (`Divisor.chart_deg`) is the same one the current proof already uses. Mathematically this is expected anyway: `ℓ(D) > 0` makes `D` linearly equivalent to an effective divisor, so `deg D ≥ 0`. This hypothesis and `hdeg₁` can be deleted on their own or together.
- `(hdeg₁ : D.deg ≤ 2 * (genus k K : ℤ) - 2)` (theorem): The same reasoning applies. In `Chart.clifford` the matching binder `_hdeg₁` is never used, because the inequality follows from `Chart.clifford_of_ell_pos k K hW D hℓD hℓW`. That lemma combines `ell_add_ell_le`, `ell_canonical` and `riemann_roch` and makes no degree assumption. The intrinsic theorem can call `Chart.clifford_of_ell_pos` on `(divisorEquivChart k K).symm D` and drop `hdeg₁'`; the remaining `simpa` translation of degree and genus is unchanged. Mathematically, `ℓ(W − D) > 0` already gives `deg(W − D) ≥ 0`, so `deg D ≤ 2g − 2`. This hypothesis can be removed together with `hdeg₀`.

### [`BGS.Markoff.splitTraceCoverPolynomial_normalized_not_irreducible_when_exponents_equal_char`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MarkoffModP/BGS/Markoff/TraceCurve/IrreducibilityObstructions.lean#L131-L139) — `lean-pool-0447`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0447.lean`

- `[Fact p.Prime]` (theorem): For a field K, `[CharP K p]` already forces `p.Prime ∨ p = 0` (Mathlib's `CharP.char_is_prime_or_zero`, Mathlib/Algebra/CharP/Defs.lean). Proof without the binder: case on that lemma. In the prime case, build the instance with `haveI : Fact p.Prime := ⟨h⟩` and run the existing proof unchanged. In the case p = 0 both exponents are 0. Unfolding `splitTraceCoverPolynomial` (C α * X0^d * X1^(2e) + C β * X0^d - X0^(2d) * X1^e - X1^e) gives C 1 + C σ - 1 - 1 = C (σ - 1), a constant. If σ = 1 this is 0, which is not irreducible (`not_irreducible_zero`). Otherwise σ - 1 is a unit of K, so its image under the ring map C is a unit (`IsUnit.map`), and a unit is not irreducible (`Irreducible.not_isUnit`). So the statement holds without the primality assumption.

### [`GenLimit.FiniteWitness.Simplified.executableRun_eq`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/LanguageGeneration/FiniteWitness/Simplified/Executable.lean#L97-L105) — `lean-pool-0468`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0468.lean`

- `(hS : S.Nonempty)` (theorem): If S = ∅, both sides equal [] for every k. Simplified.Candidate M F ∅ p k q requires q.toFinset ⊆ ∅ (so q = []) and p.length < q.length = 0, which is impossible. So no candidate exists, the `dite` in sampleRun takes the else branch, and sampleRun naturalCheckpoints F ∅ k = [] by induction. Likewise testCandidate F ∅ p k q contains the same two conjuncts (q.toFinset ⊆ S, p.length < q.length), so candidateCodes F ∅ p k = ∅, and pickWord ∅ = [] by its else branch; hence executableRun F ∅ k = [] by induction. Proof without hS: split on S.Nonempty (as executableNormalized_eq in the same file already does). In the nonempty case use the existing proof. In the empty case, rewrite S = ∅ via Finset.not_nonempty_iff_eq_empty and prove executableRun F ∅ k = [] ∧ sampleRun naturalCheckpoints F ∅ k = [] by induction on k, using the two emptiness facts above. (hF is genuinely needed: without freshness there can be a step where no candidate exists, so sampleRun keeps a nonempty history while executableRun returns pickWord ∅ = [].)

### [`NavierStokes.SignedCrossDefectClass.defect_all_exponents`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/SignedCrossDefectClass.lean#L96-L111) — `lean-pool-0479`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0479.lean`

- `(hαδ : α ≤ δ)` (theorem): The conclusion asks for `MeanClass G.strip γ` for every γ. `FiniteHeadClass.meanClass_all_exponents` gives this from membership at any one exponent together with vanishing on the tail (supplied by `htail`). So the exponent α+β that `defect_mem` provides is irrelevant, and α ≤ δ is used only to reach that particular exponent.

The proof without `hαδ`: let α' = min α δ.
1. `primary_bounds` holds at exponent α' with no hypothesis. Its proof becomes `((f.old_bounds i j).mono_exponent (min_le_left α δ)).sub ((f.difference_bounds i j).mono_exponent (min_le_right α δ))` followed by the same `.congr`/`sub_sub_cancel`. `LabelSumBounds` `mono_exponent` only needs β ≤ α plus the strip facts `epsilon_pos`/`epsilon_le_one`, and `sub` is already used in the original proof.
2. `harmonic_covariance_sum_mem` takes arbitrary exponents for its two families. With the primary family at α' and `f.tangent_bounds` at β, the `crossTensor_mem` argument gives `TensorClass G.strip (α' + β) (crossTensor f a)`.
3. `G.average_mem hS` then puts the meanBar in `MeanClass G.strip (α'+β)`.
4. `physicalSigma_mem G e hr hs hc` gives class α+β. `WeightedClasses.mono_exponent` lowers this to α'+β, since α'+β ≤ α+β.
5. `Class.sub` gives the defect in class α'+β. `FiniteHeadClass.meanClass_all_exponents` with N and the same tail computation from `htail` then yields every γ.

There are no degenerate cases to check: no division or junk values occur, and every lemma used holds for arbitrary real exponents.

### [`LO.FirstOrder.Completeness.chain_monotone`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Incompleteness/Foundation/FirstOrder/Completeness/SearchTree.lean#L229-L239) — `lean-pool-0482`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0482.lean`

- `(nwf : ¬WellFounded (SearchTree.Lt T Γ))` (theorem): The statement also holds when `SearchTree.Lt T Γ` is well-founded, so a proof can split on `WellFounded (SearchTree.Lt T Γ)`. If it is not well-founded, the current proof works unchanged. If it is well-founded, the argument depends on these definitions. `chainU T Γ = descendingChain (SearchTree.Lt T Γ) ⊤`, and `descendingChain r z (i+1) = @Classical.epsilon α ⟨z⟩ (fun y => y ≺ descendingChain r z i ∧ ¬Acc r y)` (Vorspiel/Order.lean). `Classical.epsilon p` is `(strongIndefiniteDescription p h).val`, and in Init/Classical.lean that is `dite (∃ x, p x) … (fun _ => ⟨choice h, _⟩)`. Under well-foundedness every element is `Acc`, so no predicate `y ≺ chainU i ∧ ¬Acc y` has a witness. The negative branch returns `Classical.choice ⟨⊤⟩`, which does not depend on the predicate. So `chainU i = c := Classical.choice ⟨⊤⟩` for every `i ≥ 1`. Given `s ≤ u`: if `s ≥ 1`, then `chain s = c.seq = chain u`, which is trivial. If `s = 0`, then `chain 0 = Γ` (via `seq_top`), and we need `Γ ⊆ c.seq`. This holds for every search tree: induct on `SearchTreeAux T Γ n Δ`. The `zero` case gives `Γ ⊆ Γ`. The `succ a r` case gives `Γ ⊆ Δ₁ ⊆ Δ₂`, using `ReduxNat.antimonotone r`, which needs only the Encodable instances. Both cases prove the conclusion, so `nwf` can be deleted. The remaining Encodable instances cannot be removed, because `chain`/`SearchTree`/`ReduxNat` need them to elaborate.

### [`QuadraticIterates.isSquare_algebraMap_cSeq`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticIterates/ArchMath1992/DegreeCriterion.lean#L386-L419) — `lean-pool-0497`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0497.lean`

- `(hnsq : ¬IsSquare (-a : ℚ))` (theorem): The proof uses hnsq only to make f_k irreducible, hence separable, so that its roots have no repeated entries (`hnodup`). That lets it turn the product over `rootSet` into the product over the multiset `aroots`. The squareness claim does not need this. By `prod_aroots_sub_eq_cSeq a k`, which takes no hypothesis on a, (cSeq a (k+1) : ℚ̄) is the product over the multiset `aroots (fℚ[a,k])` of the factors (α - a), counted with multiplicity. For every root α, `exists_sq_eq_sub` together with `mem_rootSet_iteratedPoly_succ` gives some δ, a root of f_{k+1}, with δ^2 = α - a. Since k+1 ≤ n and `splittingField_mono` applies, δ lies in K_n. So every factor of the multiset product is the square of an element of K_n. A product of squares is a square: (∏ δ_α)^2 is the product over the multiset `(aroots).map (· - a)`, proved by induction on the multiset or with `Multiset.prod_map_mul`. Pulling this back through the injective map K_n → ℚ̄, as the existing proof does with `hinj`, finishes the proof. Repeated roots therefore do no harm, and the statement holds for every a.
- `(hm1 : 1 ≤ m)` (theorem): The only case hm1 excludes is m = 0. There `cSeq a 0 = 0` holds by rfl (lemma `cSeq_zero`), so the goal becomes IsSquare (algebraMap ℚ K_n 0) = IsSquare 0. This holds via 0 = 0 * 0 (`IsSquare.zero`/`isSquare_zero`). For m ≥ 1 the existing proof applies unchanged, so a case split on m replaces hm1. hmn is still needed: when m > n, c_m need not be a square in K_n.

### [`MetricCodes.Hamming.threshold_ceil_numerator_le_two`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MetricCodes/Foundations.lean#L6548-L6559) — `lean-pool-0503`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0503.lean`

- `(hn : 0 < n)` (theorem): `Hamming.threshold n d` is defined at Foundations.lean:4699 as `1 - 2 * (d : ℝ) / (n : ℝ)`. So the goal simplifies to `2 * ⌈δ * n⌉₊ / n ≤ 2`. If `n = 0`, the denominator is `(0 : ℝ)`, and since `x / 0 = 0` the left side is `0`, so `0 ≤ 2` holds. (The numerator is also `0`, because `⌈δ * 0⌉₊ = 0`.) If `n > 0`, the existing proof applies unchanged. So a proof without `hn` is: `rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos n with rfl | hn`; in the zero case, `unfold threshold; simp`, which gives `1 - (1 - 2*0/0) ≤ 2`, i.e. `0 ≤ 2`; in the positive case, run the current proof. The other hypothesis `hδ` cannot be removed: with δ = 2 and n = 1 we get ⌈2⌉₊ = 2, and 2*2/1 = 4 > 2.

### [`NavierStokes.ShapedWaitBounds.weighted_bound_of_angular_square`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/ShapedWaitBounds.lean#L1090-L1105) — `lean-pool-0504`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0504.lean`

- `(hB : 0 ≤ B)` (theorem): Write a₁ = angular … (c.holdStart + t, η), a₀ = angular … (c.holdStart, η) and e = exp(-2·c.lam·t). If B < 0, then hz gives 0 ≤ |z| ≤ B·a₁² ≤ 0, because a₁² ≥ 0. So B·a₁² = 0, hence a₁² = 0 (B ≠ 0) and z = 0. By `weighted_angular_square_hold c η ht` (which needs only 0 ≤ t), a₀²·e = exp t·a₁² = 0. Since e > 0, a₀² = 0, and the goal reduces to 0 ≤ B·0, which holds. So the statement stays true without hB. Proof: split on 0 ≤ B. If 0 ≤ B, keep the existing proof. If B < 0, use the degenerate argument above (abs_nonneg and nlinarith or mul_eq_zero, then the hold identity with Real.exp_pos, then simp). In the existing proof, hB is used only in the last step, to make B·a₀² nonnegative.

### [`BGS.Markoff.splitPrimitiveTraceSolutions_card_error_le_of_weilBoundAssumption`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MarkoffModP/BGS/Markoff/Endgame/PrimitiveInclusionExclusion.lean#L725-L761) — `lean-pool-0508`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0508.lean`

- `(horbitPositive : 0 < orbitExponent)` (theorem): `horbitChar : (orbitExponent : ZMod p) ≠ 0` already implies it. If `orbitExponent = 0`, the cast `((0 : ℕ) : ZMod p)` is `0` (`Nat.cast_zero`), which contradicts `horbitChar`. So the proof can start with `have horbitPositive : 0 < orbitExponent := Nat.pos_of_ne_zero (fun h => horbitChar (by simp [h]))` and then run unchanged. The only use of `horbitPositive` is as the `he` argument of `splitTracePowerRangeSolutions_count_error_le_of_weilBoundAssumption`, and the derived fact fills that slot. Positivity also follows from `horbitDvd`, since `Nat.card (ZMod p)ˣ = p - 1 > 0` and a divisor of a positive number is positive, but `horbitChar` gives the shortest route. The hypothesis is not mentioned anywhere else in the statement, so deleting it needs no other change.

### [`KasamiCyclicAdditive.Phase.weilSum_eq`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/KasamiCyclicAdditive/Phase/RootCount.lean#L518-L579) — `lean-pool-0509`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0509.lean`

- `(hm : m ≠ 0)` (theorem): `m` does not occur in the conclusion; it is only a witness, via `hmD`, that `D` is invertible mod `n = Fintype.card Kˣ`. If `m = 0`, then `hmD` says `0 ≡ 1 [MOD n]`, i.e. `0 % n = 1 % n`. `n = 0` is impossible because `Kˣ` is nonempty, and `n ≥ 2` is impossible because `0 ≠ 1`, so `n = 1`. Then `1 * D ≡ 1 [MOD 1]` holds trivially, and the theorem as stated applies with `m := 1` (`1 ≠ 0`, all other hypotheses unchanged). It gives the same conclusion, since the conclusion does not involve `m`. So the proof is: case split on `m = 0`; in that case use the existing statement with `m = 1`, otherwise use the existing proof. By contrast `hD` is needed: with `D = 0`, `K` is the field with two elements, `u = v = 1` and `A = 0`. The left side is `4·ψ(1) = -4` and the right side is `2·(0 - 1) = -2`.

### [`NavierStokes.ActualCurrentParticularBounds.mode_jet_bound_local`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/ActualCurrentParticularBounds.lean#L535-L559) — `lean-pool-0531`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0531.lean`

- `(hA : 0 ≤ A)` (theorem): `hA` follows from `hab`. The bound `hab : ∀ i ≤ m, ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ i a x‖ ≤ A` includes the case `i = 0`, since `0 ≤ m` always holds for a natural number `m`. So `(norm_nonneg _).trans (hab 0 (Nat.zero_le m))` proves `0 ≤ A`. There is no degenerate case: even when `m = 0`, the index `0` is covered. The proof uses `hA` only once, as the `hP : 0 ≤ P` argument of `PhysicalGraphBounds.pointwise_product_jet_bound`. That argument can be replaced by the term above, and nothing else in the proof changes.

### [`AVenhance.Infra.Section4.iterate_physical_stream_kernel_gains`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AnomalousDiffusion/AVenhance/Infra/Section4/IteratesPhysicalKernelGains.lean#L19-L37) — `lean-pool-0534`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0534.lean`

- `(hFp : 0 < F)` (theorem): hFp follows from the other hypotheses. By hF, F = e ^ (1 + γ/2) * L. The factor e ^ (1 + γ/2) is positive because e > 0 (Real.rpow_pos_of_pos he), and L > 0 by hL, so mul_pos gives 0 < F. A proof without hFp first derives this with `have hFp : 0 < F := hF ▸ mul_pos (Real.rpow_pos_of_pos he _) hL` and then runs the existing proof unchanged.
- `(hL : 0 < L)` (theorem): hL follows from the other hypotheses. Given hF, he and hFp, we have F = e^(1+γ/2) * L with e^(1+γ/2) > 0 and F > 0, so L > 0 (for example pos_of_mul_pos_right, or L = F / e^(1+γ/2) > 0). A proof without hL derives it this way and runs the existing proof. The statement does not even need L > 0: with P = e^(2+γ) > 0 we have F^2 = P*L^2 and F^4 = P^2*L^4. If L = 0, then F = 0, and both sides of both inequalities are 0 under the division-by-zero convention. If L ≠ 0, the L^2 or L^4 factor cancels from both sides. So hFp and hL could even be removed together.
- `(hκ : 0 < κ)` (theorem): Without hκ the statement still holds. From hlower and c > 0, a ≥ 0, e^(2+γ) > 0, we get κ ≥ c*a*e^(2+γ) ≥ 0. If κ > 0, the original proof applies. If κ = 0, both left-hand sides are 0, since x/0 = 0 and 16/κ = 0. The right-hand sides (48*2^19/c)/F^2 and (C/c^2)/F^4 are ≥ 0 because c > 0 and even powers are ≥ 0. So 0 ≤ RHS holds by positivity. A proof without hκ does a case split on κ = 0 or 0 < κ (using `lt_of_le_of_ne` together with `0 ≤ κ`, which comes from hlower and positivity), simplifies with div_zero in the κ = 0 case, and uses the existing proof in the other case.

### [`Stafford38.WeylEulerRemainder.normalForm_sub_X_pow_hasPositiveCoordinateFactor`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Stafford38/Stafford38/Weyl/EulerRemainder.lean#L260-L275) — `lean-pool-0539`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0539.lean`

- `[Algebra ℚ B]` (variable): **The statement elaborates without it.** `CoordinateStage`, `coordinateDerivation`, `normalForm`, `PairStage`, `pairCoordinate`, `pairMomentum`, `pairOldSubring` and `eulerSubring` are all defined from `[Ring B]` alone. The same file already states `HasPositiveCoordinateFactor B N _` under `omit [Algebra ℚ B] in` (`hasPositiveCoordinateFactor_zero`, `hasPositiveCoordinateFactor_add`). It also states the support lemma `support_sub_X_pow_lt` about `Polynomial (CoordinateStage)` that way. The local `Algebra ℚ` instances on the stages are not used by the statement.

**Where ℚ enters the current proof.** `support_sub_X_pow_lt` already omits the instance. `hasPositiveCoordinateFactor_of_outer_support` uses ℚ only through `orderedMonomial_positive_factors` and `orderedMonomial_eq_eulerSubring_mul_coordinate`. There ℚ appears in two places:
- `eulerPolynomialEval : Polynomial ℚ →+* A`, together with `hscalar` (`rational_mem_pairOldSubring`), which places p^j x^j and x^j p^j in `eulerSubring C x p = Subring.closure (C ∪ {x, x*p})`.
- `coordinate_mul_eulerPolynomial`.

**Why integer coefficients suffice.** In WeylPurePower.lean, `d_pow_mul_x_pow` proves `d^n * x^n = rising x d n`. Here `rising x d n` is the product of the factors `x*d + (k+1 : ℕ)`. Likewise `x_pow_mul_d_pow` gives `falling x d n`, the product of the factors `x*d - k`, and `x_mul_falling` gives `x * falling = fallingShift * x`, where `fallingShift` is the product of the factors `x*d - (k+1)`. These lemmas use only ring arithmetic and the Weyl relation, never `algebraMap ℚ`; the ℚ instance is only an auto-included section variable there. Each factor is `x*p` plus or minus a natural-number cast, so it lies in any subring containing `x*p`. Hence `rising`, `falling` and `fallingShift` all lie in `eulerSubring C x p` without `hscalar`.

**How the proof changes.** In `orderedMonomial_positive_factors` and the factorization b·x^(N+a)·p^j = V·x, replace `eulerPolynomialEval x p f` by `rising`/`falling`/`fallingShift`. The rest of the argument goes through unchanged: the monomial decomposition, commuting x past the coefficients, additivity, and the zero case. So the theorem holds and is provable for any `[Ring B]`; the argument has no degenerate cases to check (trivial B is just a special case). The proof itself needs no change beyond that substitution, since the steps of this theorem never use ℚ directly.

### [`QuadraticCarleson.LacunaryMiddleOperator.integrable_middleRangeInput`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticCarleson/LacunaryMiddleOperator.lean#L98-L104) — `lean-pool-0544`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0544.lean`

- `(hAk : 0 ≤ A k)` (theorem): The case `0 ≤ A k` is handled by the existing proof. If `¬ 0 ≤ A k`, i.e. `A k < 0`, the bad part is identically zero. `InMagnitudeLevel A k t` (PositiveEndpointOptimization.lean:38) is `0 ≤ t ∧ t ≤ A 0` when `k = 0`, and `A (k-1) < t ∧ t ≤ A k` otherwise. In both cases it forces `t ≤ A k < 0`, which is impossible for `t = ‖f x‖ ≥ 0`. So `magnitudeLevelSet A f k = ∅`, and then `levelRestricted A f k = ∅.indicator f = 0`. Next, `levelAverage A f k z R = ⨍ x in I, 0 = 0`, and `levelAtom A f k z R = I.indicator (fun _ ↦ 0 - 0) = 0`. Hence every term of `stoppingScaleLevelBadPart A f j k` (a tsum of indicators of `levelAtom` values) is 0, so the function is 0. `middleRangeInput A f k B c m` is a finite sum (over `S B c m`) of these functions, so it is `0`, which is integrable (`integrable_zero`). Proof without the hypothesis: `by_cases hAk : 0 ≤ A k`. In the first case use the current proof. In the second case show the level set is empty from the definition of `InMagnitudeLevel`, simplify `middleRangeInput` to `0`, and conclude with `integrable_zero`. The hypothesis is only needed in the library because `integrable_levelAtom` and `tsum_atomL1Mass_le_two_mul_global_level_mass` take it. It is not needed for the truth of this statement.

### [`moebius_restricted_sum`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticIterates/Mathlib/NumberTheory/Moebius.lean#L118-L151) — `lean-pool-0547`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0547.lean`

- `(hm : 1 ≤ m)` (theorem): Follows from hn and hmn: if m = 0 then 0 ∣ n forces n = 0, contradicting 1 ≤ n. In the proof, replace the `hm_pos` line with a derivation from hn and hmn (e.g. `Nat.pos_of_ne_zero (by rintro rfl; simp_all)`); the rest is unchanged. Note: hm and hn cannot both be deleted (m = n = 0 makes the statement false: 0 = 1).
- `(hn : 1 ≤ n)` (theorem): With hm kept, the only excluded case is n = 0. There `Nat.divisorsAntidiagonal_zero : divisorsAntidiagonal 0 = ∅` makes the left-hand sum 0, and the right-hand side `if 0 = m then 1 else 0` is 0 because m ≥ 1. Proof: case split on n = 0, close that case by simp, otherwise run the existing proof. (It can be deleted on its own, but not together with hm.)
- `(hmn : m ∣ n)` (theorem): If ¬ m ∣ n, the filtered set is empty: an x in n.divisorsAntidiagonal has x.1 * x.2 = n (Nat.mem_divisorsAntidiagonal), so m ∣ x.2 would give m ∣ n. The sum is therefore 0. The right-hand side is also 0, since n = m would give m ∣ n via m ∣ m. Proof: `by_cases hmn : m ∣ n`; the positive case is the existing proof; the negative case uses Finset.sum_eq_zero (or filter_false_of_mem) on the left and if_neg on the right.

### [`TauCeti.DavisKahan.sinTwoTheta_addBounded_gauge_of_intervalExterior`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/DavisKahan/DavisKahan/DoubleAngle/UnboundedIdeal.lean#L540-L567) — `lean-pool-0550`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0550.lean`

- `(hβα : β ≤ α)` (theorem): `hβα` appears in no other binder and not in the conclusion, so deleting it leaves the statement well-formed. It is only passed on to `sinTwoTheta_addBounded_gauge_of_spectrum_gap`. The new proof splits on `β ≤ α`. If `β ≤ α`, keep the current proof. If `α < β`, then `Set.Icc β α = ∅`, so `hBsub` forces `B = ∅`, and the statement holds trivially for the following reasons.

1. `selfAdjointSpectralSubspace A hA B hB` is by definition `pvmRangeSubspace (spectralPVM hA) B hB = (P.proj B hB).range`. By `ProjValMeasure.proj_empty` (`P.proj ∅ _ = 0`) and proof irrelevance for `hB`, this subspace is `range 0 = ⊥`.
2. `sinTwoThetaIdealBlock U V = U.starProjection ∘L (...)`, so each of its values lies in `U = ⊥`. By ext, the block is the zero operator.
3. `N.Mem 0` holds by `zero_mem` in `OperatorIdeal/CanonicalRealView.lean`, and `N.gaugeReal 0 = 0` by `gaugeReal_zero`. So the left side of the inequality is `δ * 0 = 0`.
4. `N.gaugeReal E` is an `ENNReal.toReal`, so it is nonnegative (`gaugeReal_nonneg`, whose proof is just `ENNReal.toReal_nonneg`). Hence `0 ≤ 2 * N.gaugeReal E`.

So the theorem holds without `hβα`. In the case `α < β`, `hBcomplDisj` merely says the interval is empty, which is consistent with the other hypotheses, so no case is lost.

### [`CKN.Core.Endgame.regular_point_of_holder_norm`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CaffarelliKohnNirenberg/Core/Endgame/HolderGluing.lean#L105-L115) — `lean-pool-0551`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0551.lean`

- `(hγ1 : γ ≤ 1)` (theorem): `IsRegularPoint Ω I u z` asks for some exponent γ' with 0 < γ' ≤ 1. It does not have to be the γ from the hypotheses, and `ParabolicHolderVecNormLE` already gives a uniform sup bound B. So the case γ > 1 can be reduced to exponent 1.

Proof without `hγ1`: split on whether γ ≤ 1.
- If γ ≤ 1, the current proof works unchanged.
- If γ > 1, use `holder_norm_mono hw hVU` to get B, K ≥ 0 with `vec3EuclideanNorm (w z) ≤ B` and `vec3EuclideanNorm (w z - w y) ≤ K * d^γ` on V, where d = `parabolicDist z y`. Here d ≥ 0, since `dist_eq_parabolicDist` identifies it with `dist`. Show `ParabolicHolderVecOn V w 1` with constants B and K' = max K (2B):
  - If d ≤ 1: d^γ ≤ d by `Real.rpow_le_self_of_le_one` (needs 0 ≤ d ≤ 1 ≤ γ). So the difference is at most K·d ≤ K'·d^1, using `Real.rpow_one`.
  - If d > 1: the triangle inequality for `vec3EuclideanNorm`, proved as in the file's `euclidean_norm_sub_le` via `vec3EuclideanNorm_eq_l2` and `norm_sub_le`, bounds the difference by 2B ≤ 2B·d ≤ K'·d^1.

  Then `regular_point_of_holder_on_open hV hz hVΩ one_pos le_rfl (ae_restrict_of_ae_restrict_of_subset hVU hwu) this` concludes.

Edge cases: d = 0 is in the first case, and 0^γ = 0 ≤ 0 holds. Nothing else uses γ ≤ 1. So the statement stays true and provable without `hγ1`.

### [`Complexity.RAM.RegisterStore.DenseOverlay.Snapshot.encodedStoreLength_run_le_internal`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/BeyondBethe/Complexitylib/Models/RandomAccessMachine/Simulation/RegisterStore/DenseOverlay/Internal.lean#L409-L466) — `lean-pool-0555`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0555.lean`

- `(hcanonical : Canonical snapshot.overlay)` (theorem): The proof uses hcanonical only to apply `decode_step_internal` (to rewrite `(snapshot.step program input).decode input` as `RAM.step program (snapshot.decode input)`) and to carry the invariant through the induction via `step_canonical_internal`. The per-step size bound `encodedStoreLength_step_le_internal` takes no canonicity hypothesis. `decode_step_internal` holds for every overlay. It reduces to `decode_stepInstr_internal`, whose only use of Canonical is `DenseOverlay.read_write_internal`, i.e. `RegisterStore.read_write_internal` applied with value `value + 1`, which is never 0. The store-level lemma needs `AddressesNodup` only when the value is 0, because then `write` removes just the first matching entry and a duplicate could show through. For a nonzero value v, a direct induction on the list proves `RegisterStore.read (RegisterStore.write s a v) t = Function.update (RegisterStore.read s) a v t` for any store s. Nil case: `write [] a v = [(a,v)]`. Cons case (b, x) :: rest: if a = b, `write` gives `(a,v) :: rest`, and both sides read v at t = a and `read rest t` otherwise. If a ≠ b, `write` gives `(b,x) :: write rest a v`; at t = b both sides give x, and for other t the induction hypothesis applies. So `DenseOverlay.read input (write overlay a v) = Function.update (read input overlay) a v` holds unconditionally. The `jz`, `jmp` and `halt` cases never needed Canonical. Hence `decode_step` holds for every snapshot, and the same induction on fuel (generalizing snapshot) goes through without hcanonical: the halted case is unchanged, and in the non-halted case the induction hypothesis is applied to `snapshot.step program input` with no side condition, followed by the same `rw [hdecode]` and calc.

### [`CKN.gaussCarlemanPhase_scalarLaplacian`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CaffarelliKohnNirenberg/Leray/Support/CarlemanGaussDerivatives.lean#L96-L103) — `lean-pool-0563`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0563.lean`

- `(ht : 0 < z.2)` (theorem): Write z = (x, t). By definition, `gaussCarlemanPhase q (y, t) = (-(8 * t)⁻¹) • ∑ i, y i ^ 2 - q * Real.log (gaussCarlemanTimeWeight t)`. `spatialPartial` (line 29 of `Statements/SpatialPartial.lean`) only differentiates in the space variable `y` with `t` fixed. So the `log` term is a constant for every real `t`: its sign, and the junk value of `log`, never matter. `scalarLaplacian` is `∑ i, spatialSecondPartial _ i i z`, and `spatialSecondPartial` differentiates `spatialPartial` again in space.

The statement is true for all t:
- The first spatial partial is `-(8t)⁻¹ * (2 * x i)`. This equals `-x i / (4t)` for every t, including t = 0 where both sides are 0, since `(8t)⁻¹ * 2 = (4t)⁻¹` holds identically when `0⁻¹ = 0`.
- The second partial is then `-(1/(4t)) * δ_ij` for every t.
- So the Laplacian is `-3/(4t)`. At t = 0 the phase is constant in y, so the Laplacian is 0, and `-3/(4*0) = 0` as well.

How the proof changes: the proof only uses `ht` through `gaussCarlemanPhase_spatialSecondPartial`, which uses it only through `gaussCarlemanPhase_spatialPartial`. In both lemmas, `ht` is used only by `field_simp [ne_of_gt ht]`, to close purely algebraic goals:
- in `gaussCarlemanPhase_spatialPartial`: `-(8t)⁻¹ * (2 * x i) = -x i / (4t)`;
- in `gaussCarlemanPhase_spatialSecondPartial`: `-(1/(4t)) * 1 = -1/(4t)`.
These are field identities that hold without `t ≠ 0`, and `ring` (which normalizes inverses in a field) closes them directly. The other steps (`fderiv_sub_const`, `fderiv_fun_const_smul`, `fderiv_apply`, `sum_sq_spatial_deriv`) never use positivity. The helper lemmas can therefore be restated without `ht`, and this theorem's proof (`rw [scalarLaplacian]; simp_rw [...]; simp; ring`) goes through unchanged.

### [`CKN.Core.Step4.fixed_pressure_cylinder_subset_ball`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CaffarelliKohnNirenberg/Core/Step4/WeakGradientGluingTFixedForceMorrey.lean#L39-L46) — `lean-pool-0566`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0566.lean`

- `(hR : 0 < R)` (theorem): Definitions involved: `parabolicCylinder x t r = vec3Ball x r ×ˢ Ioc (t - r^2) t`, where membership in `vec3Ball x r` means `vec3EuclideanNorm (y - x) < r`. The lemma `metricBall_eq_parabolicBall` holds for every real `r` with no sign condition, and it rewrites `Metric.ball z₀ R` as `vec3Ball z₀.1 R ×ˢ Ioo (z₀.2 - R^2) (z₀.2 + R^2)`. Take `z` in the cylinder. Its space component satisfies the same condition in both sets. Its time `s` satisfies `z₀.2 + R^2/4 - R^2 < s ≤ z₀.2 + R^2/4`, so `z₀.2 - R^2 < s < z₀.2 + R^2` follows whenever `R^2 > 0`, i.e. whenever `R ≠ 0`. The sign of `R` does not matter.

Degenerate case `R = 0`: the time interval is `Ioc (z₀.2) (z₀.2)`, which is empty, so the cylinder is empty and the inclusion is trivial. The same contradiction also follows from `hz.1 : vec3EuclideanNorm _ < 0`, because `vec3EuclideanNorm` is a `Real.sqrt` and so nonnegative (`vec3EuclideanNorm_nonneg`).

Proof without `hR`: do `rcases eq_or_ne R 0 with rfl | hR`. In the zero case, `hz.2.1` and `hz.2.2` simplify to `z₀.2 < s ≤ z₀.2`, and `linarith` closes the goal. In the nonzero case, `hs : 0 < R^2` comes from `pow_pos`/`sq_pos_of_ne_zero` (or `positivity` with `hR`), and the existing `nlinarith only` steps go through unchanged.

### [`EulerFiniteGrades.convolution_strict_congr`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/PacketProfileRecursion.lean#L42-L55) — `lean-pool-0576`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0576.lean`

- `(hMp : p ≤ M)` (theorem): `convolution M B u v n = ∑ i ∈ range (M+1), ∑ j ∈ range (M+1), if i+j=n then B (u i) (v j) else 0` (FiniteGradeAlgebra.lean:41). The proof only uses hMp to rewrite with `convolution_eq_range`. Instead, unfold `convolution` and compare term by term: `sum_congr rfl` twice, then `split_ifs`. For a term with i+j=p: if i=0, then `hu 0 hp` and `hu0` give u' 0 = u 0 = 0, so both sides are `B 0 _ = 0` (`map_zero`, `LinearMap.zero_apply`). If j=0, the same argument applies with `map_zero` in the second argument. Otherwise i,j ≥ 1, so i = p-j < p and j < p, and `hu i`, `hu j` rewrite u' to u. No relation between p and M is used. In particular, when p > M every term with i+j=p already has i,j ≥ 1, and when p > 2M both sides are 0. So the statement remains true and provable without hMp.

### [`abs_sInf_image_sub_sInf_image_le`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MovingSofa/Development/Geometry/Foundations/Development001.lean#L6301-L6319) — `lean-pool-0579`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0579.lean`

- `(hg : BddBelow (g '' s))` (theorem): Implied by hf and h. If b is a lower bound of f '' s, then for every x ∈ s, h gives f x - g x ≤ |f x - g x| ≤ C (abs_le), so g x ≥ f x - C ≥ b - C. Hence b - C is a lower bound of g '' s. The proof can build hg this way at the start and continue as written. (hf and hg can even be dropped together: if f '' s is not bounded below, the same argument shows g '' s is not either. Real.sInf_of_not_bddBelow then makes both infima 0, and the goal 0 ≤ C follows from h at a point of the nonempty s, since 0 ≤ |f x - g x| ≤ C.)
- `(hf : BddBelow (f '' s))` (theorem): Symmetric to hg, so it is implied by hg and h. If b is a lower bound of g '' s, then for x ∈ s, h gives g x - f x ≤ C (abs_le / abs_sub_comm), so f x ≥ g x - C ≥ b - C. Hence BddBelow (f '' s) holds and the existing proof goes through. hs cannot be removed: for empty s with C < 0, both infima are sInf ∅ = 0 and |0 - 0| ≤ C fails.

### [`fourier_markov_bad_set`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/LatticeTriangle/Solution.lean#L5765-L5784) — `lean-pool-0589`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0589.lean`

- `(hn : 2 ≤ n)` (theorem): This follows from `hq_pos` and `hq_bound`. Casting gives 1 ≤ (q:ℝ) < (n:ℝ)/2, so (n:ℝ) > 2. Since n is a natural number, n ≥ 3 ≥ 2. A proof without `hn` first derives `have hn : 2 ≤ n` from these two facts (cast `hq_pos` to ℝ, combine with `hq_bound`, then `exact_mod_cast` / `linarith`), and then runs the existing proof unchanged: `error_split_bound n hn …` and `combine_error_P0_Palpha n hn`. Deleting this one hypothesis alone works. `hq_bound` can also be deleted (see the other entry); dropping both together still works, using the case split described there.
- `(hq_bound : (q : ℝ) < (n : ℝ) / 2)` (theorem): This is unnecessary because when it fails the conclusion holds trivially. Split on whether (q:ℝ) < n/2. If it holds, use the existing proof. Otherwise q ≥ n/2: take B = ∅. Then `B.card = 0 ≤ …` holds in ℕ. The ∀-clause is vacuous: from `1 ≤ p` and `(p:ℝ) + q < n/2 ≤ q` we get (p:ℝ) < 0, which contradicts p ≥ 1 (by `linarith` after casting `hp_pos`). So the statement stays true and provable without `hq_bound`, with or without `hn` (in the case q < n/2, `hn` can be recovered as in the other entry).

### [`AVenhance.Infra.Section5.Contracts.twistie5ND_split`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AnomalousDiffusion/AVenhance/Infra/Section5/Contracts/TermCenteredSplit.lean#L49-L58) — `lean-pool-0593`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0593.lean`

- `(hm : 1 ≤ m)` (theorem): The proof uses `hm` in exactly one place: the call `Infra.Section3.xiMK_odd_support_finite I hm t`. That call supplies the finiteness argument `hfin : {k | c k ≠ 0}.Finite` that `sd_split_odd` needs. The other ingredients do not take `hm`: `twistie5ND_eq_defect` is proved by `rfl` and takes `(m : ℕ)` only; `sd_defect5_contDiff` and `tf_G_slice_contDiff` likewise take no `1 ≤ m` hypothesis. The finiteness also holds for every `m`. In `Infra/Section4/LocalFinite.lean`, `AVenhance.Ingredients.xiMK_support_finite (m : ℕ) (t : ℝ) : {k : ℤ | I.xiMK m k t ≠ 0}.Finite` has no hypothesis on `m`. Its proof uses only `I.tau_pos' m`, which is also unconditional in `m`, together with `finite_lattice_near` and the support bounds of `xi`. `Infra/Section5/FrozenAnsatzTime.lean` similarly has `xiMKNearSupport_finite` with no hypothesis on `m`. To get the statement for odd indices, take the preimage under `Subtype.val`, as the proof of `xiMK_odd_support_finite` already does: `Set.Finite.preimage (f := fun k : {k : ℤ // Odd k} => k.1)` with `Subtype.ext` for injectivity. Replacing `Infra.Section3.xiMK_odd_support_finite I hm t` with this term proves the theorem without `hm`. The case `m = 0` is therefore covered too: the identity is a pure finite-sum calculus fact once the support is finite. `hm` appears nowhere else in the statement.

### [`HahnSeries.Nonpositive.hasLowerTruncationDegree_strictTail`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ConwayRefinement/ConwayRefinement/HahnSeries/Germ/AlgebraicIndependence/CofactorInduction.lean#L164-L180) — `lean-pool-0603`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0603.lean`

- `(hc : c < 0)` (theorem): The proof uses `hc` only in the case `c < 0`. The statement also holds when `0 ≤ c`, so a proof can begin with `by_cases hc : c < 0`. If `c < 0`, the existing proof works unchanged. Otherwise `0 ≤ c`, and then `strictTail c b = 0`. To see this, recall that `strictTail c b` is defined as `⟨truncGT c ↑b, _⟩`. By `HahnSeries.coeff_truncGT`, its coefficient at `g` is `if c < g then b.coeff g else 0`. When `c < g`, we have `g > c ≥ 0`, so `g ∉ Iic 0`. Since `b.property` says that the support of `b` lies in `Iic 0`, `b.coeff g = 0`. So every coefficient vanishes, and `Subtype.ext` together with `HahnSeries.ext` gives `strictTail c b = 0`. Then `hasLowerTruncationDegree_zero m` (in LowerTruncationDegree.lean, stated under the same variables) proves `HasLowerTruncationDegree 0 m`, which is the conclusion. In this branch the hypothesis `h` is not even needed. The other binders cannot be removed: `h` is essential for `c < 0`, `b`, `m` and `c` appear in the conclusion, and all the section instances are needed to elaborate `ν = cantorBendixsonDegreeValuation`, whose underlying valuation uses multiplicativity under the full set of hypotheses.

### [`E4_fiber_subset_pos_neg`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/RamanujanTauMissesPrimes/Solution.lean#L2740-L2756) — `lean-pool-0604`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0604.lean`

- `(hx : (x : ℝ) > X ^ ((1 : ℝ) / 11))` (theorem): `hx` only matters for elements of the left-hand set, and every such element already gives it. By the definition `E4Set X = {p | (p.1 : ℝ) > X ^ (1/11) ∧ 1 ≤ |p.2^2 - 5*p.1^22| ∧ |…| ≤ 4*X}`, the membership `hu : (x, u) ∈ E4Set X` (introduced by `intro u hu`) contains `hu.1 : (x : ℝ) > X ^ (1/11)` up to defeq. So the proof works unchanged once `hx` is replaced by `hu.1` inside the `intro`: `five_x22_sub_4X_pos X hX x hu.1` and `u_ne_zero_of_fiber X hX x hu.1 …`. The conclusion does not mention `hx`.
- `(hX : 4 < X)` (theorem): The proof uses `hX` only to show `u ≠ 0`, through `5*x^22 - 4*X > 0`, and that holds for every real X once `hu` is assumed. The interval bounds come from `|u^2 - 5x^22| ≤ 4X` (lemma `u_sq_in_interval`, which has no hypothesis on X). Case X < 0: then 4X < 0 ≤ |…|, so the fiber is empty and the inclusion is vacuous. Case 0 ≤ X ≤ 1: since x ≥ 1, 5x^22 ≥ 5 > 4 ≥ 4X. Case X > 1: the library lemma `x11_gt_X_of_x_gt` (needs only `1 < X` and `hx`, or `hu.1`) gives x^11 > X. Since x ≥ 1, x^22 ≥ x^11 > X, so 5x^22 > 4X. In every case 5x^22 - 4X > 0. Then u = 0 would give 0 ≥ 5x^22 - 4X > 0, a contradiction, which is the same `nlinarith` step as in `u_ne_zero_of_fiber`. The case split on the sign of u then goes through as before. This works whether or not `hx` is kept, because `hu.1` supplies it, so `hX` and `hx` can each be deleted, and also both together.

### [`NavierStokes.ActivationBounds.NaturalOverlap.reference_histories_independent`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/ActivationStocks.lean#L674-L694) — `lean-pool-0605`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0605.lean`

- `(hX : 0 ≤ p.1)` (theorem): By `profileHistory_eq_initial_add_primitive`, `profileHistory P r p = profileInitial P r p.2 + ∫ x in 0..p.1, radialDensity r x (P.f (x,p.2)) (P.U (x,p.2))`. Here `primitive F p := ∫ x in (0:ℝ)..p.1, F (x, p.2)` and `profileDensity P r p = radialDensity r p.1 (P.f p) (P.U p)`, so the density at a point depends only on `f` and `U` at that same point. The initial terms agree, as in the given proof (`profileInitial_congr`). For the integral, use `intervalIntegral.integral_congr`. It only needs the integrands to agree on `uIcc 0 p.1`, whatever the sign of `p.1`. Every `x` in that set satisfies `x ≤ max 0 p.1`. This is `≤ radius N.endpoint δᵢ = N.endpoint * Real.exp δᵢ`: that radius is positive (`N.endpoint_pos`, `Real.exp_pos`), and `p.1 ≤ radius` by `hpᵢ`. So `refF_eq_natural` / `refU_eq_natural` (which need only `hη` and `x ≤ N.endpoint * Real.exp δ`) give `A.f = N.f = B.f` and `A.U = N.U = B.U` at `(x, p.2)`. When `p.1 < 0`, every such `x` is ≤ 0 < `N.endpoint`, and `refF_eq_natural_initial` already gives the same equalities. `hX` is only used to rewrite `uIcc 0 X` as `Icc 0 X` inside `profileHistory_congr_up_to`. Restating that congruence step with `uIcc`, or doing the integral congruence inline as above, removes the need for it. So the statement still holds and is provable without `hX`.

### [`NavierStokes.MeanRankUpdate.rank_update_meanClass`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/MeanRankUpdate.lean#L962-L1004) — `lean-pool-0608`
