- `(hB : B ≠ 0)` (theorem): hB is used only through rank_kernels_meanClass → angularModel_contDiffOn / axialModel_contDiffOn. There shapedAmplitude_ne_zero supplies the condition hCn (C p ≠ 0) for FiveRowRank.deltaV_joint_contDiffOn / gamma_joint_contDiffOn. When B = 0 the result still holds. modelAmplitude coord 0 y = 0/(1+η^2) = 0, and because x/0 = 0 in Lean, angularDebt 0 d = ![0, -(d 0)/(2*0), d 2/0] = ![0,0,0] and axialDebt 0 d = ![0, -(d 1)/0] = ![0,0] for every d. So the H inside scaleFamily (angularFamily/desiredAxialFamily) becomes fun p => deltaV lam 0 a b 0 p.2 (resp. gamma …). This is ContDiff by FiveRowRank.deltaV_contDiff / gamma_contDiff composed with snd. scaleFamily_contDiffOn then applies, since it needs only ell and U smooth and ell ≠ 0 (modelLength = √y.1 > 0 on positiveTime). Hence angularModel_contDiffOn and axialModel_contDiffOn hold without hB, by splitting on whether B = 0. No other step uses hB: chartKernel_contDiffOn, chartKernel_finiteJetBounds, angular/axial_kernel_zero, meanClass_of_interior_support and chartAngular_eq_sum / chartAxial_eq_sum do not take it. So the theorem stays true and provable after deleting hB.

### [`NavierStokes.PhaseJetBounds.PolynomialJets.const`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/CurlClassBounds.lean#L624-L635) — `lean-pool-0609`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0609.lean`

- `(hC : 1 ≤ C)` (theorem): `PolynomialJets D f` (the structure at line 589 of CurlClassBounds.lean) asks for smoothness, plus, for each N, some constant `C' ≥ 1` and exponent `m` with `FiniteJetBound N (f i) (D.carrier i) (C' * D.scale i ^ m)`. That constant is existentially quantified, so we are free to choose it. Without `hC`, take `C' := max C 1`. Then `1 ≤ C'` holds by `le_max_right`. Since `D.scale i ≥ 1` (`D.one_le_scale`), we have `0 ≤ D.scale i ^ m` (`pow_nonneg`). For n = 0, `‖c i‖ ≤ C * scale^m ≤ max C 1 * scale^m`, using `hc i` and `mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right (le_max_left C 1)`. For n = k+1, the iterated derivative of a constant is 0 (`iteratedFDeriv_succ_const`), and `0 ≤ max C 1 * scale^m` because both factors are nonnegative. Smoothness is `contDiffOn_const` as before. So the statement is still true and provable with `hc` alone, the conclusion is unchanged, and `C` stays in the statement because `hc` mentions it.

### [`LeanPool.PoincareThreeBody.resonantCombinedCorrection_periodic`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/PoincareThreeBody/ResonantActionObstruction.lean#L323-L334) — `lean-pool-0612`

- `(hp : 0 < p)` (theorem): `resonantOrbitPeriod p` is defined as `2 * Real.pi * p` (ResonantOrbit.lean:30). When `p = 0` it equals `2 * π * ((0 : ℕ) : ℝ) = 0`, so both sides of the equation are `resonantCombinedCorrection F 0 q eccentricity orientation 0` and the statement holds by reflexivity. The proof without `hp` splits on `p`: `rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos p with rfl | hp`. The case `p = 0` closes with `simp [resonantOrbitPeriod]` (`Nat.cast_zero`, `mul_zero`). The case `0 < p` runs the existing proof unchanged, using `resonantDisturbingFunction_add_period` and `resonantCandidateCorrection_periodic` with the recovered `hp`. The rest of the statement does not mention `hp`, so it can be deleted. I am not claiming the eccentricity bounds can be dropped: `eccentricAnomaly` is an `invFun` whose periodicity relies on strict monotonicity, which needs `0 ≤ e < 1`.

### [`EulerH6Nonlinear.sobolev_add_le`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/H6NonlinearProduct.lean#L94-L114) — `lean-pool-0615`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0615.lean`

- `(hgL2 : ∀ j ≤ q, ∀ w : Fin j → Fin 4, MemLp (iteratedFieldDerivative period w g) 2 (liftMeasure period))` (theorem): `liftSobolevNorm` is a sum of `(eLpNorm (iteratedFieldDerivative period w ·) 2 μ).toReal` terms, so it is enough to prove the inequality term by term. Write Df, Dg for the iterated derivatives. By `word_add` (which needs only hf and hg), D(f+g) = Df + Dg. hfL2 gives eLpNorm Df < ⊤. Case eLpNorm Dg < ⊤: the existing proof goes through unchanged: `ENNReal.toReal_mono` applied to `eLpNorm_add_le`, then `ENNReal.toReal_add`. Case eLpNorm Dg = ⊤: write Dg = (Df+Dg) + (-Df). Then `eLpNorm_add_le` with p = 2 ≥ 1 and `eLpNorm_neg` give ⊤ = eLpNorm Dg ≤ eLpNorm(Df+Dg) + eLpNorm Df. Since eLpNorm Df is finite, eLpNorm(Df+Dg) = ⊤. Its toReal is 0, which is ≤ the right-hand side because both summands are `toReal`s and hence ≥ 0. In current Mathlib, `eLpNorm_add_le` and `eLpNorm_neg` need no measurability assumption: `eLpNorm` is defined to be ∞ for non-AEStronglyMeasurable functions. The library's proof already calls `eLpNorm_add_le` with no measurability arguments. In any case, all the derivatives here are continuous by `iteratedFieldDerivative_smooth` and `smoothField_continuous`, so they are measurable. By symmetry, `hfL2` can be deleted instead, keeping `hgL2`. The two cannot both be deleted: if Df and Dg both have infinite L² norm while Df + Dg has finite nonzero norm, the left side is positive and the right side is 0.
- `(hfL2 : ∀ j ≤ q, ∀ w : Fin j → Fin 4, MemLp (iteratedFieldDerivative period w f) 2 (liftMeasure period))` (theorem): This is the same argument as for hgL2 with f and g swapped. Keep hgL2, so eLpNorm Dg < ⊤. If eLpNorm Df < ⊤, the original proof applies. If eLpNorm Df = ⊤, then from Df = (Df+Dg) + (-Dg), `eLpNorm_add_le` and `eLpNorm_neg` give eLpNorm(Df+Dg) = ⊤. Its toReal is 0, which is ≤ the nonnegative right-hand side. This hypothesis can be deleted only as an alternative to deleting hgL2, not together with it (same counterexample as above).

### [`HahnSeries.Germ.LimitOrdinalRelationAtCutoff.α_constantCoeff`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ConwayRefinement/ConwayRefinement/HahnSeries/Germ/AlgebraicIndependence/PartialDerivativeIndices.lean#L100-L106) — `lean-pool-0631`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0631.lean`

- `(hx : OrdinalGraded.IsMinimalSystem (DirectSum.rangeLof K (cantorBendixsonDegreeValuation (G := G) (R := K)).Component) wt xg)` (variable): The proof uses hx only through `S.β_ne_zero hx`, and that lemma uses hx only to get `hx.ne_zero S.B₀ : wt S.B₀ ≠ 0`. `S.β ≠ 0` follows from the fields of S alone. Suppose S.β = 0. Rewriting `S.term_lt` with `NatOrdinal.wpow_zero` gives `leastTerm (wt S.B₀) < 1`. By the contrapositive of `NatOrdinal.one_le_leastTerm` (a ≠ 0 → 1 ≤ leastTerm a), wt S.B₀ = 0. Then `S.max` gives wt i = 0 for every i ∈ S.F.vars. `S.ne_zero` gives a monomial d with coeff d S.F ≠ 0, and every i ∈ d.support is in S.F.vars (mem_vars). So `Finsupp.weight wt d = ∑ d i • wt i = 0`, and by `S.hom` this weight is α, so α = 0. This contradicts `S.vars_lt S.B₀ S.mem : wt S.B₀ < α` (0 < 0). With this argument in place of `S.β_ne_zero hx`, the rest of the proof (constantCoeff_add, constantCoeff_nsmul, S.vars_limit, partGE_eq_self_of_forall_le S.degHD_terms, constantCoeff_partGE) is unchanged and does not use hx. hx does not appear in the statement. It can be removed with `omit hx in`, as the file already does for the next theorem, `cdeg_ne_zero`.

### [`MarkoffRiemannRoch.FunctionField.Chart.finiteAdeleFiltDiff_quotient_add_eq`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MarkoffModP/RiemannRoch/AdeleSpace/FilterChain.lean#L623-L627) — `lean-pool-0640`

- `(hN : N = M + (N - M))` (theorem): `DivisorA k K` is defined as `PlaceA k K →₀ ℤ` (an abbrev in RiemannRoch/Place.lean), which is an additive commutative group. So `N = M + (N - M)` holds for every `M` and `N`: it is `(add_sub_cancel M N).symm`, where Mathlib's `add_sub_cancel (a b : G) : a + (b - a) = b` is the additive version (via to_additive) of `mul_div_cancel` in Mathlib/Algebra/Group/Basic.lean. Without `hN`, the proof becomes `(add_sub_cancel M N) ▸ finiteAdeleFiltDiff_quotient_add_effective k K M (N - M) hE`, or the same with `rw [← add_sub_cancel M N]` or `simpa using ...`. The hypothesis never excludes any case, so the statement stays true.

### [`NavierStokes.CandidateConsequences.futureJet_eq_full_of_pos`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/GermCandidateAssembly.lean#L787-L791) — `lean-pool-0644`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0644.lean`

- `(hf : ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ f futureDomain)` (theorem): `futureJet f m` is defined as `iteratedFDerivWithin ℝ m f futureDomain`, where `futureDomain = Ici 0 ×ˢ univ`. When `0 < t`, `futureDomain` is a neighbourhood of `(t, x)`; the existing proof already shows this with `prod_mem_nhds (Ici_mem_nhds ht) univ_mem`. Iterated derivatives within a set depend only on the germ of that set at the point. Mathlib states this as `iteratedFDerivWithin_congr_set (h : s =ᶠ[𝓝 x] t) (n : ℕ) : iteratedFDerivWithin 𝕜 n f s x = iteratedFDerivWithin 𝕜 n f t x`, and it needs no differentiability of `f`. So a proof without `hf` is: `Filter.eventuallyEqSet_univ.mpr` (older name `eventuallyEq_univ`) turns the neighbourhood fact into `futureDomain =ᶠ[𝓝 (t,x)] univ`. Then `iteratedFDerivWithin_congr_set` with that and `m` gives `futureJet f m (t,x) = iteratedFDerivWithin ℝ m f univ (t,x)`, and `iteratedFDerivWithin_univ` turns the right-hand side into `iteratedFDeriv ℝ m f (t,x)`. Mathlib's own `iteratedFDerivWithin_of_isOpen` is proved the same way. No degenerate case is a problem: the identity holds for every `f`, because both sides are defined for every function, using junk values (zero derivatives) where `f` is not differentiable. `hf` is not mentioned anywhere else in the statement.

### [`Bananas.int_eq_of_emod_sub_eq_zero_of_fundamental`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/BrillNoetherGraphs/Bananas/Transmission/ExactTorsionAPI.lean#L201-L220) — `lean-pool-0648`

To delete it, add `have hk : 0 < k := by omega` (or `lt_of_le_of_lt hb0 hb`) at the start of the proof. The rest of the proof, which uses `hk` only in `abs_of_pos hk`, then works unchanged. None of the other hypotheses can be dropped. Without `hb0`: take b = c - k with c = 0, so b - c = -k, `(-k) % k = 0`, and b ≠ c. Without `hb`: take b = c + k. The cases without `hc0` or `hc` are symmetric.

### [`MetricCodes.Hamming.binaryRate_le_of_positive_feasible_bound`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MetricCodes/Foundations.lean#L6675-L6682) — `lean-pool-0650`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0650.lean`

- `(hb : 0 < b)` (theorem): `Feasible δ a b` (line 5115) unfolds to `0 ≤ b ∧ b < a ∧ a ≤ 1/2 ∧ 1 - 2δ < hammingGamma a b`. So the hypothesis `h` already gives `0 ≤ b`, and the only case `hb` rules out is `b = 0`. The proof splits on `h.1.eq_or_lt`.

**Case `0 < b`:** the current proof works unchanged.

**Case `b = 0`:**
- `transverseDegree 0 n = ⌊0 * n⌋₊ = 0`.
- `hammingFibreDimension n 0 = booleanHarmonicDimension n 0 = 1`, by the definition at line 162 and the abbrev at line 188.
- Hence `windowFibreQuotient a 0 n = (ambientDimension n 0 (longitudinalDegree a n) : ℝ) / 1`.
- The right-hand side of the conclusion becomes `binaryEntropy a - 0`, using `binaryEntropy_zero` (line 110).
- `tendsto_logb_ambientDimension` (line 6218) needs only `0 ≤ b`, `b < a` and `a ≤ 1/2`, all supplied by `h`. It gives `logb 2 (windowFibreQuotient a 0 n) / n → binaryEntropy a`.
- To add the constant `C = spectralPrefactor δ a b`, follow the proof of `tendsto_logb_const_mul_windowFibreQuotient`: `logb 2 (C * q) / n = logb 2 C / n + logb 2 q / n`. This needs `C > 0`, which is `spectralPrefactor_pos h`, and `q > 0`. Here `q` is the ambient dimension. It is at least `n.choose (longitudinalDegree a n)` by `choose_le_ambientDimension` (needs `0 ≤ longitudinalDegree a n`, which is trivial), and that binomial is positive by `longitudinalDegree_le_dimension`.
- With this limit, the rest of the proof of `binaryRate_le_of_eventually_windowFibreQuotient` goes through unchanged: `binaryRate_le_of_eventually`, plus `codeNumber_pos` and monotonicity of `logb`.

So the statement is true and provable without `hb`; only the `b = 0` branch needs this extra argument.

### [`BGS.Markoff.weightedSplitTraceLaurent_smooth_of_partialLocalizations`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MarkoffModP/BGS/Markoff/TraceCurve/LaurentSmooth.lean#L385-L424) — `lean-pool-0653`

That lemma needs no hypotheses because the chart has a submersive presentation whose Jacobian is a unit after localization. `WeightedSplitTracePartialXLocalization` is an `abbrev` for `Localization.Away (weightedSplitTraceLaurentPartialX alpha beta d e)` (line 140), so its type is the type of hX. To delete hX, add `have hX := weightedSplitTracePartialXLocalization_smooth alpha beta d e` at the start of the proof; nothing else changes. It has no hypotheses on the parameters and is proved through a submersive presentation. `WeightedSplitTracePartialYLocalization` is an `abbrev` for `Localization.Away (weightedSplitTraceLaurentPartialY alpha beta d e)` (line 290), so its type is the type of hY. To delete hY, add `have hY := weightedSplitTracePartialYLocalization_smooth alpha beta d e` at the start of the proof; the rest of the proof is unchanged. hspan, by contrast, depends on nondegeneracy conditions on alpha, beta, d, e and the characteristic of K, so it cannot be removed. hY can be deleted on its own, and also together with hX.

### [`QuadraticIterates.section1_equiv`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticIterates/ArchMath1992/Main.lean#L191-L199) — `lean-pool-0667`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0667.lean`

- `(ha : ¬IsSquare (-a : ℚ))` (theorem): The TFAE holds without `ha`. Split on whether `-a` is a square in ℚ.

**Case 1: `-a` is not a square.** The current proof applies (`section1_a_iff_b`, `section1_b_iff_c`).

**Case 2: `-a` is a square, with n = 0.** Statements 2 and 3 are vacuously true, since `TwoIndependent` over `Fin 0` is empty. Statement 1 holds by the `zero` case of `section1_a_iff_b`: `nonempty_mulEquiv_of_finrank_eq` plus `splittingField_zero_eq_bot`. That step does not use `ha`.

**Case 2: `-a` is a square, with n ≥ 1.** All three statements are false.
- Statement 2: `cSeq_one` gives `cSeq a 1 = -a`. Taking S = {0}, the product is `-a`, a square, so `TwoIndependent` fails.
- Statement 3: likewise, `bSeq_one` gives `bSeq a 1 = -a`.
- Statement 1: `embed_equiv a k` (Iterates.lean, no `ha` hypothesis) says that an iso at level k+1 holds iff the iso at level k holds and `relfinrank K_k K_{k+1} = 2^2^k`. By downward induction, an iso at level n ≥ 1 gives one at level 1. Then `relfinrank K_0 K_1 = 2` must hold. But K_0 = ⊥ by `splittingField_zero_eq_bot`, so this relfinrank equals `finrank ℚ K_1`. Write a = -q² with q ∈ ℚ. Then f_1 = X² - q² has roots ±q ∈ ℚ, so K_1 = ⊥ and its finrank is 1 ≠ 2. So there is no iso.

In every case the three statements are equivalent, so the theorem stays true and provable without `ha`.

### [`ESS.uc_neighborhood_boxes_subset`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/EscauriazaSereginSverak/Linear/UCNeighborhoodBoxes.lean#L27-L83) — `lean-pool-0672`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0672.lean`

- `(hL : 0 < L)` (theorem): This follows from `hr : 0 < r` and `hrL : r < L / 4`, since together they give `0 < L / 4`, so `0 < L`. The proof uses `hL` only in `hrL' : r < L := by linarith only [hrL, hL]`. Changing that line to `linarith only [hrL, hr]` makes `hL` unnecessary. The other hypotheses can stay as they are.
- `(hS : 0 < S)` (theorem): This follows from `hr` and `hrS : r < Real.sqrt (S / 2)`, since they give `0 < Real.sqrt (S / 2)`. `Real.sqrt_pos` then gives `0 < S / 2`, hence `0 < S`. The proof uses `hS` only to get `hS2 : 0 ≤ S / 2`, and that also follows from `(Real.sqrt_pos.mp (hr.trans hrS)).le`. The rest of the proof does not change.
- `(hr : 0 < r)` (theorem): The definition is `vec3Ball x r = {y | vec3EuclideanNorm (y - x) < r}`, and the library has the lemma `vec3EuclideanNorm_nonneg`. So if `r ≤ 0`, both `vec3Ball x r` and `vec3Ball x (2 * r)` are empty. With `spaceTimeSet Ω I = Ω ×ˢ I`, the first two conjuncts then say an empty set is a subset of `U`, which holds trivially. The third conjunct quantifies over `c ∈ vec3Ball x (2 * r)`, which is empty, so it holds vacuously. If `0 < r`, the existing proof works unchanged. So the proof starts by splitting on `0 < r`: the case `r ≤ 0` is closed with the emptiness argument and `vec3EuclideanNorm_nonneg`, and the case `0 < r` uses the current argument. Each of `hL`, `hS` and `hr` can be deleted on its own. Deleting `hr` together with `hL` or `hS` would need more care: for example, the case `r > 0` needs `hr` to recover `hL` and `hS`.

### [`Utilities.Certificate.SubdivisionGraph.Spec.regularSubdivisionGonality_graph`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/BrillNoetherGraphs/Tricycle/RegularSubdivisionBridge.lean#L165-L179) — `lean-pool-0684`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0684.lean`

- `(hlen : ∀ e : Fin p, spec.length e = 1)` (theorem): **Why the statement holds without `hlen`.** Both sides are an infimum over `k > 0` of divisorial gonalities. The CFGraph-level side, `Utilities.Gonality.regularSubdivisionGonality spec.graph`, uses `regularSubdivision spec.graph k hk = ((UnitSubdivisionPresentation.spec spec.graph).scale k hk).graph`. In that graph, each edge occurrence of `spec.graph` becomes a path of `k` unit steps. The Spec-level side, `spec.regularSubdivisionGonality`, uses `(spec.scale k hk).graph`. There, each core edge `e` becomes a path of `k * spec.length e` unit steps.

In `spec.graph`, a core edge `e` is already a path of `spec.length e` unit edges (`stepLeft`/`stepRight`, SubdivisionGraph.lean). Splitting each of those into `k` steps gives a path of `k * spec.length e` steps from tail to head. So for every `k` the two graphs are isomorphic:
- the vertex counts match: `n + Σ(L_e - 1) + Σ L_e (k - 1) = n + Σ(k L_e - 1)`;
- the unit steps biject: `Σ_e Fin (k L_e) ≃ Σ_{s} Fin k`, because the slots `s` of the unit presentation correspond to the steps of `spec`;
- the bijection respects endpoints, and orientation does not matter because `numEdges` counts `(x,y)` and `(y,x)` alike.

Hence there is a `LaplacianEquiv`, and `divisorialGonality_of_laplacianEquiv` gives `divisorialGonality (regularSubdivision spec.graph k hk) = divisorialGonality (spec.scale k hk).graph` for every `k`. The two `sInf` sets are then equal, exactly as in the current proof. Degenerate cases cause no trouble: `p = 0` gives no edges on either side, and `n > 0` holds by `core_nonempty`.

**How the proof changes.** Only the step that supplies the `LaplacianEquiv` changes. It is currently `scaleLaplacianEquiv`, built from a `Spec.Relabeling`, which needs equal slot counts and so needs `hlen`. The library already has `OneEdgeSplitRefinement.laplacianEquivOfUnorientedUnitSteps` (used in SubdivisionIso.lean). It produces a `LaplacianEquiv` between two Spec graphs from a vertex equivalence, a step equivalence, and compatibility of `unitEdge` up to orientation. The equivalences above provide those inputs. They take bookkeeping to define, but they are elementary. The rest of the proof (`congr 1; ext d; …`) stays as it is.

`hlen` is not needed to elaborate the statement: it appears nowhere else in the binders or the conclusion.

### [`Nibble.LEUnif.sum_col_two`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AsymptoticTrianglePacking/Internal/WeightedBoundedEdges.lean#L2238-L2250) — `lean-pool-0690`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0690.lean`

- `(hm : 0 < m)` (theorem): The proof uses `hm` only to get `0 < (m:ℝ)`, which it passes to the local lemma `mul_pow_sub_two_div`. The first rewrite uses `SlackR.sum_ite_at2` (line 1541). That lemma has no hypothesis on `m`, and it turns the left side into `x / m^d * m^(d-2)`. The proof already shows `2 ≤ d` from `hj : j₀ ≠ j₁` without using `hm`. So the remaining goal is `x * m^(d-2) / m^d = x / m^2`. Case split on `m`. If `0 < m`, the original `mul_pow_sub_two_div` step applies. If `m = 0`, then `(0:ℝ)^d = 0` because `d ≥ 2` (`zero_pow`). The left side is then `x * _ / 0 = 0` by `div_zero`, and the right side is `x / 0^2 = x / 0 = 0`. Directly: when `m = 0`, `Fin d` is nonempty and `Fin 0` is empty, so there are no functions `Fin d → Fin 0`. The sum is empty and equals 0, the same as the right side, which is 0 by Lean's convention that `x / 0 = 0`. So the statement holds without `hm`.

### [`TauCeti.DavisKahan1970.corollary3_1_prescribedAngleSequence_classification_real`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/DavisKahan/DavisKahan/Sources/DavisKahan1970/Section3Corollary31.lean#L773-L805) — `lean-pool-0716`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0716.lean`

- `(hlim : Filter.Tendsto θ Filter.atTop (nhds 0))` (theorem): The proof uses `hlim` in only one place: it supplies `hcompact₁` (compactness of the source pair's defect block) through `isCompactOperator_angleSequenceDefectBlock hlim`. The other library lemma used, `compactAngleEigenvalueList_genericCosineBlock_angleSequenceDatum`, needs only hθ0, hθ2 and hanti. Without `hlim`, prove each direction separately.

(→) The pair equivalence gives a linear isometric equivalence e with sourceSubspace.map e = U₂ and targetSubspace.map e = V₂. Conjugating by e intertwines the orthogonal projections, so the source defect block equals e⁻¹ ∘ (U₂ P_{V₂ᗮ} U₂) ∘ e. That operator is compact by `hcompact₂`, which gives `hcompact₁`. Then `corollary3_1_compact_defectBlock_angleList_classification_real` together with the angle-list lemma (which does not need `hlim`) yields the right-hand side.

(←) From the right-hand side, the approximation numbers of `genericCosineBlock U₂ V₂ᗮ` are sin²(θ n). That block is compact: P_{V₂ᗮ} = id − P_{V₂} by `Submodule.starProjection_orthogonal'`, as in the proof of `corollary3_1_compact_defectBlock_angleList_classification`, and then `isCompactOperator_genericCosineBlock` applies. So `ContinuousLinearMap.tendsto_approximationNumber_atTop_nhds_zero_of_isCompactOperator` (no completeness needed) gives sin²(θ n) → 0. Since 0 < θ n < π/2, sin(θ n) = √(sin²(θ n)) → 0, and θ n = arcsin(sin(θ n)) → arcsin 0 = 0. That recovers `hlim`, and the original proof finishes.

The degenerate case where θ decreases to some L > 0 is the only one where `hlim` fails; the argument above shows both sides of the `↔` are then false, so the equivalence still holds.

### [`NavierStokes.PhysicalResidualJetBounds.clm_apply_jet_bound`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/ActualPolarCoverage.lean#L273-L297) — `lean-pool-0733`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0733.lean`

- `(hA : 0 ≤ A)` (theorem): hA follows from hfa: take i = 0, which satisfies 0 ≤ m for any m : ℕ. Then 0 ≤ ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ 0 f x‖ ≤ A, so A ≥ 0. To prove the theorem without hA, add `have hA : 0 ≤ A := (norm_nonneg _).trans (hfa 0 (Nat.zero_le m))` and leave the rest of the proof unchanged. hB can be removed in the same way, so deleting both together also works.
- `(hB : 0 ≤ B)` (theorem): hB follows from hgb: take i = 0, which satisfies 0 ≤ m for any m : ℕ. Then 0 ≤ ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ 0 g x‖ ≤ B, so B ≥ 0. To prove the theorem without hB, add `have hB : 0 ≤ B := (norm_nonneg _).trans (hgb 0 (Nat.zero_le m))` and leave the rest of the proof unchanged. This does not depend on whether hA is kept, so deleting both together also works.

### [`NavierStokes.ShapeTransition.vanishingDebtBound_tendsto`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/ShapeTransition.lean#L1440-L1450) — `lean-pool-0740`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0740.lean`

- `(hP : P ≠ 0)` (theorem): `separation T C P := Real.exp T / (C * P) ^ 10`. At P = 0 the denominator is 0^10 = 0, so by the convention x / 0 = 0, `separation T C 0 = 0` for every C. Also 0 ^ (1/5 : ℝ) = 0 (Real.zero_rpow, since 1/5 ≠ 0). So `vanishingDebtBound n B K L BG KA (separation T C 0) C` reduces to (2^n*L*K^2)/C^2, which tends to 0. For the proof: `prefix_bound_tendsto` has no hypothesis on P and already handles P = 0 with a `by_cases`. hP is only used to get `separation_tendsto` and `separation_fifth_tendsto`. Split with `by_cases hP : P = 0`. If P = 0, both functions are constantly 0 (simp with separation, mul_zero, zero_pow, div_zero, Real.zero_rpow), so tendsto_const_nhds gives the limit, exactly as in the proof of `prefix_bound_tendsto`. Otherwise use the existing lemmas. The rest of the proof (convert!/ring) is unchanged.

### [`Hadamard.OrderOne.ncard_zeros_le_rpow`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/LiCriterion/Hadamard/OrderOne/TailEstimates.lean#L80-L152) — `lean-pool-0745`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0745.lean`

- `(hf_entire : Differentiable ℂ f)` (theorem): `hasFiniteOrder f` is defined (LeanPool/LiCriterion/Hadamard/Basic.lean:318) as `Differentiable ℂ f ∧ ∃ ρ₀ R₀, …`, so `hf_finite.1 : Differentiable ℂ f`. The proof passes `hf_finite.1` to `ncard_zeros_le_of_order_le_one` in place of `hf_entire`; nothing else changes.
- `(h_simple : ∀ ρ : Z.Zero, deriv f (Z.z ρ) ≠ 0)` (theorem): It is only used inside `ncard_zeros_le_of_order_le_one`, via `mem_divisor_support_of_simple_zero`, to show that each zero u with ‖u‖ ≤ r lies in the support of the divisor of f on closedBall 0 |2r|. That holds without simplicity. First, f 0 ≠ 0: otherwise `h_zeros_only` gives ρ with Z.z ρ = 0, contradicting `h_z_ne_zero`; the existing proof derives this without h_simple. So analyticOrderAt f 0 = 0 ≠ ⊤. Since f is entire and ℂ is preconnected, Mathlib's `AnalyticOnNhd.analyticOrderAt_ne_top_of_isPreconnected` gives analyticOrderAt f u ≠ ⊤. Since f u = 0, that order is also nonzero, so meromorphicOrderAt f u is a positive integer (`AnalyticAt.meromorphicOrderAt_eq`). Then `MeromorphicOn.divisor_apply` shows the divisor at u is nonzero, so u is in its support. The rest of the proof (Jensen bound `card_zeros_le_of_max_one_maxModulus`, the injectivity count, the growth bound, the final algebra) never uses h_simple. Degenerate cases: f ≡ 0 is excluded by f 0 ≠ 0; if Z.Zero is empty the count is 0 ≤ C·r^(1+ε) since C ≥ 0. Independent of the hf_entire deletion.

### [`EulerWeightedRootLimit.regularized_integral_tendsto`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/WeightedRootLimit.lean#L35-L75) — `lean-pool-0749`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0749.lean`

- `(hQ0 : ∀ u ∈ Icc s t, 0 ≤ Q u)` (theorem): The proof uses hQ0 in only one place: inside `hbound`, to apply `regularized_root_le` (which needs `0 ≤ q`) and get √(Q u + c²) ≤ √(Q u) + c, where c = cutoffScale n. This inequality holds for every real q, because Mathlib's `Real.sqrt` returns 0 on negative numbers. If q < 0, then q + c² ≤ c², so by `Real.sqrt_le_sqrt` and `Real.sqrt_sq` (c ≥ 0) we get √(q + c²) ≤ √(c²) = c = √q + c, since √q = 0 (`Real.sqrt_eq_zero'`). Alternatively, apply `regularized_root_le` to max q 0 and use monotonicity of √ together with √(max q 0) = √q. So the dominating bound ‖A u‖·(√(Q u) + 1) + ‖F u‖ still holds without hQ0. Nothing else in the proof uses hQ0. The integrability of A·√Q comes from `hA.mul_continuousOn hQ.sqrt` on the compact set Icc, and `ContinuousOn.sqrt` needs no sign condition. The pointwise limit `regularized_root_tendsto (Q u)` takes no hypothesis on q. Dominated convergence therefore goes through exactly as before, so the statement remains true and provable without hQ0.

### [`AVenhance.Infra.Section4.amnrFlowSecondEval_continuousOn_forward`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AnomalousDiffusion/AVenhance/Infra/Section4/Amnr/FlowSecondContinuity.lean#L28-L42) — `lean-pool-0751`

- `(hsT : s ≤ T)` (theorem): The conclusion is `ContinuousOn (amnrFlowSecondEval X s h k) (Icc s T ×ˢ univ)` for a function on ℝ × Vec 2. If ¬ s ≤ T, then `Icc s T = ∅` (Mathlib `Set.Icc_eq_empty : ¬a ≤ b → Icc a b = ∅`), so `Icc s T ×ˢ univ = ∅` (`Set.empty_prod`), and `continuousOn_empty` closes the goal. Proof without the hypothesis: `by_cases hsT : s ≤ T`. Use the existing proof in the first branch. In the second branch, `rw [Set.Icc_eq_empty hsT, Set.empty_prod]; exact continuousOn_empty _`. Nothing else in the statement mentions hsT.

### [`Nibble.YusterE.triangleSub_linearSized_data_of_minDeg`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AsymptoticTrianglePacking/Internal/AX1/CoreGapAX1.lean#L2421-L2433) — `lean-pool-0755`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0755.lean`

- `(h2D : Fintype.card V ≤ 2 * D)` (theorem): `h2D` is used only inside `triangleSub_degree_window`, to rewrite the cast of the truncated subtraction `2 * D - Fintype.card V` (via `Nat.cast_sub h2D`). The lemma `triangleSub_degree_ge_of_minDeg G E hD` does not need it: it gives `2 * D - card V ≤ degree E` in ℕ. From this, `2 * (D:ℝ) - card V ≤ degree E` holds in ℝ in every case. If `card V ≤ 2D`, cast as before. If not, the real left side is negative and the degree is at least 0. So the lower window `(1 - μ) * d ≤ 2D - n ≤ degree E` still follows from `hlo`. The upper bound uses only `triangleSub_degree_le_card` and `hhi`. With the window established without `h2D`, `triangleSub_linearSized_data_of_window` gives the conclusion unchanged.
- `(hcodeg : 1 ≤ μ * d)` (theorem): `hcodeg` follows from `hD`, `hlo` and `hhi`. `CodegreeBounded (triangleHypergraphSub G) (μ*d)` quantifies over pairs of distinct `x y : EdgeV G`. If `EdgeV G` is empty (no edges, which covers empty `V`), it holds vacuously. Otherwise `V` has a vertex `x`, and Mathlib's `SimpleGraph.degree_lt_card_verts` gives `D ≤ G.degree x < card V`, so `D ≤ n - 1` with `n = card V`. Subtracting `hlo : (1-μ)d ≤ 2D - n` from `hhi : n ≤ (1+μ)d` gives `2μd ≥ 2n - 2D ≥ 2`, so `1 ≤ μ * d`. Then `triangleHypergraphSub_codegreeBounded G` (codegree ≤ 1) gives the second conjunct, and the rest of the proof is unchanged.

### [`BGS.HasseWeil.natCard_aut_exactConstantExtension_over_frobeniusTwistField`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MarkoffModP/BGS/HasseWeil/ExactConstantExtensionFrobeniusTwist.lean#L336-L359) — `lean-pool-0757`

After `omit [FiniteDimensional C S] in`, every place that needs this instance gets it by instance search: the elaboration of `exactConstantExtensionField C N S hExact` in the statement, and the helper lemmas called in the proof (`finiteDimensional_exactConstantExtension_over_base`, `isGalois_exactConstantExtension_over_frobeniusTwistField`, `finrank_exactConstantExtension_over_frobeniusTwistField`). Mathlib/FieldTheory/Finite/GaloisField.lean:179 has `instance (priority := 100) {K K' : Type*} [Field K] [Field K'] [Finite K'] [Algebra K K'] : IsGalois K K'`, and that file is imported transitively through `Mathlib.FieldTheory.Finite.Extension`, which `ConstantFieldAutomorphism.lean` imports.
