### [`ViscositySolns.PerronMethodExistenceTheorem.strictBoundary_of_bentSourceOuterClosedBallGeometricSemijetLscSelectionPatch`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ViscositySolutionTheory/ViscositySolns/Existence/Perron/Method/StrictBoundary/EnvelopeAssembly.lean#L337-L368) — `lean-pool-0769`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0769.lean`

- `[LocallyCompactSpace C]` (theorem): The instance follows from `hCopen : IsOpen C`. `Point n` is an `abbrev` for `Fin n -> Real` (Foundation.lean:28), so it carries the product topology. Mathlib has the instance `LocallyCompactSpace (Fin n → ℝ)`: a finite product of locally compact spaces is locally compact. Mathlib's `IsOpen.locallyCompactSpace` (Topology/Compactness/LocallyCompact.lean:224) takes `[LocallyCompactSpace X]` and `(hs : IsOpen s)` and returns `LocallyCompactSpace s`. So `hCopen.locallyCompactSpace` gives `LocallyCompactSpace C`. The rest of the statement does not need the instance to elaborate. These definitions take only sets, functions and operators and have no instance argument: `DirichletBarrierPair`, `DirichletComparisonPrinciple`, `BoundaryLowerTraceOn`, `BoundaryUpperTraceOn`, `PerronLowerEnvelopeBentQuadraticSourceOuterClosedBallGeometricSemijetLscSelectionPatch`, `DirichletSolutionOn`, `upperEnvelope` and `perronEnvelope`. Fix to the proof: start with `haveI : LocallyCompactSpace C := hCopen.locallyCompactSpace`, then make the same call to `of_perronFamily_top_and_bentSourceOuterClosedBallGeometricSemijetLscSelectionPatch` with the same arguments. The theorem therefore stays true and provable without the binder.

### [`TauCeti.DavisKahan1970.approximationSingularValue_complexifyTrialData_residual`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/DavisKahan/DavisKahan/Sources/DavisKahan1970/DirectedUnboundedReal.lean#L160-L177) — `lean-pool-0772`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0772.lean`

- `[CompleteSpace E]` (variable): **The statement does not need it to elaborate.** `complexifyTrialData`, the `HasOrthogonalProjection` instance on `complexifySubmodule Z` used by `Theorem63TrialData (complexifySubmodule Z) (complexifySubmodule V)`, and `approximationSingularValue` (defined as `K.approximationNumber n`, an infimum over operators of rank ≤ n) all work without completeness. Evidence: `complexifyTrialData_residual_apply` and `complexifyTrialData_action_apply`, a few lines above in the same file, are stated with `omit [CompleteSpace E] in`, mention `complexifyTrialData data`, and compile.

**The current proof does use it.** The proof goes through `approximationSingularValue_complexify` → `approximationNumber_le_complexify` → `exists_linearIndependent_lowerBound_of_lt_approximationNumber_real`. That last lemma uses `T.adjoint` and the continuous functional calculus, so it needs both spaces complete.

**The statement is still true for an incomplete E, by a completion argument.**
1. Let X be a normed space densely and isometrically embedded in a complete space X̂, likewise Y in Ŷ, and let T̂ be the continuous extension of T. Then a_n(T) = a_n(T̂).
   - a_n(T̂) ≤ a_n(T): any R : X → Y of rank ≤ n extends to R̂ of rank ≤ n with ‖T̂ − R̂‖ = ‖T − R‖, by density.
   - a_n(T) ≤ a_n(T̂): take R̂ of rank k ≤ n and write R̂ restricted to X as Σ φ_i ⊗ y_i, with y_i a basis of its range in Ŷ and φ_i continuous functionals on X. Replace each y_i by some y_i' ∈ Y with ‖y_i − y_i'‖ < δ. The new operator has rank ≤ k, and its distance to T is at most ‖T̂ − R̂‖ + δ Σ‖φ_i‖.
2. Apply step 1 twice:
   - to the real residual Z → E, using the real Hilbert completions Ẑ and Ê (Mathlib has an `InnerProductSpace` instance on `UniformSpace.Completion`);
   - to its complexification, which sits densely and isometrically inside the complexification of the completed residual Ẑ → Ê.
   The library's lemma `approximationSingularValue_complexify` applies to the completed residual, and its conclusion transfers back through both equalities.
3. The coordinate change through `complexifySubmoduleEquiv` is handled by `SameApproximationSingularValues.of_isometricEquiv_comp`, as in the current proof. I did not check that lemma's own hypotheses; even if it needed completeness, a_n is unchanged by composing with isometric equivalences, by the definition as an infimum.

**Degenerate cases.** If Z = 0 or E = 0, every operator involved has norm 0, so both sides are 0. The junk value of the infimum is the same on both sides.

The new proof is much longer and needs the completion machinery, but this assumption is not needed for the result.

### [`Nivat.Dynamics.eventually_norm_add_lt_of_normal_tendsto`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Nivat/Dynamics/HalfPlanePair.lean#L167-L190) — `lean-pool-0774`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0774.lean`

- `(hp : ∀ n, p n ≠ 0)` (theorem): The proof uses `hp` only once, to get `hr0 : 0 < ‖p n‖` for the `n` produced by `filter_upwards`. The conclusion is an eventual statement, so positivity of `‖p n‖` is only needed eventually. That already follows from `hr : Tendsto (fun n => ‖p n‖) atTop atTop`: `hr.eventually_gt_atTop 0` gives `∀ᶠ n in atTop, 0 < ‖p n‖`. The repaired proof is `filter_upwards [he, hr.eventually_gt_atTop 0] with n hn hr0`; every other step stays as it is. Finitely many indices with `p n = 0` cause no trouble: there `‖p n‖⁻¹ • p n = 0`, and the limit `ν` and the steps `hinv`, `hinner`, `hg` and `he` never use `p n ≠ 0`. Degenerate case: if every `p n` were `0`, then `hr` would fail because the norms do not tend to infinity, so the statement is vacuous there too.

### [`MarkovProcess.ContinuousPath.coordinate_exitTime_mem_frontier`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MarkovProcess/MarkovProcess/Path/ExitTime.lean#L218-L252) — `lean-pool-0777`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0777.lean`

- `(hU : IsOpen U)` (theorem): `exitTime U omega` is defined as `sInf {s : ℝ≥0∞ | ∃ t : ℝ≥0, s = t ∧ omega t ∉ U}`. The proof uses `hU` in two places: through `exitTime_le_iff_mem_hitsSetBy` to show `omega tau ∉ U`, and through `hU.frontier_eq` at the end. Without openness, `omega tau` may lie in `U`, but the conclusion still holds. Use `frontier_eq_closure_inter_closure : frontier s = closure s ∩ closure sᶜ` (Mathlib, Topology/Closure.lean) and prove the two memberships separately. (1) `omega tau ∈ closure U`: `mem_of_lt_exitTime` does not need `hU` and gives `omega s ∈ U` for every `s < tau`. If `tau = 0`, then `omega 0 ∈ U` by `h0`. If `tau > 0`, the left-limit argument already in the proof works unchanged. (2) `omega tau ∈ closure Uᶜ`: `hfin` makes the exit time finite, so for every ε > 0, `sInf_lt_iff` (Order/CompleteLattice/Defs.lean, valid in the complete linear order ℝ≥0∞) gives some `t` with `omega t ∉ U` and `tau ≤ t < tau + ε`. Here `tau ≤ t` follows from `sInf_le`. These times converge to `tau`, so by continuity of `omega`, `omega tau` is a limit of points of `Uᶜ` and lies in `closure Uᶜ`. Degenerate cases: `tau = 0` is covered by `h0` together with step (2). The case `exitTime = ⊤` is excluded by `hfin`, which is still needed (for `U = univ` the frontier is empty). `h0` is also still needed (take a constant path outside `closure U`). So the statement stays true and provable without `hU`.

### [`LeanEval.Topology.ClassificationOfSurfaces.Moise.PolygonalCircle.ProperChord.chord_inter_firstEdge`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ClassificationOfSurfaces/Moise/PolygonalCrosscut.lean#L1234-L1266) — `lean-pool-0785`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0785.lean`

- `(hk2 : 2 ≤ k)` (theorem): The proof uses `hk2` only to show `hQnot : C.Q ∉ J.edgeSegment 0`. That fact follows from the chord's properness alone. Suppose `C.Q ∈ J.edgeSegment 0`. `J.edgeSegment 0` is by definition `segment ℝ (J.vertex 0) (J.vertex 1)`, so it is convex (`convex_segment`). It contains `C.P`, by `hP` and `left_mem_segment`. So `Convex.segment_subset` gives `segment ℝ C.P C.Q ⊆ J.edgeSegment 0`, and this edge is contained in `J.carrier` (`edgeSegment_subset_carrier`). Then `C.segment_inter_carrier` turns this into `segment ℝ C.P C.Q ⊆ {C.P, C.Q}`, which contradicts `C.interior_nonempty` (the midpoint is in the segment but is neither endpoint, because `C.ne`). The rest of the proof stays the same. So the theorem remains true without `hk2`. The degenerate cases k = 0 and k = 1 cannot happen under the remaining hypotheses: k = 0 gives P = Q, and k = 1 makes the chord an edge of the polygon. The same argument also removes `hk` and `hQ`, so each of the three can be deleted on its own, and all three together as well.
- `(hk : k + 1 < J.n)` (theorem): `hk` is used only inside the proof of `hQnot : C.Q ∉ J.edgeSegment 0` (to compute `ZMod.val`). `hQnot` can be proved without any index arithmetic. If `C.Q ∈ J.edgeSegment 0`, then `C.P` is also in it (from `hP`), and the edge is convex (`convex_segment`). So `Convex.segment_subset` gives `segment ℝ C.P C.Q ⊆ J.edgeSegment 0 ⊆ J.carrier`, and `C.segment_inter_carrier` then gives `segment ℝ C.P C.Q ⊆ {C.P, C.Q}`. This contradicts `C.interior_nonempty`. The rest of the proof does not change. The `let : Fact (1 < J.n)` line is also unnecessary, because `J.three_le` provides it anyway.
- `(hQ : J.vertex (k : ZMod J.n) = C.Q)` (theorem): `hQ` is used only to prove `hQnot : C.Q ∉ J.edgeSegment 0`. For every proper chord this holds whenever `C.P ∈ J.edgeSegment 0`, which follows from `hP`. If `C.Q` were also on that edge, convexity of the segment (`convex_segment`, `Convex.segment_subset`) would give `segment ℝ C.P C.Q ⊆ J.edgeSegment 0 ⊆ J.carrier`. By `C.segment_inter_carrier` this segment would then be contained in `{C.P, C.Q}`, contradicting `C.interior_nonempty` (which relies on `C.ne`). The rest of the proof (`segment_inter_carrier`, `segment_symm`, and `hPmem` from `hP`) does not use `hQ`. Note that `hP` itself is needed, because the conclusion requires `C.P ∈ J.edgeSegment 0`.

### [`NavierStokes.ActualParticularCycleData.native_source_class`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/ActualParticularCycleData.lean#L2462-L2468) — `lean-pool-0787`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0787.lean`

- `(hj : j ≠ 0)` (theorem): The proof only uses `hj` to discharge the `j ≠ 0` guard in `UniformVelocity`. For j ≠ 0 the current proof still works.

Why the family is zero at j = 0: `currentSource x 0 l n = ParticularWaveAssembly.sourceFamily ... 0 n = angleLift (residualSource c u b G A 0 n)`. `ParticularWaveAssembly.residualSource_zero` states `residualSource c u b G A 0 n x = 0`; it comes from `HarmonicResidual.residualBlock_zero_mode`, which says `velocity n i 0 = 0`. `angleLift f z = f (z.1.1, z.2)`, so `currentSource x 0` is the constant zero family.

The conclusion `UniformWaveClass` unfolds to `LabelSumBounds.UniformClass`, which has three fields. At j = 0 each one holds:
(1) `weight_nonneg`: this depends only on the strip and the weight, not on the family. Take it from `(ActualParticularStageControls.current_source_class _ (native_residual H) 1 one_ne_zero).weight_nonneg`.
(2) `smooth`: the family is constant, so `contDiffOn_const` applies.
(3) `bounds`: take C = 0 and p = 0. The norm is 0 by Mathlib's `iteratedFDeriv_zero`. `WeightedClasses.majorant s w α 0 p n x = 0 * ... = 0`, so `0 ≤ 0` holds.

So the new proof is: `rcases eq_or_ne j 0` and use the existing proof when j ≠ 0. No degenerate case is left out: the theorem is quantified over all `j : ℤ`, and the weight nonnegativity at j = 0 is borrowed from the j = 1 instance.

### [`CriticalPortraits.level_lt_of_val_lt`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/CriticalPortraits/Injectivity.lean#L660-L665) — `lean-pool-0790`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0790.lean`

- `[NeZero (d * m)]` (theorem): The statement does not need this instance to elaborate: Mathlib defines `ZMod.val : ZMod n → ℕ` for every `n`, including `n = 0`, where it is `Int.natAbs`. The statement is also true without it, because it is just a fact about two natural numbers: if `a % m = b % m` and `a < b`, then `a / m < b / m`. Here `a = x.val` and `b = y.val`. Degenerate cases: if `m = 0`, then `a % 0 = a`, so `hfib` gives `a = b`, which contradicts `h`. If `m > 0`, write `a = m*(a/m) + r` and `b = m*(b/m) + r` with the same remainder `r`. Then `a < b` forces `a/m < b/m`. The case `d = 0` with `m > 0` gives `ZMod 0 = ℤ`, and the argument above still applies to the natAbs values. So the proof can avoid the helper lemmas, which use `NeZero`, and use plain `Nat` arithmetic instead: `Nat.div_add_mod` on `a` and `b` plus `omega`/`nlinarith`, or a contradiction from `b/m ≤ a/m` together with `hfib`.

### [`SoficGroups.MidrankPermutationEnergy.partitionVertexMidrank_permutation_energy_tendsto_zero`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NonSoficGroup/Foundations.lean#L7811-L7887) — `lean-pool-0796`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0796.lean`

- `(hN : ∀ n, 0 < N n)` (theorem): The statement holds for every real sequence N. Write E n for the energy sum and B n = 2·(crossing sum) + 2·(rank-decrease sum). These are real numbers with 0 ≤ E n (a sum of squares) and E n ≤ B n. The bound E n ≤ B n is the private lemma `sum_partitionVertexMidrank_permutation_energy_le` (line 7785), which takes no hypothesis on N. Then |E n / N n| = E n / |N n| ≤ B n / |N n| = |B n / N n|. This uses `abs_div` and dividing an inequality by the nonnegative number |N n|. The case N n = 0 is covered by the junk value x / 0 = 0: both sides are 0. Also B n / N n = 2·(cross/N n) + 2·(rank/N n), and this tends to 0 by `hcross` and `hrank`, exactly as in the existing `hlimit` step. So `squeeze_zero_norm'` (or `squeeze_zero'` applied to the absolute values) gives that E n / N n tends to 0. Only the existing `hnonnegative` and `hupper` steps use `hN`; they are replaced by this absolute-value bound. Negative values of N are no problem either: E/N and B/N then both lie in [B/N, 0].

### [`ESS.heatRegEnergy_fderiv_eq_sum`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/EscauriazaSereginSverak/PartV/ForcedHeatCriticalEnergy.lean#L62-L65) — `lean-pool-0799`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0799.lean`

- `(hη : 0 < η)` (theorem): Definitions in HeatEntropy.lean:
- `heatRegSq η v = (∑ i, v i ^ 2) + η ^ 2`;
- `heatRegEnergy η v = (1/3) * q ^ (3/2 : ℝ) - (η/2) * q + η^3/6` with `q = heatRegSq η v`;
- `heatRegTest η v i = (q ^ (1/2 : ℝ) - η) * v i`.

The identity holds for every real η.

**Second step needs nothing.** The step `heatRegTest_pairing` (the sum equals `(q^(1/2) - η) * heatRegDot v w`) already has no hypothesis on η.

**Where hη is used.** In `heatRegEnergy_fderiv`, `hη` is used only to get the side condition `q v ≠ 0` for `DifferentiableAt.rpow_const` and `HasFDerivAt.rpow_const` (through `heatRegSq_rpow_fderiv`) at exponent p = 3/2. The `1/2`-power fact `hhalf` built there is never used afterwards.

**Replacement.** Mathlib's `HasFDerivAt.rpow_const (hf : HasFDerivAt f f' x) (h : f x ≠ 0 ∨ 1 ≤ p)` (Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Pow/Deriv.lean:574) and `DifferentiableAt.rpow_const` (line 588) also accept `Or.inr (by norm_num : (1:ℝ) ≤ 3/2)`. With that, `fun z => q z ^ (3/2)` is differentiable at every point, for any η, with derivative `(3/2) * q v ^ (1/2) • fderiv q v`.

**Computation.** `heatRegSq_fderiv` holds for all η and gives `fderiv q v w = 2 * heatRegDot v w`. So the derivative of the energy at v in direction w is
`(1/3)(3/2) q^(1/2) · 2·dot - (η/2) · 2·dot = (q^(1/2) - η) · dot`.
This is the same algebra (`ring`) as in the existing proof.

**Degenerate case η = 0, v = 0.** Here q v = 0. The `1 ≤ 3/2` branch still gives the derivative `(3/2) * 0^(1/2) • _ = 0`. The right-hand side is `(0^(1/2) - 0) * 0 = 0`, so both sides agree.

The statement is therefore true and provable without `hη`.

### [`ScottishBook155.protectedExtensionSourceEmbedding_preservesUpTo`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ScottishBook155/ProtectedExtensionAssembly.lean#L184-L202) — `lean-pool-0812`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0812.lean`

- `(hr : 0 < r)` (theorem): `PreservesUpTo r f` is defined in Paper1.lean as `∀ ⦃x y⦄, dist x y ≤ r → dist (f x) (f y) = dist x y`. Without `hr`, split on the sign of `r`. If `0 < r`, use the existing proof unchanged. If `r ≤ 0`, then `hd : dist x z ≤ r` gives `dist x z ≤ 0`. The space `OneSum M = WithLp 1 (M × ℝ)` is a normed additive group, so it is a genuine metric space, and `dist_le_zero` gives `x = z`. After `subst`, both sides are `0` by `dist_self`. So the conclusion holds for every `r`, and the statement does not mention `hr` anywhere else; it is only passed to the auxiliary lemma in the positive case. The degenerate case `r = 0` is covered by the same argument.

### [`NavierStokes.NaturalProfile.pullback_second_Y`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/NaturalProfile.lean#L2214-L2236) — `lean-pool-0816`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0816.lean`

- `(hF : ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ F (AxisEvaluation.strip window 20))` (theorem): The identity holds for every function F. By definition, pullback Λ F (X, p.2) = F (rescalePoint Λ (X, p.2)) = F (Λ * X, p.2). Let g Y := F (Y, p.2). Mathlib's `deriv_comp_mul_left` (Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/Deriv/CompMul.lean, `deriv (f <| c * ·) x = c • deriv f (c * x)`) needs no differentiability, because deriv is 0 off the set where the function is differentiable. Applying it pointwise gives deriv (fun X => g (Λ*X)) = fun X => Λ * deriv g (Λ*X). Then `deriv_const_mul_field'` (no hypotheses) and `deriv_comp_mul_left` applied to deriv g give Λ * (Λ * deriv (deriv g) (Λ * p.1)). After rewriting with `iteratedDeriv_succ` and `iteratedDeriv_one`, `ring` closes the goal. For Λ = 0 both sides are 0. So the proof can be rewritten without hF. It can be removed independently of hp, and both can be removed together.
- `(hp : p ∈ domain Λ)` (theorem): The same argument works at every point p and uses no information about where p lies. deriv_comp_mul_left and deriv_const_mul_field' are equalities of functions or hold at every point, with no hypotheses, so the identity iteratedDeriv 2 (fun X => F (Λ*X, p.2)) p.1 = Λ^2 * iteratedDeriv 2 (fun Y => F (Y, p.2)) (Λ*p.1) holds for all p. hp is mentioned nowhere else in the statement (Λ and p both appear in the conclusion), so deleting it needs no other change to the statement. It can be removed independently of hF, and both can be removed together.

### [`NavierStokes.AngularMomentReset.angular_weight_formula`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/UniformAngularReset.lean#L654-L670) — `lean-pool-0825`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0825.lean`

- `(hX : 0 ≤ X0)` (theorem): Definitions: radiusX X0 y = X0 * exp y, baseH = √(2 * radiusX X0 y) * baseE, baseE = e0 * exp((-1/2 - lam) * y), angularSlope lam = 1 - lam. The identity holds for every X0. If X0 < 0, both √(2 * X0 * exp y) and √(2 * X0) are 0 by Real.sqrt's junk-value convention, so both sides are 0. In the proof, hX is only used to discharge the `by positivity` side goal 0 ≤ 2 * X0 when `hs` rewrites with Real.sqrt_mul. Replace that with `Real.sqrt_mul' _ (Real.exp_pos y).le` (Mathlib/Analysis/Real/Sqrt.lean:369: √(x * y) = √x * √y given only 0 ≤ y). This gives √(2 * X0 * exp y) = √(2 * X0) * √(exp y) for any X0. sqrt_exp_half then turns √(exp y) into exp (y / 2). The rest of the proof (ring, exp_add, congr) does not use hX.

### [`QuadraticCarleson.HilbertPrincipalValueClosure.ae_forall_lacunary_exists_quadraticPrincipalValue`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticCarleson/HilbertPrincipalValueClosure.lean#L244-L248) — `lean-pool-0837`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0837.lean`

- `(hweak : HasUniformHilbertMaximalWeakBound C)` (theorem): The library proves this hypothesis outright, for a specific constant. `QuadraticCarleson.HilbertMaximalWeakResolved.hasUniformHilbertMaximalWeakBound : HasUniformHilbertMaximalWeakBound (ENNReal.ofReal hilbertWeakConstant)` (HilbertMaximalWeakResolved.lean:205) has no hypotheses. It rests on `ordinaryHilbertMaximal_weak_bound`, a Calderón–Zygmund argument with no extra assumptions, and a grep of the QuadraticCarleson directory finds no `sorry` or `axiom`. The same file also proves the hypothesis-free `HilbertMaximalWeakResolved.ae_forall_real_exists_quadraticPrincipalValue (f : L0Infinity) : ∀ᵐ x, ∀ lam : ℝ, ∃ z, HasQuadraticPrincipalValue lam f x z`.

So after deleting `hweak`, the statement is still true. The same two-line proof works: `filter_upwards [HilbertMaximalWeakResolved.ae_forall_real_exists_quadraticPrincipalValue f] with x hx; exact fun m ↦ hx (dyadicModulation m)`. Equivalently, pass `hasUniformHilbertMaximalWeakBound` to the local `ae_forall_real_exists_quadraticPrincipalValue`.

There is no circularity: a grep shows the theorem under review is never used, so the resolved weak bound does not depend on it. The catch is that the proof needs a file downstream of this one, so the theorem would have to move to HilbertMaximalWeakResolved.lean (or later) to be proved there.

Once `hweak` is gone, the implicit `{C : ℝ≥0∞}` is no longer mentioned anywhere and can be dropped too. `C` cannot be deleted on its own, because `hweak` mentions it.

### [`QuadraticCarleson.KaltonEndpoint.integralOn_le_of_weakL1Bound`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/QuadraticCarleson/QuadraticCarleson/KaltonEndpoint.lean#L117-L222) — `lean-pool-0839`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0839.lean`

- `(hf0 : ∀ x, 0 ≤ f x)` (theorem): The bound stays true for any f, by reducing to f⁺ = fun x ↦ max (f x) 0. First, the right-hand side is positive: hqM gives 2A ≤ M·m, so M·m/(2A) ≥ 1, the log is ≥ 0, and RHS ≥ 2A > 0. Next, f⁺ satisfies every other hypothesis. It is measurable. For a > 0, {f⁺ > a} = {f > a}, so HasWeakL1Bound μ A f⁺ holds (the 0 ≤ A part is unchanged). On s, f⁺ ≤ max M 0 = M, since M ≥ 2A/m > 0. And 0 ≤ f⁺ everywhere. So the existing theorem gives ∫_s f⁺ ≤ RHS. Finally, ∫_s f ≤ ∫_s f⁺. If f is integrable on s, use setIntegral_mono, since f⁺ is integrable on s: it is measurable, lies in [0, M] on s, and μ s < ∞. If f is not integrable on s, then ∫_s f = 0 ≤ ∫_s f⁺. Degenerate cases (μ s = 0, f ≤ 0 on s) are covered by the same argument.
- `(hf : Measurable f)` (theorem): Deleting this alone (keeping hf0) leaves the statement true. Case 1: f is not AEStronglyMeasurable for μ.restrict s. Then ∫_s f = 0 by the junk convention, and 0 < 2A ≤ RHS, because hqM gives M·m/(2A) ≥ 1 and so the log is ≥ 0. Case 2: f is AEStronglyMeasurable. Let h be its measurable version (AEStronglyMeasurable.mk) and set g x = if x ∈ s then max (min (h x) M) 0 else 0. g is measurable because s is measurable (hs). Also g ≥ 0 and g ≤ M on s. Since 0 ≤ f ≤ M on s and h = f a.e. on s, g = f μ.restrict s-a.e., so ∫_s g = ∫_s f. Weak bound for g: for a > 0, {g > a} ⊆ {f > a} ∪ N, where N ⊆ s is the μ-null set where h ≠ f (its μ-measure is at most μ.restrict s {h ≠ f} = 0). So μ{g > a} ≤ μ{f > a}, and HasWeakL1Bound μ A g holds. Applying the theorem to g gives the bound. This hypothesis and hf0 can each be removed on its own; the argument for hf uses hf0, so removing both would need the two reductions combined (f⁺ and then the measurable version), which also works.

### [`Schoenflies.localGrid_frame_subset`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Schoenflies/QuantitativeForwardStages.lean#L31-L57) — `lean-pool-0844`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0844.lean`

- `(hs : 0 < s)` (theorem): The statement stays true for every real s. Case s < 0: `closedSquare p s = {x | supDist x p ≤ s}` is empty because `supDist_nonneg` (Square.lean) gives 0 ≤ supDist, so the left-hand side is empty and the inclusion is trivial. Case s ≥ 0 (this includes s = 0, where the frame is {p}): the existing proof still works with one change. The helpers `mem_cover_of_coord_eq` and `mem_cover_of_coord_eq'` use `hs` only through `localGridX_strictMono` / `localGridY_strictMono`, and only to get `.monotone`. That is, they need the grid coordinates `uniformCoord a (2*s/k) i = a + i*(2*s/k)` to be monotone (the `hxmono`/`hymono` hypotheses, and `segment_eq_Icc` on indices 0 ≤ k). Monotonicity already holds when the step 2*s/k ≥ 0, i.e. for s ≥ 0, because i ↦ a + i*h is monotone for h ≥ 0. Nothing else in the proof uses hs: `localGridX_last`/`localGridY_last` and `gridVEdge_mem_gridEdges` need only hk. So the proof splits on the sign of s: the empty-square argument when s < 0, and the same proof with monotonicity from the nonnegative step when s ≥ 0. Note that hk cannot be removed: when k = 0, `gridEdges … 0 0` is empty, so the cover is empty, while for s ≥ 0 the frame contains the point (p 0 + s, p 1).

### [`combined_step_card`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/AndersonConjecture/Jensen/Construction/ChainHelpers.lean#L65-L91) — `lean-pool-0859`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0859.lean`

- `(hT_aleph0 : Cardinal.aleph0 < Cardinal.mk T)` (theorem): The other hypotheses already imply this one, because a complete local domain whose maximal ideal is nonzero is uncountable.

(1) The maximal ideal M is nonzero. This is the opening step of the proof of `combined_step`: a nonzero r ∈ q would otherwise be a unit, so q = ⊤, contradicting `hq_prime`.

(2) Pick x ∈ M with x ≠ 0. Since T is a domain, every power x^n is nonzero. `IsAdicComplete` extends `IsHausdorff`, so ⋂ₙ M^n = 0. Hence for every n there is m > n with x^n ∉ M^m. Build exponents n₀ < n₁ < … with x^{n_i} ∉ M^{n_{i+1}}.

(3) For ε : ℕ → Bool, the partial sums Σ_{i<k} ε_i x^{n_i} form a Cauchy sequence for the M-adic filtration (x^{n_i} ∈ M^{n_i}). `IsPrecomplete` gives a limit L_ε.

(4) If ε and ε' first differ at index j, then L_ε − L_ε' ≡ ±x^{n_j} mod M^{n_{j+1}}. All later terms lie in M^{n_{j+1}}, and L_ε agrees with its partial sums modulo M^{n_{j+1}}. Since x^{n_j} ∉ M^{n_{j+1}}, L_ε ≠ L_ε'.

So ε ↦ L_ε is an injection (ℕ → Bool) ↪ T, which gives #T ≥ 2^ℵ₀ > ℵ₀.

The proof only uses `hT_aleph0` in two places: when calling `combined_step`, and in `max_lt hT_aleph0 hR_card` for the last conjunct. In both places one can use the derived fact ℵ₀ < #T instead. Degenerate cases do not arise: q ≠ ⊥ and q prime force M ≠ 0, so T is not a field and is infinite.

### [`measure_integralDensitySetAbove_eq_zero`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/FormalizingGMT/Densities/SingularIntegralDensities.lean#L165-L177) — `lean-pool-0866`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0866.lean`

- `(hε : 0 < ε)` (theorem): For every ε : ℝ≥0∞ we have 0 ≤ ε, so the set {x | ε < integralDensityLimsup μ f s x} is contained in {x | 0 < integralDensityLimsup μ f s x}, which is `integralDensitySet μ f s` by definition. That set has measure zero by `measure_integralDensitySet_eq_zero`. When ε = 0 the two sets are equal, so the excluded case also holds. Proof: replace `lt_trans hε hx` with `lt_of_le_of_lt (zero_le _) hx` (or `pos_of_gt hx`).
- `(hμ : IsRadon μ)` (theorem): It is only passed to `measure_integralDensitySet_eq_zero`, which only passes it to `ae_integralDensityLimsup_eq_zero`. There the binder is `_hμ` and the proof (lines 93–150) never uses it: the proof only uses the doubling Vitali family, `VitaliFamily.ae_tendsto_lintegral_enorm_sub_div`, `hf` and `hdim`. Without hμ, copy that proof body to get `∀ᵐ x ∂μ, integralDensityLimsup μ f s x = 0`. Then finish as now with `measure_mono_null` (from ε < L x we get L x ≠ 0).
- `(hs : 0 ≤ s)` (theorem): Like hμ, it only reaches `ae_integralDensityLimsup_eq_zero`, where it is the unused binder `_hs`. That proof only divides by `ENNReal.ofReal (r ^ s)`. In ℝ≥0∞, monotonicity of division (`gcongr`) and `ENNReal.Tendsto.const_mul` hold for any denominator, so nothing needs s ≥ 0. The statement holds for negative s by the same argument: inline that proof without hs, then apply `measure_mono_null` as before.

### [`NavierStokes.NominalConeAssembly.Witness.prefix_coordinates`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/NominalConeAssembly.lean#L1574-L1586) — `lean-pool-0886`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0886.lean`

- `(hf : W.controls.f p ≠ 0)` (theorem): hf is only needed because the current proof goes through ActivationStocks.profiles_stocks_congr, which rewrites with the closed form profileStockOne_eq (requires f p ≠ 0). The statement itself does not need it. Let P = W.profiles and Q = W.controls.profiles W.separated. Their U fields are definitionally equal, their pressure0 fields are equal by rfl, and prefix_f gives P.f q = Q.f q for every q with q.1 ≤ heatJoin. shearA (-2 p.1 radialPartial f p / f p) agrees by prefix_f plus radialPartial_of_left (uses hp, hp.1, hR). shearB (-2 p.1 radialPartial U p / E p) agrees because U is identical and E p = sqrt(2 p.1) f p. ReferenceBounds.p1 = p.1 * (primitive (angularSource h) p / (p.1 * H p)) / L, and H p = 2 p.1 f p agrees at p. angularSource at (x, η) depends on W h (which depends only on U), U, H, radialPartial H and parameterPartial H. H agrees on the open set {q.1 < heatJoin}, so its fderiv agrees there. The integrands of primitive therefore agree for x < heatJoin, i.e. a.e. on the integration interval (0, p.1] ⊆ (-∞, heatJoin], and the primitives are equal (intervalIntegral.integral_congr_ae). If f p = 0, the division by zero gives the same junk value on both sides. p2 = p.1 * (primitive axialSource p / p.1 / L) / E p is handled the same way: axialSource uses W, U and its partials (identical), f^2, pressure = pressure0 + primitive f^2 (which agrees on {q.1 ≤ heatJoin}) and parameterPartial pressure (which agrees on the open set {q.1 < heatJoin}). E p agrees. So all four equalities hold without hf.
- `(hX : 0 < p.1)` (theorem): hX is likewise only used for the closed-form stock lemmas (profileStockOne_eq, profileStockTwo_eq) and for history_of_prefix, inside coordinates_of_prefix. Without it, use the direct argument given for hf. heatJoin_pos gives 0 < heatJoin, so for p.1 ≤ heatJoin the integration interval between 0 and p.1 lies in (-∞, heatJoin] whatever the sign of p.1, and prefix_f holds for every q with q.1 ≤ heatJoin, negative q.1 included. So the integrands of primitive (angularSource h) and primitive (axialSource h) agree a.e. (they can differ only at x = heatJoin), and pressure agrees on {q.1 ≤ heatJoin}. shearA and shearB agree by radialPartial_of_left (uses hp, hp.1, hR) and pointwise agreement of f and E at p. When p.1 = 0 all four quantities are 0 or the same junk value on both sides; when p.1 < 0, E p = sqrt(2 p.1) * f p = 0 on both sides, so p2 and shearB are both 0, and p1 and shearA agree as above. hX and hf can each be removed, and also both together.

### [`Zeta5Irrational.tc_247`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Zeta5Irrational/Table/Intervals.lean#L1873-L1878) — `lean-pool-0894`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0894.lean`

- `0 < t` (theorem): This premise is a binder of the ∀ statement after the colon, not a binder before it. It is implied by the first premise `(6742465461111 / 32000000000000 : ℝ) ≤ t`, because that lower bound (≈ 0.2107) is positive, so `0 < t` follows by `lt_of_lt_of_le (by norm_num) h1` or by linarith. The proof never uses it: `check_right` (LeanPool/Zeta5Irrational/TableCheck.lean:52) starts with `intro t ht1 ht2 _`, discarding the positivity premise, and uses only `l ≤ t`, `t ≤ r` and the monotonicity lemmas `Uρ_monotoneOn` and `Vfield_monotoneOn`. Without the premise, the statement is proved by `fun t h1 h2 => check_right ... t h1 h2 (by linarith)`.

### [`TauCeti.DavisKahan.Sylvester.measurable_chosenFiniteStepSymbol`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/DavisKahan/DavisKahan/Sylvester/FiniteStepCalculus.lean#L346-L383) — `lean-pool-0900`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0900.lean`

- `(hdisj : Set.PairwiseDisjoint Set.univ cell)` (theorem): The definition (line 316) is `chosenFiniteStepSymbol cell rep x = if hx : ∃ i, x ∈ cell i then rep (Classical.choose hx) else x`. In `Classical.choose {α} {p : α → Prop} (h : ∃ a, p a)` the predicate is `p_x = fun i => x ∈ cell i`. Take two points x, y that lie in exactly the same cells (∀ i, x ∈ cell i ↔ y ∈ cell i). Then `p_x = p_y` by `funext` and `propext`, and by proof irrelevance `Classical.choose hx = Classical.choose hy`. So `f x = f y`, even when the cells overlap. Hence f is constant on each atom `A_S = {x | ∀ i, x ∈ cell i ↔ i ∈ S}` with S ⊆ Fin n nonempty, and f = id on `A_∅`, the complement of `⋃ i, cell i`. There are finitely many atoms, and each is measurable because it is a finite intersection of the measurable sets `cell i` or their complements (from `hcell`). So f is measurable: for measurable B, `f ⁻¹' B = (A_∅ ∩ B) ∪ ⋃_{S ≠ ∅, c_S ∈ B} A_S`, where c_S is the constant value of f on A_S. You can also write f as a finite piecewise function of constants and `id` over these atoms and use `Measurable.piecewise`. The case n = 0 is trivial because then f = id. The existing proof uses `hdisj` only to rewrite f as an indicator sum. The argument above replaces that step, and the statement stays true and provable without `hdisj`.

### [`Lean4LPD.PauliString.pauliNorm_layerStep_error_le_model_of_source_regime`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/LowWeightPauliDynamics/Pauli/LayerError.lean#L577-L616) — `lean-pool-0903`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0903.lean`

- `(hΓ : 0 < Γ)` (theorem): `Admissible r m G` is defined (Constants/C0.lean) as `1 ≤ r ∧ 0 ≤ m ∧ 0 < G ∧ m ≤ r ∧ 8 * (m + 1) ^ 2 ≤ r * G`. So `hadm : Admissible (r:ℝ) (m:ℝ) (Γ:ℝ)` contains `0 < (Γ:ℝ)` (`hadm.2.2.1`), and `exact_mod_cast hadm.2.2.1` gives `0 < Γ` in ℕ. The new proof defines hΓ this way and leaves the rest unchanged. This can be deleted independently of ht (both can also be deleted together).
- `(ht : 0 ≤ t)` (theorem): Implied by ha, haA, hα and hr. From hr : 5 ≤ r we get (r:ℝ) > 0. ha : 0 ≤ a and haA : a ≤ α * t / r give 0 ≤ α * t / r, hence 0 ≤ α * t (le_div_iff₀). With hα : 0 < α this gives 0 ≤ t (e.g. by nlinarith / nonneg_of_mul_nonneg_right). If t < 0, then α*t/r < 0 ≤ a, which contradicts haA, so no case is missed. The proof derives ht first and then calls pauliNorm_layerStep_error_le_model as before. This can be deleted independently of hΓ (both can also be deleted together).

### [`AleksandrovDifferentiability.ae_restrict_sourceOpenCube_secondOrderDifferentiableAt_of_directionalCluster`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ViscositySolutionTheory/AleksandrovDifferentiability/Statements/Cube/SecondOrder/Assembly.lean#L274-L301) — `lean-pool-0904`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0904.lean`

- `(hfiniteStatement : ConvexFiniteUpperContactOpeningAEOnCubeStatement n)` (theorem): `ConvexFiniteUpperContactOpeningAEOnCubeStatement n` is a Prop (Statements/Cube/Basic/Sets.lean:108). The library proves it for every n, with no arguments, as `ConvexFiniteUpperContactOpeningAEOnCubeStatement.of_convexSourceRightDerivStieltjes` (Stieltjes/Measurability/Wrappers.lean:844). Final.lean already passes that term in this argument position. The proof stays the same, with `hfiniteStatement` replaced by `ConvexFiniteUpperContactOpeningAEOnCubeStatement.of_convexSourceRightDerivStieltjes`. A grep finds no `sorry` or `axiom` in the AleksandrovDifferentiability tree. This hypothesis can be removed on its own, or together with `hdom`.
- `(hdom : ∀ (x : SourceCubeSpace n) (m : ℕ), x ∈ cubeGoodSet n u (m : ℝ) → ∀ᶠ y in 𝓝[sourceOpenCube n (3 / 2 : ℝ) \ {x}] x, ∀ p, SubgradientOn (sourceOpenCube n 3) u y p → ∀ z, ∃ q ∈ GradientClusterSet ..., inner ℝ p z ≤ inner ℝ q z)` (theorem): `hdom` is used only to produce the cluster-density hypothesis of `ae_restrict_sourceOpenCube_secondOrderDifferentiableAt_of_clusterDensity`. The library proves the full conclusion from `hn`, `hbounded` and `hconvex` alone: `ae_restrict_sourceOpenCube_secondOrderDifferentiableAt` (ClusterDensity/Final.lean:178) has the same statement without `hfiniteStatement` and without `hdom`. It obtains the cluster density from `SourceCubeSubgradientClusterDensity.of_straszewiczCompactConvex` and `straszewiczCompactConvexStatement`, which is proved for finite-dimensional E in RockafellarCluster/Straszewicz.lean:35. No `sorry` or `axiom` appears in that tree. So without `hdom` the theorem is still true and has the one-line proof `ae_restrict_sourceOpenCube_secondOrderDifferentiableAt hn hbounded hconvex`. That module imports this file, but the statement remains provable. This hypothesis can be removed on its own, or together with `hfiniteStatement`.

### [`KasamiCyclicAdditive.Phase.sum_psi_mul_powD`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/KasamiCyclicAdditive/Phase/PowerMap.lean#L60-L68) — `lean-pool-0907`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0907.lean`

- `(hm : m ≠ 0)` (theorem): The integer `m` only serves as a witness that `D` is invertible modulo `q-1 = Fintype.card Kˣ`. Suppose `m = 0`. Then `hmD` reads `0 ≡ 1 [MOD card Kˣ]`, so `card Kˣ ∣ 1` and `card Kˣ = 1`. In that case `1 * D ≡ 1 [MOD 1]` holds trivially (`Nat.modEq_one`). So we can call `powD_bijective` with `m := 1`, using `one_ne_zero`, `hD` and that congruence (after rewriting `card Kˣ = 1`), and the rest of the proof goes through unchanged. If `m ≠ 0`, the current proof applies as written. So the new proof just adds `by_cases hm : m = 0` and replaces the witness in the degenerate case. The statement is still true: when `card Kˣ = 1`, K = F₂ and y^D = y for every D ≥ 1. By contrast, `hD` is really needed. With K = F₂ and D = 0, `hmD` holds (everything is congruent mod 1). Then the left side is 2ψ(1) = -2 for C = 1 and primitive ψ, which is not 0.

### [`Schoenflies.localGridEdges_nondeg`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/Schoenflies/GridAttach.lean#L502-L505) — `lean-pool-0915`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0915.lean`

- `(hk : 1 ≤ k)` (theorem): The only case that `hk` excludes is `k = 0`, and there the statement holds vacuously. By definition, `localGridEdges p s 0 = gridEdges (localGridX p s 0) (localGridY p s 0) 0 0`, and `gridEdges xc yc m n = (List.range m).flatMap (fun i => stripEdges xc yc i n)` (SquareMeshConnected.lean:463). With `m = 0` this is `(List.range 0).flatMap _ = []`, so `P ∈ localGridEdges p s 0` is false. Proof without `hk`: split with `rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos k with rfl | hk`. In the zero case, `simp [localGridEdges, gridEdges] at hP` closes the goal, since membership in `[]` is false. In the positive case `hk : 0 < k`, which is `1 ≤ k`, and the existing proof goes through unchanged (`gridEdges_nondeg` with `localGridX_strictMono hs hk` and `localGridY_strictMono hs hk`). By contrast, `hs` is needed: if `s = 0` and `k ≥ 1`, the step `2 * s / k` is `0`, so all grid points coincide and every edge is degenerate.

### [`Bananas.sectionFive_inversion_lower_bound_of_involutive_transmission`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/BrillNoetherGraphs/Bananas/Sections/SectionFiveStatements.lean#L90-L101) — `lean-pool-0919`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0919.lean`

- `(hk : 0 < k)` (theorem): `sectionFiveRankDropSum M D k` is defined as `∑ m : Fin k, (...)`. When k = 0 this is an empty sum, so it equals 0. The right-hand side `kInversionCount k tau` is a natural number (`Set.ncard`) cast to ℤ, so it is ≥ 0. Hence the inequality holds trivially for k = 0, whatever M, D and tau are. Proof without hk: case on k. For k = 0, unfold sectionFiveRankDropSum, simplify the empty sum over Fin 0 (Finset.univ_eq_empty / Finset.sum_empty) and close `0 ≤ (n : ℤ)` with Int.natCast_nonneg or positivity. For k = n+1, apply sectionFive_inversion_lower_bound_of_involutive_transmission_connected as in the current proof, with `Nat.succ_pos n` in place of hk.

### [`AleksandrovDifferentiability.exists_mathlib_exposedPoint_inner_lt_of_isCompact_of_inner_lt`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/ViscositySolutionTheory/AleksandrovDifferentiability/Analysis/RockafellarCluster/SupportingBall.lean#L150-L190) — `lean-pool-0922`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0922.lean`

- `[CompleteSpace E]` (variable): The theorem picks up `[CompleteSpace E]` automatically from the `variable` line, because the instance mentions only `E`. The result is still true without it. It is the farthest-point argument: on a compact set in any real inner product space, take a point that maximises the distance to a suitably shifted centre. That point is exposed by the supporting ball, and it lies outside the halfspace. Completeness plays no role in this argument.

In the Lean proof, every step except one already compiles without completeness. `exists_exposesPoint_and_inner_lt_of_forall_norm_sub_sq_lt` (SupportingBall.lean:120) and `exposesPoint_of_forall_norm_sub_le` (line 108) are both marked `omit [CompleteSpace E] in`. The other steps (`IsCompact.exists_isMaxOn`, `fun_prop`, `nlinarith`, `field_simp`) are generic.

The one step that uses completeness is `ExposesPoint.mem_mathlib_exposedPoints` (Exposed/Mathlib.lean:71). It builds its witness as `InnerProductSpace.toDual ℝ E normal`, and `toDual` requires `[CompleteSpace E]` (Mathlib Dual.lean:131–136). This witness only needs to be some `l : StrongDual ℝ E` with `l y = inner ℝ normal y`. `InnerProductSpace.toDualMap ℝ E normal` works and needs no completeness (Dual.lean:67, variables at line 55 have no `CompleteSpace`). It satisfies `toDualMap_apply_apply : toDualMap 𝕜 E x y = ⟪x, y⟫` (Dual.lean:77); `innerSL ℝ normal` would also work. With that witness the rest of the proof of `mem_mathlib_exposedPoints` goes through unchanged. So that lemma, and with it this theorem, can be stated with `omit [CompleteSpace E] in`.

Degenerate cases: `s` cannot be empty, because `hx : x ∈ s` is a hypothesis. If `E` is trivial, then `inner ℝ normal x = 0`, and the conclusion holds with `p = x`, which is an exposed point of the singleton `s`. The general argument covers this case anyway.

### [`EulerAllOrderDriftCorrection.Budget.fieldTower_weightedNorm_delta`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/AllOrderDriftRadiusBounds.lean#L84-L100) — `lean-pool-0925`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0925.lean`

- `(hN : N + 6 ≤ s)` (theorem): The proof uses `hN` only to call `weightedNorm_unique`. That lemma says two Sobolev fields with the same value have equal weighted norms, provided both have level at least N+6. Here it turns the norm of `realization s t` into the norm of `B.solution (N+6) t`. Everything else in the proof works at level N+6 and does not involve s: `value_eq`, `solution_value_common`, `weightedNorm_le_energy`, `solution_energy`.

Without `hN`, equality may fail, but an inequality is enough: weightedNorm(realization s t) ≤ weightedNorm(solution (N+6) t). After that the old proof finishes unchanged. The inequality is checked one term at a time.
- `weightedNorm` is ∑_{n ≤ N} weight ρ n * blockNorm, and `blockNorm` is ∑_{r ≤ 6} levelNorm J (n+r). Here J = toJet u, which is a `SpatialJet` of depth s.
- `levelNorm` (JetProductBounds.lean) is defined by recursion on the jet. It returns 0 for a positive order at a `.zero` node, and at a `.succ` node it sums over the lower jets. By induction on J: levelNorm J m = 0 whenever m > depth.
- So every level m > s on the left side is 0. That is ≤ the matching level on the right, which is nonnegative by `levelNorm_nonneg`.
- For m ≤ s (and m ≤ N+6), the two levels are equal. This uses `levelNorm_eq_words` together with `SpatialJet.word_unique` (PressureJetIdentities.lean), which needs only equal values (`hv`) and m within both depths. This is the same step `blockNorm_unique` uses.
- The weights are nonnegative: `weight_pos` applies because B.radius t ≥ B.initialRadius/2 > 0 (`radius_bounds`, `radius_pos`).

Summing with `Finset.sum_le_sum` gives the inequality. Then chain it with `weightedNorm_le_energy` and `solution_energy`, exactly as the current proof does. Degenerate case: when s < N+6 the left side only gets smaller, so the bound holds for every s.

### [`EulerChildParticleTime.displacement_time`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/Euler/PhysicalChildParent.lean#L389-L395) — `lean-pool-0927`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0927.lean`

- `[CompleteSpace E]` (variable): The theorem picks up `[CompleteSpace E]` from `variable` because it is instance-implicit and its type mentions only `E`, which is already an argument. The proof needs it only because it calls `TimeDerivative.compDisplacement` (SmoothTimeFieldChain.lean), which is stated under `variable [CompleteSpace V]`. That lemma uses completeness only for its proof method: it passes from the interior derivative to the endpoints of `Icc 0 T` through `EulerSeparatingTimeDerivative.hasDerivWithinAt`, which relies on a Bochner integral identity.

The chain rule it proves holds without completeness. Take `t ∈ Icc 0 T`, a fixed `x`, and write y_s = x + D(s,x), y₀ = x + D(t,x). Split
A(s,y_s) − A(t,y₀) = [A(s,y_s) − A(s,y₀)] + [A(s,y₀) − A(t,y₀)].
1. The second bracket is (s−t)·A₁(t,y₀) + o(s−t) within `Icc 0 T`, because `hP` applied at the point y₀ is exactly that statement.
2. For the first bracket, apply the mean value inequality `Convex.norm_image_sub_le_of_norm_fderiv_le'` (Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/MeanValue.lean). It needs only normed spaces, not completeness. Use it on `A.field s` along the segment [y₀, y_s] with φ = fderiv (A.field t) y₀. This gives
‖A(s,y_s) − A(s,y₀) − φ(y_s − y₀)‖ ≤ sup over the segment of ‖A′(s,z) − A′(t,y₀)‖ · ‖y_s − y₀‖.
3. That sup tends to 0. Indeed `A.jet 1`, and hence `derivativeField`, is a continuous path into the bounded continuous functions, so ‖A′(s,·) − A′(t,·)‖∞ → 0. In addition A′(t,·) is continuous at y₀, and y_s → y₀.
4. By `hD`, y_s − y₀ = (s−t)·D₁(t,x) + o(s−t). Hence the first bracket is (s−t)·A′(t,y₀)(D₁(t,x)) + o(s−t).

Adding the two parts gives the derivative A₁(t,y₀) + A′(t,y₀)(D₁(t,x)) within `Icc 0 T`, which is the conclusion of `TimeDerivative.compDisplacement`. If T = 0, the claim is just the same estimate on a singleton, so nothing breaks.

As a second route, one can push A and A₁ forward along the isometric embedding of E into its completion, with D and D₁ unchanged, apply the existing complete-space lemma there, and pull the result back. This works because `HasDerivWithinAt` is reflected by a linear isometry.

The rest of the proof uses `TimeDerivative.add`, `congr_fields`, `simp` and `abel`, and none of these need completeness. So `displacement_time` stays true and provable after `omit [CompleteSpace E] in`.

### [`NavierStokes.TemporalMeanUpdate.axialUpdate_supported`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/TemporalMeanUpdate.lean#L766-L772) — `lean-pool-0937`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0937.lean`

- `(hf : ContDiff ℝ ∞ f)` (theorem): The only use of hf is desiredIncrement_smooth h n hf hp, inside axialPotential_supported. It feeds PressureStream.streamPotential_supported, then RadialPullback.physicalCompact_supported, which uses smoothness only as continuity for TransportPrimitive.canonicalCompact_supported → compactIntegral_supported → pastIntegral_eq_zero_of_le. Every other step (desiredIncrement_supported, temporalInverse_supported, centered_supported, weightedSource_supported, normalizeSource_supported, pullback_supported, divideRadius_supported, streamGamma_supported) needs only the support hypothesis hs. In pastIntegral_eq_zero_of_le, continuity only handles the endpoint u = 0 when z.1 = a. For u < 0, (shift M v z u).1 = z.1 + u < a, so the integrand vanishes by the support hypothesis alone. The single point {0} has Lebesgue measure zero, so ∫ u in Iic 0, ... = 0 for any g: use MeasureTheory.integral_Iic_eq_integral_Iio and then setIntegral_eq_zero_of_forall_eq_zero, or setIntegral_eq_zero_of_ae_eq_zero. The right edge (pastIntegral_eq_total_of_ge) already needs no continuity. So the conclusion follows from hs, ha, hab and hd for arbitrary f. Deleting hf alone works; hf and hp can also be deleted together.
- `(hp : PressureStream.TorusPeriodicLift f)` (theorem): hp is used only in desiredIncrement_smooth h n hf hp, to get smoothness of desiredIncrement. As argued for hf, that smoothness is needed only as continuity in pastIntegral_eq_zero_of_le, where an almost-everywhere argument replaces it: the integrand vanishes for u < 0 by the support hypothesis, and {0} is Lebesgue-null (MeasureTheory.integral_Iic_eq_integral_Iio, then setIntegral_eq_zero_of_forall_eq_zero). The rest of the support chain (desiredIncrement_supported, temporalInverse_supported, centered_supported, weightedSource_supported, normalizeSource_supported, pullback_supported, divideRadius_supported, streamGamma_supported) uses only hs. So the radial support of axialUpdate holds without hp. hp can be deleted on its own, or together with hf.

### [`KaltonRoberts.card_perm_constrained_step`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/KaltonRoberts/PippengerProof.lean#L56-L177) — `lean-pool-0943`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0943.lean`

- `(hs : s < t)` (theorem): Write A_k for the number of permutations σ of Fin n with σ i < t for all i < k. The claim is A_{s+1}·(n−s) = A_s·(t−s). Keep ht : t ≤ n and suppose s < t fails, i.e. t ≤ s. Truncated subtraction gives t − s = 0, so the RHS is 0. If n ≤ s, then n − s = 0 and the LHS is 0. If s < n, the s+1 indices 0..s all lie in Fin n, and a permutation counted by A_{s+1} would map them injectively into {v < t}, which has t ≤ s < s+1 elements. That is impossible, so A_{s+1} = 0 and the LHS is 0. The degenerate cases (n = 0, s = t) fall under these. Proof without hs: `by_cases hst : s < t`. The first branch runs the existing proof, whose only uses of hs are to show s < n via linarith with ht. In the other branch, rewrite with Nat.sub_eq_zero_of_le, handle n ≤ s the same way, and otherwise show the subtype is empty by pigeonhole: Fintype.card_le_of_injective on j : Fin (s+1) ↦ ⟨σ ⟨j,_⟩, _⟩ : Fin t would give s+1 ≤ t, a contradiction. By contrast ht is necessary: n=0, s=0, t=1 gives 1·0 = 1·1.

### [`NavierStokes.PulseEnergyHistory.normalized_history_bound`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/PulseCone.lean#L1133-L1168) — `lean-pool-0945`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0945.lean`

- `(hlam : 0 < lam)` (theorem): Implied by hy and hy' together with the junk-value conventions. If lam < 0, then 13/lam < 0, so hy' gives y < 0, which contradicts hy. If lam = 0, then 13/0 = 0, so 0 ≤ y ≤ 0 and y = 0. Also hinit becomes |H 0| ≤ N*C/0 = 0, so H 0 = 0. The left side is then |H 0|/(N*exp 0) = 0 and the right side is (…)/0 = 0, so 0 ≤ 0 holds. So we may first case-split on lam ≤ 0 and finish as above. In the lam > 0 case the existing proof applies unchanged.
- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (theorem): Implied by hinit, hN and hlam: 0 ≤ |H 0| ≤ N*C/lam with N > 0 and lam > 0 gives C ≥ 0 (by div_nonneg_iff / pos_of_mul_pos-type reasoning, or nlinarith). The proof only uses C ≥ 0 implicitly in the final `positivity` call, so we add this derived fact first.
- `(hM : 0 ≤ M)` (theorem): Implied by hsource, hy and hN: 0 ∈ Icc 0 y because hy gives 0 ≤ y, so 0 ≤ |g 0| ≤ N*M. With N > 0 this gives M ≥ 0. The proof uses hM in `mul_nonneg hN.le hM` (replace it with the derived N*M ≥ 0) and in `positivity` (supply the derived M ≥ 0).
- `(hN : 0 < N)` (theorem): Removable given hC and hM (but not together with them). If N = 0, the left side is |H y|/0 = 0, and the right side (C+13M)*exp 26/lam is ≥ 0 by hC, hM and hlam. If N < 0, then hsource at t = 0 (in Icc because of hy) gives 0 ≤ N*M, so M ≤ 0, and with hM, M = 0. Then g = 0 on Icc 0 y, so H y = H 0 by the same FTC step (heq). Also hinit gives |H 0| ≤ N*C/lam ≤ 0 because C ≥ 0, so H y = 0. The left side is 0 and the right side is ≥ 0. In the N > 0 case the existing proof applies.
- `(hg : Continuous g)` (theorem): Continuity of g is used only to make g interval-integrable for the FTC step. Instead we can bound |H y - H 0| ≤ N*M*y directly with Mathlib's mean value inequality `norm_image_sub_le_of_norm_deriv_le_segment'` (Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/MeanValue.lean). It needs `∀ x ∈ Icc 0 y, HasDerivWithinAt H (g x) (Icc 0 y) x`, which follows from hd via `HasDerivAt.hasDerivWithinAt`, and `∀ x ∈ Ico 0 y, ‖g x‖ ≤ N*M`, which follows from hsource. Applied at x = y this gives |H y - H 0| ≤ N*M*(y - 0), and the rest of the proof (the hvalue calc and hfactor) is unchanged.

### [`BGS.Markoff.cageTraceBranchPolynomial_separable`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/MarkoffModP/BGS/Markoff/Cage/ShiftedTraceCurveIrreducibility.lean#L98-L118) — `lean-pool-0957`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0957.lean`

- `(h3 : (3 : K) ≠ 0)` (theorem): The polynomial is `cageTraceBranchPolynomial = C 9 * X ^ 2 - C 4` (line 41). Proof without h3: split on whether `(3 : K) = 0`. If `(3 : K) ≠ 0`, use the existing proof. If `(3 : K) = 0`, then `(9 : K) = 3 * 3 = 0` and `(4 : K) = 3 + 1 = 1`. So the polynomial equals `C 0 * X ^ 2 - C 1 = C (-1)`. Mathlib's `Polynomial.separable_C` (`(C r).Separable ↔ IsUnit r`, FieldTheory/Separable.lean:75) shows this is separable, because `-1` is a unit. So the statement holds in characteristic 3 as well, and h3 can be deleted. h2 is still needed: in characteristic 2 we have 9 = 1 and 4 = 0, so the polynomial is `X ^ 2`, which is not separable.

### [`NavierStokes.PulseAmplitude.errorConstant_ge`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/PulseAmplitude.lean#L2836-L2850) — `lean-pool-0973`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0973.lean`

- `(hP : 0 < P)` (theorem): `errorConstant P m = 1 + prefixBoundConstant P m + correctionEnergyConstant P m + 2 * OutgoingTail.flattenLength + 4 * TailEnergyBounds.tailConstant`. The proof uses `hP` only to get `hc : 0 < correctionEnergyConstant P m`. All four inequalities only need `0 ≤ correctionEnergyConstant P m`. That holds for every `P`, because `correctionEnergyConstant P m = 104 * OutgoingPulseBounds.correctionJetBound P m 0 ^ 2`, which is nonnegative by `positivity` (or `mul_nonneg (by norm_num) (sq_nonneg _)`). The other facts the proof uses need no hypothesis on `P`: `prefixBoundConstant_pos P m`, `OutgoingTail.flattenLength_pos` and `TailEnergyBounds.tailConstant_pos`. Check each conjunct with p, c, f, t > 0, except c ≥ 0. (1) p ≤ 1+p+c+2f+4t needs c ≥ 0. (2) c ≤ 1+p+c+2f+4t needs p > 0. (3) t/2 ≤ … holds because 4t ≥ t/2 and every other term is ≥ 0. (4) f+2t ≤ … holds since 2f ≥ f and 4t ≥ 2t. So the proof works without `hP` if `hc` is replaced by `hc : 0 ≤ correctionEnergyConstant P m := by unfold correctionEnergyConstant; positivity`, and the same `linarith only` calls still close the goals. `P` itself stays, since the conclusion mentions it.

### [`PerUnitMerge.complement_map_injective`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/LatticeTriangle/Solution.lean#L4569-L4583) — `lean-pool-0974`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0974.lean`

- `(hb_pos : 1 ≤ b)` (theorem): The only case `hb_pos` excludes is b = 0, and the statement still holds there. `hb_lt` then gives d > 0. From v % d = 0 and d ∣ n, d divides n - v; since v < n, also n - v ≥ d. So n - v - d = n - v - (d - 0) is a multiple of d, with no truncation. For multiples of d, equal quotients by d mean equal values, so n - v₁ = n - v₂, and v₁ = v₂ because both are < n. This is the same argument as for b ≥ 1, where n - v is a positive number ≡ d - b (mod d), hence ≥ d - b. Proof sketch: get the divisibility of n - vᵢ - (d - b) by d and the bound d - b ≤ n - vᵢ directly (e.g. `Nat.dvd_sub` with d ∣ n and d ∣ vᵢ, then omega). Then use the cancellation for exact division as the current proof does (`Nat.div_mul_cancel`, then omega). The `n < 2 * v` conditions are not needed. `hb_lt` can be deleted as well (see the other entry).
- `(hb_lt : b < d)` (theorem): If `hb_lt` fails, i.e. d ≤ b, the conclusion holds vacuously. If d = 0, then `hd_dvd : 0 ∣ n` forces n = 0, so `v₁ < n` is impossible. If d > 0, then v₁ % d < d ≤ b (`Nat.mod_lt`), which contradicts v₁ % d = b. When b < d, the existing proof applies unchanged. A new proof splits on `b < d`: use the current argument in that case, and in the other case derive a contradiction from the hypotheses on v₁ (`Nat.mod_lt`, or `Nat.eq_zero_of_zero_dvd` when d = 0, plus omega). `hb_pos` can be deleted as well, independently or together with this one.

### [`integrand_bound_on_annulus`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/LeanModularForms/GeneralizedResidueTheory/PVInfrastructure/StepBounds.lean#L769-L784) — `lean-pool-0999`

Snippet: `samples/lean-pool/lean-pool-0999.lean`

- `(ht_pos : 0 < |t - t₀|)` (theorem): Without ht_pos, only the case t = t₀ is new. If t ≠ t₀, ht_pos follows from abs_pos.mpr (sub_ne_zero.mpr h) and the original proof (norm_sub_norm_le + linarith with hr_bounded) applies. If t = t₀, the left side is ‖(γ t₀ - γ t₀)⁻¹ * deriv γ t₀‖ = ‖0⁻¹ * _‖ = 0 (since (0:ℂ)⁻¹ = 0), and the right side is |0|⁻¹ + C = 0 + C = C (since (0:ℝ)⁻¹ = 0). So it suffices that 0 ≤ C. Here ht_lt gives 0 = |t₀ - t₀| < δ₀. Taking t' = t₀ + δ₀/2 gives 0 < |t' - t₀| = δ₀/2 < δ₀, so hr_bounded t' yields 0 ≤ ‖…‖ ≤ C. Hence the inequality holds in every case, and the proof only needs a case split on t = t₀.


## FLT (Anthropic) (203 flagged)

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_full_eq_adjoin_primes.ModularCurve.W1.roots_prime_at_slot_roots_nodup`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_full_eq_adjoin_primes.lean#L306-L312) — `flt-anthropic-0001`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0001.lean`

- `(hζ : IsPrimitiveRoot (ζ : K) N)` (theorem): The conclusion never mentions N or ζ. It only says that the roots in LaurentSeries K of P := Φ_p(X, twisted j(q^{pe})) have no duplicates. The hypotheses hζ and hpN are only there so that K contains a primitive p-th root of unity, which lets P split explicitly over LaurentSeries K. The statement still holds without them, by base change. Let L be a field extension of K containing a primitive p-th root of unity ζ', e.g. CyclotomicField p K. This exists because char K = 0 (from [Algebra ℚ K]), so (p : K) ≠ 0. Let f = algebraMap K L. The library's coeffMap f : LaurentSeries K →+* LaurentSeries L acts coefficientwise. It commutes with qExpand (the library lemma coeffMap_qExpand). It commutes with qTwist, with u replaced by Units.map f u, because qTwistFun multiplies coefficient k by u^k. It also satisfies coeffMap f ∘ coeffEmb K = coeffEmb L, by uniqueness of ℚ-algebra maps and coeffMap_coeffMap. So P.map (coeffMap f) is the same polynomial built over L. In L, apply the existing lemmas with N := p, ζ := ζ' (so hpN is dvd_refl): roots_prime_at_slot gives p+1 roots and roots_prime_at_slot_nodup says they are distinct. The mapped polynomial is monic of degree p+1 (data.monic, natDegree_eq), so it splits. By Mathlib's Polynomial.nodup_roots_iff_of_splits it is separable. Polynomial.separable_map, using that LaurentSeries K is a field, then makes P separable, and Polynomial.nodup_roots gives P.roots.Nodup. No degenerate case is an issue: K is an arbitrary characteristic-0 field, which is exactly the case the base change handles. The proof needs this rewrite, but every Mathlib lemma it uses was found in the sources.
- `(hpN : p ∣ N)` (theorem): Same argument as for hζ. The conclusion does not involve N or ζ. The base change to L = CyclotomicField p K, applying roots_prime_at_slot and roots_prime_at_slot_nodup there with N := p and a primitive p-th root of unity of L, together with Polynomial.nodup_roots_iff_of_splits, Polynomial.separable_map and Polynomial.nodup_roots, proves the statement without assuming that p divides N.
- `[NeZero N]` (theorem): N enters only through hζ and hpN, and the conclusion does not depend on N. The base-change proof described for hζ uses neither the given N nor the given ζ. It works in CyclotomicField p K with N := p, where NeZero p comes from p being prime. So the statement holds without NeZero N; N = 0, where IsPrimitiveRoot ζ 0 means ζ has infinite order, is no problem.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_FullLevel_mem_of_coe_mem_nonunits_of_isMaximal_of_mem_ssJSet_chartAlgFin.P3aW_node14.chartAlgFin_eq_of_mem_of_mem`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_FullLevel_mem_of_coe_mem_nonunits_of_isMaximal_of_mem_ssJSet_chartAlgFin.lean#L96-L99) — `flt-anthropic-0008`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0008.lean`

- `[Fact (a ≠ 0)]` (theorem): The statement does not need this instance to elaborate. `chartAlgFin R F j` is the abbrev `chartAlg R F {j}` (Definitions/Def_AlgebraicCurve_TwoChartIntegralModel.lean:142). `chartAlg S` is defined as the subalgebra `{x | IsIntegral (Algebra.adjoin R S) x}`, which does not depend on `j ≠ 0`. The same file shows that the abbrev does not take the `Fact`: `coe_jChartFin` is stated with `omit [Fact (j ≠ 0)] in` and still mentions `jChartFin R F j : chartAlgFin R F j`, which would fail to elaborate otherwise. The theorem is also true without the assumption, since the argument is pure integrality. The current proof only uses the `Fact`s to call `chartAlgFin_le_of_mem` (lines 77-94 of the same file), which takes them as binders, but that proof never uses them. It uses only `adjoin_le_chartAlg`, `Algebra.adjoin_le`, `IsIntegral.map_of_comp_eq` and `isIntegral_trans`, none of which need nonzeroness. Inlining that proof twice, or dropping the `Fact` binders from `chartAlgFin_le_of_mem` as well, gives the antisymmetry without this hypothesis. The case `a = 0` is covered: then `chartAlgFin R F 0` is the integral closure of the image of R, and the same argument applies.
- `[Fact (b ≠ 0)]` (theorem): The argument is the same as for `[Fact (a ≠ 0)]`. `chartAlgFin R F b = chartAlg R F {b}` does not take a `Fact (b ≠ 0)` instance; this is shown by the `omit [Fact (j ≠ 0)] in` on `coe_jChartFin`, which still elaborates `chartAlgFin R F j`. The inclusion proof in `chartAlgFin_le_of_mem` (adjoin ≤ chartAlg, then `isIntegral_trans` through the integral extension `Algebra.adjoin R {b} → chartAlgFin R F b`) never uses `b ≠ 0`. Inlining that proof, or removing the unused `Fact` binders from the helper lemma, proves the equality by `le_antisymm` without this hypothesis. Each of the two `Fact` binders can be removed on its own, and both can also be removed together.

### [`P2MW.S_exists_isIdempotentElem_mul_iterate_eq_zero_sum_iterate_eq_one_of_not_isField.C2idemCore.IdempotentOrbit.exists_idempotent_orbit_or_isField`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_exists_isIdempotentElem_mul_iterate_eq_zero_sum_iterate_eq_one_of_not_isField.lean#L172-L278) — `flt-anthropic-0014`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0014.lean`

- `[Finite ι]` (theorem): The other hypotheses force ι to be finite. Empty ι: R is trivial (φ.toEquiv.subsingleton) and the first disjunct holds with e = 0, as in the existing proof. Otherwise pick j₀. Let t = conj φ s; t^n = id with n ≥ 1, so t is a ring automorphism of P = Π i, F i. Each F i is a field, so the idempotents of P are exactly the {0,1}-valued functions, i.e. indicators of arbitrary subsets of ι, even when ι is infinite. Both t and t⁻¹ preserve the order e ≤ e' ↔ e*e' = e, so t sends the minimal nonzero idempotents ind {i} to each other: t (ind {i}) = ind {ψ i} for a permutation ψ. From t^n = id and injectivity of ind on singletons, ψ^[n] = id, so the orbit O = (range n).image (ψ^[·] j₀) is a finite set mapped onto itself by ψ. Since ind O is a finite sum of singleton indicators, t (ind O) = ind (ψ '' O) = ind O. So a = φ.symm (ind O) is an idempotent fixed by s. By hfix, a = f x; since f is injective, x is an idempotent of the field k, hence 0 or 1, so ind O is 0 or 1. As j₀ ∈ O, ind O ≠ 0, so ind O = 1, which gives O = univ. Hence ι is finite. Then Fintype.ofFinite supplies the [Fintype ι] that the helper exists_indexMap_of_iterate_eq needs, and the existing proof applies unchanged. The statement is therefore still true and provable without [Finite ι].

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_XOneP_ringEquiv_algEquiv_symm_eq_algEquiv_diamond_of_generatorLaw_x1_mul.Conj6.comp_heckeDiag_ne_zero`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_XOneP_ringEquiv_algEquiv_symm_eq_algEquiv_diamond_of_generatorLaw_x1_mul.lean#L664-L678) — `flt-anthropic-0018`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0018.lean`

- `(hp : p ≠ 0)` (theorem): `ModularForm.heckeDiagMatrix p` is defined as `if hp : p = 0 then 1 else upperTriangularGL p 0 1 _` (Definitions/Def_ModularForm_HeckeOperator.lean), and the library proves `heckeDiagMatrix_zero : heckeDiagMatrix 0 = 1`. The action of GL (Fin 2) ℝ on ℍ is Mathlib's `UpperHalfPlane.glAction`, a `MulAction`, so `1 • τ = τ` by `one_smul`. When p = 0 the function in the conclusion is therefore `fun τ => F τ = F`, which is nonzero by `hF`. Proof without hp: split on `p = 0`. If p = 0, `simp [ModularForm.heckeDiagMatrix_zero]` reduces the goal to `F ≠ 0`, which is `hF`. If p ≠ 0, the existing proof works with the case hypothesis in place of hp. hp appears nowhere else in the statement.

So the proof can start with `have hN0 : N ≠ 0 := by rintro rfl; exact hN3 2 Nat.prime_two (dvd_zero _)` and keep the rest of the existing argument unchanged. ### [`P2MW.S_ExtCitation_LocalLevel_exists_level_frobenius_pow_dvd_and_apply_eq.P2mS17LB.main`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ExtCitation_LocalLevel_exists_level_frobenius_pow_dvd_and_apply_eq.lean#L49-L111) — `flt-anthropic-0046`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0046.lean`

- `(hn : 0 < n)` (theorem): `hn` follows from `hα : α ^ n = ((q : ℕ) : AlgebraicClosure ℚ)`. If `n = 0`, then `hα` reads `1 = ((q : ℕ) : AlgebraicClosure ℚ)`. `AlgebraicClosure ℚ` is a field of characteristic zero, so `Nat.cast_eq_one` (or `Nat.cast_injective`) gives `(q : ℕ) = 1`. That contradicts `q.2 : (q : ℕ).Prime`, for example via `Nat.Prime.one_lt`. So the proof can open with `have hn : 0 < n := Nat.pos_of_ne_zero (by rintro rfl; rw [pow_zero] at hα; exact q.2.ne_one (by exact_mod_cast hα.symm))`, and the rest of the proof stays as it is. The proof uses `hn` only through `hn.ne'` and `Nat.mul_pos hn _`. The other hypotheses do look needed. `hd` is needed: with `d = 0`, `n * d ∣ m` forces `m = 0`, but every finite-level `H q F` contains a positive power of `φ` (by `exists_frobenius_pow_inv_mul_mem_inertia_sup_level`). `[FiniteDimensional ℚ F₀]` is needed because the conclusion asks for a finite-dimensional `F ≥ F₀`.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_principal_le_closure_divisor_laurentIntegral_diamondAutHBar.PLCDSol.exists_gamma0_apply_eq`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_principal_le_closure_divisor_laurentIntegral_diamondAutHBar.lean#L883-L889) — `flt-anthropic-0048`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0048.lean`

- `[NeZero M]` (variable): The theorem takes [NeZero M] automatically because it is instance-implicit and M is an argument. The proof only uses it through `CohCarrier.gamma0Units_surjective`, which requires [NeZero M]. The statement is true for M = 0 as well. Mathlib defines `Gamma0 N` as `{g | (g 1 0 : ZMod N) = 0}`, and `ZMod 0 = ℤ`, so membership in `Gamma0 0` means the lower-left entry is 0. By `Int.units_eq_one_or`, d = 1 or d = -1. For d = 1 take γ = 1: it lies in Gamma0 0 and γ 0 0 = 1. For d = -1 take γ = -1 in SL(2,ℤ): its lower-left entry is 0 and γ 0 0 = -1, which casts to -1 = d. Proof without the instance: case on M = 0 and use these witnesses. Otherwise build `⟨h⟩ : NeZero M` locally and run the existing proof. The statement mentions no other instance, so dropping it (via `omit [NeZero M] in`) leaves the statement well-formed. This proof has not been compiled.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_relfinrank_qExpFunctionFieldC_gammaH_gammaH_inf_gamma0_mul_eq_add_one.CohCarrier.gammaH_inf_gamma0_mul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_relfinrank_qExpFunctionFieldC_gammaH_gammaH_inf_gamma0_mul_eq_add_one.lean#L104-L128) — `flt-anthropic-0049`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0049.lean`

- `[NeZero N]` (theorem): The statement does not need it to elaborate. `CohCarrier.GammaH M H := (H.comap (gamma0Units M)).map (Gamma0 M).subtype` is defined for any `M : ℕ` with no NeZero assumption (Definitions/Def_CohCarrier_Level.lean), and so are `gamma0Units` and `Gamma0_d_mul_a`. `ZMod.unitsMap hm := Units.map (castHom hm _)` needs only divisibility. The proof never uses it: it only uses `map_intCast`, `mem_GammaH_iff`, `Subgroup.mem_comap`, `Gamma0_mem` and `map_zero`, plus `simp` with `gamma0Units`, `Gamma0Map`, `ZMod.unitsMap_def` and `hcast`, and none of these needs NeZero. If simp turns castHom into ZMod.cast, `ZMod.cast_intCast` (from memory: needs `m ∣ n` and CharP, not NeZero) handles it. The statement is also true when N = 0, where ZMod 0 = ℤ: reducing mod N preserves the lower-left entry being 0 and the image of the (1,1) entry. It can be dropped on its own or together with [NeZero (N * ℓ)].
- `[NeZero (N * ℓ)]` (theorem): The same reasoning applies: `GammaH (N*ℓ)`, `Gamma0 (N*ℓ)` and `ZMod.unitsMap (dvd_mul_right N ℓ)` all elaborate with no NeZero instance, and no step of the proof uses one. Only the next theorem in the file uses `ZMod.unitsMap_surjective`, which requires `[NeZero m]`; this proof does not. Mathematically the identity GammaH N H' ⊓ Gamma0 (N ℓ) = GammaH (N ℓ) (comap H') holds for all N and ℓ: an element of Gamma0 (Nℓ) is in Gamma0 N by reducing mod N, and its unit image in (ZMod N)ˣ is the reduction of its image in (ZMod (Nℓ))ˣ. This is also true when Nℓ = 0. It can be dropped on its own or together with [NeZero N].

### [`TateCurve.pointX_inv_mul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/Definitions/Def_TateCurve_XMultStructure.lean#L171-L175) — `flt-anthropic-0057`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0057.lean`

- `(hu0 : u ≠ 0)` (theorem): If u = 0, then u⁻¹ * v = 0⁻¹ * v = 0 (inv_zero, zero_mul) and u * v⁻¹ = 0 * v⁻¹ = 0. Both sides become `pointX q 0`, so the goal is closed by `subst; simp`. If u ≠ 0, the existing proof applies, and its only use of hu0 is to get `u * v⁻¹ ≠ 0` for `pointX_inv`. A proof is `by_cases hu : u = 0; · simp [hu]` followed by the current proof. This can be deleted together with hv0 and hq0.
- `(hv0 : v ≠ 0)` (theorem): If v = 0, then u⁻¹ * 0 = 0 and u * 0⁻¹ = u * 0 = 0 (inv_zero, mul_zero). Both sides are `pointX q 0`, so the case is closed by `simp`. Otherwise the existing proof applies, and its only use of hv0 is the non-zeroness argument passed to `pointX_inv`. This can be deleted independently of, or together with, hu0.
- `(hq0 : q ≠ 0)` (theorem): The only use of hq0 is to call `TateCurve.pointX_inv` (proved in P2M/Sol/S_TateCurve_pointX_inv.lean). That proof needs q ≠ 0 only to get `q ^ n * w ≠ 0` before applying `xfun_inv`. It can be redone without hq0. For each n, `xTerm q w⁻¹ (-n) = xfun ((q^n)⁻¹ * w⁻¹) = xfun ((q^n * w)⁻¹)`, using zpow_neg and mul_inv, which hold in any field even when q = 0. If `q^n * w = 0`, both sides are `xfun 0` by inv_zero. Otherwise `xfun_inv` (hypotheses w' ≠ 0 and w' ≠ 1, the second given by `hdiv n`) gives `xfun (q^n*w)`. The rest of that proof (`tsum_comp_neg`, `tsum_congr`, `congr 1` on `pointX`) does not use q ≠ 0. Concretely, when q = 0 only the n = 0 term is nonzero: `xfun w⁻¹ = xfun w` with w ≠ 1 from `hdiv 0`, or w = 0 trivially. So the statement still holds without hq0.

### [`P2MW.S_IsDedekindDomain_HeightOneSpectrum_Extension_relIndex_adicCompletionIntegers_comap_sub_mulLeft_eq_absNorm_pow_min_of_ramificationIdx_eq_one.P2mLocalIndex.main`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_IsDedekindDomain_HeightOneSpectrum_Extension_relIndex_adicCompletionIntegers_comap_sub_mulLeft_eq_absNorm_pow_min_of_ramificationIdx_eq_one.lean#L843-L876) — `flt-anthropic-0059`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0059.lean`

- `(hres : ∃ y : w.1.adicCompletion L, ‖y‖ ≤ 1 ∧ ‖θ y - y‖ = 1)` (theorem): Implied by hw, hθ and hprime. `w : v.Extension (𝓞 L)` means `w.1.under (𝓞 K) = v`, so hw says w is unramified over v. The file proves `isField_V` from hw alone: V = k_v ⊗ O_w is a field of degree d = [L_w:K_v] over k_v, so the local extension is unramified with residue degree d. By hθ, θ has order d ≥ |Aut(L_w/K_v)|, so L_w/K_v is Galois (cyclic) with group generated by θ. In an unramified Galois extension the inertia group is trivial, so reduction is an isomorphism Gal(L_w/K_v) ≅ Gal(k_w/k_v), and θ maps to θ̄ of order d. Since d is prime (hprime), d ≥ 2 and θ̄ ≠ 1. Pick ȳ with θ̄ȳ ≠ ȳ and lift it to y ∈ O_w: then ‖y‖ ≤ 1, and θy − y is integral but not in the maximal ideal, hence a unit, so ‖θy − y‖ = 1. In the proof, hres is only used to get `sigmaV_ne_one`, which this argument supplies; the rest of the proof is unchanged.
- `(hprime : (Module.finrank (v.adicCompletion K) (w.1.adicCompletion L)).Prime)` (theorem): The proof uses hprime only for d > 0 (`hprime.pos`) and to get `orderOf_sigmaV` through `orderOf_eq_prime`. First, d > 0 holds anyway because L_w/K_v is finite-dimensional. Second, orderOf σ = d holds without primality. hθ makes L_w/K_v Galois with group ⟨θ⟩, and hw (via `isField_V`) makes it unramified. Then O_w = O_v[α] with α lifting a generator of the residue extension, and the minimal polynomial of α has separable reduction (finite fields). So θ^k(α) ≡ α mod ϖ forces θ^k(α) = α, i.e. θ^k = id. Hence σ^k ≠ 1 for 0 < k < d, and σ^d = 1 by `sigmaV_pow_finrank`. With orderOf σ = d, the rest of `finrank_ker_le_one` (fixed field of ⟨σ⟩ is k_v, Hilbert-90 style kernel bound) and the rest of the proof go through unchanged. Degenerate cases: hres forces θ ≠ id, so d ≥ 2; and in the d = 1 case the formula q^{min(s,m)} can be checked directly. Each of hres and hprime is claimed deletable on its own (the other kept); deleting both together was not checked.

So J₁ f ∈ F by `F.sub_mem`. The proof then goes through unchanged after `have hF₁ := ...`. Only one of hF₁, hF₂, hF₃ can be deleted at a time: each is derived from the other two.
- `(hF₂ : ∀ f ∈ F, J₂ f ∈ F)` (theorem): This follows from hF₁, hF₃ and h31. For f ∈ F, h31 gives J₂ f = J₃ (J₁ f) - J₁ (J₃ f). Both terms lie in F by hF₁ and hF₃, so J₂ f ∈ F by `F.sub_mem`. The rest of the proof is unchanged. This cannot be combined with deleting hF₁ or hF₃.
- `(hF₃ : ∀ f ∈ F, J₃ f ∈ F)` (theorem): This follows from hF₁, hF₂ and h12. For f ∈ F, h12 gives J₃ f = J₁ (J₂ f) - J₂ (J₁ f). Both terms lie in F by hF₁ and hF₂, so J₃ f ∈ F by `F.sub_mem`. The rest of the proof is unchanged. This cannot be combined with deleting hF₁ or hF₂.
- `(hskew₃ : ∀ x ∈ F, ∀ y ∈ F, B (J₃ x) y = -B x (J₃ y))` (theorem): This follows from hskew₁, hskew₂, h12, hlin, hsymm and the invariance hypotheses. The proof already derives hBsub (subtraction in the first argument, from hlin) and hBsub' (subtraction in the second argument, from hlin and hsymm). For x, y ∈ F, all of J₁J₂x, J₂J₁x, J₁J₂y and J₂J₁y lie in F. Then B (J₃ x) y = B (J₁J₂x - J₂J₁x) y = B (J₁J₂x) y - B (J₂J₁x) y = -B (J₂x) (J₁y) + B (J₁x) (J₂y) = B x (J₂J₁y) - B x (J₁J₂y) = -B x (J₁J₂y - J₂J₁y) = -B x (J₃ y). The steps use h12, hBsub, hskew₁ and hskew₂ twice each, hBsub', and h12 again. Proving this right after hBsub' leaves the rest of the proof unchanged. Only one of hskew₁, hskew₂, hskew₃ can be deleted at a time.
- `(hskew₁ : ∀ x ∈ F, ∀ y ∈ F, B (J₁ x) y = -B x (J₁ y))` (theorem): This is the same argument with h23 (J₁ = J₂J₃ - J₃J₂ on F): B (J₁ x) y = B (J₂J₃x) y - B (J₃J₂x) y = B x (J₃J₂y) - B x (J₂J₃y) = -B x (J₁ y). It uses hskew₂, hskew₃, hBsub and hBsub', which come from hlin and hsymm. The rest of the proof is unchanged. This cannot be combined with deleting hskew₂ or hskew₃.
- `(hskew₂ : ∀ x ∈ F, ∀ y ∈ F, B (J₂ x) y = -B x (J₂ y))` (theorem): This is the same argument with h31 (J₂ = J₃J₁ - J₁J₃ on F): B (J₂ x) y = B (J₃J₁x) y - B (J₁J₃x) y = B x (J₁J₃y) - B x (J₃J₁y) = -B x (J₂ y). It uses hskew₁, hskew₃, hBsub and hBsub'. The rest of the proof is unchanged. This cannot be combined with deleting hskew₁ or hskew₃.

For any `x : E`, `x ^ p ^ 0` might not lie in `M`, but `x ^ p ^ r = iterateFrobenius E p r x` lies in `(iterateFrobenius E p r).fieldRange`, which equals `(algebraMap M E).fieldRange` by `hrange`, so `x ^ p ^ r ∈ (algebraMap M E).range` (via `RingHom.mem_fieldRange`).       θ t = θ' (Subalgebra.inclusion (CuspForm.heckeAlgebra_mono (Finset.coe_subset.mpr hS₀)) t))` (theorem): The proof never refers to `hθ`. The only name in the proof that starts with `hθ` is `hθ'U`, which is a different hypothesis. No other binder or the conclusion mentions `hθ`, so deleting it does not affect elaboration: `θ` is still bound and still determines the types of `Ψ`, `hΨ`, `u` and `hu`.

The proof does not need it. It gets the points `χ i` from `CuspForm.heckeLocal.exists_points_jointly_injective_of_charpoly_frobenius_eq_of_isAbsolutelyIrreducible`. I read that lemma's signature: it takes hp2, hp𝒪, ρbar, habs, S, S₀, hS, hpS, hS₀, N, hNS, hNS₀, hNp, hunr, hst, hsq, hord, θ', hT, hU0, hU1 and hTp, and does not involve θ or hθ. So the existing proof compiles unchanged without `hθ`, and the theorem stays true.

### [`P2MW.S_NumberField_TateGlobal_zetaIntegral_meromorphic_continuation_fe.M4aD2Skeleton.zetaIntegral_eq_setIntegral_theta'`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_NumberField_TateGlobal_zetaIntegral_meromorphic_continuation_fe.lean#L6363-L6392) — `flt-anthropic-0122`
