- `(hD'm : MeasurableSet D')` (theorem): The proof uses hD'm only in its call to `MeasureTheory.setIntegral_congr_fun hD'm`, and that step does not need it. The pointwise identity there holds for every x, with no condition on x ∈ D'. `hterm` is proved for an arbitrary x using only `chi_mul_principal`, `ideleNorm_mul_principal`, `mul_comm` and `mul_assoc`. After `tsum_congr hterm, tsum_mul_right`, the left side is `(∑' a, f ↑(x * ↑a)) * (χ x * ‖x‖^s)`. This is definitionally `ThetaSum F f x * (...)`, since `ThetaSum` is defined as exactly that tsum (line 5808), which is why the final `rfl` closes the goal. So the two integrands are equal as functions. The step can therefore be replaced by `congr 1; funext x` (or `integral_congr_ae (Filter.Eventually.of_forall ...)` for the restricted measure), which needs no measurability of D'. The other step, `M4aD2Glue.integral_eq_setIntegral_tsum_smul` (line 5498), takes only `IsFundamentalDomain G D μ` and `Integrable f μ`, not `MeasurableSet D`. D' appears elsewhere in the statement only through hD' and the set integral, neither of which needs hD'm to elaborate.
- `[BorelSpace (AdeleRing (𝓞 F) F)]` (theorem): No other part of the statement needs this instance. `ideleNorm` (Def_NumberField_TateGlobalZeta.lean:19) is `distribHaarChar (AdeleRing ..) x` with no measurable-space argument. `zetaIntegral` takes only a measurable space on the units. `schwartzBruhat` and `ThetaSum` take no measure-theoretic instances. `NumberField.TateGlobal.integrable_zetaIntegrand` (Theorems/Thm_NumberField_TateGlobal_integrable_zetaIntegrand.lean) takes only `MeasurableSpace`/`BorelSpace` on the units. The only place the proof uses the instance is `ideleNorm_mul_principal F a x`, whose statement `ideleNorm F (↑a * x) = ideleNorm F x` does not involve the measurable structure. It can be called with a locally chosen Borel structure, `@ideleNorm_mul_principal F _ _ (borel _) ⟨rfl⟩ a x`, which proves the same equality. So the theorem stays provable without this instance. (`[MeasurableSpace (AdeleRing (𝓞 F) F)]` is then also unused, but it cannot be deleted while the BorelSpace binder, which mentions it, is still present, so only the BorelSpace binder is reported as deletable on its own.)

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0123.lean`

- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (theorem): The statement stays true without the hypothesis. Under the remaining hypotheses (1 ≤ r, 0 < α, 0 < δ), the left side is C times three factors: (1 + (r*α)⁻¹^2) > 0, (1 + (r*δ)⁻¹^2) > 0, and min 1 ((r*α)⁻¹^N) + min 1 ((r*δ)⁻¹^N) ≥ 0. Call their product X ≥ 0. If C ≤ 0, the left side is ≤ 0. Taking B = 0 then works: 0 ≤ B holds and the right side is 0. Proof without hC: case-split on 0 ≤ C, using the existing proof in the positive case and B = 0 otherwise (the left side is nonpositive by mul_nonpos_of_nonpos_of_nonneg-style reasoning). Alternatively, use the witness max C 0 * (1 + e^2) * (1 + D⁻¹) * (e^N + d^N), which is nonnegative, and first bound C * X ≤ max C 0 * X since X ≥ 0; the existing calc then runs with max C 0 in place of C. The other hypotheses are genuinely needed when C > 0. Without hc, H can be arbitrarily small, so α → 0 makes the left side unbounded while the right side stays near B. Without hD, the constraint D ≤ αδ is vacuous: fix H = c, take α = cδ and let δ → 0.

### [`P2MW.S_WeierstrassCurve_exists_prePsi_eq_expand.WeierstrassCurve.Universal.Ψ₂Sq_𝓦p_ne_zero`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_WeierstrassCurve_exists_prePsi_eq_expand.lean#L273-L277) — `flt-anthropic-0125`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0125.lean`

- `(hp2 : p ≠ 2)` (theorem): Mathlib defines Ψ₂Sq = C 4 * X^3 + C b₂ * X^2 + C (2*b₄) * X + C b₆, with b₂ = a₁^2 + 4*a₂. For 𝓦p p = ⟨X 0, X 1, X 2, X 3, X 4⟩ over Rp p = MvPolynomial (Fin 5) (ZMod p), the degree-2 coefficient of Ψ₂Sq is X 0 ^ 2 + 4 * X 1. Its MvPolynomial coefficient at Finsupp.single 0 2 is 1 + 4*0 = 1, which is nonzero because ZMod p is nontrivial for any prime p, including p = 2. So Ψ₂Sq ≠ 0 for every prime p. Only the current proof needs hp2, because it uses the degree-3 coefficient 4, which vanishes when p = 2. Replacement proof: take Polynomial.coeff _ 2 of h and simp to X 0 ^ 2 + 4 * X 1 = 0, then take MvPolynomial.coeff (Finsupp.single 0 2) of both sides to get 1 = 0 in ZMod p, contradicting nontriviality (this uses Fact p.Prime). Fact p.Prime itself cannot be dropped: for p = 1, ZMod 1 is the zero ring and Ψ₂Sq = 0.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_FullLevel_supersingularProlongation_exists_finite_generators_regular_off_ends_residueField_of_eq_three_of_dvd.AlgebraicCurve.HolRingFGAux.exists_fin_generators`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_FullLevel_supersingularProlongation_exists_finite_generators_regular_off_ends_residueField_of_eq_three_of_dvd.lean#L590-L610) — `flt-anthropic-0132`

- `(hN : N.Nonempty)` (theorem): hN is only used to call holRing_fg (via exists_fn, which needs a point of N). For N = ∅ the statement still holds with n = 0: the first conjunct is vacuous (Fin 0 is empty), and for the second, if f = 0 it lies in any subalgebra. If f ≠ 0 and f is in every place's valuation ring, then 0 ≤ v.ord f for all v (mem_toValuationSubring_iff_ord / ord_nonneg_iff in the library). HasPrincipalDivisors (a parent of IsCurveOver) gives a divisor D with D v = v.ord f and degree D = 0. Since k is algebraically closed, every place has degree 1 (IsCurveOver.deg_eq_one_of_isAlgClosed), so degree D = Σ_{v ∈ support} D v (degree_eq_sum in the same file). A zero sum of nonnegative terms forces every v.ord f = 0. Then AlgebraicCurve.Place.mem_range_algebraMap_of_forall_ord_eq_zero_of_isAlgClosed, applied to the separating transcendental j from IsCurveOver.exists_separating_transcendental (which needs PerfectField k, from IsAlgClosed, and EssFiniteType), gives f ∈ range (algebraMap k K) ⊆ Algebra.adjoin k ∅. So: split on N.eq_empty_or_nonempty, use this argument with n = 0 in the empty case, and keep the existing proof otherwise.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_contDiff_and_finsum_smoothedFundamental_eq_one.ModularCurve.SmoothedFundamentalProof.finite_support_smul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_contDiff_and_finsum_smoothedFundamental_eq_one.lean#L435-L442) — `flt-anthropic-0144`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0144.lean`

- `[Γ.FiniteIndex]` (variable): `ModularCurve.smoothedFundamental Γ T z` is a `finsum` over `SL(2,ℤ) ⧸ Γ` (Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_SmoothedFundamental.lean, line 170). That definition needs no finiteness; with infinite index the finsum may take the junk value 0, which only shrinks the support. The current proof uses FiniteIndex only through `carrier`/`isCompact_carrierH`/`mem_carrier_of_ne_zero`, i.e. through the Fintype instance on the quotient. Alternative proof without it: let S = {δ : SL(2,ℤ) | puCut T (mob δ τ) ≠ 0}, which is finite by `hasFiniteSupport_puCut_mob T τ` (no Γ, no FiniteIndex). If smoothedFundamental Γ T (γ•τ) ≠ 0, then by the contrapositive of `finsum_eq_zero_of_forall_eq_zero` (additive form of `finprod_eq_one_of_forall_eq_one`) some coset q has puCut T (mob q.out (γ•τ)) ≠ 0. By `coe_smul` and `mob_mob` this equals puCut T (mob (q.out * γ) τ), so δ := q.out*γ ∈ S. Since γ ∈ Γ, (q.out)⁻¹δ = γ ∈ Γ, so ⟦δ⟧ = q (left cosets) and γ = (⟦δ⟧.out)⁻¹ * δ. Hence the support lies in the image of the finite set S under δ ↦ (⟦δ⟧.out)⁻¹δ and is finite. Degenerate cases: infinite index only shrinks the support; when Γ is trivial the support still injects into S.

### [`P2MW.S_GoodReductionJacobian_RelativeGroupLaw_isFinite_and_flat_of_surjective_of_isFinite_pullback_snd.P2mFlatIsog.ringKrullDim_stalk_eq`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_GoodReductionJacobian_RelativeGroupLaw_isFinite_and_flat_of_surjective_of_isFinite_pullback_snd.lean#L246-L300) — `flt-anthropic-0150`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0150.lean`

- `[IsIntegral Y]` (theorem): The statement stays true without `[IsIntegral Y]`. The only place the proof uses it is to show that φ = h.app V : A := Γ(Y,V) → B := Γ(X,h⁻¹V) is injective (through `basicOpen_eq_bot_iff`, which needs Y reduced), and to treat A as a domain in `height_comap_eq`.

Without the hypothesis, an affine-local argument works. Let I = ker φ. Since h is surjective, the induced map Spec B → Spec A is surjective, so every prime of A contains I. Hence I ⊆ nilradical(A); in fact the earlier basic-open argument shows directly that ker φ consists of nilpotents. Because B is a domain (X is integral and h⁻¹V is a nonempty affine open), A/I embeds into B, so A/I is a domain. Also:
- A/I is finitely generated over K, as a quotient of A, which is finite type through fY (`exists_algebra_finiteType`, which does not need integrality).
- B is integral over A/I.

So `height_comap_eq K` applies to the injective integral map A/I → B. It gives ht(p/I) = ht(q), where q = px and p = py = comap q. Since I lies in every prime of A, the primes of A below p correspond to the primes of A/I below p/I, so ht p = ht(p/I). The two `IsLocalization.AtPrime ... ringKrullDim_eq_height` rewrites need neither integrality nor reducedness of Y, so they still finish the proof.

Degenerate cases: Y is nonempty because it contains h.base x. Y is irreducible as the image of the irreducible X under the surjection h, so nothing else is lost.

Geometrically, h factors through Y_red, and the local rings of Y and Y_red differ only by nilpotents, which leaves the Krull dimension unchanged.

The proof never uses it either. It builds `IsIrreducible ρ` from `hirr` and `Nontrivial V`, gets `Invertible (Nat.card (GL2 q) : K)` from `CharZero`, then applies `Representation.char_orthonormal ρ ρ`. That lemma only assumes `[Fintype G] [Invertible (Nat.card G : k)] [IsAlgClosed k] [IsIrreducible ρ] [IsIrreducible σ]`; it derives finite-dimensionality internally via `ρ.asModuleEquiv.finiteDimensional`. On paper it also follows from the other hypotheses: for any nonzero v (V is nontrivial), the span of the finite orbit G·v is a nonzero subrepresentation, so by `hirr` it is all of V, hence V is finite-dimensional. So the theorem holds and the same proof goes through without this binder.

- `[IsNoetherianRing A]` (variable): ChartData f only needs [CommRing A]. In V_eq_O the Noetherian hypotheses are used only through isNoetherian_X, which makes Uo a Noetherian space and so gives QuasiCompact D.ψ. That is what makes X' = ψ.image integral (Mathlib's `IsDominant f.toImage` instance needs `[QuasiCompact f]`), and that feeds irreducibleSpace_X'/isIntegral_X', IsDominant ψk (used by ak_eq_bk) and the preconnectedness of O k. QuasiCompact D.ψ also follows from IsSeparated f alone. Each U k is affine: c k is a closed immersion into an affine Spec, closed immersions are IsAffineHom (ClosedImmersion.lean:147), and `isAffine_of_isAffineHom` applies; so U k is compact. X is quasi-separated (f separated, target affine). So Uo, a finite intersection of compact opens, is compact; X itself is compact as the finite union of the U k, which covers m = 0. Then Uo.ι is quasi-compact by `quasiCompact_of_compactSpace`. pullback.fst f D.prπ is proper (as in the `IsProper D.π` instance), hence quasi-separated, so `QuasiCompact.of_comp` with ψ ≫ pullback.fst = Uo.ι gives QuasiCompact ψ. With that, the rest of the proof goes through unchanged; it uses IsSeparated f and IsIntegral X but not this hypothesis. Removable on its own, and together with [LocallyOfFiniteType f] and [QuasiCompact f].
- `[LocallyOfFiniteType f]` (variable): Same argument. Its only role is in isNoetherian_X (via LocallyOfFiniteType.isLocallyNoetherian), which gives QuasiCompact D.ψ. That can be derived instead from IsSeparated f: the U k are affine because c k is a closed immersion into an affine scheme (`isAffine_of_isAffineHom`); X is quasi-separated, so Uo is compact; Uo.ι is quasi-compact (`quasiCompact_of_compactSpace`); pullback.fst f prπ is proper, hence quasi-separated; and `QuasiCompact.of_comp` finishes. The rest of the proof (integrality of X', ak_eq_bk, isClosedImmersion_ιΩ, properness of ek, the clopen argument) does not otherwise use this hypothesis.
- `[QuasiCompact f]` (variable): Same argument. It is used only in isNoetherian_X (to get CompactSpace X), which in turn gives QuasiCompact D.ψ. Here X is already compact, being covered by the finitely many U k, each affine because c k is a closed immersion into an affine Spec. QuasiCompact ψ follows from IsSeparated f as follows: Uo is compact in the quasi-separated X, so Uo.ι is quasi-compact (`quasiCompact_of_compactSpace`); pullback.fst f prπ is proper, hence quasi-separated; and `QuasiCompact.of_comp` applies to ψ ≫ pullback.fst = Uo.ι. No other step of V_eq_O needs this hypothesis.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicCurve_RationalFunctionField_stichtenothGenusExists_of_ratFunc_tower.AlgebraicCurve.AlgebraicCurve.adeleBddPrincipal_eq_comap`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicCurve_RationalFunctionField_stichtenothGenusExists_of_ratFunc_tower.lean#L791-L793) — `flt-anthropic-0183`

Write S = adeleSpace K F, A = adeleBdd D and B = globalSub K F. The claim is comap S.subtype A ⊔ comap S.subtype B = comap S.subtype (A ⊔ B). This only needs A ≤ S, which is `adeleBdd_le_adeleSpace` and is proved with no instance, since S is defined as the supremum of the adeleBdd submodules. The instance is used only through `globalSub_le_adeleSpace` (B ≤ S, via `diagonal_mem_adeleSpace`), and that inclusion is not needed. Proof without it: (≤) is `sup_le (Submodule.comap_mono le_sup_left) (Submodule.comap_mono le_sup_right)`. (≥) take x ∈ S with (x : Place K F → F) ∈ A ⊔ B. By `Submodule.mem_sup` we get x = a + b with a ∈ A and b ∈ B. Then a ∈ S by `adeleBdd_le_adeleSpace`, so b = x - a ∈ S. Hence ⟨a, _⟩ lies in comap A, ⟨b, _⟩ lies in comap B, and their sum is x, so x is in the left-hand side. Equivalently, this is the modular law (`sup_inf_assoc_of_le` in Mathlib/Order/ModularLattice.lean, for the modular lattice of submodules) transported along the subtype. No degenerate cases arise: the argument is pure linear algebra and works for any D, K and F.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_DRModel_exists_curveModel_closedImmersion_pair_pFibre_cover_levelSet_singleton.GLUECI.eq_span_tInvChart_of_mem`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_DRModel_exists_curveModel_closedImmersion_pair_pFibre_cover_levelSet_singleton.lean#L2727-L2734) — `flt-anthropic-0199`

Suppose t is not transcendental, so it is algebraic over κ (the Fact t ≠ 0 is omitted, so t = 0 is allowed). (1) If t ≠ 0: t is algebraic, hence integral, over the field κ. By `IsIntegral.tower_top` it is integral over adjoin κ {t⁻¹}, so t ∈ chartRing κ {t⁻¹}. Then tInvChart κ t * ⟨t,_⟩ = 1, so tInvChart is a unit. Having it in the prime ideal 𝔮 contradicts IsPrime.ne_top, so `h` is false and the statement is vacuous. (2) If t = 0: t⁻¹ = 0 and tInvChart = 0. By hchartInv every element of the ring is aeval 0 f = algebraMap κ L (f.coeff 0), so every nonzero element is a unit and the prime 𝔮 must be ⊥ = Ideal.span {0}. The conclusion holds. So the new proof is: if t is transcendental, use the existing argument (eq_span_of_pow_mem with transcendental_inv); otherwise use the argument above. hchartInv cannot be dropped: with L = κ(u) and t = u⁻², chartRing κ {u²} = κ[u] and the prime (u) ∋ u² is not span{u²}.

### [`P2MW.S_PowerSeries_dvd_of_forall_taylorShift_order_le.CuspDisc.Bdd.mul_small`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_PowerSeries_dvd_of_forall_taylorShift_order_le.lean#L132-L135) — `flt-anthropic-0231`

- `(hC : 0 ≤ C)` (theorem): Implied by hP. `Bdd C P` unfolds to `∀ k, ‖coeff k P‖ ≤ C` (line 20), so `hP 0` with `norm_nonneg` gives `0 ≤ C`. The proof becomes: `have hC : 0 ≤ C := (norm_nonneg _).trans (hP 0)`, then the same `rw [mul_comm ...]; exact hQ.mul hρ0 hρ1 hC' hC hP`. This holds whichever of the other removable hypotheses are also deleted.
- `(hC' : 0 ≤ C')` (theorem): Implied by hQ. `Small ρ C' m Q` unfolds to `∀ k, ‖coeff k Q‖ ≤ C' * ρ ^ (m - k)` (line 18). At `k = m` the exponent is `m - m = 0`, so `hQ m` reads `‖coeff m Q‖ ≤ C' * 1`, and `norm_nonneg` gives `0 ≤ C'`. Rebuild it with `have hC' : 0 ≤ C' := by have := hQ m; simp at this; exact (norm_nonneg _).trans this`, then call `Small.mul` as before.
- `(hρ0 : 0 ≤ ρ)` (theorem): The statement still holds for ρ < 0; the proof splits on the sign of ρ. If 0 ≤ ρ, the original proof works. If ρ < 0 and m = 0, every exponent `0 - k` is 0, so `small_zero_iff` turns hQ into `Bdd C' Q` and the goal into `Bdd (C*C') (P*Q)`. `Bdd.mul hC hC' hP hQ` (line 137, no ρ involved) closes it. If ρ < 0 and m ≥ 1, `hQ (m-1)` gives `0 ≤ ‖coeff (m-1) Q‖ ≤ C' * ρ ^ 1 = C' * ρ`, so `C' ≤ 0`. With `0 ≤ C'` (hC', or derived from `hQ m`) this gives `C' = 0`. Then every bound in hQ is `0 * ... = 0`, so all coefficients of Q vanish, Q = 0 and P * Q = 0. The goal `‖coeff n 0‖ ≤ C * 0 * ρ ^ (m - n)` becomes `0 ≤ 0`. By contrast, hρ1 is needed: ρ = 2, m = 1, C = C' = 1, P = X, Q = 2 satisfy hP and hQ, but coeff 1 (P*Q) = 2 > 1 = 1·1·2⁰.

### [`P2MW.S_Algebra_exists_isStandardEtale_mvPolynomial_localizationAway_of_smooth_of_kaehlerDifferential.EtaleCoordK1a.rankAtStalk_eq`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_Algebra_exists_isStandardEtale_mvPolynomial_localizationAway_of_smooth_of_kaehlerDifferential.lean#L13-L24) — `flt-anthropic-0236`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0236.lean`

- `[Module.Flat S M]` (theorem): rankAtStalk M p is defined as finrank over Localization.AtPrime p of LocalizedModule p.primeCompl M. S is a domain, so 0 ∈ p and every element of p.primeCompl is nonzero, hence a non-zero-divisor: p.primeCompl ≤ nonZeroDivisors S. Two Mathlib lemmas in LinearAlgebra/Dimension/Localization.lean need only this inclusion, with no flatness or finiteness assumption. IsLocalization.rank_eq gives that the rank over the localized ring equals the rank over S. IsLocalizedModule.rank_eq (with f := LocalizedModule.mkLinearMap) gives that the localized module has the same rank as M, which is n by hrank. Module.finrank_eq_of_rank_eq then yields finrank = n. The existing proof only uses Flat and FinitePresentation in its first step, Module.isLocallyConstant_rankAtStalk plus connectedness, which moves from p to the generic point ⊥. Dropping that step and running the remaining argument directly at p (replace ⊥ by p.asIdeal; prove hle with mem_nonZeroDivisors_of_ne_zero and p.asIdeal.zero_mem) proves the statement without this hypothesis. Flat and FinitePresentation can even be deleted together.
- `[Module.FinitePresentation S M]` (theorem): Same argument as for Module.Flat. In a domain, p.primeCompl ≤ nonZeroDivisors S for every prime p, because 0 ∈ p. Hence IsLocalization.rank_eq and IsLocalizedModule.rank_eq, which assume only this inclusion, give that the rank of LocalizedModule p.primeCompl M over Localization.AtPrime p equals Module.rank S M = n. Module.finrank_eq_of_rank_eq then gives rankAtStalk M p = n. Finite presentation is only used for the local-constancy step (Module.isLocallyConstant_rankAtStalk), and that step is not needed. hrank already forces the rank to be finite, so no finiteness of M is needed.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_seven_le_ordDiff_D_jqModC_of_ord_eq_six.SupersingularTop3.mem_of_ord_nonneg`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_seven_le_ordDiff_D_jqModC_of_ord_eq_six.lean#L40-L46) — `flt-anthropic-0240`

So for f = 0 the conclusion holds by `zero_mem _`, whatever `w.ord 0` and `h` say. Proof without `hf`: `by_cases hf : f = 0`. In the case f = 0, `subst`/rewrite and close with `zero_mem _`. In the case f ≠ 0, the existing proof runs unchanged; it is the only place `hf` is used, via `w.exists_unit_mul_zpow hf hπ`. The other arguments are needed. `h` is essential: the subring is ≠ ⊤ by the `ne_top'` field, so not every f lies in it. `w` and the instances appear in the statement, and `Place K F` needs `[Algebra K F]`.

### [`P2MW.S_RubinSilverberg_exists_polynomial_rsFamily.M4cP2.D52.PhiA_eq`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_RubinSilverberg_exists_polynomial_rsFamily.lean#L410-L414) — `flt-anthropic-0247`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0247.lean`

- `(hj : j < 21)` (theorem): `PhiPlusA j` and `PhiMinusA j` are the integer polynomial `sidePlusA`/`sideMinusA (hornerC naS X) (hornerC ngS X) (hornerC dtpS X) (hornerC a4zS X) (X + 600) t` with `t := (j : ℤ[X])` plugged in. So the identity holds for every `j`, not only for `j < 21`.

Argument: take `t` as a new variable `T` over the integral domain `ℤ[X]`. Let `G := sidePlusA (...) T - sideMinusA (...) T` in `ℤ[X][T]`.

**Degree of `G` in `T` is at most 20.**
- In `lhsPairAG`, `P5 = (na*T + …)^5` and `R = (ng*T + …)^5 * w^4` each have degree ≤ 5 in `T`. Every monomial `P5^a * R^b` has `a + b = 4`, so it has degree ≤ 20.
- The other part is `hornerG` of a list with one entry per row of `tabA`. `tabA` has exactly 21 rows, so this part also has degree ≤ 20 in `T`.

**`G` has 21 roots.** Evaluating `T := (j : ℤ[X])` turns `G` into `PhiPlusA j - PhiMinusA j`; this is what `map_sidePlusA`/`map_sideMinusA` give for the evaluation ring hom. The current theorem, which the existing proof establishes using `hj`, says this is 0 for `j = 0, …, 20`. These 21 values are distinct in the integral domain `ℤ[X]`.

**Conclusion.** `G` has degree ≤ 20 and 21 distinct roots, so `G = 0`. This is `eq_zero_of_natDegree_lt_card_of_eval_eq_zero`, the lemma the current proof already uses. Evaluating `G = 0` at `T = j` gives `PhiPlusA j = PhiMinusA j` for every natural `j`.

There are no degenerate cases to check: `j : ℕ` is unconstrained, and no junk values are involved.

**Proof without `hj`.** First prove the restricted statement exactly as now. Then prove the `T`-degree bound on `G` with `compute_degree`, after unfolding as in `natDegree_PhiA_le`. Finally apply the roots argument above.

The same reasoning works from the bivariate view: `F(s, t)` has degree ≤ 196 in `s` and ≤ 20 in `t` and vanishes on the 197×21 grid, so it is identically zero.

### [`P2MW.S_WeierstrassCurve_isTwoKernel_X_sub_C_coordsOrZero_of_addOrderOf_eq_two.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_WeierstrassCurve_isTwoKernel_X_sub_C_coordsOrZero_of_addOrderOf_eq_two.lean#L16-L30) — `flt-anthropic-0249`

The group law on `W.toAffine.Point`, which `addOrderOf` relies on, only needs `[Field F] [DecidableEq F]` (Mathlib Affine/Point.lean, around line 920). The first two goals are pure polynomial facts. The third goal is closed by `WeierstrassCurve.eval_Psi2Sq_of_two_nsmul_eq_zero`, whose signature is `{F} [Field F] [DecidableEq F] {W : WeierstrassCurve.Affine F} {x y} (h : W.Nonsingular x y) (h2 : 2 • some h = 0)`; it does not take an `IsElliptic` instance. So the current proof still compiles after deleting the binder. `[DecidableEq F]` cannot be deleted in the same way: it is needed to elaborate the `AddCommGroup` instance behind `addOrderOf Q`, and `Classical` is not open.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicCurve_TranscendenceTower_degree_poleDivisor_eq_finrank.AlgebraicCurve.AlgebraicCurve.Place.stichtenothGenusExists_of_isFractionRing`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicCurve_TranscendenceTower_degree_poleDivisor_eq_finrank.lean#L2159-L2166) — `flt-anthropic-0261`

Both classes are Props, so the instances are defeq by proof irrelevance. Nothing in the statement (`T.RegularOutside A`, `LSpace 0`, `StichtenothGenusExists K F`) needs HasPrincipalDivisors to elaborate. The same proof `stichtenothGenusExists_of_hasPoleDivisorPackage (hasPoleDivisorPackage_of_isFractionRing T hA)` goes through with the instance found from IsCurveOver (e.g. after `omit [HasPrincipalDivisors K F] in`). P has fields `hn_pos : 0 < P.n` and `degB_eq : Divisor.degree P.B = P.n`. If `Place K F` were empty, `Divisor K F = Place K F →₀ ℤ` would be a subsingleton, so `P.B = 0`, and `Divisor.degree 0 = 0` (degree is an AddMonoidHom, `map_zero`), contradicting `0 < n`. So the new proof first obtains P, uses this short argument (`haveI : Nonempty (Place K F) := by by_contra h; rw [not_nonempty_iff] at h; ...`) to get the instance, and then applies `stichtenothGenusExists_of_hasPoleDivisorPackage ⟨P⟩` as before.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicGeometry_exists_restrict_action_quotient_clauses_of_isClopen_of_isConnected.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicGeometry_exists_restrict_action_quotient_clauses_of_isClopen_of_isConnected.lean#L229-L413) — `flt-anthropic-0266`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0266.lean`

- `[Finite G]` (theorem): The main proof uses finiteness only through the helper `range_ι_comp_eq_univ` (and `le_iSup_preimage_imagePreimage`, which calls it). Inside that helper the only use is `hWclosed : IsClosed W`, which applies `isClosed_iUnion_of_finite` to W = ⋃ g, (ρ g)⁻¹(U). That fact is only used to get h1 : IsClosed (π '' W). The proof itself shows hπW : π '' W = π '' U without any finiteness. So h1 also follows from `π.isClosedMap _ hUcl` (π is finite, hence universally closed), since U is closed. The rest of the clopen argument needs W to be open (any union of opens is) and its complement to be closed, which is true. Everything else in the main proof works for an arbitrary index type G: the `⨆ g : G` covers, the gluing `existsUnique_gluing'` and `eq_of_locally_eq'`. No other step needs `Fintype G`. So the statement stays true and provable for any group G.
- `(hsurj : Function.Surjective π.base)` (theorem): Surjectivity follows from `[IsFinite π]` and `hsec`. A finite morphism is universally closed, so `π.isClosedMap` holds and the range R = π '' univ is closed. Let V : X.Opens be the open complement of R. Then π ⁻¹ᵁ V = ⊥, so Γ(C, π ⁻¹ᵁ V) is terminal and hence subsingleton. The main proof already uses this fact: `CommRingCat.subsingleton_of_isTerminal (C.sheaf.isTerminalOfEqEmpty _)`. By `hsec V`, the map π.app V : Γ(X, V) → Γ(C, π ⁻¹ᵁ V) is injective, so Γ(X, V) is subsingleton too. If V contained a point, Mathlib's instance `Scheme.component_nontrivial` (Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/Properties.lean) would make Γ(X, V) nontrivial, which is a contradiction. So V is empty and π.base is surjective. The proof only uses hsurj by passing it to the helper lemmas, so this derived fact can be passed instead. This works with or without `[Finite G]`, so either hypothesis can be deleted on its own.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_PlaceSpecialization_isGoodDiv_heckeDivBar_self_and_glueData_mem_admissible.UqNSol.fstDiv_sum`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_PlaceSpecialization_isGoodDiv_heckeDivBar_self_and_glueData_mem_admissible.lean#L358-L366) — `flt-anthropic-0271`

- `(h2 : finrank A V = 2)` (theorem): `h2` follows from `hφ` and the other hypotheses. `[IsLocalRing A]` extends `Nontrivial A`, and a nontrivial commutative ring satisfies `StrongRankCondition` (Mathlib's `commRing_strongRankCondition` instance). Because `V` is free and finite, Mathlib's `LinearMap.charpoly_natDegree` (Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Charpoly/Basic.lean) gives `natDegree φ.charpoly = finrank A V`. By `hφ`, `φ.charpoly = (X - C α) * (X - C β)`. Each factor is monic of `natDegree` 1 (`monic_X_sub_C`, `natDegree_X_sub_C`; this uses `Nontrivial A`). So by `Monic.natDegree_mul` the product has `natDegree` 2, hence `finrank A V = 2`. The degenerate case where `A` is the zero ring cannot occur, since a local ring is nontrivial. So the proof can start with `have h2 : finrank A V = 2 := by rw [← LinearMap.charpoly_natDegree φ, hφ, (monic_X_sub_C α).natDegree_mul (monic_X_sub_C β), natDegree_X_sub_C, natDegree_X_sub_C]`, and the rest of the proof stays the same.

The proof without `ha` first splits on `by_cases ha : a = 0`. In the case a = 0, `subst` and then `simp`, or `iff_of_true` with the two facts above, closes the goal. - `[Algebra.IsSeparable K K']` (variable): The proof only uses separability to build `Field.powerBasisOfFiniteOfSeparable K K'`. Without the instance, the theorem can be proved by `by_cases h : Algebra.IsSeparable K K'`. If `h` holds, the existing proof works unchanged. If it fails, both sides are 0. On the right, `Algebra.trace_eq_zero_of_not_isSeparable` (Mathlib/RingTheory/Trace/Basic.lean:276; it needs no finiteness) gives `Algebra.trace K K' = 0`, so the right side is `algebraMap K F 0 = 0`. On the left, pick `θ : K'` with `¬ (minpoly K θ).Separable`. The file's own lemma `minpoly_algebraMap_eq_map` needs only `[FiniteDimensional K K']` and `hconst`, and gives `minpoly F (algebraMap K' F' θ) = (minpoly K θ).map (algebraMap K F)`. By `Polynomial.separable_map` (Mathlib/FieldTheory/Separable.lean:310) this polynomial is not separable either. So `algebraMap K' F' θ` is not separable over `F`, hence `¬ Algebra.IsSeparable F F'`, and `Algebra.trace_eq_zero_of_not_isSeparable` gives `Algebra.trace F F' = 0`. That makes the left side 0 too. There are no degenerate cases to worry about, since all four types are fields. The other hypotheses are needed: dropping `FiniteDimensional K K'` breaks the statement (K' = F = F' = K(t), where `trace` of `t` is `t` on one side and 0 on the other), and dropping either scalar-tower instance breaks it too (counterexample: all four fields equal to Q(√2) with K' → F' the conjugation map).

The hypothesis also follows from the others. In Mathlib's `RingTheory/Localization/FractionRing.lean` there is an instance (priority 100) `FaithfulSMul R K` derived from `[IsFractionRing R K]`, using `IsFractionRing.injective`. That instance needs no domain hypothesis. `K` is a field, so it is a domain. Then `IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul A K : IsDomain A` (in `Algebra/Algebra/Basic.lean`, with variables `[CommSemiring R] [Semiring A] [Algebra R A] [FaithfulSMul R A] [IsDomain A]`) gives the instance. The function `isDomain_iff_isField` in the same FractionRing file uses exactly this argument.

So the proof without the hypothesis starts with `haveI : IsDomain A := IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul A K` and then continues unchanged. The calls to `GenLift.exists_ringHom_forall_sub_mem` and the other helpers get their `[IsDomain A]` from this local instance.

The other hypotheses are genuinely needed:
- `IsAlgClosed K`: counterexample A = ℤ_p, H = A[X]/(X²−p), ψ sending X ↦ 0.
- `IsIntegrallyClosed A`: counterexample A = k + I inside a valuation ring with algebraically closed fraction field, H = A[X]/(X²−x²).
- `Module.Flat`: counterexample H = residue field.
- `IsLocalRing`: needed for the statement to elaborate.

Here `NeZero (p : ℚ)` holds because ℚ has characteristic zero and p ≠ 0 (p is prime; the file's scoped instance `neZero_of_fact_prime` gives `NeZero p`). In the whole proof chain (`unitsActHomeo`, `continuous_unitsAct`, `map_unitsAct_eq_smul`, `measurePreserving_unitsAct`), the only use of `NumberField K` is line 175: `haveI : FiniteDimensional K L := Module.Finite.of_restrictScalars_finite ℚ K L`. This is used to show that `σ` has finite order. The other steps depend only on `L`: Haar uniqueness via `isMulLeftInvariant_eq_smul`, local compactness and second countability of the ideles of L, and the compact-neighbourhood argument showing `c = 1`. Without `NumberField K` we can still get `FiniteDimensional K L`, and the statement stays true. The earlier lemmas in this file also take `[NumberField K]` as an automatic instance argument but never use it in their proofs. So the statement stays true without `hu`, including the empty-multiset case, where `hu` is vacuous anyway. The proof barely changes. ### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_MultCovering_FamData_t_zeroChart_of_orth.P2MZX5A.exists_poly_of_orders`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_MultCovering_FamData_t_zeroChart_of_orth.lean#L988-L1007) — `flt-anthropic-0323`

The proof uses `ha` only for `Finset.card_image_of_injective` and `prod_image_eq_aeval_nodePoly`. Without it: let S := image a univ and apply `ModularCurve.exists_prod_mul_eq_aeval_of_forall_ord_nonneg_of_forall_neg_one_le_ord` with S and n = 0 as now. Its hypotheses come from hreg/hnode/hinf and do not need injectivity. This gives Q₀ with (Q₀ ≠ 0 → natDegree Q₀ ≤ S.card) and (∏_{s∈S}(jB - s)) * φ = aeval jB Q₀. Grouping the factors of nodePoly a by value (Finset.prod_image'/prod_fiberwise) gives nodePoly a = ∏_{s∈S}(X - C s)^{#fiber s}, and every fiber is non-empty. So nodePoly a = P_S * R with R monic of degree m - S.card. Take Q := Q₀ * R. Then φ * aeval jB (nodePoly a) = aeval jB Q₀ * aeval jB R = aeval jB Q. Q is 0 if Q₀ = 0, and otherwise natDegree Q ≤ S.card + (m - S.card) = m, since S.card ≤ m. The case m = 0 is trivial; when a repeats values, the repeated factors are absorbed into R.

Since `c.det` is a unit of ℝ, `c.det.val ≠ 0` holds by `Units.ne_zero`. So `r * r ≠ 0`, and `r ≠ 0` follows (e.g. `left_ne_zero_of_mul`, or `mul_self_ne_zero.mp`). To prove the theorem without `hr`, first derive `have hr : r ≠ 0 := by intro h; apply c.det.ne_zero; rw [hdet, h, mul_zero]` right after `hdet`. The rest of the proof then runs unchanged. ### [`P2MW.S_ModularForm_exists_gamma0_forall_tendsto_slash_atImInfty_of_three_le.A2EIS.tendsto_eisensteinSeries`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularForm_exists_gamma0_forall_tendsto_slash_atImInfty_of_three_le.lean#L183-L205) — `flt-anthropic-0329`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0329.lean`

- `[NeZero N]` (theorem): Nothing in the statement needs `NeZero N` to elaborate. In Mathlib, `gammaSet N r a` and `eisensteinSeries a k` (EisensteinSeries/Defs.lean) are defined for every `N`, and `cst` is too. The proof only uses `NeZero` in the step `ModularGroup_T_zpow_mem_verticalStrip z (NeZero.pos N)`, which moves a point into the vertical strip, and that step fails only when N = 0. So the theorem can be proved by splitting on whether N = 0. If N ≠ 0, we get the instance and the existing proof applies. If N = 0, then `ZMod 0 = ℤ` and the cast ℤ → ZMod 0 is the identity, so `gammaSet 0 1 a = {v | v = a ∧ gcd (v 0) (v 1) = 1}`. This set is `{a}` if a is coprime and empty otherwise. Since it has at most one element, `eisensteinSeries a k z` equals `eisSummand k a z` or `0`, and the limit can be checked case by case. (1) a is not coprime: the series is 0. Also a ≠ ![0,±1] because those vectors are coprime, so `cst k a = 0`. (2) a 0 ≠ 0 and a is coprime: the series is `eisSummand k a z`, which tends to `lim k a = 0` by the file's `tendsto_eisSummand hk` (it has no NeZero). Also a ≠ ![0,±1], so `cst k a = 0`. (3) a 0 = 0 and a is coprime: then |a 1| = 1, so a = ![0,1] (constant value 1 = cst) or a = ![0,-1] (constant value (-1)^(-k) = cst). In all three cases the limit is `cst k a`. Equivalently, for N = 0, take the finite sum over the subsingleton `gammaSet` of `tendsto_eisSummand`, and the resulting sum of `lim` values is `cst k a`; the proof of `tsum_lim` only uses `mem_gammaSet_iff_of_apply_zero`, which has no `NeZero`. So the theorem stays true and provable without `[NeZero N]`.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_slopeLaw_ssAnnulus_zeroChart_of_chartSpec_univ.Ws23.SlopeLaw0.coe_smul_eq`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_slopeLaw_ssAnnulus_zeroChart_of_chartSpec_univ.lean#L621-L626) — `flt-anthropic-0331`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0331.lean`

- `[Fact q.Prime]` (variable): The theorem takes this instance-implicit variable only because q appears in the statement via `modularFunctionFieldBar (1 * q)`. `modularFunctionFieldBar` is an abbrev defined in Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_ArithmeticGalois.lean (line 111) under only `variable (N : ℕ)`: `laurentBaseChange (AlgebraicClosure ℚ) (modularFunctionFieldFull N)`. It has no NeZero, primality or other condition on N, so the statement elaborates for every q, including q = 0 and q = 1. The proof is `rw [Algebra.smul_def]; rfl`. It uses only the default Algebra structure and coercion of an IntermediateField, where `c • g = algebraMap c * g` and the subtype coercion of a product is the product of the coercions. Nothing depends on q being prime. Writing `omit [Fact q.Prime] in` before the theorem leaves the statement and proof unchanged.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicGeometry_OModulePresheaf_exists_isCoherent_linearEquiv_of_forall_basicOpen_of_isBasis.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicGeometry_OModulePresheaf_exists_isCoherent_linearEquiv_of_forall_basicOpen_of_isBasis.lean#L1019-L1061) — `flt-anthropic-0333`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0333.lean`

- `(res_refl : ∀ (W : ↥B) (x : M W), res (le_refl W.1.1) x = x)` (theorem): `res_refl` follows from `res_comp` and `hqc` together. Fix `W : ↥B` and `x : M W`, and write `r := res (le_refl W.1.1)`. Any two proofs of `W.1.1 ≤ W.1.1` are equal by proof irrelevance, so every `res h` with `h : W.1.1 ≤ W.1.1` is the same map `r`.

(1) `r` is idempotent. Apply `res_comp` with `W = W' = W''` and `h = h' = le_refl _`. This gives `r x = r (r x)`, because `(le_refl _).trans (le_refl _)` is again a proof of `W ≤ W`.

(2) `r` is injective. Mathlib has `Scheme.basicOpen_one : X.basicOpen (1 : Γ(X, U)) = U` (Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/Scheme.lean:718). So `hqc W W 1 (V.basicOpen_one).symm` applies. Its second part says: if `r y = 0` then `∃ n, (1 : Γ(V, W.1.1)) ^ n • y = 0`, and `one_pow` and `one_smul` turn this into `y = 0`.

Take `y = r x - x`. By `map_sub` and (1), `r y = r (r x) - r x = 0`, so by (2) `y = 0`, i.e. `r x = x`. This is exactly `res_refl`.

The theorem's proof then changes in one place: build `D` with `res_refl := <the argument above>` and keep everything else. Nothing in the statement refers to `res_refl`, so it can be deleted without other changes.

### [`P2MW.S_HaarQuotient_lintegral_density_mul_eq_one.HaarQuotient.SEC.measurable_weight_mul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_HaarQuotient_lintegral_density_mul_eq_one.lean#L64-L65) — `flt-anthropic-0393`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0393.lean`

- `[μH.IsHaarMeasure]` (variable): This instance is pulled in automatically because its type only mentions arguments, but nothing uses it. The statement only involves `weight H μH`. `weight` (Definitions/Def_HaarQuotient.lean) needs only `[Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] [MeasurableSpace G]`, a subgroup and a measure. The proof is `measurable_weight` composed with the continuity of `y ↦ ↑y * g`. `measurable_weight` takes the same section instances, but its proof (`weight_eq`, then `Measurable.ennreal_tsum` and measurability of the indicators of open sets) never uses Haar properties of μH. So you can add `omit [μH.IsHaarMeasure] in` to both lemmas, or inline that short proof, and the result still holds. Measurability does not depend on which measure μH is.
- `[μH.IsMulRightInvariant]` (variable): Same reasoning as for `IsHaarMeasure`. The instance is pulled in automatically and never used. Neither `measurable_weight` nor the composition with the continuous map `y ↦ ↑y * g` needs any invariance of μH. Measurability is a property of the function alone, and it does not depend on μH.
- `[LocallyCompactSpace G]` (variable): `weight H μH` is defined as `if h : SigmaCompactSpace G ∧ WeaklyLocallyCompactSpace G then (tsum of constants times indicators of `interior (CompactExhaustion.choice G (n+1))`) else 0`. A proof that needs no local compactness: `unfold weight; split_ifs with h`. In the positive branch, take the instances from `h` and apply `Measurable.ennreal_tsum fun n => measurable_const.mul (measurable_const.indicator isOpen_interior.measurableSet)`. This needs only `BorelSpace G`, as in the existing `measurable_weight` proof. The negative branch is `measurable_const`. Then compose with `(continuous_subtype_val.mul continuous_const).measurable`, which uses `IsTopologicalGroup` and Borel measurability. So `weight` is measurable without `LocallyCompactSpace`, and the statement stays true and provable.
- `[SecondCountableTopology G]` (variable): The argument is the same as for `LocallyCompactSpace`. Second countability is only used, through instance synthesis, to obtain `SigmaCompactSpace G` and to choose the `dif_pos` branch in `weight_eq`. Splitting on the `dif` in the definition of `weight` instead gives measurability in both branches: the tsum of indicators of open sets with constant coefficients, or the constant 0. Neither branch needs second countability. The composition with the continuous map `y ↦ ↑y * g` is unchanged.

### [`P2MW.S_LocalNewvector_PSCarrier_isIrreducibleGLRep_of_hasCharConductor_of_ratio.FLT.PrincipalSeriesGL2Padic.PSCarrier.G.eq_zero_of_cells`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_LocalNewvector_PSCarrier_isIrreducibleGLRep_of_hasCharConductor_of_ratio.lean#L5034-L5038) — `flt-anthropic-0413`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0413.lean`

- `(hw : toFn p μ₁ μ₂ f (weyl p) = 0)` (theorem): `PSCarrier p μ₁ μ₂` is the subtype `principalSeries p μ₁ μ₂` (Definitions/Def_LocalNewvector_PrincipalSeriesCarrier.lean). Its carrier consists of functions `f` with `IsLocallyConstant f` and the rule `f (borelElem p a₁ a₂ x * g) = μ₁ a₁ * μ₂ a₂ * halfModulus p a₁ a₂ * f g`. Because `f` is locally constant, `hw` follows from `hN`.

(1) Points of the big cell. `borelElem a₁ a₂ x * lowerUnip y = !![a₁ + x*y, x; a₂*y, a₂]`. For ε ∈ ℚ_[p] with ε ≠ 0, take a₂ = ε, y = 1/ε, x = 1 and a₁ = -1/ε. This gives g_ε := !![0,1;1,ε] = borelElem(-1/ε, ε, 1) * lowerUnip(1/ε). The equivariance rule and `hN (1/ε)` give f(g_ε) = (scalar) * 0 = 0.

(2) Closeness to the Weyl element. `weyl p` is !![0,1;1,0] (`coe_weyl`). As ε → 0 (for example ε = p^n), g_ε tends to `weyl p`, and its inverse !![-ε,1;1,0] tends to `weyl p` = `(weyl p)⁻¹`. So g_ε tends to `weyl p` in the topology on units, which is induced by `embedProduct` from the product topology on matrices. I found no instance in the library that overrides the topology on GL.

(3) Conclusion. `IsLocallyConstant f` makes f⁻¹' {f (weyl p)} an open set containing `weyl p`. By (2) it contains some g_ε with ε ≠ 0, so f (weyl p) = f g_ε = 0 by (1).

New proof: derive `hw` this way, then apply `ext_cells` exactly as now. Degenerate cases do not arise: p is prime, so ℚ_[p] is a nondiscrete valued field, and the scalar factor does not matter because it multiplies 0.

`hN` cannot be dropped: the single value at the Weyl element does not determine a nonzero element of the principal series.

### [`P2MW.S_CerednikDrinfeld_QM_FakeEllipticCurve_existsUnique_sectionAt_eq_of_nsmulPt_eq_one.GLUEbc.discreteTopology_of_isFinite`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_CerednikDrinfeld_QM_FakeEllipticCurve_existsUnique_sectionAt_eq_of_nsmulPt_eq_one.lean#L178-L184) — `flt-anthropic-0414`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0414.lean`

- `[IsAlgClosed K]` (variable): The theorem takes this instance only because its type mentions only `K`, which the statement uses. Nothing in the statement or the proof needs `K` to be algebraically closed; `Field K` is enough. Step 1, `h0 : DiscreteTopology (Spec (CommRingCat.of K))`, is proved by `Subsingleton.elim`. The needed `Subsingleton` comes from Mathlib's `instance {R : Type*} [Field R] : Unique (PrimeSpectrum R)` (RingTheory/Spectrum/Prime/Basic.lean:399), and its `Spec` form in AlgebraicGeometry/Scheme.lean:627. Both assume only `Field`. The remaining steps use `isDiscrete_univ_iff`, `Set.preimage_univ` and `g.isDiscrete_preimage` for the finite morphism `g`. None of these involves algebraic closure. So the same proof compiles after `omit [IsAlgClosed K] in`. Mathematically the claim also holds for any field: Spec K is a single point, and a scheme finite over it is the spectrum of a finite K-algebra, which is Artinian and so has finitely many points and is discrete. There is no degenerate case to check, since `K` is a field and therefore nontrivial.

### [`P2MW.S_TwistedUnipotentTerm_isLocallyConstant_integral_setIntegral_integral_semiLocalCharacter_mul_twist_and_hasCompactSupport.K6A.continuous_sG`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_TwistedUnipotentTerm_isLocallyConstant_integral_setIntegral_integral_semiLocalCharacter_mul_twist_and_hasCompactSupport.lean#L247-L262) — `flt-anthropic-0426`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0426.lean`

- `[IsTopologicalRing (L ⊗[K] v.adicCompletion K)]` (theorem): The proof never uses this assumption. It has four ingredients, and I checked the source of each:

1. `Units.continuous_iff`, `Units.continuous_val` and `Units.continuous_coe_inv` (Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Constructions.lean) only need `[TopologicalSpace M] [Monoid M]`. The topology on units comes from the embedding into `M × M`.
2. `Continuous.matrix_map` (Mathlib/Topology/Instances/Matrix.lean) only needs topological spaces on the entry types.
3. `continuous_congr K L v σ` is declared in the same file without any `IsTopologicalRing` assumption. It is proved by `IsModuleTopology.continuous_of_linearMap`.
4. The `funext κ; rw [← map_inv]; rfl` step is purely algebraic.

The hypothesis is also not needed to state the theorem. `IsTopologicalRing` is a Prop-valued mixin that takes the existing `TopologicalSpace` instance as an argument, so it provides no topology. The topologies on `GL (Fin 2) (L ⊗[K] v.adicCompletion K)` (units of the matrix ring with the product topology) are found without it.

So after deleting the binder, the same proof compiles unchanged and the statement is still true.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicCurve_finrank_omegaSpace_eq_ell_canonical_sub_of_genusReached.AlgebraicCurve.AlgebraicCurve.IntegralBasisInLSpace.gate_stichtenothGenus_le_of_transcendenceTower`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicCurve_finrank_omegaSpace_eq_ell_canonical_sub_of_genusReached.lean#L2018-L2024) — `flt-anthropic-0439`

The theorem also takes the section variables `[FiniteDimensional E F]` and `[Algebra.IsSeparable E F]`. With these, the imported theorem `AlgebraicCurve.Place.exists_restrict_eq` (in Theorems/Thm_AlgebraicCurve_Place_exists_restrict_eq.lean, signature `[FiniteDimensional F' M] [Algebra.IsSeparable F' M] (w : Place K F') : ∃ W : Place K M, W.restrict F' = w`) gives a place of F over `T.v`. So the new proof starts with `haveI : Nonempty (Place K F) := ⟨(Place.exists_restrict_eq (M := F) T.v).choose⟩` and then calls `gate_stichtenothGenus_le_of_poleDivisorPackage (PoleDivisorPackage.ofTranscendenceTower T IB) h` as before. Separately, the proof of `PoleDivisorPackage.degree_sub_ell_le` (lines 1668-1717) does not seem to use the binder at all: none of `ell_nsmul_sub_pos`, `degree_nsmul_sub_ell_le`, `finiteDimensional_lSpace` (which has `omit [Nonempty (Place K F)]`) or `Divisor.degree_nonneg_of_nonneg` takes it.
- `[FiniteDimensional K (LSpace (0 : Divisor K F))]` (theorem): It follows from `h` and `[IsCurveOver K F]`. `RiemannGenusReachedAt γ D₀` (Definitions/Def_AlgebraicCurve_AdelicIndex.lean:406) has a field `finite : FiniteDimensional K (LSpace D₀)`. Write D₂ := D₀ ⊔ 0, so D₀ ≤ D₂ and 0 ≤ D₂. Step 1: `module_finite_adeleBdd_quotient` (needs only `[IsCurveOver K F]`, line 658) says the adelic quotient for D₀ ≤ D₂ is finite. `lSpaceQuotientToAdeleBddQuotient_injective` embeds `LSpace D₂ ⧸ (LSpace D₀).comap (LSpace D₂).subtype` into it, so `Module.Finite.of_injective` makes that quotient finite. Step 2: the comap submodule is isomorphic to `LSpace D₀` by `Submodule.comapSubtypeEquivOfLe`, so it is finite, and `Module.Finite.of_submodule_quotient` makes `LSpace D₂` finite. Step 3: `LSpace 0` embeds into `LSpace D₂` by `Submodule.inclusion (lSpace_mono le_sup_right)`, so it is finite. This is the argument of `finiteDimensional_lSpace` (lines 812-829) with D₀ in place of 0. After `haveI` of this instance, the existing proof `gate_stichtenothGenus_le_of_poleDivisorPackage (PoleDivisorPackage.ofTranscendenceTower T IB) h` goes through unchanged. Degenerate cases need no separate treatment, since `h.finite` is always available. This removal does not depend on the `Nonempty` binder, so the two can be removed together.

### [`P2MW.S_AutomorphicForm_GL2Real_eq_neg_eight_mul_pi_of_forall_tendsto_ellipticTransform_entrySlice.AutomorphicForm.GL2Real.RHead.dθMm_identity`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AutomorphicForm_GL2Real_eq_neg_eight_mul_pi_of_forall_tendsto_ellipticTransform_entrySlice.lean#L728-L733) — `flt-anthropic-0442`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0442.lean`

- `(hq : q.1 ≠ 0)` (theorem): Write s = sin θ, c = cos θ, S = s^2 + q.2^2. From the definitions at lines 639–653, dθMm = !![-(r s), -(r(2sc/q.1)); 0, -(r s)] and duMm = (r S/q.1^2)•e01 + r•e10, so duMm + (-r)•e10 = (r S/q.1^2)•e01. Entry by entry, the LHS is (0,0) = (1,1) = -(r s)/q.1, (1,0) = 0, (0,1) = -2rsc/q.1^2. The RHS is (0,0) = (1,1) = -(r s/q.1), (1,0) = 0 (the r and -r cancel), (0,1) = -(2sc/S)·(r S/q.1^2). If q.1 = 0, Mathlib's junk value x/0 = 0 and 0⁻¹ = 0 make every entry on both sides 0, so the identity still holds. If q.1 ≠ 0, the original argument applies. Proof without hq: split on q.1 = 0. In that case `simp` with div_zero/inv_zero closes it. Otherwise run the same `ext; fin_cases; simp; field_simp; ring` proof. hq and hsv can each be deleted, and so can both together.
- `(hsv : Real.sin θ ^ 2 + q.2 ^ 2 ≠ 0)` (theorem): With the same entry computation (s = sin θ, c = cos θ, S = s^2 + q.2^2), S only appears in the (0,1) entry: the RHS has -(2sc/S)·(r S/q.1^2) and the LHS has -2rsc/q.1^2. If S = 0, the RHS entry is 0 because 2sc/0 = 0. A sum of two squares is zero only if both are zero, so s = 0 and the LHS entry is 0 too. The other entries do not involve S. So the identity holds without hsv. Proof: split on S = 0. In that case get sin θ = 0 (e.g. from `add_eq_zero_iff_of_nonneg` with `sq_nonneg`, then `pow_eq_zero_iff`) and finish with `simp`. Otherwise use the original proof. This deletion is independent of hq: both can be dropped, separately or together.

### [`P2MW.S_IharaTower_RungDatum_exists_restrict.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_IharaTower_RungDatum_exists_restrict.lean#L10-L36) — `flt-anthropic-0455`

Suppose `ι x = ι y`. For every `z`, `hB` gives `P₁.B x z = Pₐ.B (ι x) (ι z) = Pₐ.B (ι y) (ι z) = P₁.B y z`. So `P₁.B x = P₁.B y` by `LinearMap.ext`, and `P₁.perfect.1` (injectivity of `P₁.B`) gives `x = y`. To remove the hypothesis, add one line at the start of the proof: `have hι : Function.Injective ι := fun x y hxy => P₁.perfect.1 (LinearMap.ext fun z => by rw [hB, hB, hxy])`. The rest of the proof stays as written: `LinearEquiv.ofInjective ι hι` and so on. No degenerate case causes trouble, because the derivation is purely algebraic and holds for any types and rings.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicGeometry_Polarisation_LocIsoOnBase_mumfordBundle_of_isInvertible.R2LocIso.locIsoOnBase_mumfordBundle`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicGeometry_Polarisation_LocIsoOnBase_mumfordBundle_of_isInvertible.lean#L40-L57) — `flt-anthropic-0457`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0457.lean`

- `(h𝓛 : Scheme.Modules.IsInvertible 𝓛)` (theorem): The proof uses invertibility at only one point: `locIsoOnBase_dual`, which calls `IsInvertible.pullback_dual_monoidalV2` along the open immersion `(f ⁻¹ᵁ U).ι` to get `j^*(dual 𝓛) ≅ dual (j^* 𝓛)`. Steps h1, h2 and h3 and `LocIsoOnBase.tensor` do not need invertibility. `LocIsoOnBase g M M'` (Def_AlgebraicGeometry_PolarisationRosati) only asks that, around each point of the base, the pullbacks of M and M' along the open immersion `(g ⁻¹ᵁ U).ι` be isomorphic. `Scheme.Modules.dual L` is defined as `(ihom L).obj (𝟙_ _)`. Here `ihom` is the internal Hom of the sheafified monoidal structure on X.Modules (`Monoidal.Reflective.monoidalClosed`), so up to isomorphism it is the sheaf Hom 𝓗om(L, 𝒪_X). Pulling back along an open immersion j : V ↪ X is restriction to V, and restriction commutes with sheaf Hom for every sheaf of modules (Stacks 01CM: 𝓗om(F,G)|_V = 𝓗om(F|_V, G|_V)). So `j^*(dual M) ≅ dual (j^* M)` holds for every 𝓛, invertible or not. Then `locIsoOnBase_dual` holds without the invertibility hypotheses: compose this iso with `dualIso e` as in the existing proof. The rest of the proof is unchanged. I did not find a Mathlib lemma stating this compatibility for open immersions, so that step would need a new proof. Deleting h𝓛 alone leaves a true statement, and so does deleting h𝓛' alone or both together.
- `(h𝓛' : Scheme.Modules.IsInvertible 𝓛')` (theorem): Same argument as for h𝓛, applied to 𝓛'. The only use is `pullback_dual_monoidalV2 (f ⁻¹ᵁ U).ι h𝓛'` inside `locIsoOnBase_dual`. It provides the iso `j^*(dual 𝓛') ≅ dual (j^* 𝓛')` for the open immersion j = `(f ⁻¹ᵁ U).ι`. That iso holds for any 𝒪_A-module, because the internal Hom on X.Modules is (up to iso) sheaf 𝓗om, and sheaf 𝓗om commutes with restriction to an open subset (Stacks 01CM). So the conclusion still holds without h𝓛', and the rest of the proof (pullback_of_comp_eq, tensor) does not use it.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_IsLevelPStructure_isUnit_xP_sub_xQ.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_IsLevelPStructure_isUnit_xP_sub_xQ.lean#L8-L20) — `flt-anthropic-0462`

Taking P = Q = (0,1) on y² = x³ + 1 over a field then gives a level structure where xP - xQ = 0 is not a unit.
- `(hℓ3 : 3 ≤ ℓ)` (theorem): Given [Fact ℓ.Prime], we have ℓ ≥ 2, so only ℓ = 2 needs a new argument. Then hD.preΨ_P : (W.preΨ ((2:ℕ):ℤ)).eval D.xP = 0. After Nat.cast_ofNat, Mathlib's `WeierstrassCurve.preΨ_two : W.preΨ 2 = 1` and Polynomial.eval_one turn this into (1 : T) = 0. So T is a subsingleton and IsUnit (D.xP - D.xQ) follows from isUnit_of_subsingleton. For ℓ ≥ 3 the existing proof applies. ### [`P2MW.S_AutomorphicForm_exists_isCompact_forall_twistedCentralizer_eq_scalar_mul_of_not_isSigmaConjugate_scalar_of_finrank_eq_two.P2mTwistedCentralizerModCentre.inverse_eq`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AutomorphicForm_exists_isCompact_forall_twistedCentralizer_eq_scalar_mul_of_not_isSigmaConjugate_scalar_of_finrank_eq_two.lean#L139-L148) — `flt-anthropic-0467`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0467.lean`

- `[CharZero K]` (variable): `[CharZero K]` is used only once in the proof chain: `exists_eq_algebraMap_of_sigmaTensor_eq` divides by 2 to show that a σ-fixed element of L ⊗[K] A lies in the image of A. The statement holds in every characteristic. The proof is not a small edit: that lemma needs a new proof, with a case split in characteristic 2.

We need e·σ̃(e) ∈ algebraMap A (L⊗A) for every e, where σ̃ = sigmaTensor. Once that holds, the rest goes through as written: nrm e = pr(algebraMap r) = r by `pr_algebraMap`; r ≠ 0 when e is a unit, as in `isUnit_iff_nrm_ne_zero` (otherwise σ̃ e = 0, so e = 0); then e·σ̃e·r⁻¹ = 1 and `Units.inv_eq_of_mul_eq_one_right` give the formula.

To get e·σ̃(e) ∈ A without char 0, there are two cases, since finrank 2 makes L/K a finite quadratic extension:

(1) L/K is Galois, which is always the case outside characteristic 2. Then hgen means σ generates Aut(L/K), so the σ-fixed field is K (`IsGalois.fixedField_top`). Taking invariants is the kernel of the K-linear map σ−1. Tensoring over the field K with A is exact, so the fixed points of σ⊗id on L⊗A are exactly K⊗A, the image of A. And e·σ̃e is fixed, because σ̃ is a multiplicative involution (σ² = 1 since |Aut| = 2) and the ring is commutative. No division by 2 is needed.

(2) L/K is purely inseparable (characteristic 2). Then Aut(L/K) is trivial, so σ = id. Every l ∈ L has l² ∈ K. For e = Σ lᵢ⊗aᵢ, Frobenius is additive, so e·σ̃e = e² = Σ lᵢ²⊗aᵢ², which lies in K⊗A = image of A.

A uniform argument also works. In both cases l·σ(l) ∈ K for every l (it is the norm, or l²). By polarization, l_iσl_j + l_jσl_i ∈ K. Expanding e·σ̃e = Σ_{i,j} l_iσ(l_j)⊗a_ia_j then shows it lies in K⊗A.

So the conclusion stays true and provable after dropping `[CharZero K]` (through `omit [CharZero K] in`), keeping h2 and hgen. Those two are genuinely needed: with σ = id on ℚ(√2)/ℚ, or with finrank 3, the formula fails.

### [`P2MW.S_ExtCitation_LocalLevel_exists_relative_ramification_inertia_Rw.Ws26T4.isLocalHom_algebraMap_intClosure`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ExtCitation_LocalLevel_exists_relative_ramification_inertia_Rw.lean#L115-L126) — `flt-anthropic-0474`

- `[IsLocalRing (integralClosure ℤ_[q] Kw)]` (theorem): `IsLocalHom` is defined for any monoid hom and does not need a local-ring structure on the codomain, so the conclusion elaborates without this instance. The proof never uses it. It only uses `hz.map (intClosureEquivRw q Kw)`, `norm_eq_one_of_isUnit`, `coe_intClosureEquivRw`, a `rfl`, `PadicAlgCl.norm_extends` and `PadicInt.isUnit_iff`. None of these needs `IsLocalRing` (`intClosureEquivRw` and `norm_eq_one_of_isUnit` are stated earlier in the file without it). So the proof works unchanged once the binder is deleted.
- `[FiniteDimensional ℚ_[q] Kw]` (variable): Kw is mentioned in the statement, so this instance is pulled in. The current proof does use it, through `intClosureEquivRw` → `mem_Rw_iff_mem_integralClosure` → `mem_Rw_iff_isIntegral`, which assumes `FiniteDimensional`. The statement is still true without it, by another proof. Take z : ℤ_[q] whose image in integralClosure ℤ_[q] Kw is a unit, with inverse w. Since Kw is a field, z ≠ 0 and (w : Kw) = algebraMap ℚ_[q] Kw (z : ℚ_[q])⁻¹. This element is integral over ℤ_[q]. Because algebraMap ℚ_[q] Kw is injective and the scalar tower ℤ_[q] → ℚ_[q] → Kw holds, `isIntegral_algebraMap_iff` shows that (z : ℚ_[q])⁻¹ is integral over ℤ_[q]. ℤ_[q] is a DVR (Mathlib instance in `PadicIntegers.lean`), hence integrally closed, and `PadicInt.isFractionRing` gives `IsFractionRing ℤ_[p] ℚ_[p]`. So `IsIntegrallyClosed.isIntegral_iff` puts (z : ℚ_[q])⁻¹ in ℤ_[q], and z is a unit in ℤ_[q]. No finiteness is needed, and there are no degenerate cases because Kw is a field. The `IsLocalRing` instance can be deleted as well; the two deletions are independent.

By `J_succ`, `J (m+1) k w t = (cI t)⁻¹ * (...)`, where `cI t = ((2 * Real.pi * t : ℝ) : ℂ) * Complex.I`. At t = 0 we get `cI 0 = 0`, and `(0 : ℂ)⁻¹ = 0` by Mathlib's junk-value convention, so `Jg k w 0 = 0` for every w. A proof without `ht`: split on whether t = 0. If t ≠ 0, keep the existing proof. If t = 0, show `(fun w => Jg k w 0) = fun _ => 0` with `funext w; unfold Jg nw; rw [J_succ]; simp [cI]`, then close the goal with `analyticOnNhd_const`. So the statement holds without `ht`.

### [`NumberField.ArchIdele.exists_above`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/Definitions/Def_NumberField_ArchimedeanIdeleModule.lean#L152-L153) — `flt-anthropic-0480`

Then `InfinitePlace.comap_surjective v` closes the goal. Mathematically E embeds in the number field K, so it is automatically a subfield of a finite extension of ℚ; no degenerate case arises. ### [`P2MW.S_Ihara_pow_card_mem_mennickeQ_mul.Ihara.pow_card_mem_mennickeQ_mul_impl`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_Ihara_pow_card_mem_mennickeQ_mul.lean#L1106-L1198) — `flt-anthropic-0484`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0484.lean`

- `[NeZero m']` (theorem): The statement does not need this instance to elaborate. `slAwayReduction`, `principalCongruenceAway`, `MennickeCSP` and `mennickeQ` (Definitions/Def_IharaMennickeCarrier.lean) take no `NeZero` argument, and neither does `Nat.card`. The instance follows from `hm'n : Nat.Coprime m' (q ^ 2 - 1)`. If m' = 0, then `Nat.coprime_zero_left` gives q^2 - 1 = 1, so q^2 = 2. No natural number satisfies this: q ≤ 1 gives q^2 ≤ 1, and q ≥ 2 gives q^2 ≥ 4. So the proof can start with `haveI : NeZero m' := ⟨...⟩` and continue unchanged.
- `[NeZero m₅]` (theorem): The statement does not need this instance to elaborate (no definition used takes `NeZero`). It follows from `h5 : ∀ p, p.Prime → p ∣ m₅ → 5 ≤ p`. If m₅ = 0, then `h5 2 Nat.prime_two (dvd_zero 2)` gives 5 ≤ 2, a contradiction. So `haveI : NeZero m₅` is derived at the start of the proof and the rest is unchanged.
- `[NeZero m₆]` (theorem): The statement does not need this instance to elaborate. The proof splits on whether m₆ = 0. If m₆ ≠ 0, the instance is available and the original proof applies. If m₆ = 0, then SL(2, ZMod 0) = SL(2, ℤ). This group is infinite, because n ↦ !![1, n; 0, 1] is an injective map from ℤ into it. So `Nat.card_eq_zero_of_infinite` gives Nat.card = 0, and the conclusion becomes x ^ 0 = 1 ∈ mennickeQ ..., which holds by `one_mem`. This case is also consistent with the other hypotheses (m₅ = m' = q = 1), so it is a real case that has to be handled, and it holds.
- `[NeZero q]` (variable): The theorem takes this `variable` instance because q is an argument. Nothing in the statement needs it: `ZAway q` is just `Localization.Away (q : ℤ)`, and the other definitions take no `NeZero`. It follows from `hm''q` and `hsat`. If q = 0, then `hm''q : Nat.Coprime (m₅ * m₆) 0` gives m₅ * m₆ = 1 (`Nat.coprime_zero_right`). With truncated subtraction, `hsat` then reads 0 ^ 2 - 1 = 0 ∣ 1, which is false. So the proof can derive `haveI : NeZero q` (needed for `mennickeQ_mul_eq_inf`) and proceed as before.

This uses only `[CommRing 𝒪]`, which makes `Module.End 𝒪 V` an `𝒪`-module, together with `[Module.IsTorsionFree 𝒪 V]`. So the same proof goes through unchanged after `omit [IsDomain 𝒪] in`. Its body `X - X ^ q` needs only `q`, `k` and `[Field k]`, so `P0 q k` elaborates without the instance. The theorem takes it only through the instance-implicit auto-inclusion rule. Mathematically: `k` is algebraically closed of characteristic `q` (`charP_of_galoisField` is just `charP_of_injective_algebraMap' (GaloisField q 2) q`). So `X - X^q` has natDegree `q` (by `one_lt_q`, i.e. `(Fact.out : q.Prime).one_lt`, and `natDegree_sub_eq_right_of_natDegree_lt`), derivative `1`, hence is separable and has `q` distinct roots. None of these steps uses the coordinate ring being a domain. The existing helper lemmas `natDegree_P0`, `derivative_P0` and `separable_P0` carry the instance only as an unused section variable. They can be stated with `omit [IsDomain (CoordRing q k)] in`, or their proofs inlined. The final step (`Multiset.toFinset_card_of_nodup`, `nodup_roots`, `IsAlgClosed.splits`, `natDegree_eq_card_roots`) also needs no such instance.

It is not mentioned in the statement; the proof uses it only to build the inverse lift. So the proof can start with `have he' : e' ≫ b = b := by rw [← he, ← Category.assoc, h2, Category.id_comp]` and then run unchanged. The other hypotheses cannot be dropped: he is used inside the statement, and h1 and h2 are each needed. With X = S the map is e itself, and with only one of h1/h2, e can be a split mono or split epi that is not an iso (e.g. a shift on a countable disjoint union of copies of S over S).

### [`P2MW.S_AlgHom_exists_algEquiv_comp_eq_of_isAlgClosed.W2G9.existsUnique_algHom_realizing_pointsMap_of_etale_absoluteGaloisGroup`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgHom_exists_algEquiv_comp_eq_of_isAlgClosed.lean#L361-L371) — `flt-anthropic-0547`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0547.lean`

- `[CharZero K]` (theorem): The current proof uses `[CharZero K]` only to get `IsGalois K (AlgebraicClosure K)`, which the library's general descent lemma `existsUnique_algHom_realizing_equivariant_pointsMap_of_etale` requires. Nothing in the statement needs it to elaborate: `Field.absoluteGaloisGroup K` is defined as `AlgebraicClosure K ≃ₐ[K] AlgebraicClosure K` for any field. I believe the statement holds over any field.

Argument without the hypothesis:
(1) `B` and `C` are étale, so every element of them is separable over K. Hence every K-algebra map `B → K̄` or `C → K̄` lands in the separable closure `K^sep ⊆ K̄`.
(2) Restriction `Aut(K̄/K) → Gal(K^sep/K)` is surjective. An automorphism of `K^sep` extends to an endomorphism of the algebraic extension `K̄`, which is bijective. The file already uses the Mathlib lemma for this, `IsAlgClosed.surjective_restrictDomain_of_isAlgebraic`. So `hΦ` says exactly that `Φ` is equivariant for `Gal(K^sep/K)` acting on `K^sep`-points.
(3) Galois descent for finite étale algebras over the Galois extension `K^sep/K` then gives a unique `ψ : B →ₐ[K] C` realizing `Φ`. Concretely, the library's argument (decompose into products of finite separable field extensions, then use conjugacy and fixed fields) can be rerun with the fixed-field step taken inside `K^sep`, where elements fixed by every Galois automorphism do lie in K. Uniqueness uses only that points separate morphisms of étale algebras (`algHom_ext_of_points`).

Degenerate case: if `C` is the zero ring, `C` has no points and `B → 0` is unique, so the statement holds trivially.

This needs a new proof. The existing lemma cannot be applied directly, because `K̄` is not Galois over K in characteristic p and `K^sep` is not algebraically closed. The mathematical statement itself does not depend on characteristic zero.

This can be removed on its own, and also together with `hT0`.

### [`P2MW.S_Representation_exists_isCompl_forall_mem_of_compactSpace_of_continuous.solution`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_Representation_exists_isCompl_forall_mem_of_compactSpace_of_continuous.lean#L11-L112) — `flt-anthropic-0551`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0551.lean`

- `[IsTopologicalGroup H]` (theorem): The current proof uses the Haar measure on H, which needs a compact topological group. Without that assumption the statement still holds. Since H is compact, hπ makes every coefficient k ↦ ℓ(π k v) bounded. Applied to k⁻¹ as well, the subgroup G = π(H) ⊆ GL(E) is bounded together with its inverses. So the determinants of its elements stay bounded away from 0, and the closure K of G in End(E) is a compact subgroup of GL(E). GL(E) with its usual topology is a topological group, so K is a compact topological group. Let K act on E by inclusion. Its coefficients g ↦ ℓ(g v) are continuous, since evaluation is continuous on finite-dimensional End(E). The set {g : g(P) ⊆ P} is closed, so P is K-invariant. The theorem as stated, applied to K (a genuine topological group), gives a K-invariant complement Pc of P. Pc is then π(H)-invariant because π(H) ⊆ K. Degenerate cases (E = 0, P = ⊥ or ⊤) are trivial. So the statement holds without [IsTopologicalGroup H]; the proof becomes this reduction to K instead of a Haar average over H. CompactSpace H stays and is genuinely needed (see the ℤ example in the answer).
- `[FiniteDimensional ℂ E]` (theorem): This can be deleted on its own, with IsTopologicalGroup kept. Fix v and let S = π(H)v be its orbit. For every linear functional ℓ, the set ℓ(S) is bounded, because it is the image of compact H under a continuous map (hπ). If S spanned an infinite-dimensional space, take linearly independent s_n in S and extend ℓ(s_n) = n to a linear functional on E (always possible over a field). Then ℓ(S) is unbounded, a contradiction. So W_v = span(S) is finite-dimensional and π-invariant. Restricted to W_v the representation still satisfies hπ, since every functional on W_v extends to E. The finite-dimensional theorem then shows that every invariant subspace of W_v has an invariant complement. So W_v is a semisimple module over the group algebra ℂ[H]. E is the sum of the W_v, and a sum of semisimple submodules is semisimple, so E is a semisimple ℂ[H]-module. Hence the invariant subspace P has an invariant complement. Degenerate cases (E = 0, P = ⊥ or ⊤) are trivial. The proof changes from averaging a projection to this local-finiteness and semisimplicity argument; I have not formalised it.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularForm_exists_katzGamma0Form_evalCusp_eq_of_five_le.CMP.SI_Wq_c₄`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularForm_exists_katzGamma0Form_evalCusp_eq_of_five_le.lean#L755-L758) — `flt-anthropic-0553`

That lemma uses it only to get the side condition `∀ a ∈ Finset.Icc 1 ((p - 1) / 2), IsUnit (((W.ΨSq a).eval x))`, which it gets from `isUnit_ΨSq_univData p hp2` or `isUnit_ΨSq_map p hp2`. That condition is the only hypothesis of the two lemmas the proof relies on: `LevelP.quotientByLine_map` (Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_KatzLevelPQuotient.lean:75) and `ModularCurve.LevelP.quotientByLine_variableChange` (Theorems/Thm_ModularCurve_LevelP_quotientByLine_variableChange.lean). Neither lemma assumes p ≠ 2.

When p = 2, `(p - 1) / 2 = 0`, so `Finset.Icc 1 0` is empty and the side condition holds vacuously (`by simp` or `fun a ha => by simp at ha`). When p ≠ 2, the existing lemmas `isUnit_ΨSq_univData` and `isUnit_ΨSq_map` apply.

So the new proof splits on `p = 2`. Each case gives the unit condition, and with it the same rewriting chain as before goes through: `Wq_map`, `hW`, `LevelPData.variableChange_xQ`, `quotientByLine_variableChange`, then `variableChange_c₄` and `map_c₄`.

Alternative for p = 2: there `veluTLine` and `veluWLine` are sums over an empty Finset, so they are 0 and `Wq 2 = univCurveT 2` after simplifying `a₄ - 5*0` and `a₆ - b₂*0 - 7*0`. The goal then follows from the existing `SI_c₄`, which has no `hp2`.

The statement `SI p 4 ((Wq p).c₄)` does not mention `hp2`, so deleting it is well-formed.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicCurve_Place_CanonicalLocalResidueDataK_res_differentialCoeff_D_mul_pow_inv_eq_zero_of_surjective_algebraMap.AlgebraicCurve.Place.CanonicalLocalResidueDataK.CanonicalLocalResidueDataK.res_differentialCoeff_D_of_mem_poleSubmodule`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicCurve_Place_CanonicalLocalResidueDataK_res_differentialCoeff_D_mul_pow_inv_eq_zero_of_surjective_algebraMap.lean#L131-L172) — `flt-anthropic-0560`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0560.lean`

- `[Nontrivial Ω[F⁄K]]` (variable): `differentialCoeff ω` is defined (Def_ModularCurve_CanonicalDivisor.lean) as `if h : ∃ f, ω = f • v.dCoord then h.choose else 0`, so it makes sense without any instance. Split on `subsingleton_or_nontrivial Ω[F⁄K]`. In the nontrivial case the existing proof applies unchanged. In the subsingleton case `D g = 0 = D 0`, so `v.differentialCoeff (D g) = v.differentialCoeff (D 0)`. By `hint 0 (zero_mem _)` this lies in `v.toValuationSubring`, so `R.res_of_mem` gives `R.res (...) = 0`. The conclusion therefore holds with or without the instance. It can be removed independently of `[v.DCoordGenerates]`.
- `[v.DCoordGenerates]` (variable): Run the same induction on N. The base case uses only `hint` and `R.res_of_mem`. In the successor step, write g = g₁ + a·π^{-(N+1)} with g₁ ∈ poleSubmodule N, as the existing proof does via hsurj and `laurentTail_remainder_mem_poleSubmodule`; neither needs the instance. Leibniz plus `D_pow_succ_inv`, which is instance-free, give D(a·π^{-(N+1)}) = e • dCoord with e = algebraMap K F (c·(-(N+1)))·(π^(N+2))⁻¹. If dCoord = 0, then D g = D g₁ and the induction hypothesis finishes. If dCoord ≠ 0 and D g is not of the form f • dCoord, then differentialCoeff (D g) = 0 by `dif_neg`, and its residue is 0 by `map_zero`. If dCoord ≠ 0 and D g = f • dCoord, the coefficient is unique: from (x - f) • dCoord = 0 and `smul_eq_zero` over the field F, x = f. So differentialCoeff (D g) = f, and since D g₁ = (f - e) • dCoord, differentialCoeff (D g₁) = f - e. Hence res f = res (f - e) + res e = 0 + 0, by the induction hypothesis and `R.res_algebraMap_mul_uniformizer_pow_inv` (hn : 1 ≤ N+1). So the statement holds without the instance; it is used only by the lemmas called in the current proof. It can be removed independently of `[Nontrivial Ω[F⁄K]]`.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_isIntegral_jqNModC_of_modularPolynomialData.ModularCurve.SepFibre.slice_jqModC_separable_of_prim`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_isIntegral_jqNModC_of_modularPolynomialData.lean#L211-L215) — `flt-anthropic-0565`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0565.lean`

- `(hsymm : EvalSymm data.Φ)` (variable): `hsymm` follows from the other hypotheses `[hN : Fact N.Prime]` and `data : ModularPolynomialData N`. The library proves exactly this as `ModularCurve.ModularPolynomialData.evalSymm_of_prime (p : ℕ) [hp : Fact (Nat.Prime p)] (data : ModularPolynomialData p) : EvalSymm data.Φ`, in Theorems/Thm_ModularCurve_ModularPolynomialData_evalSymm_of_prime.lean. Its proof combines `exists_modularPolynomialData_evalSymm` with `ModularPolynomialData.eq_of_prime`: for prime N, any two modular-polynomial data are equal. The mathematical reason is that a monic Φ of degree ψ(N) = N+1 vanishing at (j, j_N) must be the minimal polynomial, i.e. the classical modular polynomial, which is symmetric. So after removing `hsymm` (via `omit hsymm in`, or by dropping it from `include`), the proof becomes `rw [← separable_map (qExpand K N), slice_map]; exact slice_jqNModC_separable K data (ModularPolynomialData.evalSymm_of_prime N data) ζ hζ`, using the same `NeZero N` instance derived from `hN`. I did not check whether importing that theorem here would create an import cycle; if it does, the lemma would have to be called from a file that can import both. The other hypotheses are all needed. `hN` is needed to elaborate `ModularPolynomialData N`, which requires `[NeZero N]`, and to get degree N+1. `ζ` appears in `hζ`. `hζ` is needed: in characteristic N, Kronecker's congruence makes Φ(j,Y) ≡ (Y − j^N)(Y^N − j), which is not separable, so the statement fails without a primitive N-th root.

### [`P2MW.S_Rep_extInflR_comp_homSeqTwo_g_eq_zero_of_forall_exists_eq_smul.DefectXDies.extend_of_forall_exists_eq_smul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_Rep_extInflR_comp_homSeqTwo_g_eq_zero_of_forall_exists_eq_smul.lean#L18-L85) — `flt-anthropic-0592`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0592.lean`

- `(hB : ∀ b : Bc, p • b = 0)` (theorem): It follows from `[Module (ZMod p) Bc]`. By `Nat.cast_smul_eq_nsmul` (Mathlib/Algebra/Module/NatInt.lean) we get `p • b = ((p : ZMod p)) • b`, and `(p : ZMod p) = 0` (`ZMod.natCast_self`), so `p • b = 0 • b = 0`. The proof uses `hB` once, in `hpx`; there it can be replaced by `fun b => by rw [← Nat.cast_smul_eq_nsmul (ZMod p), ZMod.natCast_self, zero_smul]`. This deletion and the deletion of `[Module (ZMod p) Bc]` (next entry) are alternatives: each can go on its own, but not both.
- `[Module (ZMod p) Bc]` (variable): This is a binder of the theorem, not a `variable`. Nothing else in the statement uses it: `p • b` in `hB` is ℕ-smul. It can be rebuilt from `hB` with `AddCommGroup.zmodModule hB` (Mathlib/Algebra/Module/ZMod.lean), via `letI`. The proof only uses facts that hold for any `ZMod p`-module structure: the basis `Module.Basis.ofVectorSpace`, and `Int.cast_smul_eq_zsmul`, which holds for every module structure. So the same proof goes through. This deletion and the deletion of `hB` are alternatives, not both together.
- `(hε : Function.Surjective ε)` (theorem): The conclusion does not mention `Bc` or `ε`. Without surjectivity, apply the theorem to the corestriction `ε' : P →ₗ[ℤ] LinearMap.range ε` (`LinearMap.rangeRestrict`), which is surjective. It has the same kernel as `ε`, so `Function.Exact ι ε'` still holds. `LinearMap.range ε` is finite, as a subtype of a finite type. It is p-torsion, because `hB` restricts to it. It gets a `ZMod p`-module structure from `AddCommGroup.zmodModule`. The theorem for `ε'` then gives the same conclusion. Equivalently, in the proof take the basis of the range of `ε` and lift its elements, instead of lifting a basis of `Bc`.
- `[Finite Bc]` (theorem): Finiteness is only used to make the basis index set a `Fintype`, so that `Fintype.linearCombination` and finite sums can be used. For prime p, `Bc` is an `F_p`-vector space and always has a basis `bB`, possibly infinite. The same argument works with `Finsupp` in place of functions. Take `s : R × (I →₀ ℤ) → P`, `(r, c) ↦ ι r + Finsupp.linearCombination ℤ x c`, and `χ₀` likewise with `e'`. Surjectivity of `s`: write `bB.repr (ε y)`, a finsupp, and lift its coefficients to ℤ. Kernel containment: from `ι r + Σ c_i x_i = 0`, applying `ε` and using linear independence of `bB` gives `p ∣ c_i`. Then injectivity of `ι` gives `r = -Σ m_i rr_i`, and `χ₀` vanishes exactly as before. The lift through `quotKerEquivOfSurjective` is unchanged. So the statement holds without `[Finite Bc]`, as long as `hp` is kept.
- `[hp : Fact p.Prime]` (theorem): The statement stays true for every p, with `[Finite Bc]` kept. p = 0: `hu` forces `u r = 0 • e' = 0`, so `χ = 0` works. p = 1: `Bc` is trivial, `ι` is bijective by exactness and `hε`, and `χ = u ∘ ι⁻¹`. p ≥ 2: by `AddCommGroup.equiv_directSum_zmod_of_finite` (Mathlib/GroupTheory/FiniteAbelian/Basic.lean), `Bc` is a finite direct sum of cyclic groups. They have generators `b_i` of orders `d_i`, and `d_i ∣ p` by `hB`. Lift each `b_i` to `x_i ∈ P`. Exactness gives `d_i • x_i = ι(r_i)`, and `hu` gives `u(r_i) = p • e'_i`. Define `χ₀ : R × (I → ℤ) → E'` by `(r, c) ↦ u r + Σ c_i (p/d_i) • e'_i`, and `s` as in the proof; `s` is surjective just as before. If `ι r + Σ c_i x_i = 0`, applying `ε` gives `d_i ∣ c_i`, say `c_i = d_i m_i`. Injectivity of `ι` gives `r = -Σ m_i r_i`. Then `χ₀(r, c) = -Σ m_i p e'_i + Σ m_i p e'_i = 0`. So `χ₀` factors through `P`, and it extends `u`. Only the basis step of the proof changes (cyclic decomposition instead of an `F_p`-basis), and nothing else in the statement needs primality: `ZMod p` is a ring for every p. Deleting both this and `[Finite Bc]` together is not claimed.
