### [`P2MW.S_ValuationSubring_exists_isAlgClosed_wittVector_ringHom_completion_of_liesOverPrime.WittUnramSol.exists_pow_mem_span_p`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ValuationSubring_exists_isAlgClosed_wittVector_ringHom_completion_of_liesOverPrime.lean#L141-L147) — `flt-anthropic-0600`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0600.lean`

- `(hA : A.LiesOverPrime p)` (variable): `hA` reaches the theorem only through `include hA`. The statement never mentions it, so `omit hA in` removes it without touching anything else.

Definition: `LiesOverPrime A q := (q : L) ∈ A.nonunits`. By Mathlib's `ValuationSubring.mem_nonunits_iff`, this means `A.valuation (p : L) < 1`. Proof without `hA`: split on whether `A.LiesOverPrime p` holds.

(1) If it holds, the existing proof works unchanged.

(2) If it fails, then `¬ A.valuation (p:ℚ̄) < 1`, so `A.valuation p ≥ 1`. The natural number `p` lies in the subring `A`, so `ValuationSubring.valuation_le_one` gives `A.valuation p ≤ 1`, hence `A.valuation p = 1`.
- The file's own lemmas `coe_p`, `coe_natCast_completion` and `v_coe_algClosure` give `Valued.v (((p:ℕ) : OA A) : CA A) = A.valuation (p : ℚ̄) = 1`.
- The file's lemma `isUnit_iff_v_eq_one` then shows that `(p : OA A)` is a unit.
- So `Ideal.span {(p : OA A)} = ⊤` (`Ideal.span_singleton_eq_top`), and any `n`, e.g. `n = 0`, works.

None of these lemmas needs `hA`, so the theorem is true and provable without it.

`hp` cannot be removed instead. With `p = 0`, `LiesOverPrime 0` holds trivially and `span {0} = ⊥`. If `A` is, say, a valuation over 2, then `x = 2` lies in the maximal ideal, but `2^n ≠ 0`, so the statement fails.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicCurve_finrank_twistedNodeCompatible_add_min_card_le_of_ringEquiv_ratFunc.AlgebraicCurve.AlgebraicCurve.RationalFunctionField.finiteDimensional_twistedPairs`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicCurve_finrank_twistedNodeCompatible_add_min_card_le_of_ringEquiv_ratFunc.lean#L671-L676) — `flt-anthropic-0607`

- `[DecidableEq k]` (variable): This instance is an argument only because its type mentions just `k`; the statement does not use it. `twistFn` is a def. Lean's `removeUnused` drops section variables a def does not use, and its body `(X - C b) ^ E (placeOfPoint k b)` needs no decidability. `placeOfPoint` (= `finitePlace K (irreducible_X_sub_C a)`) and `twistedPairs` (only `[Field K] [Field F] [Algebra K F]`) do not need it either. Only the proof uses it, through `twistedPairs_eq_map` (via `twistWeight`, which uses `Finset.erase`). That lemma's left-hand side does not contain the instance, so the proof becomes `classical; rw [twistedPairs_eq_map E₁ E₂ a b lam]; infer_instance`. No degenerate cases arise, since only decidability changes.
- `[DecidableEq (RatFunc k)]` (variable): Same argument as for `[DecidableEq k]`: nothing in the statement (`twistedPairs`, `twistFn`, `placeOfPoint`, `Divisor`) needs this instance. The proof uses it only inside `twistedPairs_eq_map` (via `ord_placeInfty` and `exists_eq_div_divisorFn_of_mem`), and `classical` supplies it there. The rewrite still matches because the left-hand side of `twistedPairs_eq_map` does not contain the instance.
- `[IsAlgClosed k]` (variable): The statement elaborates with only `[Field k]`: `twistFn`, `placeOfPoint` and `twistedPairs` do not take this instance, since unused section variables are dropped from defs. The claim stays true for any field `k`. The twisted-pairs space is a submodule of `riemannRochSpace E₁ × riemannRochSpace E₂`. For `RatFunc k`, every place over `k` is either the place of a monic irreducible `π` or the place at infinity, so `L(D) ⊆ Q⁻¹·k[X]_{<N}` with `Q = ∏ π^{max(D π, 0)}` and `N` bounded using `D(∞)`. Hence `L(D)` is finite-dimensional, and submodules of finite-dimensional spaces and their products are finite-dimensional. Caveat: the existing proof uses this instance through `exists_eq_div_divisorFn_of_mem` and `eq_placeOfPoint_or_eq_placeInfty`, which assume `IsAlgClosed`. A proof without it needs the classification of places of `k(x)` for a general field. That step is standard mathematics but would have to be written; I found no existing lemma for it. All three instances can be deleted independently, and also together.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_exists_isBigO_slash_realize_mul_deriv_realize_of_forall_ordDifferential_nonneg.ModularCurve.CuspDecayAux.meromorphicAt_F`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_exists_isBigO_slash_realize_mul_deriv_realize_of_forall_ordDifferential_nonneg.lean#L302-L305) — `flt-anthropic-0608`

- `[NeZero N]` (variable): The statement `MeromorphicAt (F N x σ) 0` still elaborates without `[NeZero N]`. The nearby lemma `F_eq_T` is stated under `omit [NeZero N] in` and mentions `F N x σ`, `realize N x` and `T N`, and it compiles, so none of these needs the instance.

The statement also stays true when N = 0, and the proof splits on `N = 0`:

- **Case N ≠ 0.** Build `haveI : NeZero N := ⟨h⟩` and run the existing proof unchanged (`analyticAt_Phi`, `F_eventuallyEq`).
- **Case N = 0.** Take q ≠ 0. `F_eq_T` (which does not need NeZero) gives `F 0 x σ q = realize 0 x (σ • T 0 q)`. By definition, `T 0 q = ofComplex (Periodic.invQParam (0:ℝ) q)`. In Mathlib, `invQParam h q = h / (2πI) * log q`, so here it equals `0 / (2πI) * log q = 0`. Hence on 𝓝[≠] 0 the function `F 0 x σ` equals the constant `realize 0 x (σ • ofComplex 0)`. A constant function is analytic, so `analyticAt_const.meromorphicAt.congr` finishes. `MeromorphicAt.congr` in Mathlib's `Analysis/Meromorphic/Basic.lean` only needs equality on 𝓝[≠] x, so the value of the junk update at 0 in `Periodic.cuspFunction` does not matter.

So the theorem holds for every N, and `omit [NeZero N] in` together with this case split proves it.

### [`P2MW.S_Complex_integral_modularFundamentalDomain_eq_boundary_of_hasFDerivAt.Complex.GreenFD.mem_D_neg_r`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_Complex_integral_modularFundamentalDomain_eq_boundary_of_hasFDerivAt.lean#L300-L303) — `flt-anthropic-0624`

- `(hy1 : y ≤ 1)` (theorem): The statement is still true for y > 1 because of the junk value of `Real.sqrt`. Here `r y = Real.sqrt (1 - y^2)`. If y > 1, then 1 - y^2 ≤ 0, so `r y = 0` (by `Real.sqrt_eq_zero'`). The point is then ⟨0, y⟩ (written ⟨-0, y⟩). It lies in `D = {z | |z.re| ≤ 1/2 ∧ 1 ≤ ‖z‖ ∧ 0 < z.im}` because |0| ≤ 1/2, ‖z‖ = y > 1 and y > 0. In terms of `mk_mem_D`: |-0| ≤ 1/2, and `h (-0) = h 0 = Real.sqrt 1 = 1 ≤ y`.

Proof without `hy1`: split on `y ≤ 1`. If y ≤ 1, use the existing proof. Otherwise rewrite `r y = 0` with `Real.sqrt_eq_zero'` (using `nlinarith` for 1 - y^2 ≤ 0), then `simp [h]` gives h 0 = 1 ≤ y. The other hypothesis is needed and is kept: the bound `r y ≤ 1/2` (`r_le_half`) uses `hy : √3/2 ≤ y`. For example, y = 0 gives the point (-1, 0), which is not in D. The section variables (Φ, U, hδ, …) are not mentioned in the statement, so the theorem does not take them as arguments.

- `[NeZero N]` (variable): `[NeZero N]` comes from `variable (N : ℕ) [NeZero N]`. It becomes an argument only because `N` appears in the statement through `modularFunctionFieldBar N`. Nothing in the statement needs it to elaborate. In `Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_ArithmeticGalois.lean`, `modularFunctionFieldBar` is declared under `variable (N : ℕ)` with no `NeZero` instance, so neither it nor `modularFunctionFieldFull`, which its body uses, can depend on `NeZero N`. The other names in the statement are generic over any field `F` with `[Field F] [Algebra (AlgebraicClosure ℚ) F]` and never mention `N`: `Place` (in `Def_AlgebraicCurve_DivisorClassGroup`), `Place.evalAt` (in `Def_AlgebraicCurve_PlaceEvaluation`), `pivotIndex` (in `Def_ModularCurve_JZeroHeightForm`) and `regVal` (in `Def_AlgebraicCurve_ChordalProximity`). The proof just unfolds the definition, `rw [regVal]; simp only [dif_pos hr]`, so the lemma is a definitional unfolding that holds for every `N`, including `N = 0`. Adding `omit [NeZero N] in` keeps the same proof working. The other arguments are needed: `hr` appears in the conclusion (`pivotIndex s o hr`), and `s`, `o`, `t`, `k`, `e` and `u` all appear in the conclusion.

It is always true by the product formula. In this theorem `α` is by definition the library's `moduleChar F` (`(NNReal.toRealHom.toMonoidHom.comp (distribHaarChar 𝔸)).toHomUnits`); the proof's `rfl` steps (`hEE`) confirm the two agree definitionally. `IsPrincipalTrivial α` unfolds to `∀ u : Fˣ, α (Units.map (algebraMap F 𝔸) u) = 1`. The library proves `NumberField.AdeleRing.distribHaarChar_algebraMap` (Theorems/Thm_NumberField_AdeleRing_distribHaarChar_algebraMap.lean): for any number field `F` with `[MeasurableSpace 𝔸] [BorelSpace 𝔸]`, both of which the theorem already has, and any `a : Fˣ`, `distribHaarChar 𝔸 (Units.map (algebraMap F 𝔸).toMonoidHom a) = 1`. Mathlib's `distribHaarChar` sets up its own Borel structure internally (`letI := borel A`), so it does not depend on the measurable-space instance in scope. Replacement proof, built inside the proof and passed to the helper lemma in place of `_hprin`: `fun u => Units.ext (by rw [val_moduleChar_apply, Units.val_one]; unfold NumberField.TateGlobal.ideleNorm; erw [NumberField.AdeleRing.distribHaarChar_algebraMap F u]; simp)`. The library already uses exactly this argument for F = ℚ, in S_AutomorphicForm_exists_rs22GlobalIntegral_..._rat.lean at lines 264–271. This general-F version has not been compiled, but nothing in the argument depends on F = ℚ. No degenerate case applies: F is a number field, so Fˣ is non-empty and the statement is never vacuous.

### [`P2MW.S_SchwartzMap_tsum_eq_tsum_fourier_euclideanSpace.PiPoissonSummation.periodize_eq_tsum_piFourierIntegral`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_SchwartzMap_tsum_eq_tsum_fourier_euclideanSpace.lean#L318-L338) — `flt-anthropic-0656`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0656.lean`

- `(hf_int : Integrable (⇑f) (volume : Measure (d → ℝ)))` (theorem): `hf_int` follows from `hf`, so it can be dropped. Because `f` is continuous, it is `AEStronglyMeasurable`, so the only thing to check is that `∫⁻ ‖f‖ₑ` is finite. Take the compact cube K = `Set.Icc 0 1`, the same `hK` that the proof of `mFourierCoeff_eq_piFourierIntegral` already builds. That proof also has `hFD`, which says that `pi univ (fun _ => Ico 0 1)` is an additive fundamental domain for the ℤ-lattice. Mathlib's `IsAddFundamentalDomain.lintegral_eq_tsum''` (the additive form of `lintegral_eq_tsum''` in MeasureTheory/Group/FundamentalDomain.lean) then gives `∫⁻ ‖f‖ₑ = ∑' g, ∫⁻ x in cube, ‖f (g +ᵥ x)‖ₑ`. Each summand is at most `‖(f.comp (addRight (intVec n))).restrict K‖ * vol(cube) = ‖…restrict K‖`, using `norm_coe_le_norm` and the fact that the cube has volume 1, exactly as in the existing estimate `hG_sum_int`. The right-hand side is summable by `hf K`, so the integral is finite and `f` is integrable. The degenerate case `d` empty is harmless: `d → ℝ` is then a single point and `volume` is a probability measure. So `mFourierCoeff_eq_piFourierIntegral` can derive `hef_int` from `hf`, and the rest of the proof is unchanged. This matches Mathlib's one-dimensional `Real.tsum_eq_tsum_fourier`, which takes only `h_norm` (the same as `hf`) and `h_sum`, with no integrability hypothesis.

B is finite over R, so the Mathlib instance `Module.Finite.base_change` (Mathlib/RingTheory/TensorProduct/Finite.lean) makes A ⊗[R] B finite over A. For each i, the A-linear map (LinearMap.proj i).comp e.toLinearMap : A ⊗[R] B → S i is surjective: any y comes from e.symm (Pi.single i y), using classical DecidableEq. So `Module.Finite.of_surjective` (Mathlib/RingTheory/Finiteness/Basic.lean) gives Module.Finite A (S i). Add `haveI : ∀ i, Module.Finite A (S i) := fun i => Module.Finite.of_surjective _ (surj i)` at the start of the proof. The rest of the proof (the rewrites with gapsw7_x3x_trace_baseChange, Algebra.trace_eq_of_algEquiv and gapsw7_x3x_trace_pi) then runs unchanged. Neither the statement nor any other binder mentions this instance. It is deletable on its own, together with [Module.Finite R B] it is not claimed.
- `[Module.Finite R B]` (theorem): This follows from the other hypotheses. If A is a trivial ring (Subsingleton), both sides of the equation lie in A and are equal. Otherwise A is nontrivial. Each S i is free and finite over A and ι is a Fintype, so ∀ i, S i is finite over A (Module.Finite.pi), and through e so is A ⊗[R] B (Module.Finite.equiv). A ⊗[R] B also has the base-changed basis (Algebra.TensorProduct.basis) indexed by Module.Free.ChooseBasisIndex R B. The lemma `Module.Finite.finite_basis` (Mathlib/RingTheory/Finiteness/Cardinality.lean: [Nontrivial R] [Module.Finite R M] (b : Basis ι R M) : Finite ι) shows that this index type is finite. Then `Module.Finite.of_basis` applied to Module.Free.chooseBasis R B gives Module.Finite R B, and the original proof applies. This is deletable on its own, with [∀ i, Module.Finite A (S i)] still present; it is not claimed that both can be deleted together.

The proof never uses it either. It gets surjectivity from `ZMod.ringHom_surjective`, which in Mathlib (Data/ZMod/Basic.lean) holds for any ring hom `R →+* ZMod n` with no condition on `n`. It builds the isomorphism `e` from `Ideal.quotientEquivAlgOfEq` and `Ideal.quotientKerAlgEquivOfSurjective`, and the rest is a tensor-product induction plus injectivity of `eB`; none of these steps involves primality. ### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_exists_linearMap_injective_range_eq_dual_multiplicativeSubmodule_tateModule_jH_twoCuspForms_of_ordinary_of_mem_infSubgroup.WilesDAssembly.moduleFinite_X0`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_exists_linearMap_injective_range_eq_dual_multiplicativeSubmodule_tateModule_jH_twoCuspForms_of_ordinary_of_mem_infSubgroup.lean#L724-L724) — `flt-anthropic-0692`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0692.lean`

- `[IsScalarTower ℤ_[p] 𝕋 (TateModule p (ModularCurve.JH M H))]` (variable): The theorem pulls this instance in automatically, because every variable its type mentions is already an argument. The statement `Module.Finite (ZMod p) (↥P0 →+ ZMod p)` does not need it to elaborate. The only place it is used is in the local instance `moduleFinite_P0`, through `P0.restrictScalars ℤ_[p]`. But it can be derived from the other hypotheses. Let T = TateModule p (JH M H). By `ModularCurve.moduleFinite_and_free_padicInt_tateModule_jH M p H`, which only needs `[NeZero M] [Fact p.Prime]`, T is a finite free ℤ_[p]-module, so the intersection of the p^k • T over all k is 0. Take c ∈ ℤ_[p] and x ∈ T. For each k, write c = n + p^k·b with n ∈ ℕ; this is the same kind of decomposition (`exists_eq_natCast_add_mul`) used in `moduleFinite_hom` in this file. Since `algebraMap ℤ_[p] 𝕋` is a ring hom, it sends n to n and p^k to p^k. So (algebraMap c) • x − c • x = p^k • ((algebraMap b) • x − b • x), which lies in p^k • T for every k and is therefore 0. Hence algebraMap c • x = c • x, and (c • t) • x = (algebraMap c * t) • x = algebraMap c • (t • x) = c • (t • x). That is the `IsScalarTower` instance. With it rebuilt locally, the existing proof goes through unchanged: P0 is a ℤ_[p]-submodule of the finite module T over the Noetherian ring ℤ_[p], so it is finite, and `moduleFinite_hom p ↥P0` concludes. No degenerate case causes trouble: if T = 0, everything is trivially finite.

- `[W.IsElliptic]` (variable): This instance comes in because `W` appears in the type of `H`. The structure `Hyp` does not need it: it is declared before `section Frame` without any IsElliptic instances. The external lemmas the proof calls (exists_isDualPair_of_mem_rationalHomSet, exists_mem_rationalHomSet_isDualPair_and_add_eq_smul_id, surjective_of_mem_rationalHomSet, comp_mem_rationalHomSet) take only `[E.IsElliptic]`/`[X₀.IsElliptic]` and never mention W. The section helpers `compEnd'`, `H.image_kernelIdealSet_eq` and `H.nsmul_id_ne_zero'` receive it only through the section variables. Their bodies never use it: one builds a subtype, one is an `ext`, and the third uses only Fact/CharP/IsAlgClosed/`[E.IsElliptic]`. So the proof goes through after inlining or omitting it in those helpers.
- `[W'.IsElliptic]` (variable): Same as for `[W.IsElliptic]`. It is pulled in only because `W'` occurs in the type of `H`. Neither `Hyp` nor any step of the proof (nor the helpers it calls, once their unused section instances are omitted) uses an IsElliptic instance on `W'`.
- `[Algebra (ZMod q') κ]` (variable): It is included only because `q'` is an argument through `H`. Nothing in the statement or the proof uses it. The only q'-dependent step is `H.nsmul_id_ne_zero'`, and its proof uses only `Fact q'.Prime`, `CharP.cast_eq_zero_iff κ q'`, `Nat.exists_infinite_primes` and `exists_addOrderOf_eq_prime_of_isAlgClosed`. No other lemma used mentions ZMod q'.
- `[Algebra.IsAlgebraic (ZMod q') κ]` (variable): It is included only because its dependencies are arguments, and it is never used. As with `[Algebra (ZMod q') κ]`, no lemma in the proof, including the body of `nsmul_id_ne_zero'`, needs algebraicity over ZMod q'.
- `(H : Hyp q' X₀ Λ θ x y W χ d W' χ' d' ℓ)` (variable): The proof uses `H` only to get `n • AddMonoidHom.id X₀.toAffine.Point ≠ 0` for `n > 0`, via `H.nsmul_id_ne_zero'`. `compEnd'` and `image_kernelIdealSet_eq` are a subtype construction and a set-image rewrite whose bodies never use `H`. The fact can be proved without `H` or any characteristic assumption. Pick a prime r ≥ max n (ringChar κ) + 1 (Nat.exists_infinite_primes). If (r : κ) = 0, then ringChar κ ∣ r (ringChar.spec), so ringChar κ is 1 or r. It is not 1 (CharP.ringChar_ne_one, since κ is a field) and it is not r because r > ringChar κ. So (r : κ) ≠ 0. Then exists_addOrderOf_eq_prime_of_isAlgClosed gives a point R of order r, and n • R = 0 would force r ∣ n with r > n > 0, which is impossible. Everything else in the proof needs only `[IsAlgClosed κ]`, `[DecidableEq κ]`, `[X₀.IsElliptic]`, `[E.IsElliptic]` and the explicit binders.
- `[CharP κ q']` (variable): It is used only inside `nsmul_id_ne_zero'`, to show that a prime r ≠ q' has (r : κ) ≠ 0. The ringChar argument replaces this: take a prime r > max n (ringChar κ); then ringChar.spec and CharP.ringChar_ne_one give (r : κ) ≠ 0. The rest of the proof is unchanged, and `Hyp` does not require this instance.
- `[Fact q'.Prime]` (variable): It is used only inside `nsmul_id_ne_zero'` (Nat.prime_dvd_prime_iff_eq Fact.out). That step can be replaced by the ringChar argument (ringChar.spec, CharP.ringChar_ne_one), which needs no assumption on q'. `Hyp` does not need this instance to elaborate.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_XHDRModelAtP_exists_chartAlgFin_algEquiv_iotaFin_comp_w_eq_of_atkinLehner_generic_of_unitsMap.WChart.wBar_fst`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_XHDRModelAtP_exists_chartAlgFin_algEquiv_iotaFin_comp_w_eq_of_atkinLehner_generic_of_unitsMap.lean#L44-L45) — `flt-anthropic-0701`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0701.lean`

- `[NeZero M]` (variable): `wBar_fst` takes `[NeZero M]` only because theorems automatically include instance-implicit section variables whose variables are already arguments. Nothing in the statement or proof needs it.

1. Lean's `structure` and `def` commands keep only the section variables actually used. Lean core's Structure.lean / MutualInductive.lean call `Meta.removeUnused`, and MutualDef uses `withUsed` for non-theorems.
2. No field of `XHDRModelAtP` uses `NeZero M`. The fields only reach `M` through `CohCarrier.GammaH M H`, `xHFunctionFieldBar M H` / `xHFunctionField M H`, `diamondAutHBar M H d`, `infSubgroup p M H hpM` (body `H.map (ZMod.unitsMap ...)`), `ZMod.unitsMap` and `Nat.div_dvd_of_dvd`. None of these take `[NeZero M]`. The only library instance that needs it, `GammaH_finiteIndex`, is never required: `qExpFunctionFieldC` and `ModularForm` take no finite-index instance. So the structure `XHDRModelAtP p M H hpM hj` has no `NeZero M` parameter.
3. `Xbar` and `wBar` are definitions using only `𝔓` and its implicit arguments, so they don't take it either.
4. The conclusion `wBar 𝔓 ≫ pullback.fst _ _ = pullback.fst _ _ ≫ 𝔓.w.hom` is a plain equation of morphisms. It holds for every `M`, including `M = 0`.

With `omit [NeZero M] in`, the existing proof `unfold wBar; exact pullback.lift_fst _ _ _` should go through unchanged (not checked by running Lean).

By contrast, `[Fact p.Prime]` is needed: `XHDRModelAtP`'s fields use `qExpFrobeniusPlaceModL`, which requires it through `qExpFrobeniusModL_isIntegral`.

- `(hr : r.Prime)` (theorem): The proof uses `hr` in two places, and `hrw` covers both. (1) The step `hr0 : (r : w.adicCompletion ℚ) ^ n ≠ 0` uses `hr.ne_zero`, but `r ≠ 0` already follows from `hrw`. If `r = 0`, then `((0 : ℕ) : 𝓞 ℚ) = 0 ∈ w.asIdeal` by `Ideal.zero_mem` / `Submodule.zero_mem`, which contradicts `hrw`. So the step can be replaced by `have hr0' : r ≠ 0 := by rintro rfl; exact hrw (by simp)`, followed by `pow_ne_zero _ (by exact_mod_cast hr0')` as before. (2) The step `inv_natCast_pow_mem w hr hrw n` (line 269 of the same file) takes `hr` but never uses it. Its proof only rewrites with `valuedAdicCompletion_eq_valuation` and `valuation_eq_one_iff_notMem` and then closes with `hrw`. The same argument therefore works inline without `hr`: the valuation of `(r : w.adicCompletion ℚ)` is 1, so the valuation of its n-th power and of the inverse is 1, and the inverse lies in `adicCompletionIntegers`. Nothing else in the proof uses primality, and the statement is still true for any natural `r` with `(r : 𝓞 ℚ) ∉ w.asIdeal`. The degenerate case `r = 0` cannot occur because `hrw` rules it out. When `r = 1` the statement reduces directly to `hα`.

### [`P2MW.S_HeckeEis_exists_basis_coeffH1par_int_complex.HeckeEis.C2Aux.moduleFinite_coeffParabolicCocycles`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_HeckeEis_exists_basis_coeffH1par_int_complex.lean#L140-L152) — `flt-anthropic-0737`

- `[NeZero N]` (variable): The proof uses `NeZero N` only to obtain `HeckeEis.ZQAux.instGroupFG_Gamma0 N` (finite generation of `Gamma0 N`, proved via finite index, which needs N ≠ 0). For N = 0 the result still holds. Mathlib defines `Gamma0 N` as `{g | (g 1 0 : ZMod N) = 0}`, and `ZMod 0 = ℤ`, so `Gamma0 0` is the upper-triangular subgroup of SL(2,ℤ). From det = ad = 1 we get a = d = ±1, so each element equals a • T^(a·b) with T = `ModularGroup.T`. Hence `Gamma0 0` is generated by the two elements `⟨T, _⟩` and `⟨-1, _⟩` and is finitely generated (it is abelian, isomorphic to ℤ × ℤ/2). New proof: split on N = 0. When N ≠ 0, use the existing instance. When N = 0, give the explicit two-element generating set, whose closure is everything by the short argument above. Everything else is unchanged: `coeffCocycles_eq_of_eqOn` only needs `Subgroup.closure s = ⊤`, and `Module.Finite.of_injective` into the Noetherian module `S → BinaryForm ℤ n` does not involve N. So `Module.Finite ℤ (coeffParabolicCocycles …)` holds for every N.

Because `I ≠ ⊥`, the base is a natural number that is at least 1, so it is neither 0 nor ⊤. Mathlib's `ENNReal.rpow_ne_top_of_ne_zero {x : ℝ≥0∞} {y : ℝ} (hx : x ≠ 0) (hx' : x ≠ ⊤) : x ^ y ≠ ⊤` puts no condition on the sign of the exponent, so the statement holds for every real `s`. ### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicCurve_Place_exists_toValuationSubring_eq_comap_of_ne_top.AlgebraicCurve.Place.irreducible_mk_comap'`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicCurve_Place_exists_toValuationSubring_eq_comap_of_ne_top.lean#L191-L224) — `flt-anthropic-0768`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0768.lean`

- `(hg0 : g ≠ 0)` (theorem): `hg0` follows from `hne` and `hge`. `Place.ord` is defined as `-(WithZero.log (v.adicValuation f))`, and Definitions/Def_AlgebraicCurve_DivisorClassGroup.lean proves the simp lemma `ord_zero : v.ord 0 = 0`. If g = 0, then `algebraMap F F' 0 = 0` (`map_zero`), so `hge` becomes `0 = comapRamIdx (F := F) w`. This contradicts `comapRamIdx_pos`, which needs only `hne` and is already used on the proof's first line to get `hepos : 0 < comapRamIdx (F := F) w`. Proof without the binder: after `hepos`, add `have hg0 : g ≠ 0 := by rintro rfl; rw [map_zero, w.ord_zero] at hge; omega` (or `simp at hge; omega`); the rest of the proof is unchanged. `hne` itself is needed: if the comap is ⊤, every nonzero element has order 0, so `comapRamIdx = sInf ∅ = 0`, and a `g` of order 0 is a unit, not irreducible.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_jWidth_mul_ord_eq_ord_aeval_of_coe_eq_hasseRootFn_pow.HasseOrdBody.ord_aeval_eq_zero`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_jWidth_mul_ord_eq_ord_aeval_of_coe_eq_hasseRootFn_pow.lean#L92-L115) — `flt-anthropic-0769`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0769.lean`

- `(hJc : ∀ r : K, J - algebraMap K F r ≠ 0)` (theorem): `hj₀` already implies `hJc`. Suppose `J - algebraMap K F r = 0` for some `r`. Then `J - algebraMap K F j₀ = algebraMap K F (r - j₀)`. If `r = j₀`, this is `0`, and `Place.ord_zero` (Def_AlgebraicCurve_DivisorClassGroup.lean:125, `ord` is `-log adicValuation`) gives `ord 0 = 0`. If `r ≠ j₀`, it is a nonzero constant, and `HasseOrdBody.ord_algebraMap` (same file, line 27) gives ord `0`. Both cases contradict `1 ≤ v.ord (J - algebraMap K F j₀)`. So the new proof first derives `hJc` from `hj₀` this way, then runs the existing proof unchanged. These hypotheses can be removed independently of one another; all three can also be removed together, using the Taylor argument given under `[IsAlgClosed K]`.
- `(hQ : Q.Monic)` (theorem): `hev` forces `Q ≠ 0`. With `IsAlgClosed`, `Splits.eq_prod_roots` gives `Q = C Q.leadingCoeff * ∏ (X - C r)`. So `aeval J Q = algebraMap K F Q.leadingCoeff * ∏ (J - algebraMap r)`. Apply `v.ord_mul`: both factors are nonzero, since the leading coefficient is nonzero and each `J - algebraMap r ≠ 0` by `hJc`. The constant factor has ord `0` by `ord_algebraMap`, and the product has ord `0` exactly as in the current proof. Use `mem_roots` with `Q ≠ 0` from `hev` in place of `hQ.ne_zero`. So the result stays `0` without monicity.
- `[IsAlgClosed K]` (theorem): The statement holds over any field `K`, by a direct ultrametric argument. Taylor-expand `Q` at `j₀` (`Polynomial.taylor`): `aeval J Q = Q.eval j₀ + Σ_{i≥1} c_i (J - algebraMap j₀)^i` with `c_i ∈ K`. Let `w = v.adicValuation`. By `hj₀`, `w(J - j₀) < 1`. Each term with `i ≥ 1` has `w ≤ w(J - j₀)^i < 1`: constants have `w ≤ 1` by `algebraMap_mem'`, and zero terms are trivially below 1. So by `Valuation.map_sum_lt` (Mathlib RingTheory/Valuation/Basic.lean:194) the tail has `w < 1`. `algebraMap (Q.eval j₀)` is a nonzero constant with `w = 1` (ord 0 by `ord_algebraMap`, using `hev`). Hence `Valuation.map_add_eq_of_lt_left` gives `w(aeval J Q) = 1`, i.e. ord `0`. No splitting is needed.

### [`P2MW.S_PDivisibleGroup_free_cotangent_of_isArtinianRing_of_pow_eq_zero.PDivFreeCot.iInf_ker_phi`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_PDivisibleGroup_free_cotangent_of_isArtinianRing_of_pow_eq_zero.lean#L802-L807) — `flt-anthropic-0775`

- `[CharP (kk R) p]` (variable): The statement only involves `phi G hp d`, which is defined in section Tower without any CharP assumption, so this instance is not needed to elaborate the statement. The proof needs it only indirectly, through `eq_zero_of_forall_phi` → `coeff_mem_pow` → `coeff_mem_maximalIdeal_of_phi_eq_zero`, and it follows from the other hypotheses `[Fact p.Prime]` and `hp : (p : R) ∈ maximalIdeal R`. Mapping to the residue field gives `(p : kk R) = 0`, and for prime `p` this gives `CharP (kk R) p` via `CharP.charP_iff_prime_eq_zero`. The library's own `solution` theorem (lines 966-969) derives the instance exactly this way: `haveI : CharP (kk R) p := by refine (CharP.charP_iff_prime_eq_zero Fact.out).2 ?_; rw [← map_natCast (residue R), IsLocalRing.residue_eq_zero_iff]; exact hp`. Adding that `haveI` at the start of the proof and keeping the rest unchanged proves the theorem without the binder (use `omit [CharP (kk R) p] in`).

### [`P2MW.S_LanglandsTunnell_Converse_ArchDatumR_W_diagOne_mul_iwasawa_eq_psi_mul_centralChar_mul_W_diagOne_of_weightZero.Ws23K7kit.W_diagOne_mul_gM`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_LanglandsTunnell_Converse_ArchDatumR_W_diagOne_mul_iwasawa_eq_psi_mul_centralChar_mul_W_diagOne_of_weightZero.lean#L63-L77) — `flt-anthropic-0780`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0780.lean`

- `(hy₂ : 0 < y₂)` (theorem): The proof only ever uses `hy₂.ne'`, i.e. `y₂ ≠ 0`. It needs this in three places: in `hτ` (through `div_ne_zero`), in `diagOne_mul_gM` (which takes only `y₂ ≠ 0`) and in `D.central_law y₂ _ _` (which takes only `z ≠ 0`). The conclusion is written with `|y₂|`, so it makes sense for negative `y₂` too. Without `hy₂`, split on whether `y₂ = 0`.

(1) If `y₂ ≠ 0`, the existing proof works unchanged, using that fact in place of `hy₂.ne'`.

(2) If `y₂ = 0`, the right-hand side contains the factor `((|0| : ℝ) : ℂ) = 0`, so it is `0`. On the left, `ArchR.diagOne c * gM x y₁ 0 θ = !![c*y₁*cos θ, -(c*y₁*sin θ); 0, 0]`, a matrix `g` whose second row is zero. For such a `g`, `unip t * g = g` for every `t`, so `D.unip_law (1/2) g` gives `D.W g = psi (1/2) * D.W g`. By the definition `psi x = exp (2πI x)`, `psi (1/2) = exp (π I) = -1`. Hence `D.W g = -D.W g`, so `D.W g = 0`, and both sides are `0`.

So the statement holds for every real `y₂`. By contrast, `hc` and `hy₁` are genuinely needed. `ArchDatumR` constrains `W` on singular matrices only through `unip_law` and `central_law`; its other fields only concern invertible matrices, or measure-zero sets in the integrals. One can therefore add to `W` a function supported on rank-1 matrices that are not invariant under rotation. Then `W (diagOne 0 * rotM θ) ≠ W (diagOne 0)`, and the statement fails when `c = 0` or `y₁ = 0`.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_XOneP_exists_opens_smooth_comp_toBase_of_subsingleton_minimalPrimes_fibre_twoChartIntegralModel_x1_mul.LocOrd.finite_residueField`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_XOneP_exists_opens_smooth_comp_toBase_of_subsingleton_minimalPrimes_fibre_twoChartIntegralModel_x1_mul.lean#L190-L199) — `flt-anthropic-0782`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0782.lean`

- `(hAp : (p : A) ∈ IsLocalRing.maximalIdeal A)` (theorem): Not used in the statement. Without it the claim still holds: any DVR A whose fraction field L is a number field has finite residue field. (1) Some prime q lies in the maximal ideal m: otherwise every nonzero integer is a unit in A, so ℚ ⊆ A; A is integrally closed and L is algebraic over ℚ, so A = L, a field, which is impossible for a DVR. (2) A contains O_L, since its elements are integral over ℤ ⊆ A and lie in L = Frac A. (3) P = m ∩ O_L is a nonzero prime (it contains q), hence maximal in the Dedekind ring O_L. (4) A is local and dominates the valuation ring (O_L)_P with the same fraction field; if some x ∈ A were not in (O_L)_P, then x⁻¹ would lie in the maximal ideal of (O_L)_P, hence in m, so x would be both a unit and a non-unit of A. Thus A = (O_L)_P. (5) The residue field is O_L/P, which is finite. L is a number field here because it is ℚ(ζ_p). The current proof uses hAp only for the CharP / ℤ-surjectivity shortcut; the argument above replaces it. This proof is a sketch and has not been formalised.
- `[Fact p.Prime]` (theorem): Not needed for the statement to elaborate (IsCyclotomicExtension {p} ℚ L needs no primality). For any p, L is a number field: for p = 0 the definition only uses nonzero n ∈ S, so the roots generate nothing and L = ℚ; for p = 1, hAp says 1 lies in the maximal ideal, impossible, so the statement is vacuous; for p ≥ 2, L = ℚ(ζ_p) is finite over ℚ. The general argument given for hAp (a DVR with number-field fraction field equals (O_L)_P for a maximal ideal P of O_L, so its residue field O_L/P is finite) then proves the conclusion without primality. The current proof uses primality only through ZMod p / CharP and the cyclotomic surjectivity lemma, which this argument replaces. The argument also seems to allow deleting this hypothesis together with hAp. This proof is a sketch and has not been formalised.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicCurve_TranscendenceTower_poleDivisor_apply.AlgebraicCurve.AlgebraicCurve.RiemannGenusReachedAt.gate_indexOfSpecialty_at_witness`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicCurve_TranscendenceTower_poleDivisor_apply.lean#L1072-L1079) — `flt-anthropic-0795`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0795.lean`

- `[FiniteDimensional K (LSpace (0 : Divisor K F))]` (theorem): The statement does not need this instance to elaborate: `indexOfSpecialty` is a plain `Module.finrank`, and `RiemannGenusReachedAt` uses `ell`, which is also a `finrank`. The instance follows from `h.finite : FiniteDimensional K (LSpace D₀)` together with `[IsCurveOver K F]`. Take `D' := 0 ⊔ D₀`, so `D₀ ≤ D'`. `module_finite_adeleBdd_quotient` (which needs only `IsCurveOver`) shows that `adeleBdd D' ⧸ adeleBdd D₀` is finite. `lSpaceQuotientToAdeleBddQuotient_injective` embeds `LSpace D' ⧸ LSpace D₀` into it, so that quotient is finite too. `LSpace D₀` is finite, so `Module.Finite.of_submodule_quotient` gives that `LSpace D'` is finite. `LSpace 0` then injects into it through `Submodule.inclusion (lSpace_mono le_sup_left)`. This is exactly the proof of `finiteDimensional_lSpace`, with `D₀` in place of `0`. After a `haveI` of this instance, the existing proof `indexOfSpecialty_eq_zero_of_genusReached h` goes through unchanged.
- `[Nonempty (Place K F)]` (theorem): The statement only mentions `Place K F` through types that exist whether or not it is empty, so the instance is not needed to elaborate. Without it, split on `isEmpty_or_nonempty (Place K F)`. If `Place K F` is nonempty, use `indexOfSpecialty_eq_zero_of_genusReached h` as now. If it is empty, `Place K F → F` is a `Unique` type (`Pi.uniqueOfIsEmpty`). Then the submodule `adeleSpace K F` is a subsingleton, and so is any quotient of it, so `indexOfSpecialty D₀ = finrank K (adeleSpace K F ⧸ _) = 0` by `Module.finrank_zero_of_subsingleton`. The statement therefore holds in both cases. This deletion is independent of the deletion of the FiniteDimensional instance.

### [`P2MW.S_AlgebraicCurve_exists_separating_transcendental_of_perfectField.AlgebraicCurve.SeparatingTranscendentalOfPerfectField.isAlgebraic_adjoin_of_transcendental`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_AlgebraicCurve_exists_separating_transcendental_of_perfectField.lean#L28-L42) — `flt-anthropic-0805`

- `(hx : Transcendental K x)` (theorem): `hx` follows from `hfd` and `ht`. `Transcendental K x` is defined as `¬IsAlgebraic K x`, so argue by contradiction and suppose `IsAlgebraic K x`. Over a field this gives `IsIntegral K x` (`IsAlgebraic.isIntegral`). Then `IntermediateField.adjoin.finiteDimensional` (Mathlib, IntermediateField/Adjoin/Basic.lean:486) gives `FiniteDimensional K K⟮x⟯`. Together with `hfd : FiniteDimensional K⟮x⟯ F`, `Module.Finite.trans` (RingTheory/Finiteness/Basic.lean:365) gives `FiniteDimensional K F`; the needed scalar tower K → K⟮x⟯ → F is a standard instance. So F is algebraic over K (`Algebra.IsAlgebraic.of_finite`), hence `t` is algebraic over K, which contradicts `ht`. With `hx` recovered this way at the start of the proof, the existing proof goes through unchanged, so the theorem stays true and provable without `hx`. No degenerate case gets in the way: whenever `x` is algebraic, the other hypotheses already contradict each other.

- `[CharP F p]` (theorem): Nothing else in the statement uses p: `red N k F : F ⊗[ℤ] intForms N k →ₗ[F] PowerSeries F` only needs `[Field F]`. The conclusion is true for every field F. The existing proof covers characteristic p prime, through `linearIndependent_redL`. That leaves characteristic 0, which can be proved as follows. `exists_basis_intForms` (it needs only `[NeZero N]`) gives a finite ℤ-basis b of intForms. Write `z i m := zc (b i) m ∈ ℤ`. The integer vectors `zcHom (b i)` are ℤ-linearly independent, because `zcHom_injective` holds and b is a basis. Suppose `∑ c i • redL (b i) = 0` in F[[X]]. Taking coefficient m gives `∑ c i * (z i m : F) = 0` for every m. Since F has characteristic 0 it is a ℚ-vector space (via `DivisionRing.toRatAlgebra`), so choose a ℚ-basis e of F. Applying `e.repr · j`, which is ℚ-linear, gives `∑ e.repr (c i) j * z i m = 0` in ℚ for every m. This mirrors the existing proof, with ZMod p replaced by ℚ. Multiply by a common denominator D to get integers `d i` with `∑ d i * z i m = 0` for all m. Then `zcHom (∑ d i • b i) = 0`, so `∑ d i • b i = 0`, so every `d i = 0`, hence every `e.repr (c i) j = 0`, hence every `c i = 0`. So the redL (b i) are F-linearly independent in every characteristic. The final step of the current proof (expand y in the basis `Algebra.TensorProduct.basis F b` and use independence) then works unchanged. Removing `[CharP F p]` makes p and `[Fact p.Prime]` unused, but they remain valid arguments.
- `[Fact p.Prime]` (theorem): Delete only this binder and keep `[CharP F p]`. Since F is a field, `CharP.char_is_prime_or_zero` gives that p is prime or p = 0. If p is prime, the existing proof applies; the `Fact` instance can be rebuilt from the primality just obtained. If p = 0, F has characteristic 0, and the argument above applies: pick a ℚ-basis of F, reduce the F-linear relation to ℚ-linear relations among the integer q-expansion vectors `zc (b i)`, clear denominators, and use `zcHom_injective` together with the fact that b is a ℤ-basis. So the statement stays true and provable. Each of `[Fact p.Prime]` and `[CharP F p]` can be deleted on its own, and both can also be deleted together.

Mathlib's `Scheme.Hom.ideal_ker_le (f : X.Hom Y) (U : Y.affineOpens) : f.ker.ideal U ≤ RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom` (Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/IdealSheaf/Basic.lean:694) gives that inclusion with no QuasiCompact assumption, so the rewrite can become `have hs' : f.app U s = 0 := f.ideal_ker_le U hs`. Why the statement is true without QC: if `f.app U s = 0`, then `f ⁻¹ᵁ D(s)` is empty, so the open set `D(s)` misses `range f` and hence its closure. Then `h` makes `u ⁻¹ᵁ D(s)` empty, and since `T` is reduced, `u.app U s = 0`. Quasi-compactness is only needed for the reverse inclusion (support of `f.ker` equal to the closure of the image), which this theorem does not use.

By definition (Def_Mathlib_RingTheory_Valuation_LowerRamificationGroup.lean), `lowerRamificationGroup B G i = (𝔪^(i+1)).inertia G`, and `mem_lowerRamificationGroup` says `σ ∈ … ↔ ∀ x, σ • x - x ∈ 𝔪^(i+1)`. To prove faithfulness, suppose `∀ x, g₁ • x = g₂ • x` and set `σ = g₂⁻¹ * g₁`. Then `σ • x = x` for every `x`, so `σ • x - x = 0` lies in every power of 𝔪. Hence `σ ∈ lowerRamificationGroup B G (t+1) = ⊥` by `ht'`, so `σ = 1` and `g₁ = g₂`. Degenerate cases are covered: the argument uses only `ht'` and works for any `t`. So the proof starts with `haveI : FaithfulSMul G B := ⟨…⟩` built this way and then continues unchanged, still passing the instance to the helper lemmas (`exists_uniformizer_package`, `finsum_smul_mem_of_mem_pow_jump`, …) that require it.

So after the existing `haveI : Finite (A ⧸ …)` line, one `haveI` rebuilds the instance, and the rest of the proof, including the helper lemmas that need it, is unchanged. The algebra structure in the instance's type comes from `[LiesOver …]`, which is kept. Mathematically this is the standard fact that finite residue fields are perfect. If both this instance and `[FaithfulSMul G B]` are deleted at once, the two arguments still go through independently.

### [`P2MW.S_EisensteinWeightOne_e1Chi3IsModular.S17E1.FLT.AnalyticCore.reprCount_mul_mul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_EisensteinWeightOne_e1Chi3IsModular.lean#L3691-L3702) — `flt-anthropic-0846`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0846.lean`

- `(h1 : p % 3 = 1)` (theorem): hexForm a b = a²+ab+b², and 4·hexForm a b = (2a+b)²+3b² ≡ (2a+b)² (mod 3), so hexForm is always ≡ 0 or 1 mod 3. With hp and hπ, p is therefore either 3 or ≡ 1 mod 3; h1 only excludes p = 3, which is represented (hexForm 1 1 = 3). For p = 3 the identity still holds. reprSols_mul_eq_union (needs only hp, hπ) gives reprSols (3n) = image (emul (a,b)) (reprSols n) ∪ image (emul (a+b,-b)) (reprSols n). Because 3 ramifies, all elements of norm 3 are associates, so (a+b,-b) is a unit multiple of (a,b). reprSols n is closed under unit multiplication (mem_reprSols_iff characterises it by the norm alone), so the two images coincide. card_image_emul (no h1) then gives reprCount (3n) = reprCount n. Hence reprCount (9m) = reprCount (3m) = reprCount m, and r(9m) + r(m) = 2·r(3m); for m = 0 both sides are 1+1 = 2. Proof without h1: case on p = 3. If p ≠ 3, derive p % 3 = 1 from hπ and hp via the mod-3 computation and run the existing proof; if p = 3, use the argument above. (hp, by contrast, is needed: p = 91 is represented, satisfies 91 % 3 = 1, and fails at m = 1, since 60 ≠ 48.)

So if the binder is deleted, the same proof still compiles and the statement stays true. Deleting `e` only makes the theorem stronger. (`{n : ℕ}` cannot be deleted on its own while `e` is present, because `e`'s type mentions it. Once `e` is gone, `n` is unused as well.)

### [`P2MW.S_Submodule_exists_forall_finiteAdeleEvalAt_eq.la_mem_closure_iff_coord`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_Submodule_exists_forall_finiteAdeleEvalAt_eq.lean#L77-L96) — `flt-anthropic-0877`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0877.lean`

- `[Fintype ι]` (theorem): The statement is still true for a basis indexed by an infinite type.

**Forward direction (→).** It never uses finiteness. It relies on `AddSubgroup.closure_le` and `la_coord_tmul_mem`, and neither needs `Fintype ι`.

**Backward direction (←).** The current proof uses finiteness only through `la_sum_tmul_coord`, which writes `t = ∑ i, b i ⊗ₜ la_coord S b i t` over all of ι. Without `Fintype`, prove instead, by `TensorProduct.induction_on`, that every `t` has a finset `F` with two properties:
- `t = ∑ i ∈ F, b i ⊗ₜ la_coord S b i t`;
- `la_coord S b i t = 0` for every `i ∉ F`.

The three cases of the induction:
- **Zero:** take `F = ∅`.
- **`z ⊗ₜ s`:** take `F = (b.repr z).support`. By `la_coord_tmul`, the coordinate is `b.repr z i • s`, which is zero off the support. Expanding `z = ∑ i ∈ F, b.repr z i • b i` (`Basis.sum_repr`/`Finsupp.linearCombination`) and using `TensorProduct.smul_tmul` and `sum_tmul` gives the identity.
- **`x + y`:** take the union of the two finsets. Each coordinate vanishes outside its own finset, so both sums extend to the union, and linearity of `la_coord` finishes the case.

Then `t` is a finite sum of elements `b i ⊗ₜ c_i` with `b i ∈ span ℤ (range b)` and `c_i = la_coord S b i t ∈ R`. Each lies in the closure, so `t` does too, by `AddSubgroup.sum_mem`.

**Degenerate case.** If ι is empty, then K = 0, so K ⊗ S = 0 and both sides are trivially true.

Equivalently, `K ⊗[ℚ] S ≅ ι →₀ S` via the coordinates, which holds without any finiteness assumption.

### [`P2MW.S_CohCarrier_HeckeData_exists_linearEquiv_ML_of_degeneracy_atkinLehner_identities.TWAlpha.adjO_jO_ιO`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_CohCarrier_HeckeData_exists_linearEquiv_ML_of_degeneracy_atkinLehner_identities.lean#L676-L682) — `flt-anthropic-0890`

- `(hdV : IsUnit (DV.op (σV (Sum.inl d))))` (variable): hdV becomes an argument only through `include hdV hj h₂ h₃ h₅ in`. The statement does not mention it: adjO DV σV q d (line 546), jO j w (line 516), ιO ι w (line 486), diag, and detV DV σV q d (line 553) are plain 𝒪-linear maps that do not take hdV. The scoped instances that do take hdV (instTowerP/instTowerP') are not involved. The proof does not use it either. It rewrites with jO_ιO, which is included only with hj h₂ h₃ h₅ (line 664), and then uses adjO_apply, diag_apply and detV_apply (all rfl lemmas) and T_Dv DV σV d (line 557, no include; it only uses DV.comm), finishing with simp and module. Mathematically, with p = (v₁, v₂), jO(ιO p) = ((q+1)v₁ + T·Dv v₂, T·Dv v₁ + (q+1)·Dv v₂). Applying adjO and commuting T past Dv gives (q+1)²·Dv vᵢ − T·Dv·T·Dv vᵢ in each component, which is detV vᵢ. No invertibility of Dv is needed. So the same proof should work once hdV is removed from the include.

The library explicitly omits it for gal_algebraMap. The proof only unfolds galE and uses generic lemmas. It can be omitted independently.

If p = 0, truncated subtraction gives p - 1 = 0, so the exponent is `j + q*0 = j` and the goal is `x^j = x^j`, closed by `simp`. If p ≥ 1, `Nat.sub_add_cancel` with `1 ≤ p` gives `p - 1 + 1 = p`, and the existing induction goes through unchanged (when p = 1 the goal is trivial again since p - 1 = 0). Proof without hp: `rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos p with rfl | hp0`, close the first case with `simp`, and run the original proof with `hp0` in place of `hp.out.one_le`. It can be deleted on its own or together with `[CharP K p]`.

So the statement elaborates without `NumberField K`; it is only pulled in as an instance-implicit section variable. The claim is a matrix identity that holds over any commutative ring, so it stays true, including when the ring is trivial. The proof uses the local helper lemmas `unipotentGL2_inv`, `scalar_mul_diagUnits2` and `diagUnits2_val'`. These carry `[NumberField K]` only because they are in the same section; their proofs (`Units.ext`, `diagUnits2_val`/`coe_diagUnits2`, `Matrix.diagonal_mul_diagonal`, `fin_cases` and `simp`) are pure ring computations that never use `NumberField`. The fix is to restate them with `omit [NumberField K] in`, or to inline the same computation. The remaining steps (`Units.ext`, `ext`/`fin_cases`/`simp [Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two]`, and `linear_combination` with `Units.mul_inv u`) work in any commutative ring.

These are subalgebras and inclusions defined without the `Fact`. The file confirms this: `coe_jChartFin` is stated with `omit [Fact (j ≠ 0)] in` and mentions `jChartFin`, and it compiles. `polePrime` (line 380) is `Ideal.comap inclInf (Ideal.map inclFin 𝔭)`, which also uses no `Fact`. So the theorem still elaborates once the instance is removed with `omit [Fact (j ≠ 0)] in`.

**Only the proof uses it.** The lemmas `polePrime_isPrime`, `jInv_not_mem_polePrime`, `map_inclInf_isPrime`, `comap_map_inclFin`, `comap_map_inclInf` and `map_comap_inclFin` need it through `isLocalization_away_inclFin`/`isLocalization_away_inclInf`.

**`hj` implies `j ≠ 0`.** Suppose `j = 0`. Then `jChartFin R F j = ⟨j, _⟩` equals `0`: apply `Subtype.ext` and use `coe_jChartFin`, which holds by `rfl`. So `jChartFin R F j ∈ 𝔭` by `𝔭.zero_mem`, which contradicts `hj : jChartFin R F j ∉ 𝔭`. In the degenerate case `j = 0` the theorem is therefore vacuously true.

**How the proof changes.** Start with `haveI : Fact (j ≠ 0) := ⟨fun h0 => hj (by have : jChartFin R F j = 0 := Subtype.ext (by simp [coe_jChartFin, h0]); rw [this]; exact 𝔭.zero_mem)⟩`. The existing proof then runs unchanged.

For n = 0 the product runs over `range 0`, so it is empty and equals 1. Hence `frobPoly 0 m = coeff (m+1) 1 = 0`. So `frobSeries 0 = 1`: check coefficients with `ext j; cases j`, using `coeff_frobSeries_succ`, `Finset.range_zero`, `Finset.prod_empty` and `coeff_one`. The left side is then `coeff m (gh 1) = coeff m 0 = 0` by `gh_one` (line 47 of the file). The right side is `coeff (0*m) (gh genSeries) = coeff 0 (gh genSeries) = 0` by `zero_mul` and `coeff_zero_gh` (line 77). That lemma holds for any f, because `gh f = X * …`. For n > 0 the existing proof applies unchanged. So the theorem without `hn` is proved by `rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos n` followed by these two arguments.

### [`P2MW.S_GoodReductionJacobian_RelativeGroupLaw_isFinite_endKerStr_and_natCard_eq_endDegree_of_etale.P2mSepKer.isClosedImmersion_one`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_GoodReductionJacobian_RelativeGroupLaw_isFinite_endKerStr_and_natCard_eq_endDegree_of_etale.lean#L29-L33) — `flt-anthropic-0959`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0959.lean`

- `[IsSeparated f]` (theorem): The morphism s := (L.one (𝟙 (Spec K))).1 : Spec K ⟶ A satisfies s ≫ f = 𝟙 (field .2 of SchemeHomOver), so s is a section of f. Separatedness is not needed because the base Spec K is a one-point space. Mathlib's `isClosedImmersion_of_comp_eq_id {X Y : Scheme} [Subsingleton Y] (f : X ⟶ Y) (g : Y ⟶ X) (hg : g ≫ f = 𝟙 Y) : IsClosedImmersion g` (Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/Morphisms/ClosedImmersion.lean) proves exactly this. Its proof is local on X: on an affine open Spec R containing the image point, the map R → K has a left inverse, so it is surjective and gives a closed immersion; on an affine open missing the point, the preimage is empty and the map is trivially a closed immersion. The Subsingleton instance holds because the points of Spec (CommRingCat.of K) are PrimeSpectrum K, which has `instance [Field R] : Unique (PrimeSpectrum R)` (RingTheory/Spectrum/Prime/Basic.lean). Spec K is nonempty, so there is no other degenerate case. The replacement proof is `isClosedImmersion_of_comp_eq_id f _ (L.one _).2`, possibly with an explicit `haveI : Subsingleton (Spec (CommRingCat.of K))`. It replaces the step `IsClosedImmersion.of_comp`, the only place IsSeparated f was used. I checked this lemma in current Mathlib master but could not check the library's own Mathlib version; if the lemma is missing there, the affine-cover argument above can be written out directly.

### [`P2MW.S_Function_exists_eq_apply_sub_of_sum_range_minimalPeriod_eq_zero.Function.OrbitPrimitive.main`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_Function_exists_eq_apply_sub_of_sum_range_minimalPeriod_eq_zero.lean#L111-L127) — `flt-anthropic-0961`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0961.lean`

- `(hf : ∀ x : X, 0 < minimalPeriod f x)` (theorem): The current proof needs `hf`: it builds `orbitSetoid` (symmetry of reachability) and `idx_lt` from it. The statement itself does not need it. It is the standard fact that v = G∘f − G is solvable for every v whose sum over each periodic orbit is zero; `hv` says exactly that. Points with minimalPeriod 0 make `hv` say nothing (it reduces to the empty sum, 0 = 0), and the equation can still be solved at those points.

Construction without `hf`:
1. Let x ~ y iff ∃ a b, f^[a] x = f^[b] y. This is an equivalence relation: for transitivity, compose and shift the iterates.
2. With choice, pick a representative r in each class. If the class contains a periodic point, pick a periodic one.
3. For y in the class of r, choose a, b with f^[a] y = f^[b] r. Set G y = S(b) − ∑_{k<a} v(f^[k] y), where S(b) = ∑_{k<b} v(f^[k] r).

Well-definedness: replacing (a, b) by (a+t, b+t) adds the terms ∑_{k<t} v(f^[b+k] r) and removes ∑_{k<t} v(f^[a+k] y). These are equal because f^[a+k] y = f^[b+k] r. So any two pairs can be brought to the same a, with points f^[b] r = f^[b'] r.
- If r is not periodic, its forward orbit is injective, so b = b'. If f^[i] r = f^[j] r with i < j, then f^[i] r would be periodic, against the choice of r.
- If r is periodic with minimal period p, then b ≡ b' mod p. Also S(b+p) = S(b) + ∑_{k<p} v(f^[k] (f^[b] r)), and that sum is 0 by `hv` at f^[b] r, which also has minimal period p. Hence S(b) = S(b').

Finally, using the pair (a+1, b) for y and (a, b) for f y gives G (f y) − G y = v y.

Degenerate cases: empty X is trivial. Preperiodic tails and aperiodic components are covered by the construction. So the theorem holds and is provable (with classical choice) without `hf`, though the proof must be rewritten along these lines.

- `[DecidableEq σ]` (variable): The theorem takes this only because it is an instance-implicit section variable and `σ` is an argument. Nothing in the statement needs it. `varIdeal R σ` is defined as `Ideal.span (Set.range MvPolynomial.X)`, with only `[CommRing R]`. `Finsupp.degree` and `MvPolynomial.coeff` also need no decidability on σ. The proof uses it only through `lowVanish R σ N` (which takes `[DecidableEq σ]`) inside `varIdeal_pow_le_lowVanish`. Without the binder, the proof can start with `classical` and apply the same lemma: membership in `lowVanish` unfolds to the decidability-free condition `∀ d, d.degree < N → coeff d p = 0` (`mem_lowVanish` is `Iff.rfl`), so the conclusion is unchanged. There are no degenerate cases, since the statement does not depend on this instance at all.
- `[Fintype σ]` (variable): It is pulled in as an instance-implicit section variable. Note that `mem_lowVanish` right above it even has `omit [Fintype σ] in`. The statement is true for any σ, finite or not. Every element of `(span (range X))^N` is a combination of products of N variables times polynomials, so every monomial in its support has degree ≥ N. The current proof uses finiteness only in `varIdeal_pow_le_lowVanish`, where `Ideal.mem_span_range_iff_exists_fun` (which requires `Fintype`) writes q ∈ span(range X) as a finite sum ∑ c s * X s. Without finiteness, the same induction on N works if that step is replaced by `Submodule.span_induction` on q ∈ span(range X), proving p * q ∈ lowVanish (N+1) for p ∈ lowVanish N. The zero, addition and scalar cases hold because `lowVanish (N+1)` is an ideal (p * (a * q) = a * (p * q)). The generator case q = X s is exactly the existing `coeff_mul_X'` computation. With σ empty, varIdeal is ⊥ and every d has degree 0; for N = 0 the hypothesis `d.degree < 0` is false, and for N ≥ 1, p = 0. So the statement still holds there. The two binders can also both be deleted together.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_exists_isParabolicHom_apply_eq_period.ModularCurve.PeriodParabolic.main`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_exists_isParabolicHom_apply_eq_period.lean#L228-L231) — `flt-anthropic-0994`

Snippet: `samples/flt-anthropic/flt-anthropic-0994.lean`

- `[NeZero N]` (variable): The statement elaborates without it: `ModularCurve.period` (Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_PeriodLattice.lean, section with only `variable (N : ℕ)`), `CongruenceSubgroup.Gamma0 N`, `CuspForm` and `IsParabolicHom` do not need `NeZero`. It is only used in the proof, through `exists_hasEquivariantPrimitive` and `period_apply_eq_sub_of_hasEquivariantPrimitive`. The statement is also true for N = 0, with the proof unchanged for N ≠ 0. For N = 0, `Gamma0 0` is the set of γ with γ 1 0 = 0 in ZMod 0 = ℤ. The determinant then forces a = d = ±1, so every element is ±Tⁿ and sends I to I + m with m an integer. So `period 0 γ f` is ∫₀¹ f(I + t m)·m dt, the integral of f along a horizontal segment of integer length. Every `f : CuspForm (Gamma0 0) 2` is 1-periodic, because T ∈ Gamma0 0 and slashing by T in weight 2 is translation. It is holomorphic, and it tends to 0 at i∞: ∞ is a cusp in Mathlib's sense (`IsCusp`: T is parabolic and fixes ∞), and `zero_at_cusps'` with g = 1 in `OnePoint.IsZeroAt` gives `IsZeroAtImInfty f`. Put G(y) = ∫₀^m f(x+iy) dx. Its derivative is i(f(m+iy) − f(iy)) = 0 by periodicity, so G is constant, and G → 0 as y → ∞. Hence every period vanishes (equivalently, the q-expansion has no constant term). Then Φ = 0 witnesses the statement: Φ(ofMul γ) = 0 = period 0 γ, and IsParabolicHom holds trivially. So the theorem holds for all N without `[NeZero N]`: case-split on N = 0 and keep the existing proof otherwise. The N = 0 case needs this vanishing-period lemma to be written; I did not find a single Mathlib lemma that gives it directly.

### [`GradedRing.Veronese.adjoin_component_one_eq_top`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/graded-rings/blob/05143eb425d31ab18c726dc431b2314834ab8094/GradedRings/VeroneseDegreeOne.lean#L80-L104) — `formal-frontier-0005`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0005.lean`

- `(hn : 0 < n)` (theorem): The statement is still true when n = 0. The library proves this case itself in `GradedRings/VeroneseZero.lean`, without `hgen` and without any finiteness assumption. There, `zeroVariable 𝒮 := DirectSum.of (fun j => 𝒮 (0 * j)) 1 (zeroUnit 𝒮 1)` lies in `component 𝒮 0 1`: `mem_component_iff` gives this directly, since `component` is defined as the image of `DirectSum.of _ j`. The theorem `aeval_zeroVariable_surjective` says that `Polynomial.aeval (R := component 𝒮 0 0) (zeroVariable 𝒮)` is surjective onto `VeroneseRing 𝒮 0`. It uses the same `Algebra (component 𝒮 n 0) (VeroneseRing 𝒮 n)` instance as this statement: the subring instance for the degree-zero part of the graded ring `component 𝒮 n` from `Veronese.lean`. A proof without `hn` therefore starts by case-splitting on n. If n = 0, take any x and write it as x = aeval (zeroVariable 𝒮) p. Then x ∈ adjoin {zeroVariable 𝒮} by Mathlib's `Polynomial.aeval_mem_adjoin_singleton`, and this is contained in `Algebra.adjoin (component 𝒮 0 0) (component 𝒮 0 1)` by `Algebra.adjoin_mono`, because zeroVariable ∈ component 𝒮 0 1. So the adjoin is ⊤. If n > 0, the existing proof applies unchanged. Degenerate cases: for the trivial ring S, every type involved is a subsingleton and the claim is immediate; the n = 0 argument above makes no nontriviality assumption anyway. Deleting `hn` does not affect how the statement elaborates, because `hn` appears nowhere else in it.

### [`Nat.DivisionGrowth.affine_lower_bound`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/sequence-growth/blob/7b7db3df8a043a09d037de4ff1eced6873404fb2/SequenceGrowth/Data/Nat/DivisionGrowth.lean#L68-L94) — `formal-frontier-0117`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0117.lean`

- `(hs : 0 < s)` (theorem): `hs` only excludes the case s = 0, and the statement already holds when s = 0. Then `(s - 1 : ℕ) = 0` by truncated subtraction, so offset = n * 0 / (n - 0) = 0. Also ratio = (n : ℚ) / 0 = 0, because division by zero is 0 in ℚ. The goal becomes `0 ^ m * (u 0 - 0) + 0 ≤ u m`. For m = 0 this is `u 0 ≤ u 0`. For m = i + 1 the left side is 0, and `0 ≤ (u m : ℚ)` holds since u m is a natural number cast to ℚ. Proof without `hs`: split on `s` with `rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos s`. If s = 0, substitute and close the goal by `simp` plus `cases m` (`positivity` / `Nat.cast_nonneg` handles the successor case). If 0 < s, run the existing proof unchanged, using the new local hypothesis in place of `hs` (it is passed to `affine_step`). `hsn` is still needed: with s = n = 2, u 0 = 1 and u m = 0 for m ≥ 1, `hstep` holds but the claim at m = 1 is 1 ≤ 0, which is false.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.reducedIdentityComponent_topologicalKrullDim`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/GroupScheme/Dimension.lean#L265-L296) — `formal-frontier-0126`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0126.lean`

- `[IsAlgClosed K]` (variable): `[IsAlgClosed K]` is an argument because its type mentions `K`, which is an argument through `f`. The statement does not need it to elaborate. It is only used through `identityComponent_topologicalKrullDim`, whose proof uses `groupSchemePointComponent_surjective` (rational points meet every component). The equality also holds over any field, and the existing file's lemmas prove it.

Let H := Scheme.reductionOver (Over.mk f). The current proof already builds `GrpObj (Over.mk H.hom)` and `LocallyOfFiniteType H.hom` without IsAlgClosed: the reduction map is a closed immersion, composed with f. Let u := η[H].left (closedPoint K), and let p := (Scheme.identityComponentOne H).left (closedPoint K). Then `identityComponentι H` sends p to u, by `Scheme.identityComponentOne_comp_ι`.

(≤) `topologicalKrullDim (identityComponentOver H).left ≤ topologicalKrullDim H.left`, because the inclusion is an open embedding (`isOpenEmbedding.isInducing.topologicalKrullDim_le`, as in the existing proof).

(≥) Use `ringKrullDim_stalk_unit_eq_topologicalKrullDim K H.left H.hom` from this file. It needs only `Field K`, `GrpObj` and `LocallyOfFiniteType`, not IsAlgClosed. It gives `topologicalKrullDim H.left = ringKrullDim (stalk at u)`. By Mathlib's `ringKrullDim_stalk_eq_coheight` this equals `coheight u`. By `coheight_eq_of_isOpenImmersion` (the inclusion is an open immersion) this equals `coheight p`. Finally, `coheight p ≤ krullDim (IrreducibleCloseds _) = topologicalKrullDim (identityComponentOver H).left`, via `irreducibleSetEquivPoints` and `Order.coheight_le_krullDim`, exactly as in the first half of the proof of `ringKrullDim_stalk_unit_eq_topologicalKrullDim`.

The last step, `Scheme.reduction_topologicalKrullDim G`, does not involve K at all. Degenerate cases cause no trouble: Spec K is a nonempty one-point space, and the argument holds whether or not G is empty or of dimension ⊥.

The statement stays true, and the proof is unchanged: `limitCylinder_subset_iff_eventually F i hX hmap (limit.cone F) (limit.isLimit F) U V hUopen hUcompact hVopen` holds for any `HasLimit F` instance, because the earlier theorem works for an arbitrary limit cone `C` with `IsLimit C`.

### [`CategoryTheory.CartesianMonoidalCategory.tensorLeftIsoProd_hom_app_fst`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/scheme-properties/blob/3455b4b9192b77c1754729bafa64f99ea8c5c838/SchemeProperties/SingleCoverGroup.lean#L58-L63) — `formal-frontier-0146`

### [`IsKrasner.adjoin_rootSet_le_adjoin_rootSet_of_norm_coeff_sub_lt`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/polynomial-root-stability/blob/ceae7822f6151ec6626678db1653054d3398cff3/PolynomialRootStability/Krasner.lean#L363-L391) — `formal-frontier-0152`

### [`Subgroup.exists_continuous_coset_representative`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/profinite-groups/blob/d21abb3f7fedd7241f3dbcb91ba8eed73d2caf9c/ProfiniteGroups/ContinuousCosetRepresentative.lean#L55-L74) — `formal-frontier-0160`

### [`MvPowerSeries.isUnit_adicallyRestricted_iff_coeff_radical`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/formal-power-series/blob/9bb57357300e4fad20d074ee4cd95e8317a34c81/FormalPowerSeries/MvPowerSeries/IdealAdicRestricted/UnitDetection.lean#L83-L92) — `formal-frontier-0162`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0162.lean`

- `[IsAdicComplete I R]` (variable): Nothing in the statement mentions `IsAdicComplete`. The theorem takes it only because it is an instance variable and `I` and `R` are arguments. The existing proof needs it twice: in `isUnit_adicallyRestricted_iff_adicReduction_one` (through the geometric-series inverse) and in `isUnit_iff_quotient_of_isAdicComplete`. With the RHS as stated (a unit constant coefficient in `R` itself, and the other coefficients in `I.radical`), completeness is not needed.

The definition `adicallyRestrictedSubring I` (IdealAdicRestricted.lean:78) says: for every k, the image of the series in (R/I^k)[[σ]] is a polynomial. It does not involve completeness.

(→) If f is a unit in the subring, then `coeff 0 = constantCoeff ∘ Subring.subtype` is a ring hom, so `coeff 0 f` is a unit. Also `adicReduction I 1 f` is a unit in `MvPolynomial σ (R ⧸ I^1)`. By Mathlib's `MvPolynomial.isUnit_iff` (Algebra/MvPolynomial/Nilpotent.lean:58) together with `coeff_adicReduction` and `I^1 = I`, each `mk I (coeff α f)` with α ≠ 0 is nilpotent, i.e. `coeff α f ∈ I.radical`. This is exactly the first half of the existing proof of `..._coeff_mod_ideal`, and it does not use completeness.

(←) Since `coeff 0 f` is a unit, `MvPowerSeries.isUnit_iff_constantCoeff` gives an inverse g of f in `MvPowerSeries σ R`. It remains to show g is restricted. Fix k. The image of f in (R/I^k)[[σ]] is the coercion of a polynomial P_k. Its constant coefficient is a unit. Every other coefficient is the image of some r ∈ I.radical: if r^m ∈ I then r^{mk} ∈ I^k, so that image is nilpotent in R/I^k. By `MvPolynomial.isUnit_iff`, P_k has an inverse Q_k in (R/I^k)[σ]. Inverses are unique in (R/I^k)[[σ]], so `map (mk (I^k)) g = ↑Q_k`, a polynomial. Hence g lies in the subring, and f is a unit there.

Degenerate cases are fine. If I = ⊤, every series is restricted and I.radical = ⊤, so both sides reduce to `coeff 0 f` being a unit. A trivial R and an empty σ are also harmless. So the theorem stays true and provable after `omit [IsAdicComplete I R] in`.

### [`FundamentalGroup.existsUnique_hom_of_isOpenCover`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/fundamental-groupoids/blob/2fdb99858e3feb7a6a06ae10915f286364de2cc1/FundamentalGroupoids/AlgebraicTopology/FundamentalGroup/OpenCover.lean#L404-L527) — `formal-frontier-0164`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0164.lean`

- `(hU : IsPathConnected (U : Set X))` (theorem): The current proof uses hU, through `leftArrow` and `globalArrow`. Even so, the statement still holds without it, given `hcover`, `hI` and the base point `p`. The other hypotheses do not need to change.

Let A be the path component of p in U and A' the path component of p in V. Since U ∩ V is path-connected (hI) and contains p, we have U ∩ V ⊆ A ∩ A', and therefore A ∩ A' = U ∩ V.

(1) The path component PC of p in X lies in A ∪ A'. Take a path γ from p. By hcover and a Lebesgue-number argument, γ splits into finitely many pieces, each lying in U or in V. Every junction between a U-piece and a V-piece lies in U ∩ V ⊆ A ∩ A'. By induction, each U-piece starts in A and so stays in A, and each V-piece stays in A'.

(2) From (1), U ∩ PC = A: a point of U ∩ PC lies in A or in A' ⊆ V, and in the second case it lies in U ∩ V ⊆ A. Likewise V ∩ PC = A'. So A and A' are open in PC and cover it, both are path-connected, and their intersection U ∩ V is path-connected.

(3) Every loop at p, and every homotopy of such loops, has path-connected image containing p. So the inclusions A ↪ U, A' ↪ V and PC ↪ X induce isomorphisms of fundamental groups at p.

Now apply the theorem itself, with all its hypotheses, to the space PC and the cover A, A'. Pull φU and φV back along the isomorphisms π₁(A,p) ≅ π₁(U,p) and π₁(A',p) ≅ π₁(V,p). The compatibility condition carries over, because the map U ∩ V → U factors through A. The theorem then gives a unique φ' : π₁(PC,p) →* G. Composing φ' with the inverse of π₁(PC,p) ≅ π₁(X,p) gives the required φ. Existence and uniqueness follow because the isomorphisms in (3) are bijective and every square commutes.

The degenerate cases are covered: `p` makes U ∩ V nonempty. Hence hU can be dropped. The same argument applies to hV, and in fact both can be dropped together.
- `(hV : IsPathConnected (V : Set X))` (theorem): This is symmetric to hU. Let A' be the path component of p in V (and A that of p in U). hI and `p ∈ U ∩ V` give U ∩ V ⊆ A ∩ A'. Splitting a path into pieces by a Lebesgue-number argument, using hcover, shows that the path component PC of p in X lies in A ∪ A'. It follows that V ∩ PC = A' and U ∩ PC = A, so A and A' are an open cover of PC by path-connected sets with path-connected intersection U ∩ V. The inclusions A ↪ U, A' ↪ V and PC ↪ X induce isomorphisms on π₁ at p, because loops and homotopies at p stay in the path component of p. So the universal property for X, U, V follows from the theorem (with all its hypotheses) applied to PC, A, A', by transporting along these isomorphisms. Thus hV can be deleted; hU and hV can even be deleted simultaneously.

### [`GradedRing.Veronese.finiteType_of_finiteType`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/graded-rings/blob/05143eb425d31ab18c726dc431b2314834ab8094/GradedRings/VeroneseFiniteType.lean#L105-L126) — `formal-frontier-0179`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0179.lean`

- `(hn : 0 < n)` (theorem): For n = 0, VeroneseRing 𝒮 0 = ⨁ j, 𝒮 (0*j). Each summand is 𝒮 0 and the multiplication is graded, so the ring is a copy of (𝒮 0)[t]. The base ring component 𝒮 0 0 is the image of DirectSum.of _ 0, and it acts through Mathlib's grade-zero algebra instance, whose algebraMap is the inclusion (SetLike.GradeZero.algebraMap_apply). Let t := DirectSum.of _ 1 ⟨1, one_mem_graded⟩. By DirectSum.of_mul_of, t^j = of j 1 and of j a = algebraMap (of 0 a') * t^j, where a' is a viewed in 𝒮 0. By DirectSum.induction_on, Algebra.adjoin (component 𝒮 0 0) {t} = ⊤, so Algebra.FiniteType follows from Subalgebra.fg_def with the finite set {t}. This case needs no hypothesis (not even FiniteType of S) and the trivial ring needs no special treatment. The revised proof splits on n: the argument above for n = 0, the existing proof for 0 < n.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.Proj.degreeScaledChartMap_preimage_basicOpen`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/projective-space/blob/3d39919aac0def769a0ff8b44e62a1317d4c84fe/ProjectiveSpace/DegreeScaledMap.lean#L453-L469) — `formal-frontier-0188`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0188.lean`

- `(hn : 0 < n)` (theorem): The degree-0 case `n = 0` follows from the positive-degree case. Write `s := (i.2 : A) ∈ 𝒜 i.1` with `0 < i.1`.
(1) Apply the theorem as stated to `r := s` (with `hr := i.2.2`, `hn := i.1.2`). This gives `chartMap ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen 𝒜 s = ι ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen ℬ (f s)`, and the right side is `⊤` because `ι` is the inclusion of `basicOpen ℬ (f s)`.
(2) For `r ∈ 𝒜 0`, `r * s ∈ 𝒜 (0 + i.1)` by `SetLike.GradedMul.mul_mem`, and that degree is positive. So the stated theorem applies to `r * s`. Mathlib's `Proj.basicOpen_mul` (ProjectiveSpectrum/Basic.lean) gives `basicOpen 𝒜 (r*s) = basicOpen 𝒜 r ⊓ basicOpen 𝒜 s`. Together with `map_mul`, it also gives `basicOpen ℬ (f r * f s) = basicOpen ℬ (f r) ⊓ basicOpen ℬ (f s)`. Preimages commute with `⊓`, and by (1) both `s`-factors pull back to `⊤`. This yields `chartMap ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen 𝒜 r = ι ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen ℬ (f r)`.
So the statement holds for every `n`, and the proof is the current one applied to `r*s` and `s`, plus `basicOpen_mul`. Pointwise, the chart map sends a homogeneous prime `q` to `f⁻¹(q)`, so the equality is `r ∉ f⁻¹ q ↔ f r ∉ q`, which holds in any degree.
- `(hr : r ∈ 𝒜 n)` (theorem): The equality also holds for non-homogeneous `r`. Mathlib's `ProjectiveSpectrum.basicOpen_eq_union_of_projection` (ProjectiveSpectrum/Topology.lean) gives `basicOpen 𝒜 r = ⨆ k, basicOpen 𝒜 (proj 𝒜 k r)`. Taking preimages commutes with `⨆`. Each component `r_k := proj 𝒜 k r` lies in `𝒜 k`, so the homogeneous version applies to it, using the degree-0 argument from the other entry when `k = 0`. This gives `ι ⁻¹ᵁ basicOpen ℬ (f r_k)` for each `k`.
On the `B` side, `f r = Σ_k f r_k` with `f r_k ∈ ℬ (d*k)` (by `hdeg`), and `k ↦ d*k` is injective since `0 < d`. So `proj ℬ j (f r)` equals `f r_k` when `j = d*k` and `0` otherwise. Since `basicOpen ℬ 0 = ⊥`, the same lemma gives `basicOpen ℬ (f r) = ⨆ k, basicOpen ℬ (f r_k)`, and the two sides agree.
After the deletion, `n` and `hn` stay as harmless unused arguments, so the rest of the statement still elaborates. Pointwise, the chart map sends `q` to the homogeneous prime `f⁻¹ q`, and `r ∉ f⁻¹ q ↔ f r ∉ q` holds for every `r`.
`hr` and `hn` can each be deleted on their own, and also together.

### [`finiteGroupPresentation_iff_compact_totallyDisconnected`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/profinite-groups/blob/d21abb3f7fedd7241f3dbcb91ba8eed73d2caf9c/ProfiniteGroups/FiniteGroupCharacterization.lean#L198-L214) — `formal-frontier-0200`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0200.lean`

- `[T2Space G]` (theorem): The proof never uses it. Forward direction: CompactSpace and TotallyDisconnectedSpace are transported along the homeomorphism P.equiv.symm.toHomeomorph using Homeomorph.compactSpace and Homeomorph.totallyDisconnectedSpace, neither of which needs T2. Backward direction: `ProfiniteGrp.of` (Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Category/ProfiniteGrp/Basic.lean:81) only requires [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] [IsTopologicalGroup G] [CompactSpace G] [TotallyDisconnectedSpace G]. Its docstring says the Hausdorff condition can be omitted, because a totally disconnected group has {1} closed and is therefore Hausdorff. `FiniteGroupPresentation.ofProfiniteGrp` then gives the presentation. So the theorem and its proof go through unchanged without [T2Space G]. The trivial group needs no special treatment.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Hom.ker_radical_of_isReduced_source`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/Scheme/JointlySchemeTheoreticallyDominant.lean#L154-L160) — `formal-frontier-0232`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0232.lean`

- `[QuasiCompact f]` (theorem): The proof uses `QuasiCompact f` only to rewrite with `Hom.ker_apply`, which turns `f.ker.ideal U` into `RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom`. The statement holds for any `f`. In Mathlib, `Hom.ker f := ofIdeals fun U ↦ RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom` (IdealSheaf/Basic.lean:691). Three facts give a proof without the hypothesis: `ofIdeals J` is the largest ideal sheaf whose ideals lie below `J` (`le_ofIdeals_iff : I ≤ ofIdeals J ↔ I.ideal ≤ J`), `ideal_ker_le` gives `f.ker.ideal U ≤ RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom` with no quasi-compactness, and `radical_ideal` gives `(I.radical).ideal U = (I.ideal U).radical`. Because X is reduced, each `RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom = comap (f.app U) ⊥` is a radical ideal (`Ideal.isRadical_bot.comap _`, as in the original proof). The order `≥` is `le_radical`. For `≤`, apply `le_ofIdeals_iff.mpr`. For each affine open U, `(f.ker.ideal U).radical ≤ (RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom).radical = RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom` follows from `Ideal.radical_mono (f.ideal_ker_le U)` and the radicality just shown. Then `le_antisymm` closes the goal. No step uses quasi-compactness. Degenerate case: if X is empty, then `f.ker = ⊤` and `⊤.radical = ⊤` (`radical_top`), which agrees with the general argument.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.iInf_ker_eq_vanishingIdeal_iSup_closure_range`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/Scheme/JointlySchemeTheoreticallyDominant.lean#L176-L185) — `formal-frontier-0233`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0233.lean`

- `[∀ j, QuasiCompact (f j)]` (theorem): The proof uses quasi-compactness only through the helper `Scheme.Hom.ker_eq_vanishingIdeal_closure_range`, which relies on Mathlib's `Hom.support_ker` (that lemma assumes `[QuasiCompact f]`). For a reduced source, the equality `f.ker = vanishingIdeal (closure (range f))` holds for any morphism. Mathlib defines `Hom.ker f := ofIdeals (fun U ↦ RingHom.ker (f.app U).hom)`, the largest ideal sheaf contained in the componentwise kernels. (1) Kernel ≤ vanishing ideal: Mathlib's `Hom.range_subset_ker_support` (no quasi-compactness assumption) gives `range f ⊆ f.ker.support`. The support is closed, so `closure (range f) ≤ f.ker.support`, and the Galois connection between support and vanishing ideal gives `f.ker ≤ vanishingIdeal (closure (range f))`. (2) Vanishing ideal ≤ kernel: by `le_ofIdeals_iff`, it suffices to show `(vanishingIdeal Z).ideal U ≤ RingHom.ker (f.app U)` for each affine open `U`. Let `s ∈ Γ(Y, U)` vanish at every point of `Z ∩ U`. Then `Y.basicOpen s` is disjoint from `range f`, so `X.basicOpen (f.app U s) = f ⁻¹ᵁ Y.basicOpen s = ⊥`. Since the source is reduced, `eq_zero_of_basicOpen_eq_bot` (Mathlib, `AlgebraicGeometry/Properties.lean`) gives `f.app U s = 0`. With this per-morphism equality, the rest of the proof (`vanishingIdeal_iSup`, `congr`, `funext`) is unchanged. Degenerate cases: if `J` is empty, both sides are `⊤`; if some `X j` is empty, its kernel is `⊤` and its closure of range is `⊥`, which agree. Reducedness, by contrast, is needed: for `X = Y = Spec k[ε]/(ε²)` and `f = 𝟙`, the kernel is `⊥` but the vanishing ideal of the whole space is the nilradical, which is not `⊥`.

### [`MvPowerSeries.existsUnique_firstVariable_division_of_scalar_top`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/formal-power-series/blob/9bb57357300e4fad20d074ee4cd95e8317a34c81/FormalPowerSeries/MvPowerSeries/IdealAdicRestricted/FirstVariableDivision.lean#L112-L132) — `formal-frontier-0285`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0285.lean`

- `(hprim : Ideal.span (Set.range (fun index : Fin (n + 1) →₀ ℕ => coeff index (f : MvPowerSeries (Fin (n + 1)) V))) = ⊤)` (theorem): `hprim` follows from `htop` and `hc`. Let x = coeff ((0 : Fin n →₀ ℕ).cons d) f. Mathlib's `MvPolynomial.finSuccEquiv_coeff_coeff` (Mathlib/Algebra/MvPolynomial/Equiv.lean) with m = 0, rewritten using `htop`, gives (restrictedResidue a ham f).coeff ((0).cons d) = (MvPolynomial.C c).coeff 0 = c. By the library's `coeff_restrictedResidue`, that coefficient equals Ideal.Quotient.mk (maximalIdeal V) x. Since c ≠ 0, x is not in the maximal ideal, so `IsLocalRing.notMem_maximalIdeal` says x is a unit. x is in the range of the coefficient map, so the span contains a unit and equals ⊤ (`Ideal.eq_top_of_isUnit_mem`). The proof then goes through unchanged, using this derived fact wherever `hprim` was passed. This works independently of whether `ha` is also removed.
- `(ha : a ≠ 0)` (theorem): The case a = 0 is consistent with the other hypotheses, and the statement still holds there. If a = 0, then span{0} = ⊥, and its radical is the nilradical, which is ⊥ because V is a domain. So `hrad` forces maximalIdeal V = ⊥, which makes V a field and V ⧸ m ≅ V. `ham` holds trivially, and IsAdicComplete ⊥ V holds. In `adicallyRestrictedSubring ⊥` the k = 1 condition (V ⧸ ⊥ ≅ V) forces f to be a polynomial, so the restricted rings are exactly MvPolynomial (Fin (n+1)) V and MvPolynomial (Fin n) V. `polynomialRestrictedFinFirst` is the natural identification of V[X₁..Xₙ][X₀] with these polynomials (`coeff_polynomialRestrictedFinFirst`). `restrictedResidue f` is f itself. So `hdegree` and `htop` say that f, viewed as a polynomial in X₀ over V[X₁..Xₙ], has degree d and leading coefficient the nonzero constant c, which is a unit. Euclidean division by a polynomial with unit leading coefficient therefore gives h = q·f + P with deg P < d. The pair is unique: if q·f = P' − P with q ≠ 0, then deg(q·f) = deg q + d ≥ d (unit leading coefficient, domain), a contradiction. A proof without `ha` would split on `a = 0`: use the existing argument when a ≠ 0, and this polynomial-division argument when a = 0. That branch is substantial to write, because the current proof calls lemmas that take `ha`. `ha` and `hprim` can each be removed separately.

### [`IsFractionRing.isInteger_of_sq_mul_squarefree`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/quadratic-algebras/blob/85a6b1bc79989b524a4ecdae6cce17b712a7bd2d/QuadraticAlgebras/Squarefree.lean#L39-L62) — `formal-frontier-0311`
