Mathlib's RingTheory/Localization/FractionRing.lean (line 162) has the instance `FaithfulSMul R K`, built from `IsFractionRing.injective`; it needs only CommRing R, CommRing K, Algebra R K and IsFractionRing R K. `IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul` (Algebra/Algebra/Basic.lean, line 455) gives `IsDomain R` from `FaithfulSMul R A` and `IsDomain A`. A field is a domain, so `IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul A K : IsDomain A`; the underlying argument is `Function.Injective.isDomain` for the injective map `algebraMap A K`. A field is nontrivial, so A is nontrivial and there is no degenerate case. The statement itself (Squarefree f, algebraMap, IsLocalization.IsInteger) does not need IsDomain A to elaborate; only the proof's lemmas (num/den, num_den_reduced, isInteger_of_isUnit_den) use it. So: add `omit [IsDomain A] in` before the theorem, start the proof with `haveI : IsDomain A := IsDomain.of_faithfulSMul A K`, and leave the rest of the proof unchanged. These lemmas were checked in current Mathlib master, and the change was not compiled.

The proof uses Nonempty only through card_cast_pos (0 < card n) to apply div_le_div_iff_of_pos_right. ### [`ToricIdeals.RationalNormalCurve.spectrum_irreducible`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/toric-ideals/blob/b91a7be3aab7ee857dfe6d3c0450f0a2af454027/ToricIdeals/RationalNormalCurve.lean#L906-L911) — `formal-frontier-0417`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0417.lean`

- `(hn : 1 ≤ n)` (theorem): The proof uses `hn` only to obtain `IsDomain (Source k n ⧸ hankelIdeal k n)` from `quotient_isDomain`. The same file proves `quotient_isDomain_iff (R) (n) : IsDomain (Source R n ⧸ hankelIdeal R n) ↔ IsDomain R` at line 855, for every `n` and before this theorem. The degree-zero case is covered there: `hankelIdeal k 0 = ⊥`, so the quotient is a one-variable polynomial ring over a domain. So the proof can become `let _ := (quotient_isDomain_iff k n).2 inferInstance; infer_instance`. The final `infer_instance` step, which gives the irreducible space instance on the prime spectrum of a domain, is unchanged and does not depend on `hn`.

### [`MvPolynomial.isOpen_reduced_atPrime_pointSquareZeroQuotient_iff_finite_range`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/scheme-properties/blob/3455b4b9192b77c1754729bafa64f99ea8c5c838/SchemeProperties/PolynomialPointSquareZeroReducedLocus.lean#L40-L56) — `formal-frontier-0487`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0487.lean`

- `(ha : Function.Injective a)` (theorem): The current proof uses `ha` only to get unit separation `hsep`. That gives the isomorphism `pointSquareZeroAlgEquiv` with the trivial square-zero extension, and then the existing criterion for that extension applies. The statement itself holds for any `a : ι → K`, so the proof has to change if `ha` is removed.

By the API docs, `A := pointSquareZeroQuotient a` is K[X, T_i : i ∈ ι] / (T_i², (X − a_i)·T_i), where `X none` plays the role of X. The argument:

1. Every T_i is nilpotent, and A/(T_i)_i ≅ K[X] is reduced. So the nilradical is N = (T_i)_i, and Spec A is homeomorphic to Spec K[X].
2. Localization commutes with the nilradical. So A_p is reduced iff T_i/1 = 0 in A_p for every i, i.e. iff Ann(T_i) ⊄ p for every i. The non-reduced locus is therefore ⋃_i V(Ann T_i).
3. Ann(T_i) contains X − a_i, and every prime contains all the T_j. So V(Ann T_i) ⊆ {m_{a_i}}, where m_c = (X − c, all T_j).
4. T_i ≠ 0 in A. To see this, map A to K[X] ⊕ Kε (ε² = 0, X acting on ε by a_i), sending X ↦ X, T_i ↦ ε and T_j ↦ 0 for j ≠ i. The relations hold, including when a_j = a_i, so the map is well defined and T_i ↦ ε ≠ 0. Hence V(Ann T_i) = {m_{a_i}}.
5. The non-reduced locus is thus the set of closed points {m_c : c ∈ range a}, and distinct values c give distinct points. Through the homeomorphism with Spec K[X] (a PID), a set of closed points that misses the generic point is closed iff it is finite. So the reduced locus is open iff `Set.range a` is finite.

Duplicate values (a_i = a_j for i ≠ j) only stop T_i·T_j from vanishing. That product is nilpotent and does not change the locus. Degenerate case ι empty: A ≅ K[X] is reduced, the locus is all of Spec A, which is open, and the empty range is finite, so the statement holds.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.Scheme.Opens.appTop_surjective_of_isClopen`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/Scheme/ClopenAffine.lean#L65-L70) — `formal-frontier-0489`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0489.lean`

- `[IsAffine X]` (theorem): The statement holds for every scheme X. Let V be the open set `Uᶜ`, which is open because U is closed (`hU.1`). Then U ⊓ V = ⊥ and U ⊔ V = ⊤. Up to the canonical identification of Γ(U, ⊤) with Γ(X, U), `U.ι.appTop` is restriction of sections along U ≤ ⊤. Given a section s on U, take s on U and 0 on V. These two sections agree on U ⊓ V = ⊥ automatically, because the structure sheaf has a single section over the empty open. By the sheaf property they glue to a global section t, and t restricts to s on U. In Lean this can be done with `TopCat.Sheaf.objSupIsoProdEqLocus` (Mathlib/Topology/Sheaves/CommRingCat.lean) and its `_inv_fst` lemma, or with `TopCat.Sheaf.existsUnique_gluing`. `hU` cannot be dropped: for U = D(f) in Spec ℤ, the restriction ℤ → ℤ[1/f] is not surjective. Affineness is used only in the current proof, through `IsClosedImmersion.isAffine_surjective_of_isAffine`. The file's own docstring agrees: "The closed-immersion and restriction results hold for arbitrary schemes". Degenerate cases: if U = ∅ the target ring is the zero ring, so the map is surjective; if U = ⊤ the map is an isomorphism.

### [`MonomialOrder.existsUnique_restricted_primitive_singleton_division`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/formal-power-series/blob/9bb57357300e4fad20d074ee4cd95e8317a34c81/FormalPowerSeries/MvPowerSeries/IdealAdicRestricted/SingletonDivision.lean#L82-L135) — `formal-frontier-0502`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0502.lean`

- `(ha : a ≠ 0)` (theorem): `ha` appears nowhere else in the statement. The proof uses it only to call `exists_restricted_primitive_division`, and only for the existence part. The case a = 0 is not ruled out by the other hypotheses, and the theorem still holds in that case.

**What a = 0 forces.** `Ideal.span {0} = ⊥`. Since R is a domain, `Ideal.radical_bot_of_isReduced` gives `radical ⊥ = ⊥`. So `hrad` says `maximalIdeal R = ⊥`, which makes R a field. `ham` holds trivially, and `IsAdicComplete ⊥ R` holds as well (the ⊥-adic topology is discrete). With `IsAdicallyRestricted I f := ∀ k, {m | coeff m f ∉ I^k}.Finite` and I = ⊥, a series is restricted exactly when its support is finite. So `adicallyRestrictedSubring` is just MvPolynomial σ R over a field, and `hprim` says g ≠ 0.

**Uniqueness.** `restricted_singleton_division_unique` (same file) needs only `[IsHausdorff (span{a}) R]` (which follows from `IsAdicComplete`), `ham` and `g ≠ 0`. It does not need `ha`, so this part of the proof is unchanged.

**Existence.** `restrictedLeadingExponent_eq_degree_of_primitive_reduction` does not need `ha` either. Take p to be the image of the polynomial g in MvPolynomial σ (R ⧸ ⊥); this map is an isomorphism on coefficients. The lemma then gives that `restrictedLeadingExponent a ham g` equals `μ.degree p`, which is `μ.degree g`. Over a field the leading coefficient of g ≠ 0 is a unit, so Mathlib's `MonomialOrder.div_single` (Mathlib/RingTheory/MvPolynomial/Groebner.lean) gives `f = q * g + r` with `∀ c ∈ r.support, ¬ μ.degree g ≤ c`. That is exactly the required vanishing of r's coefficients on the cone above the leading exponent. Moving q and r back into the restricted subring (they are polynomials) gives the existence part.

**Proof without `ha`.** Split on whether a = 0. If a ≠ 0, use the existing proof. If a = 0, use the field and polynomial division argument above. The statement stays true and provable without `ha`.

### [`TopCat.Sheaf.RightDerivedPushforward.HPrimeIsoRestrictedResolutionSectionsHomology_hom_naturality`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/sheaf-cohomology/blob/93674a2a8f1ad19b2c87b20c3f5e0898b7e38f43/SheafCohomology/OpenCohomologyPushforwardResolution.lean#L760-L792) — `formal-frontier-0520`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0520.lean`

- `[(Opens.grothendieckTopology X).HasSheafCompose (CategoryTheory.forget AddCommGrpCat.{u})]` (variable): The theorem takes this variable because `HPrimeIsoHOver`, used in `HPrimeIsoRestrictedResolutionSectionsHomology`, depends on it. But Mathlib already provides it as a global instance. `GrothendieckTopology.HasSheafCompose` is a `Prop` class (Mathlib/CategoryTheory/Sites/Whiskering.lean:43). The instance `hasSheafCompose_of_preservesLimitsOfSize` (Whiskering.lean:159) gives `J.HasSheafCompose F` from `[PreservesLimitsOfSize.{v₁, max u₁ v₁} F]`. For `C = Opens X` with `X : TopCat.{u}`, both u₁ and v₁ are `u`, so it needs `PreservesLimitsOfSize.{u,u} (forget AddCommGrpCat.{u})`. That is `AddCommGrpCat.forget_preservesLimitsOfSize`, the `to_additive` version of `CommGrpCat.forget_preservesLimitsOfSize`, which holds for arbitrary universes (Mathlib/Algebra/Category/Grp/Limits.lean:404). The higher-priority `hasSheafCompose_of_preservesMulticospan` also applies through the same preservation instance. The sibling library confirms that synthesis works: SchemeProperties/IdealSheafModule.lean:54 uses `GrothendieckTopology.HasSheafCompose.isSheaf` for `(Opens.grothendieckTopology X)` and `forget AddCommGrpCat` with no such hypothesis in scope. If the variable is deleted (e.g. with `omit ... in`), every occurrence in the statement and in the lemmas the proof calls (`HPrimeEquivHOver_naturality`, `restrictedAcyclicResolution_extPositiveIsoHomology_hom_naturality_assoc`, `restrictedResolutionHomComplexIsoSectionsHomology_hom_naturality`, …) is elaborated with the same global instance. Since the class is a Prop, the statement is unchanged up to proof irrelevance, and the same proof goes through. This can be deleted independently of the over-site instance below; both can also be deleted together.
- `[((Opens.grothendieckTopology X).over ((Opens.map f).obj V)).HasSheafCompose (CategoryTheory.forget AddCommGrpCat.{u})]` (variable): Same argument for the over-site. `Over ((Opens.map f).obj V)` is a category whose objects live in `Type u` and whose morphisms live in universe `u`. So `hasSheafCompose_of_preservesLimitsOfSize` (Mathlib/CategoryTheory/Sites/Whiskering.lean:159) again needs only `PreservesLimitsOfSize.{u,u} (forget AddCommGrpCat.{u})`. `AddCommGrpCat.forget_preservesLimitsOfSize` (Grp/Limits.lean:404, `to_additive`) provides it for all universe levels. Hence `((Opens.grothendieckTopology X).over W).HasSheafCompose (forget AddCommGrpCat.{u})` is synthesized globally. `HasSheafCompose` is a `Prop` class, so after deleting the variable the statement, which needs the instance through `HPrimeIsoHOver` / `restrictToOver` / `HPrimeIsoRestrictedResolutionSectionsHomology`, elaborates to a proof-irrelevantly identical term. The lemmas used in the proof pick up the same global instance, so the proof works unchanged.

### [`ProjectiveSpace.projectivizationToProj_injective`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/projective-space/blob/3d39919aac0def769a0ff8b44e62a1317d4c84fe/ProjectiveSpace/HomogeneousCoordinateClosedPoint.lean#L115-L151) — `formal-frontier-0544`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0544.lean`

- `[Fintype ι]` (variable): The theorem takes `[Fintype ι]` as an argument: it is instance-implicit, it mentions only `ι`, and `ι` appears in the statement. The `omit` before the theorem removes only `[IsAlgClosed k]`. The statement does not need `[Fintype ι]` to elaborate. In `ProjectiveSpace/HomogeneousCoordinatePoint.lean`, `projectivizationToProj`, `homogeneousCoordinatePoint` and `homogeneousCoordinateIdeal` are defined under `variable {k : Type u} [Field k] {ι : Type v}`, with no finiteness assumption (the file header says no finiteness hypothesis on `ι` is needed). The current proof uses `[Fintype ι]` only through `standardCoordinateChartMap`, the chart lemmas and `homogeneousCoordinatePoint_mem_basicOpen_X_iff`.

The statement still holds for arbitrary `ι`, by a direct algebraic argument. Suppose `homogeneousCoordinatePoint a ha = homogeneousCoordinatePoint b hb`. Taking `asHomogeneousIdeal` and then the underlying ideal gives `homogeneousCoordinateIdeal a = homogeneousCoordinateIdeal b`; each is the kernel of `homogeneousCoordinateMap`, which sends `X i ↦ C (a i) * X`.

1. Since `b ≠ 0`, pick `i` with `b i ≠ 0`. By `X_not_mem_homogeneousCoordinateIdeal`, `X i ∉ I_b = I_a`, so `a i ≠ 0` (if `a i = 0`, then `X i` maps to `0` by `homogeneousCoordinateMap_X`).
2. For any `j`, the polynomial `C (b i) * X j - C (b j) * X i` maps under `homogeneousCoordinateMap b` to `C (b i * b j - b j * b i) * X = 0`. So it lies in `I_b = I_a`. Its image under `homogeneousCoordinateMap a` is `C (b i * a j - b j * a i) * X = 0`, which gives `b i * a j = b j * a i`.
3. Hence `a j = (a i / b i) * b j` for every `j`. `Projectivization.mk_eq_mk_iff'` then gives the equality of projective points, as in the final step of the current proof.

The argument uses only `RingHom.mem_ker`, `homogeneousCoordinateMap_X` and field arithmetic; `ι` need not be finite. The degenerate cases are vacuous: if `ι` is empty, `ι → k` has no nonzero vectors, so the projectivization is empty and the map is trivially injective.

### [`ArtinSchreierTwistedLine.scheme_connectedSpace_of_ne_zero`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/GroupScheme/ArtinSchreierTwistedLine.lean#L644-L650) — `formal-frontier-0546`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0546.lean`

- `(hp : p.Prime)` (theorem): Since K is a field with [CharP K p], Mathlib's `CharP.char_is_prime_or_zero` gives `p.Prime ∨ p = 0`. If p is prime, the existing proof applies unchanged. If p = 0, `equation K 0 t = X 0 ^ 0 - X 0 - C t * X 1 ^ 0 = C (1 - t) - X 0`, so the coordinate ring is K[X₀,X₁]/(X₀ - c) with c = 1 - t. Via `MvPolynomial.finSuccEquiv` (which sends X 0 to Polynomial.X), this is (MvPolynomial (Fin 1) K)[X]/(X - C (C c)). By `Polynomial.quotientSpanXSubCAlgEquiv` that quotient is isomorphic to MvPolynomial (Fin 1) K, a domain. So CoordinateRing K 0 t is a domain, and the same `change ConnectedSpace (PrimeSpectrum _); infer_instance` step gives connectedness. The conclusion is therefore true for every p allowed by CharP K p, and hp can be dropped by splitting into these two cases.

### [`CategoryTheory.Functor.IsMittagLeffler.nonempty_sections_of_countable_cofinal`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-direct-limits/blob/1bf0740b01951905e0ba12bd5d2bdb67819c12d1/AlgebraicDirectLimits/MittagLeffler.lean#L63-L87) — `formal-frontier-0565`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0565.lean`

- `[Nonempty J]` (theorem): The theorem is still true when J is empty. Mathlib defines `F.sections` as `{u : ∀ j, F.obj j | ∀ {j j'} (f : j ⟶ j'), F.map f (u j) = u j'}`. If `J` is empty, then `Jᵒᵖ` is empty, so the unique dependent function `fun j => isEmptyElim j.unop` is a section: the compatibility condition holds vacuously. So `F.sections.Nonempty` holds with no further hypotheses. The current proof uses `[Nonempty J]` in only one place: the library lemma `IsCofinal.exists_monotone_nat` (CofinalSequence.lean) needs it to get a nonempty cofinal set. The fix is to start the proof with `cases isEmpty_or_nonempty J`. In the empty case, close the goal with the empty section above. In the nonempty case, run the existing proof unchanged, since the instance is now available. The other hypotheses are all needed. Without `IsDirectedOrder`, take J = {a, b, c, d} with c, d ≤ a, b and every F.obj equal to Bool. Let a→c, b→c and a→d be the identity and b→d be negation. All maps are bijective, so F is Mittag-Leffler, and J is finite, so it is a countable cofinal subset of itself. But a section would need x_a = x_b and x_a = ¬x_b, so there is none. Pointwise nonemptiness, the Mittag-Leffler condition and countability are the standard necessary assumptions.

### [`ContinuousCohomology.CorestrictionTransversal.cocyclesOneCrossedIso_mackey`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/continuous-group-cohomology/blob/65345921370bfc91ac84c049ba172360704e8bab/ContinuousGroupCohomology/Mackey.lean#L1487-L1531) — `formal-frontier-0620`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0620.lean`

- `[TopRep.JointlyContinuous X]` (theorem): Neither the statement nor the proof uses this. `cocyclesOneCrossedIso` (DegreeOne.lean:451) and the things it is built from (`cocyclesOneIso`, `oneKerCrossedIso`) are defined with no JointlyContinuous assumption. The same holds for `mackeyConjugateCrossed`, `mackeyCoefficientHom` and `mackeyStabilizerToLeft` (Mackey.lean:321-373, 1445), and for Mathlib's `cochainsMap`/`cocyclesMap` (ContCohomology/Functoriality.lean, whose only variables are Ring/TopologicalSpace). The local instance `jointlyContinuous_mackeyStabilizer` would only fire if some term needed JointlyContinuous, and none does. The proof uses `cocyclesOneCrossedIso_hom_apply`, `mackeyConjugateCrossed_apply` (rfl), `HomologicalComplex.cyclesMap_i` and `cochainsMap_one_apply` (Mackey.lean:1466). None of them takes JointlyContinuous, so the same proof goes through unchanged.
- `[LocallyCompactSpace H]` (theorem): No definition in the statement needs local compactness. That covers `cocyclesMap`, `cochainsMap`, `TopRep.homogeneousCochains`, `cocyclesOneCrossedIso`, `continuousCrossedHom` and `mackeyConjugateCrossed`. The lemmas the proof uses (`cocyclesOneCrossedIso_hom_apply`, `mackeyConjugateCrossed_apply`, `HomologicalComplex.cyclesMap_i`, `cochainsMap_one_apply`) don't take it either, so the same proof works without it.
- `[LocallyCompactSpace K]` (theorem): Same reason as for `[LocallyCompactSpace H]`: no definition in the statement and no lemma in the proof needs it, so the same proof works without it.
- `[LocallyCompactSpace (mackeyOpenStabilizer H K x)]` (theorem): Same reason as for `[LocallyCompactSpace H]`: `cochainsMap_one_apply` holds for any topological group J (here the stabilizer), with no local compactness. Nothing else in the statement or proof needs it, so the proof works unchanged without it.

### [`Ideal.exists_principal_prime_over_of_norm_natAbs_eq`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/dedekind-ideal-arithmetic/blob/136eb5854609e10bef1d1ea6bd6121e60ea37111/DedekindIdealArithmetic/Ideal/PrincipalPrime.lean#L25-L42) — `formal-frontier-0670`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0670.lean`

- `[Infinite S]` (variable): Implied by the other instances. `IsDedekindDomain S` makes S a domain, hence nontrivial. `Module.Free.infinite` (Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/FreeModule/Basic.lean: `theorem infinite [Infinite R] [Nontrivial M] : Infinite M`) applied with R = ℤ and `Module.Free ℤ S` gives `Infinite S`. Mathlib's AbsNorm.lean itself uses `local instance : Infinite S := Module.Free.infinite ℤ S`. The statement (`primesOver`, `Algebra.norm`, `Submodule.IsPrincipal`) does not need `Infinite S` to elaborate. The proof can start with `haveI : Infinite S := Module.Free.infinite ℤ S` and otherwise stay unchanged.
- `[Module.Free ℤ S]` (variable): `Algebra.norm ℤ α = LinearMap.det (lmul ℤ S α)`, and `LinearMap.det` is defined as `if ∃ s : Finset S, Nonempty (Basis s ℤ S) then … else 1`. Case split. If no such finite basis exists, `Algebra.norm_eq_one_of_not_exists_basis` gives norm 1, so `hnorm` yields p = 1, contradicting `hp : Nat.Prime p`; the theorem holds vacuously. This covers, e.g., S = ZMod q, which is Dedekind and finite over ℤ but not free. If a finite basis b exists, `Module.Free.of_basis b` provides the instance, and with it `Infinite S` via `Module.Free.infinite`, so the original proof applies. The statement does not mention the instance otherwise.
- `[Module.Finite ℤ S]` (variable): Same junk-value argument. If `¬Module.Finite ℤ S`, `Algebra.norm_eq_one_of_not_module_finite` (Mathlib/RingTheory/Norm/Defs.lean) gives `Algebra.norm ℤ α = 1`, so `hnorm` forces p = 1, contradicting `hp`. Otherwise the instance is available (by case analysis / `by_cases`) and the original proof (`absNorm_span_singleton`, `span_singleton_absNorm`, `isPrime_of_irreducible_absNorm`) goes through. The statement does not otherwise need this instance.

### [`ValuationRing.principalAdicCompletion_primeHeight_comap`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/adic-modules/blob/6f6fa8b3b08a9dba6faa90e328c8d528645f7d5b/AdicModules/PrincipalAdicSpectrum.lean#L227-L239) — `formal-frontier-0677`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0677.lean`

- `(ha : a ∈ IsLocalRing.maximalIdeal V)` (theorem): `ha` follows from `[IsHausdorff (Ideal.span {a}) V]` together with `[IsDomain V]`. `V` is a valuation domain, so it is a local ring. Suppose `a ∉ maximalIdeal V`. Then `IsLocalRing.notMem_maximalIdeal` (MaximalIdeal/Basic.lean:83) says `a` is a unit, and `Ideal.span_singleton_eq_top` gives `Ideal.span {a} = ⊤`. The Hausdorff instance then becomes `IsHausdorff ⊤ V`, and `IsHausdorff.subsingleton` (AdicCompletion/Basic.lean:182) makes `V` a subsingleton. That contradicts `Nontrivial V`, which comes from `IsDomain V`. So the new proof first recovers `ha` with `by_contra h; haveI := (Ideal.span_singleton_eq_top.mpr (IsLocalRing.notMem_maximalIdeal.mp h) ▸ ‹IsHausdorff (Ideal.span {a}) V›).subsingleton; exact not_subsingleton V` (or something close), then runs the existing proof. `ha` is mentioned nowhere else in the statement. The converse fails: `[IsHausdorff]` does not follow from `ha`, because Mathlib's `IsHausdorff.of_isDomain` needs `IsNoetherianRing`, and valuation rings are not Noetherian in general.
- `[IsFractionRing V K]` (theorem): The conclusion never mentions `K`. It only involves `V`, `a`, `AdicCompletion (Ideal.span {a}) V` and `P`. `K` is used only in the proof, to call `principalAdicCompletionPrimeSpectrumOrderIso K a ha0 ha`. Without `[IsFractionRing V K]`, the proof can call it with the canonical fraction field instead: `principalAdicCompletionPrimeSpectrumOrderIso (FractionRing V) a ha0 ha`. For a domain `V`, `FractionRing V` has standard Mathlib instances `Field`, `Algebra V (FractionRing V)` and `IsFractionRing V (FractionRing V)`. The definition is universe-polymorphic in `v`, so using `FractionRing V : Type u` is fine. The binders `K`, `[Field K]` and `[Algebra V K]` stay and simply go unused. Those three cannot be removed one at a time, because each is needed for a later binder to elaborate. Only `[IsFractionRing V K]` can be removed alone.

### [`AddMonoid.End.cyclicNorm_ker_eq_one_sub_range`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/group-theory/blob/2b14628cb53e79783bd51baadcb4fc85667c8da3/GroupTheory/CyclicNorm.lean#L29-L78) — `formal-frontier-0729`

- `(_hpositive : 0 < m)` (theorem): The proof never refers to `_hpositive`; the underscore prefix also marks it as unused. Each step relies on other facts. The inclusion range ⊆ ker uses `geom_sum_mul_neg` together with `hperiod`. The inclusion ker ⊆ range uses `mul_neg_geom_sum`, `hlift` and `hinjective`. None of these lemmas needs `0 < m`. The case m = 0 is still consistent: `hperiod` reads `sigma ^ 0 = 1`, which is trivially true. `hinjective` then says that `0 • ·` is injective, which forces `A` to be a subsingleton, and in that case both sides are ⊤. ### [`CategoryTheory.Subfunctor.leftTensorRestriction_injective`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/scheme-properties/blob/3455b4b9192b77c1754729bafa64f99ea8c5c838/SchemeProperties/SingleCoverGroup.lean#L310-L328) — `formal-frontier-0785`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0785.lean`

- `[W.HasPullbacks]` (variable): The proof needs this instance only because it calls `oneCoverExtensionEquiv`. That function builds the inverse (gluing a morphism out of D) with pullbacks, in `existsUnique_oneCover_glue` (around lines 214-217 of `SingleCoverExtension.lean`). The theorem only claims injectivity, which needs no gluing.

Proof without the instance. Keep the existing steps: cancel the epi `(λ_ F).inv`, then use `leftUnitor_inv_naturality D.ι` on `hφψ` to get `D.ι ≫ α = D.ι ≫ β`, where `α = (λ_ F).inv ≫ φ` and `β = (λ_ F).inv ≫ ψ` are maps `F ⟶ F`. Now show `α = β` directly. Take an object X and a section x ∈ F(X). By `hD X x` there are U, f : U ⟶ X with `W f` and `F.map f.op x ∈ D(U)`. By naturality, `F.map f.op (α x) = α (F.map f.op x) = (D.ι ≫ α) ⟨F.map f.op x, hx⟩`, and the same holds for β. Hence `F.map f.op (α x) = F.map f.op (β x)`. Then `(Presieve.isSeparatedFor_singleton (P := F)).mp (hF f hf).isSeparatedFor` gives `α x = β x`. This is the same pattern the file already uses at line 297 of `SingleCoverExtension.lean`.

This argument uses only `hD` and `hF`, so it also covers degenerate cases such as an empty C or empty sections. The instance can be dropped on its own, and it can also be dropped together with `[W.IsStableUnderBaseChange]`.
- `[W.IsStableUnderBaseChange]` (variable): Same reason as for `[W.HasPullbacks]`. The instance is only needed to build the inverse of `oneCoverExtensionEquiv`, where `W.pullback_snd` is used in `existsUnique_oneCover_glue`.

Injectivity of restriction along `D.ι` (after rewriting with the left unitor) has a direct proof. For x ∈ F(X), `hD` gives f : U ⟶ X in W with the restriction of x in D. The hypotheses `D.ι ≫ α = D.ι ≫ β` and naturality give `F.map f.op (α x) = F.map f.op (β x)`. Separatedness for the singleton presieve, from `hF f hf` and `Presieve.isSeparatedFor_singleton`, then gives `α x = β x`. No base change is involved.

The instance can be deleted on its own, or together with `[W.HasPullbacks]`.

### [`Module.Projective.free_of_countablyGenerated`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/projective-modules/blob/630c227210958554603cb3f949c6522133d38af3/ProjectiveModules/Module/CountableLocalProjectiveFree.lean#L137-L147) — `formal-frontier-0870`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0870.lean`

- `(hP : Module.CountablyGenerated Rᵐᵒᵖ P)` (theorem): Deleting `hP` gives exactly `Module.Projective.free_of_isUnit_compl_arbitraryRank`, which the same library proves in `ProjectiveModules/Module/ArbitraryLocalProjectiveFree.lean`. That theorem has the same `R`, `P`, `[Module.Projective Rᵐᵒᵖ P]`, `I`, `hI` and `hunit`, no countable-generation assumption, and the same conclusion `Module.Free Rᵐᵒᵖ P`. This is Kaplansky's theorem: every projective module over a local ring is free, whatever its rank.

Its proof calls this theorem only on the increments `F.increment j` of a countable invariant-coordinate filtration. Those increments are countably generated by `F.increment_countablyGenerated`. So the countable case is a helper step for the general result, and the order of the proof is not circular. To prove the statement without `hP`, prove the countable case first as a private lemma (the current two-line proof via `exists_sigma_fin_rightBasis_of_countablyGenerated`). Then run the filtration and transfinite-assembly argument of `free_of_isUnit_compl_arbitraryRank`, which uses `F.increment_projective` and `F.range_free_of_free_increments`.

Degenerate cases: if `P` is trivial, it is free with the empty basis. Nothing else depends on `hP`: it is not mentioned in any other binder or in the conclusion.

The other hypotheses are needed. Without `hI`, take `I = ⊤`: `hunit` becomes vacuous and `R` is an arbitrary ring, so projective modules need not be free. Without `hunit`, `R` is again arbitrary. Without projectivity, the statement fails for non-free modules such as a nonzero quotient of a field by nothing… more concretely, any non-free module over a local ring, e.g. `ℤ/p²` viewed as a module over the localization of `ℤ` at `p` is not free.

### [`Matrix.normalizedRowRank_mem_Icc`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/stable-range/blob/c283b01ed9ebc33c7eefcdb4f3d2ca45141f469e/StableRange/DivisionRingRank.lean#L370-L379) — `formal-frontier-0887`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0887.lean`

- `[Nonempty n]` (variable): `normalizedRowRank A` is defined (line 338) as `(rowRank A : ℝ) / Fintype.card n`. The proof uses `[Nonempty n]` only through the private lemma `card_cast_pos`, which gives `0 < card n`. That fact is not needed. For the lower bound, `div_nonneg (Nat.cast_nonneg _) (Nat.cast_nonneg _)` works. For the upper bound, `rowRank_le_card A` (line 63, stated before the `PositiveSize` section, so it does not use `Nonempty`) gives `(rowRank A : ℝ) ≤ card n` after a cast. Then Mathlib's `div_le_one_of_le₀ (h : a ≤ b) (hb : 0 ≤ b) : a / b ≤ 1` (Mathlib/Algebra/Order/GroupWithZero/Basic.lean) applies with `hb := Nat.cast_nonneg _`. Degenerate case: if `n` is empty, `card n = 0` and the value is `0 / 0 = 0 ∈ Icc 0 1`, so the statement still holds. The library itself already uses `omit [Nonempty n] in` for nearby lemmas such as `normalizedRowRank_zero`.

### [`FrobeniusTwistedLine.SatisfiesEquation.add`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/GroupScheme/FrobeniusTwistedLine.lean#L412-L418) — `formal-frontier-0947`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0947.lean`

- `[Fact p.Prime]` (theorem): SatisfiesEquation p t x y is `y ^ p - algebraMap K A t * x ^ p = 0`. Without primality the result still holds because A is an algebra over a field K. Case A is trivial (Subsingleton): the conclusion is an equation in A, so it holds trivially. Case A is nontrivial: `Algebra.charP_iff K A p` (Mathlib/Algebra/CharP/Algebra.lean; needs Field K, CommSemiring A, Nontrivial A) turns CharP A p into CharP K p. Then `CharP.char_is_prime_or_zero` (Mathlib/Algebra/CharP/Defs.lean; K is a field, so it has no zero divisors and is nontrivial) gives p.Prime ∨ p = 0. If p is prime, build `Fact p.Prime` from that and run the original proof (add_pow_char etc.) unchanged. If p = 0, then by pow_zero SatisfiesEquation 0 t x y reduces to `1 - algebraMap K A t * 1 = 0`, which does not involve x or y, so h₁ itself proves the conclusion. Composite characteristics such as ZMod 4, where additivity would fail, cannot occur: no field K has an algebra map into such a ring.

### [`AlgebraicGeometry.EquivalenceRelation.finrankOpen_equalizer_isLocalizationAway_of_basicOpen_eq`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/algebraic-groups/blob/1e9a9adc519130a217a8622576f8ec07d0b62027/AlgebraicGroups/Scheme/EquivalenceRelationRankEqualizer.lean#L116-L158) — `formal-frontier-0957`

Snippet: `samples/formal-frontier/formal-frontier-0957.lean`

- `[IsAffine X]` (variable): The statement does not need it to elaborate. `finrankOpen` and `preimage_finrankOpen_eq` need only Flat, IsFinite and LocallyOfFinitePresentation. `appTop_restriction_square(_of_eq)` (ClopenAffine.lean) work for any morphism. The current proof uses it only because it goes through `exists_finrankOpen_equalizer_isLocalizationAway` and the affine-only `Scheme.Opens.basicOpen_injOn_isIdempotentElem`. Direct proof for an arbitrary scheme, using `he`: (1) Let W = X.basicOpen (1-e). At every point the germs of e and 1-e add up to 1 in a local ring, so one is a unit; hence U ∪ W = ⊤. Since e(1-e)=0, U ∩ W = ⊥. An idempotent that is a unit equals 1, so e restricts to 1 on U and to 0 on W. (2) By the sheaf property, restriction Γ(X,⊤) → Γ(X,U) is surjective (glue a section with 0 on W), and x|U = y|U ↔ e*x = e*y. So Mathlib's `IsLocalization.away_of_isIdempotentElem_of_mul` gives IsLocalization.Away e Γ(X,U). (3) `Scheme.preimage_basicOpen_top` gives V = p₁⁻¹ᵁ U = R.basicOpen (p₁.appTop e), and p₁.appTop e is idempotent, so the same argument gives IsLocalization.Away (s e) Γ(R,V). (4) `AlgHom.equalizerMapOfCommuting_isLocalizationAway` (AlgebraicGroups/RingTheory/Equalizer.lean, no affineness) with hs, ht and he then gives exactly the conclusion. This is not jointly deletable with `he`, which this argument uses.
- `(he : IsIdempotentElem e)` (theorem): Keeping [IsAffine X]: R is affine too, since IsFinite p₁ makes p₁ an affine morphism. Write A = Γ(X,⊤), B = Γ(R,⊤), E = equalizer s t. By `IsAffineOpen.isLocalization_basicOpen` and hbasic, Γ(X,U) = Γ(X, X.basicOpen e) is IsLocalization.Away e over A. By `Scheme.preimage_basicOpen_top`, V = R.basicOpen (s e), so Γ(R,V) is the localization B_{s e}. Because e ∈ E we have s e = t e, so s - t : A → B is E-linear. Localizing the exact sequence 0 → E → A → B at the powers of e over E (localization is exact) gives E_e ≅ ker(A_e → B_{se}) = equalizer sU tU, compatibly with φ. Concretely: if a/eⁿ is in the local equalizer then s(e)^m(s a − t a) = 0, so e^m a ∈ E (surjectivity). If x ∈ E and eⁿx = 0 in A, the same holds in E (injectivity). Images of e are units. Idempotence is never used. This is not jointly deletable with [IsAffine X]: the argument for deleting [IsAffine X] uses idempotence.
